# Nanmukan Tiruvandhadi

## 1. Nanmuganai

*nārāyaṇan – śrīman nārāyaṇa (nārāyaṇa, consort of śrī mahālakśmi) nānmuganai padaittān – created brahmā vho has four faces nānmuganum – tat brahmā too tāṇ mugamāy – keeping himself as te primary entity sankaranai – rudra tān padaittān – created himself yān mugamāy – ī, keeping myself as primary entity āḻ poruḷai – tese meanings vhic are difficult to knov andādi mēlittu – in te form of andādi (anta + ādi – a form of poetry vherein te last letter/vord/line of one verse becomes te first letter/vord/line of next verse) aṟivittēn – instructed nīr – you (vho are listening to tis) tērndu – analysing sindāmal – ensuring tat tese meanings do not slip out koḷmin – retain in your mind*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**nānmuganai nāyāyaṇan padaittān – sarvēśvaran (supreme being) is called by the unique name of nārāyaṇa since he is the refuge for the nāra samūha (nārā: – collection of all the jīvātmās and acētana objects) and has the nārā: as his refuge; also, he is the means as well as the end benefit for the jīvātmās. Such nārāyaṇa created nānmugan (brahmā) with four heads to pervade in all four directions and carry out creation and to keep reciting simultaneously the four vēdas which aid in the creation.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the first pāsuram, āḻvār instructs others (samsāris or dwellers in materialistic realm) that brahmā and Siva were described by vēdas (spiritual texts) as  created entities and that just as they were primary entities for the expansion of samsāram (materialistic realm), he (āḻvār) is determined to instruct the samsāris the rare meanings, asking them to uplift themselves through this instruction. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: nānmuganai nārāyaṇan padaittān nānmuganum tānmugamāy sankaranaittān padaittān yāṇmugamāy andādi mēlittu aṟivittēn āḻporuḷai sindāmal koṇminīr tērndu*

**nārāyaṇan** – this is the unique name for supreme being which manifests his all pervading quality, his being the sustenance for all, his being the means for śrīvaikuṇṭam and his being the final benefit for those who attain him.

**nānmuganai** – this is the name for the one who has creation alone as his responsibility and who is controlled by his senses.

**padaittān** – created all the primary entities of universe as well as the creator \[brahmā\]. nārāyaṇōpaniśat says “*nārāyaṇād brahmā jāyatē*” (brahmā was born out of nārāyaṇa); subhālōpaniśat says “*tatra brahmā caturmukhōjāyatē*” (there, brahmā, with four faces, was born); nārāyaṇavalli says “*yō brahmāṇam vidadādipūrvam*” (…one who created brahmā earlier…); muṇḍakōpaniśat says “*brahmā dēvānām pratamas sambhabhūva*” (bhramā was born ahead of all dēvas); mahōpaniśat says “*yannābhipadmādabhavan mahātmā prajāpati:*” (from whose navel like lotus the great ātmā brahmā was born); at the end of vēdas it says “*sa prajāpatirēka: puśkaraparṇē sambhavat*” (prajāpati \[brahmā\] was born on a lotus). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) mercifully mentions about such brahmā as having been created by nārāyaṇa.

**nānmuganum tān mugamāy sankaranaittān padaittān** – that brahmā, keeping himself as the primary entity, created Siva. āḻvār reminisces the words mentioned in vēdas such as “*virūpākśāya brahmaṇa: putrāya jyēśtāya*” (to virūpākśa \[rudra\] who is the first son of brahmā), “*tryakśa: śūlabhāṇi: puruśō’jāyata*” (having trident, the puruśa (jīvātmā) with three eyes was born), “*samvatsarē kumārō’jāyata*” (in a year (at one point of time) rudra was born as a son to brahmā) etc.

**tānmugamāyp padaittān** – after receiving instruction about vēdas from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), without having the need to approach emperumān for any clarification, brahmā made himself as the primary entity and created Siva. We can also construe this as “*lalātāt krōdajō rudra:*” (rudra was born out of the forehead of brahmā).

**yān mugamāy andādi mēlittu aṟivittēn āḻporuḷai** – just as brahmā, as the primary entity, created for the expansion of samsāram, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that he is going to instruct these difficult-to-know meanings for its annihilation, on his own, without  the jīvātmās asking his for it.

**andādi mēlittu aṟivittēn** – just as one engraves on copper plate and stones, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that he is writing these meanings in the form of an *andādi* (a classification of poems).

**āḻporuḷai aṟivittēn** – āḻvār says that he magnanimously gave these meanings which are similar to gemstones in the ocean of *śruti* (vēdas) and *smruti* (purāṇas and itihāsas), being unable to bear the sorrows of samsāris.

**āḻporuḷai andādi mēlittu aṟivittēn** – this is like embedding ruby stones in gold.

**āḻporuḷai** – from the fact that brahmā, Siva et al were created, it is clear that they will get destroyed one day. *āḻporuḷ* refers to the fact that they get destroyed. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that he is mentioning that brahmā, Siva et al are entities who get destroyed and it is this meaning that he wants to convey.

**nīr tērndu** **sidnhāmal koṇmin** – āḻvār tells samsāris to analyse the lowliness of samsāram and the greatness of what he is saying, and being intelligent, think about which is more apt for themselves, without getting distracted.

**sindāmal koṇmin** – do not let go of the gemstone that I have donated and hold it firmly! He is praying to samsāris in a way which is similar to one falling at the feet of another to take milk.

An interesting meaning is given by vaikuṇṭavāsi śrī prativādi bhayankaram aṇṇangarācāryar svāmy for the usage of the past tense in [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/)’s last line: *aṟivittēn āḻporuḷai* – āḻvār says that he has announced the inner meanings even before he starts conveying them. This is because he feels that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), being *satya sankalpan* (one who fulfils what he sets out to do), wants to convey the meanings through him and also because he is firm in his mind that through his pāsurams (hymns) he is going to establish the supremacy of emperumān and hence he has decided that he has already conveyed the meanings. In tamiḻ grammar, this is referred to as *kālavaḻuvamaidi* (due to one’s clarity of mind, conveying what is going to happen as having already happened).

We will go on to the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 2. Terungal devan

*tērungāl – if one vere to analyse dēvan – supreme being oruvanē enṟu uraippar – (vise men) vill say tat tere is only one avan perumai – te greatness of tat sarvēśvaran ārum aṟiyār – no one knovs ōrum poruḷ mudivum ittanaiyē – te meaning establiśed by analysing (vēdas and itihāsas) is only tis muc ettavam seydārkkum – for all vho have carried out penance to vhatever extent aruḷ mudivadu – te final benefit of mercy āḻiyān pāl – from emperumān vho has divine disc*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**terungāl dēvan oruvanē enṟu uraippar – if one were to analyse with the assistance of pramānas (authentic texts) the wise sages will say that the supreme being is only one and that he is śrīman nārāyaṇa. The wise sages established that “tatvam jijñāsamānānām hētubis sarvatōmukhai: I tatvamēkō mahāyōgī harir nārāyaṇa smruta: īī” (those who desire to know the supreme being think that it is only nārāyaṇa who is a mahā yōgi and is known by the name hari, who is the only supreme being, for a variety of reasons). Thus āḻvār says that the claim of samsāris that there are three supreme beings is incorrect.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After āḻvār mentioned in the previous pāsuram that brahmā, rudra et al were created by nārāyaṇa, he was asked by some samsāris “It is well known in the world that brahmā, viṣṇu and Siva are the three deities for creation, protection and annihilation respectively; thus, while it is apt to accept that all three are supreme beings, could you say that only nārāyaṇa is the supreme being?”. āḻvār responds in this pāsuram through the path of itihāsams (epics). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: tērungāl dēvan oruvanē enṟuraippar ārum aṟiyār avan perumai ōrum poruḷ mudivum ittanaiyē ettavam seydārkkum aruḷ mudivadu āḻiyān pāl*

**ārum aṟiyār avan perumai** – no one can estimate his greatness

**ōrum poruḷ mudivum ittanaiyē** – the established meaning of all that has been mentioned in śrutis (vēdas or sacred texts) and smrutis (itihāsas and purāṇas or epics and ancient histories) is only this much. Many quotations have been given to establish this statement, as detailed below:

**vēda vākyas: (quotations from sacred texts)**

- *divyō dēva ēkō nārāyaṇa* – nārāyaṇa is the only divine dēva
- *īśatē dēva ēka:* – dēva alone controls cit (sentient) and acit (insentient) entities
- *ēkō dēvas sarvabhūtēśu gūda:* – there is only one dēva who is hidden in all creations
- *ēka svatantrō jagatō babhūva:* – there is only one independent lord for the entire universe
- *bhūtasya jāta*: *patirēka āsīt* – he was the only lord for all creations
- *ēkō ha vai nārāyaṇa āsīt* – only nārāyaṇa was there at the beginning

**smruti vākyas (quotations from itihāsas and purāṇas)**

- *ēka śāstā na dvitīyō’sti kaścit* – there is only one lord; no one else
- *ēkas tiśtati viśvātmā sa ca nārāyaṇa*: – only nārāyaṇa who is the ātmā for the entire universe, remains at the end
- *ēka Eva janārdana*: – it is only janārdana who carries out all the three primary functions

**ārum aṟiyār avan perumai ōrum poruḷ mudivum ittanaiyē** – Has it not been mentioned that the greatness of supreme being is not known to brahmā, rudra, great sages such as sanaka et al and to himself \[emperumān\] who is the controller of all, in various authentic texts!

**vēdas (sacred texts) say:**

- *ka ittā vēda yatra sa*: – who knows his basic nature and qualities
- *sō anga vēda yadi vā na vēda* – does he know or not know (his own greatness)
- *yasyāmatam tasya matam* – one who does not know brahmam (supreme being) knows brahmam
- *yatō vācō nivarttantē* – after not attaining whom, the words returned
- *I tē viṣṇō jāyamānō na jātō dēva mahimna: paramantamāpa* – Oh viṣṇu!  No one among those who is taking birth, who has been born (and who is going to be born) knows the limit of your greatness
- *ētāvā nasya mahimā atō jyāyāmśca pūruśa:* – is this all his greatness? Is that supreme being not greater than these!

**śrī rāmāyaṇam**

- *tvam hi lōka gatirdēva na tvām kēsana jānatē* – Oh dēva! You are the refuge for the world,; none knows you

**mahābhāratam mōkśa darmam**

- *prajāpatinca rudrancāpyahamēva srujāmi vai* I *tau ca mām na vijānītō mama māyāprahamōtau* – only I create brahmā and rudra. They too, who are bewildered by my māyā (prakruti or primordial matter) do not know me
- *na sa śakyas tvayā draśtum mayānyair vāpi sattama* I *sarvēśām sākśibhūtō’sau na grāhya: kēnacit kvacit* – Oh child rudra! That supreme being is not apt to be known by you or by me (brahmā) or by anyone else. He knows everything, being the witness to everything. This supreme being is not capable of being known by anyone at any point of time.

**viṣṇusdarmam** says “*na yasya rūpam na balaprabhāvau na ca svabhāva: paramasya pumsa:* I *vijñāyatē sarvapitāmahādyais tam vāsudēvam praṇamāmyacintyam*” – I salute that vāsudēva whose divine form and basic nature are not known to brahmā, rudra et al and who cannot be known.

**viṣṇu purāṇam**

- *yannāyam bhagavān brahmā jānāti paramam padam* I  *tannatā: sma jagaddāma tava sarvagatācyuta* īī – Oh acyuta! We salute that divine form of yours, being the final refuge and sustenance for the entire universe, which bhagavān brahmā doesn’t know.
- *yan na dēvā na munayō nacāham na ca Sankara:* I  *jānanti paramēśasya tad viṣṇōr paramam padam* īī – we salute that viṣṇu who, being the final refuge and supreme lord, who is not known by dēvas (celestial entities), sages, me (brahmā) and Sankara.

**varāha purāṇam** says “*adyāpi mām na jānanti rudrēndrās sapitāmahā:*” (brahmā, rudra, indra et al do not know me even today)

[**śrī bhagavat gītā**](http://githa.koyil.org/)

- *nāham prkāśas sarvasya yōgamāyāsamāvruta:* I  *mūdō’yam nābi jānāti lōkō māmajamavyayam* īī – I, covered by the yōgamāyā (primordial matter), will not be visible to all. This ignorant world will not know me, the birtless and indelible entity)
- *mām tu vēda na kaścana* – no one knows me

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) was asked “We hear that brahmā, rudra et al grant great benefits to those who carry out penance towards them; hence, can we not take it that they are also supreme beings?” He responds..

**ettavam seydārkkum aruḷ mudivadu āḻiyān pāl** – irrespective of the extent of penance carried out by anyone, the final benefit is granted by the one who carries the divine disc. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that even if one carries out penance towards brahmā, rudra et al, the benefit is granted by śrīman nārāyaṇa, as the indvelling soul of brahmā, rudra et al.

**aruḷ mudivu adu** – those benefits which are accrued through (emperumān’s) mercy.

**ettavam …. ōrum poruḷ mudivum ittanaiyē** – if one were to examine the vēdas and smrutis, it would be clear that for those who carry out means (for attaining emperumān) the benefit is attained through nārāyaṇa only. In bhavruca śruti it is said “*asya dēvasya mīduśō vayā viṣṇōrēśa pravrutē  havirbhī: vidēhi rudrōrudriyam mahattvam*” (rudra attained his greatness only after carrying out penance towards viṣṇu). The same meaning has been quoted in mahābhārata which says “*mayā sruśta: purā brahmā math yajñamayajat svayam* I *tatas tasmai varān prītō tadāMyahamanuttamān* īī *matputratvanca kalpādau lōkādyakśatvamēva ca* īī ” (once upon a time, brahmā, who was created by me, carried out a ritual relating to me. Being pleased by that, I granted him the superior boons of being my son and being the head of universe) and “*mahādēvas sarvamēdē mahātmā hūtvātmānam dēvadēvō babhūva*” (in the sarvamēda yajña (ritual), mahādēva (rudra) offered himself as the sacrificial material and became dēvadēva)

Thus, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/), in this pāsuram, established that nārāyaṇa is the supreme lord and brahmā, rudra et al get their benefits granted by nārāyaṇa only.

We will take up the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 3. Palil kidandaduvum

*pālil kidandaduvum – reclining on tiruppāṟkadal, te milky ocean paṇḍu arangam mēyaduvuam – at an earlier point of time, dvelling in tiruvarangam (śrīrangam) ālil tuyinṟaduvum – sleeping on a tender banyan leaf jñālattu oruporuḷai – one vho is te only causative factor for te vorlds vānavar tam meypporuḷai – one vho is śining radiantly to te nityasūris (permanent dvellers of śrivaikuṇṭam) appil aru poruḷai – (during te time of creation) te rare entity, emperumān, vho is lying on vater yān aṟindavāṟu – as ī knov him to be ār aṟivār – vho knovs?*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**pālil kidandaduvum – being the controller of nityasūris and the causative factor for the worlds, emperumān, in order to uplift some cētanas (jīvātmās) from samsāram, thinking of taking several incarnations, lies in the milky ocean in a meditative posture.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “Is it apt on your part to say that ārum aṟiyār – (no one knows his \[emperumān’s\] greatness) as this implies that you know about him. He responds saying “ārum aṟiyār meant that no one has fully estimated his greatness. There are many who know a little bit of his greatness. But there is none among them who knows as much as I do. Those who attempt to know him through their efforts would know a little bit about his supremacy. Since he, with causeless mercy, showed himself to me, I alone know his great qualities such as simplicity etc”. Alternatively, it can be construed that he is saying “When it comes to the question of those who are carrying out means on their own to attain him, is it possible for them to know about as much as I do, who had seen him when he chose to show himself?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: pālil kidandaduvum paṇḍarangam mēyaduvum ālil tuyinṟaduvum āraṟivār jñālat toruporuḷai vānavar tam meypporuḷai appil aruporuḷai yān aṟindavāṟu*

**paṇḍarangam mēyaduvum** – dwelling aptly at trivarangam which is considered as the upper limit for emperumān’s incarnations.

**paṇḍu** – at a time when the entire area was like a forest and there was none expecting him, emperumān had reclined there with the desire “will not someone at some point of time attain me?” – the anguish of the protector more than that of the protected.

**arangam mēyaduvum** – fitting into arangam better than at śrīvaikuṇṭam. Thinking of the troubles faced by samsāris in this world, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) feels for them in his divine heart and with that warmth, comes and dwells here. When [paraśāra bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) was mercifully explaining this, he was asked by a follower “is it apt for someone who is full of happiness in śrīvaikuṇṭam to feel sorrowful?” to which bhattar responded quoting śrī rāmāyaṇa ślōkam “*vyasanēśu manuśyāṇām bhruśam bh*avati *dukkita:*” (when people (of his kingdom) suffer, śrī rāma suffers more than they). [nampiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nampillai/), in his īdu commentary for tiruvāimoḻi 4.10.2 has mercifully mentioned “if there is no way of showering his mercy, emperumān vouldn’t have created [cētanas](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) (sentient entities – the [jīvātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)) \[creating cētanas implies giving them bodies\], as he had already negated any happening for himself out of karmas (virtuous and sinful deeds) ; has it not been said ‘*sa ēkākī na ramēta*’ – nārāyaṇa was not happy at the beginning, when he was alone, even when the nityavibhūti and all his auspicious qualities were already present!”

**ālil tuyinṟaduvum** – sleeping on a tender banyan leaf after keeping all the worlds in his stomach and protecting them at the time of deluge and the cētanas were threatened

**āraṟivār** – who will know that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has such soft qualities? One may comprehend, to an extent, his greatness. It is not possible to understand his simplicity.

**yān aṟindavāṟu āraṟivār** – would anyone know, out of his own efforts, the qualities such as simplicity etc of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s as much as I, who had been manifested these by emperumān himself, would know?

When asked whether emperumān has only simplicity, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says next that even his greatness has no limits. Only if simplicity and greatness are together would it shine radiantly like a gemstone studded in gold.

**jñālattu oru poruḷai** – the unique entity which is the causative factor for the creation of the worlds as mentioned in the upaniśats such as “*sadēva sōmyēdamagra āsīt ēkamēva advatīyam*” (Oh sōmadēva! There was only one entity at the beginning and none else) and “*ēkō ha vai nārāyaṇa āsīt na brahmā nēśāna:*” (only nārāyaṇa was there at the beginning; not brahmā, not Siva)

**jñālattu oru poruḷai** – the only entity who stood as the entire worlds – as being joined with subtle forms in the beginning (during deluge when all the entities, both sentient and insentient, were part of his form, with no form or name of their own) and being joined with gross entities later on (during creation when the sentient and insentient had forms and names of their own)

**appil aru poruḷai** – the causative factor for the worlds, who created the element *appu* (water) initially, and reclined on it.

**yān aṟindavāṟu yār aṟivār** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) asks as to who knows such [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as much as he knows. Those who know his greatness do not know his simplicity and those who know his simplicity do not know his greatness. āḻvār says that he is the only one who knows both. Those who attain him on their own do not know of his simplicity. Those who worship other deities do not know of his greatness. āḻvār says that he alone knows both the qualities of emperumān.

We will move on to the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 4. Aru sadaikkarandan

*āṟu – te river gangā (Ganges) sadai – in his matted hair karandān – rudra is bearing it (gangā) in a hidden faśion aṇḍar kōn tannōdum – vit bhagavān vho is te lord of all dēvas kūṟu udaiyan enbaduvum – te vords spoken (by ignorant people) tat he is equal koḷgaittē – could it be approved? (ṇo) vēṟu oruvar illāmai ninṟānai – one vho excels in not having anyone to compare vit epporutkum sollānai – one vho has every vord used for referring to all te tings as referring to himself (tis is called as sarvaśabda vācyan – all sounds referring to him) emmānai – emperumān toguttuc connēn – ī mentioned briefly*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**āḻvār says while that there are authentic proofs in vēdas that Siva is the causative factor, he is asking the followers of Siva as to why they are not analysing whether this word Siva refers to the one on whom they have lot of affection.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the first pāsuram, āḻvār ascertained that it is nārāyaṇa who is the cause for the birth of brahmā, rudra et al. In the second pāsuram he said that since there is only one supreme being, three cannot become causative factor for the worlds. In the third pāsuram he said that only he knows the greatness as well as the simplicity of emperumān. At this point of time, devotees of Siva asked him: ēkō hi rudrō na dvītīyāya tastu: – rudra is only one; there is no one else yō dēvānām prabavaś cōdbhayaśca viśvādikō rudrō maharśi: I hiraṇyagarbham janayāmāsa pūrvam sa nōbhuddyā śubayā samyunaktu īī – let that rudra who is the causative factor for all dēvas (celestial entities), who is the cause for their development, who is the lord for the universe, who is omniscient and who initially gave birth to brahmā, grant us good knowledge tasmāt sarva gata: Siva: – thus Siva is the refuge for all sarvasya prabhum īśānam – Siva who is the lord of all… māyinam tu mahēśvaram – know that mahēśvaran is amazing jñātvā śivam śāntimadyantamēti – he attains peace after knowing Siva śivamsarva bhūtēśu gūdam – Siva who is hidden in all creations… Siva Eva kēvala: – only Siva was there īśas sarvādipatyam kurutē – īśan is the master of all bhāvābhāvakaram śivam – Siva who carries out creation and destruction… tamīśvarāṇām paramam mahēśvaram – he who is the mahēśvara (great controller) of all īśvaras.. Siva ēkō dyēya: – Siva alone is apt to be meditated upon ahamēka pratamamāsam varttāmi ca bhaviśyāmi ca nānya: kaścin mattō vyatirikta: – (I, Siva alone, was there at the beginning; am present now; will be there in future. There is no one other than myself) These followers of Siva asked āḻvār “While vēdas, at many instances, proclaim loudly that Siva is the causative factor for all the worlds and that he is the indvelling soul for all, could you say that nārāyaṇa alone is the causative factor for all the worlds? Since nārāyaṇa has been proclaimed in the vēdas in a few instances as the causative factor for the worlds and since all the above mentioned instances, quoted from vēdas, mention that Siva is the supreme being, it will be apt to consider that both are supreme beings. This being the case, how can your words ‘only I know his (nārāyaṇa’s) supremacy’ be taken at face value?”. āḻvār tells them in this pāsuram the way by which he knew that nārāyaṇa is the supreme being. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: āṟu sadaik karandān aṇḍarkōn tannōdum kūṟudaiyan enbaduvum koḷgaittē vēṟoruvan illāmai ninṟānai emmānai epporutkum sollānaic connēn toguttu Word for Word Meanings*

**āṟu sadaikkarandān** – Siva bore the river gangā, when brahmā washed the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) \[during trivikrama avatāram\] with it, in order to remove the impurities in himself. It is apt to recall what brahmā had said in īśvara samhitai “*dravibhūtas tadā darmō haribhaktyā mahāmunē* I *gruhītvā dharma pānīyam padam nātasya tuśtayē* īī *kśālitam parayā bhaktyā pādyārghyādibhir ārcitam* I *tadambu patitam druśtvā dadāra śirasā hara:*  īī *pāvanārttam jatā madyē yōjñōsmītya vadāraṇāt* I *varśāyutānyata bahūn na mumōca tadā hara:* īī” (Oh the great riśi!  During that time (when emperumān measured the worlds) darmam (righteousness) out of devotion to hari (emperumān) became a flowing water. Using that divine water, which is dharma, in order to make jagannāta (lord of the worlds, emperumān) happy, his divine feet were washed by me (brahmā).  He was vorśipped with pādyam (washing the divine feet), arjñam (washing the divine hands) etc. Looking at the water which was falling off emperumān’s divine feet, Siva determined that he is apt (to be purified) and bore that water on his head in order to purify himself. After that, for ten thousand years, rudra did not let go of that water).

**āṟu sadaikkarandān** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) is asking whether it is not proper to enquire whether rudra had the purity which is denoted by the word Siva.

**sadai** – the word *sadai* (matted hair) denotes a person who is observing penance towards a goal and hence it clearly points out that rudra is not the supreme entity.

**sadaik karandān** – he bore the water from ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s) divine feet on his matted hair.

**karandān** – just as people hide huge treasure inside trunks, Siva hid the water from emperumān’s divine feet in his matted hair.

**aṇḍarkōn tannōdum** – along with [nārāyaṇa](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who stood as the lord of dēvas due to this act of measuring the worlds.

**kūṟudaiyan enbaduvum koḷgaittē** – is it apt to accept that gangādara (one who bore gangā) is equivalent to kēśava in terms of qualities such as being a śēśi (one who accepts services from his followers), being the causative factor for all the worlds, having the ability to pervade all entities, having the ability to grant mōkśam (liberating souls from samsāram and enabling them to ascend to śrīvaikuṇṭam) etc? Did not vēda say “*ēka: prāsīsarat pādam ēka: prācīkśalan mudā* I *aparō’pyadaran mūrdnā kōdikas tēśugaṇyatām*  īī” (one raised his divine feet to high levels; one washed he divine feet; one bore the water falling from the divine feet on his head; you think and decide on who, among these three, is supreme).

When asked as to what is the reason for emphasising that Siva is not equivalent to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) on qualities such as being the lord etc, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says…

**vēṟoruvar illāmai ninṟānai** – isn’t he (emperumān) one who does not have anyone equal or superior to him? emperumān himself says in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7.6](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-6/) “*aham krutsnasya jagata: prabhava: praḷayas tatā* I *matta: parataram nānyat kincitasti danajaya:* īī” (I stand as the causative factor for the creation and destruction of the worlds; there is none superior to me) and [śrī bhagavat gītā 15.18](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/15-18/) “*yasmāt kśaramatītō’hamakśarādapi cōttama:* I *atō’smi lōkē vēdē ca pratita: puruśōttama:* īī” (being superior to kśara puruśa (insentient entities) and akśara puruśa (sentient entities) I am proclaimed as puruśōttama (best among all entities) in vēdas, epics and ancient histories). riśis (sages) say “*na daivam kēśavāt param*” (there is none superior to kēśava) and “*na param puṇḍarīkākśāt*” (there is none superior to puṇḍarīkśa (one with lotus like eyes)). vēdas say “*paramam prabhum nārāyaṇam*” ([nārāyaṇa](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is without anyone superior to him) and “*na tat samaścābhyadigaśca druśyatē*” (there is none who is seen to be equivalent or superior to him).When asked who is such an entity, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) responds …

**emmānai** – one who is my lord. This is in keeping with tirumaḻisai āḻvār’s nature of not vorśipping any other deity even by mistake. We can consider this as “one who made me, who is very lowly, as his servitor”

When āḻvār was asked “While it is mentioned in different places \[in vēdas\] that paramātmā is denoted by the sounds “*Siva, rudra, īśan, īśvaran, mahēśvaran et al*” how is it that you are saying that only [nārāyaṇa](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the supreme being?” he responds saying

**epporutkum sollānai** – one who is denoted by all the sounds (words). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says “īsn’t he referred to in vēdas as the supreme being with words such as *ākāśam, jyōti, indra, rudra, Siva, ēsa, īśvara, mahēśvara, puruśa, puruśōttama, uttama puruśa, viṣṇu, hari, acyuta, ananta, nārāyana, trivikrama, vāmana et al*?”

**epporutkum sollānai** – he is the direct reference for all sounds and the in dwelling soul for the meanings of all such sounds.  Let us consider the sound *Siva.* Since the purity which is referred to by this word is naturally present in [nārāyaṇa](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), in words such as *Siva Eva kēvala*: (only Siva was there), nārāyaṇa is directly indicated by the word Siva. In upaniśats such as atarvaśiras, in identities which are related to rudra, the word *Siva* refers to rudra and to his *antaryāmi* (indvelling soul) nārāyaṇa. Thus, in both ways (directly and through the status of being *antaryāmi*) nārāyaṇa is the meaning for all sounds.

**epporutkum sollānai** – similar to sounds such as rudra, Siva etc, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is also referred to by sounds such as ākāśa, prāṇa, indra etc. If one were to make Siva as supreme based on the sound Siva, one has to make ākāśa etc as supreme based on the sound ākāśa etc. If one were to say that sounds such as ākāśa etc have impurities in them, the same has to be said of *āṟu sadaikkarandān* also. Hence it will be correct to identify names such as ākāśa along with names such as Siva without any distinction with [nārāyaṇa](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). If one were to ask as to why sounds such as nārāyaṇa cannot be imputed to Siva, the reason will be that names such as nārāyaṇa are more famous than Siva, both in terms of sound and meaning. In other words, vēdas mention Siva, well known in the world, as having been born, as having his sins not removed and having several lowliness in him. Hence, names such as nārāyaṇa cannot be imputed for Siva. Only names such as Siva are to be ascribed to nārāyaṇa. Thus in vēdas we have verses such as “*śāsvatam śivam acyutam*”, “*viśvākśam viśva śambhuvam*”, “*ūrdvarētam virūpākśam*” “*ya: paras sa mahēśvara:*” and in śrī viṣṇu sahasranāmam verses such as “*īśāna prāṇada: prāṇa:*”, “sarva śarva Siva: stāṇu:”, “*ādi dēvō mahādēva:*”, “*rudrō bahu śirā bhabhru:*” to substantiate that names of Siva are imputed to nārāyaṇa. Hence only nārāyaṇa can be taken as being referred to as *sarvaśabda vācya* (being referred to by all sounds)

**toguttuc connēn** – I condensed his greatness and simplicity and said it in a simple way. If one desires to see the expanded way, one could see that in the pāsurams to come as well as in [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/)’s other work, tiruccanda viruttam.

We will take up the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 5. Togutta varattanay

*togutta varattanāy – earning te boons (trough penance) tōlādān – hiraṇya kaśyap vho did not lose to anyone mārvam – cest vagirtta – splitting into tvo vaḷai ugir – curled up nails tōḷ – and having śoulders mālē – ōh benefactor! nī – you ugattil – during deluge uḷ vāngi – keeping (te vorlds) inside, after annihilating oru nānṟu – during te time (of creation) uyartti – bringing (tose vorlds) outside nīyē – you, vit suc qualities aru nāngum ānāy – became te indvelling soul for te four types of entities (celestial, human, animal and botanical) aṟi – knov tis*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**varattanāy – collecting all the boons. hiraṇya kaśyap collected all the boons which were available in the world. āḻvār reminisces the many boons which hiraṇya kaśyap earned through which he would not be killed by those who were created by brahmā (celestial, human, animal and botanical entities), during night or day, in the sky or on the earth, inside or outside the house, through weapons, those which have life and those which do not have life etc.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Even after āḻvār explained clearly the supremacy of emperumān, the worldly people were muddled in their thinking. āḻvār then tells emperumān “Oh benefactor! It is very rare to find in this world those who are able to accept your supremacy. Thus, as I recite, you should mercifully listen”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: togutta varattanaāyt tōlādān mārvam vagirtta vaḷaiyugirttōḷ mālē ugattil oru nānṟu nīyuyartti uḷvāngi nīyē arunāngum ānāy aṟi*

**tōlādān** – due to the strength of such boons, he was such that he could not be defeated even by those who granted him the boons.

**mārvam vagirtta** – splitting the chest into two

**vaḷaiyugirt tōḷ** – having curved nails and shoulders. Since [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) punctured the chest of hiraṇya in deep anger, his shoulders and nails became curved. There are people who construe the word *tōḷ* as referring to divine hands; thus the meaning would change to: having divine hands with curved nails.

**mālē** – one who was very affectionate towards prahlāda even when he was in deep anger like this! nammāḻvār too mercifully said “*sīṟṟattōdu aruḷ peṟṟavan*” (one who is merciful even when he is angry). When asked by a follower as to whether it is possible to have affection and anger at the same time, udayavar (śrī rāmānujar) mercifully explained “even when the lioness is fighting with elephant, she will nurse her cubs affectionately and hence this is apt”

**mālē** – we can also construe this to mean supreme entity. Through this activity, while it is very clear that you are the lord for brahmā, rudra et al, why is it that many people are befuddled!

**mālē** – Is it not correct to analyse whether those who granted the boons are supreme or one who removed their difficulties?

Next āḻvār says that it is emperumān who, as indvelling souls of brahmā, rudra et al, carries out the tasks of creation and annihilation. When he was asked “but there are some who say that brahmā and rudra carry out the tasks of creation and annihilation”…….

**uḷ vāngi nīyē ugattil oru nānṟu nīyuyartti** – as one who keeps all the entities inside during the time of deluge and brings them out during the time of creation. This is similar to *ātapadakam* by which water which is subdued by the rays of sun comes out during rainy season. In this simile, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is like the sun, brahmā, rudra et al are like the rays of the sun and the worlds are like water. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that emperumān, utilising brahmā, rudra et al as implements, keeps the worlds inside and lets them out, at appropriate times.

Next āḻvār says that one can know emperumān is carrying out the three functions of creation, protection and annihilation through his *sarvātmabhāvam* (pervading all souls)…

**aru nāngum ānāy** – did you not become the unseen *jīvan* (life) for the four entities of *dēva* (celestial), *manuśya* (human), *tiryak* (animal) and *stāvara* (botanical)! By the usage of the sound *um* in *nāngum* it is inferred that he dwells inside *acit* (insentient) entity too. Just as it is said “*kśētrajñam cāpi mām viddi*” (know that I am (both body and) soul) the words can be transposed to *nāngum aru ānāy* meaning that emperumān is the indvelling soul of celestial, human, animal and botanical entities. This conveys that just as he carries out creation and annihilation, he also protects by being the indvelling soul.

**aru nāngum ānāy** – it is apt to reminisce on the sayings of upaniśats “*viśvātmānam parāyaṇam*”, “*antar bahiśca tat sarvam vyāpya nārāyaṇa: stita:*” (nārāyaṇa pervades both inside and outside of all) “*sarvabhūtāntarātmā nārāyaṇa:*”

**nīyē aṟi** – there is none in this samsāram (materialistic realm) who would understand even if these meanings are instructed. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) thus says that you \[emperumān\] should know and shower your grace. One can construe these verses as either *nīyē aru nāngum ānāy* or *nīyē aṟi*. appiḷḷai (one of the commentators for this prabandam) says “those who are unable to bear your greatness will suffer the fate of hiraṇya kaśyap and āḻvār is hence bringing in the reference to hiraṇya”

puttūr svāmy, who has expanded on the vyākhyānam of both [periyavāccān piḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyavachchan-pillai/) and [appiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/appillai/) has given another meaning for the second part of the pāsuram. This is based on another meaning for *uyartti* which means incarnation. The first paragraph below is the avatārikai for the pāsuram and the words following this paragraph, are the verses from the pāsuram with new meaning:

In the previous pāsuram, after [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) has spoken of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s supremacy, some people objected saying “How can nārāyaṇa be considered as supreme since he has suffered a lot during his incarnations?”, āḻvār responds in this pāsuram saying that the incarnations that he had taken due to his partiality towards devotees will only bring greatness to him and not lowliness.

**ugattil** – in every yuga (age). Hasn’t [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself mentioned mercifully in [śrī bhagavat gītā 4.8](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/4-8/) “*sambhavāmi yugē yugē*” (I incarnate in every age)?

**oru nānṟu** – on various occasions. As mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 4.9](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/4-7/) “*yadā yadā hi darmasya glānil bhavaty bhārata abyuttānam adarmasya tadātmānam srujāmyaham* ” (whenever righteousness is threatened and evil is nurtured (I incarnate myself)), on such occasions.

**nī uyartti** – you incarnate. For his nature etc, incarnation becomes a greatness. It is said “*sa u śrēyān bhavati jāyamāna:*” (he attains greatness only after being born); “[*pala piṟappāy oḷivaru muḻu nalam*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-2-elivarum-iyalvinan/)” (he attains completeness on being born radiantly in many births)

**uyartti arunāngum ānāy** – when incarnating, you are born in all types of creations such as celestial, human, animal and botanical.

**uḷ vāngi** – once the purpose of incarnation has been achieved, he returns to his paramapadam.

**nīyē aṟi** – as mentioned in authentic texts “*nā vēdavinmanutē*” (one who does not know vēdas will not know the greatness of his births) “*avajānanti mām mūdā:*” (the foolish persons denigrate [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is in human form), those who speak such lowly words do not know this meaning. Those who are said as in “*tasyā dīrā: parijānanti yōnim*” (only the brave understand his incarnations) are indeed rare. Hence [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) tells [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) that only he should understand the meaning of this.  The first interpretation explains his supremacy and this interpretation explains his simplicity.

We will take up the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 6. Ariyar samanar

*samaṇar – jainas \[Jains\] aṟiyār – do not knov te trut \[tat emperumān is supreme lord\] bavuttar – bauddas \[Buddists\] ayarttār – are confused sivappattār – śaivas \[devotees of śiva\] siṟiyār – are very lovly seppil – if (teir caracteristics are to be) spoken of veṟi āya – one vho is te epitome of fragrance māyavanai – one vho has amazing caracteristics and activities mālavanai – one vho is very affectionate (tovards his follovers) mādavanai – one vho is te consort of śrī mahālakśmi ēttādār – (tese people, mentioned above) vill not vorśip ādalāl – tus inṟu – for te present īnavarē – tey are lovly*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**aṟiyār samaṇar – jainas are those who say of entities “syādasti, syādnāsti, syādasti ca nāsti ca, syādastīti vaktavyam, syādnāstīti vaktavyam, syādasti ca nāsti cēti vaktavyam, sarvadā vaktavyam ca ” – 1. It (entity) may exist. 2. It may not exist. 3. It may or may not exist. 4. It may be said that it may be present. 5. It may be said that it may not be present. 6. It may be said that it may or may not be present. 7. It can be said in any way. Thus the jainas who talk with seven possibilities about an entity do not truly know an entity. āren’t the jainas the type of people who would say that a pot exists looking at it and would say that it does not exist \[once it is removed from its place\] and would say that anything can be said about anything.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After āḻvār mentioned “nīyē aṟi” in the previous pāsuram, emperumān asked him “In this expansive world is there no one who knows about this?”. āḻvār responds saying “in this world which is full of ignorant jainas, the confused bauddas and the lowly śaivas, all of whom either do not accept vēdas or give wrong interpretation to vēdas, who would know you truly?”. If one were to ask “There could be people similar to the mudal āḻvārs (the first three āḻvārs) who are totally devoted to emperumān; why then is āḻvār saying that there is none?”, the answer would be that āḻvār, while knowing that there would be a few such people, is disgusted that the world is full of these ignorant, confused and lowly people only. Thus there is no incongruity in saying that there is none. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: aṟiyār samaṇar ayarttār bavuttar siṟiyār sivappattār seppil veṟiyāya māyavanai mālavanai mādavanai ēttādār īnavarē ādalāl inṟu*

**aṟiyār samaṇar** – is it required to say that jainas do not know anything when they talk in contradictory terms similar to a son saying that his mother is sterile \[childless\]? It is pertinent here to recall śrī parāśara bhattar’s śrī rangarāja stavam ślōkam “*angīkrutya tu saptabangi kusrutim syādasti nāsatyātmikām viśvam tad vibhavam jagajjinamatē naikāntamācakśatē* I *binnābinnam idam tatā jagaduśē vandyā mamāmbhēdivatnūttabrahmavidē raha: paramidam rangēndra! tē cakśatām* ” (Oh śrī ranganāta! In the philosophy of jainas, who follow the wrong path which says that an entity may exist or may not exist, they say that all the worlds which are your wealth are of many types. Let those jainas instruct this most secretive message to the *bhēdābhēdi vādis* (those who say that entities are different as well as not different) who are the modern *brahmajñānis* (know very well about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) (this is a backhanded compliment)) who say that this world is same and different, similar to saying that my mother is sterile).

**ayarttār bavuttar** – bauddas are also without knowledge. This is because they do not accept ātmā who is the knover. They consider knowledge also as transient. Is there any possibility for such people to know about *īsvaran* ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/))?

**sivappattār siṟiyār** – those who are engaged with Siva are lowly in their knowledge. These people (followers of Siva) accepted *pramāṇam* (authentic texts such as vēdas) in a peculiar way. Unlike the jainas and bauddas, they accepted the vēdas. But since they also accepted āgamas, which are confusing texts, as authentic, they do not know the supreme entity who is manifested by the authentic texts. While all the vēdas affirm that supreme being is the determining cause as well as the materialistic cause for creation, the śaivas say that he is only the determining cause. Even while establishing as to who is the supreme being, they considered a human instead of nārāyaṇa. While the King of Kings is available, they went for a mendicant; while the one with lotus eyes is available, they went in for one with disfigured eyes. Hence these people are with lowly knowledge only.

**seppil** – if one were to talk about them…. Even talking about them who are lowly is disgusting. For the devotees of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), talking about these people who will make even the purely good people to become angry with their words and deeds, is a difficult task. However, if one were to talk about them in order to let the others know the truth……

**veṟi āya** – “*sarvaganda:*” (one who has all the fragrance) – emperumān is the epitome of fragrance as praised by vēdas. This implies his unparalleled enjoyability (having the best quality among those qualities which are fit to be enjoyed).

Even flowers have sweet fragrance. What is the eminence in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) having this quality?

**māyavanai** – one who has amazing activities. One who has basic nature, qualities and wealth which create a feeling of vondrousness. Since he controls *prakruti*  which is called as māya due to its wondrous qualities, emperumān is called as *māyavan*. It is pertinent to recall the vēda saying “*māyām tu prakrutim vidyānmāyinam tu mahēśvaram*” (know that prakruti is māyā and emperumān is māyi).

Even though he is one with amazing activities, is he without empathy?

**mālavanai** – one who is the epitome of affection towards his followers.

**mādavanai** – on top of all these, one who is the consort of [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/). She is the university where he learnt how to be affectionate towards followers.

**ēttādār** – these people (earlier mentioned jainas, bauddas, śaivas) do not worship.

**ādalāl īnavarē inṟu** – Since they did not worship \[the supreme being\] they remain as lowly people.

**(veṟiyāya ….. ēttādār īnavarē)** – are those, who do not worship the unique enjoyable entity, great?

**(māyavanai ēttādār īnavarē)** – Are those, who do not praise the amazing entity, great in knowledge?

**(mālavanai ēttādār īnavarē)** – Do those, who do not praise the epitome of affection, have a good heart?

**(mādavanai ēttādār īnavarē)** – Is it required to call those who do not praise even after seeing  the wealth of śrī mahālakśmi, who is the epitome of mercy, as lowly?

**inṟu īnavarē** – Since they are qualified to become elevated as devotees of supreme being at some point of time in the future, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that they are lowly, as of now.

We will move on to the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 7. Inraga nalaiyeyaga

*(Whenever it is) nin aruḷ – your grace en pāladē – vill fall on me nanṟu āga – it is certain nān unnai anṟi ilēn kaṇḍāy – you vould see tat ī have no refuge oter tan you nī – you too ennai anṟi ilai – do not have one to take care of, oter tan me*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**inṟāga nāḷaiyēyāga inicciṟidu ninṟāga ninnaruḷ en pāladē – whether it is today or tomorrow or after some more time, your flood of mercy is going to come down on me. It could get delayed a bit, but it is certain that your grace will fall on me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār becomes elated in this pāsuram saying “Oh emperumān! It is certain that I would receive your grace. Let the others do whatever they want”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: inṟāga nāḷaiyēyāga inicciṟidu ninṟāga ninnaruḷ enpāladē nanṟāga nānunnai anṟi ilēn kāṇḍāy nāraṇanē nī ennai anṟi ilai nāraṇanē – Oh nārāyaṇa! inṟu āga – it could be today nāḷaiyē āga – it could be tomorrow ini siṟidu ninṟāga – it could be after sometime*

What is the reason for saying this?

**nanṟāga nān unnai anṟi ilēn kaṇḍāy** – it is a fact that I do not have a refuge other than you. Alternatively, *nanṟāga nān* could also mean “I, who am doing well” which would mean that I have the benefit of having only you as my means. Only if I have you and another entity as means could you avoid me saying that “this is karmayōgam, this is jñāna yōgam”, just as naḷa (a king in ikśvāgu dynasty) told his wife damayanti “*ēśa pantā vidarpānām ēśa yādi hi kōsalān* ” (this is the route to reach vidarba and this is the route to reach kōsala).

When [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) asked [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) “Is there any proof that I am the means”, āḻvār says…

**nārananē** – your divine name itself is the proof that you are the means as well as the end result for the jīvas, the collection of nāras \[nara means that which is not destroyed, nāra refers to a collection of such entities\]. The meaning for the word nārāyaṇa is taken from the verse “*nāraśabdēna jīvānām samūha: prōcyatē budai* I *gatirālambanam tasya tēna nārāyaṇa: smruta:* īī” (the word nāra indicates a collection of jīvas (sentient entities) Since he is their means as well as their goal, he is referred to as nārāyaṇa )

**nān unnai anṟi ilēn kaṇḍāy nāraṇanē** – just as it is said of śrī rāma “*vyasanēśu manuśyāṇām bhruśam bhavati du:kkita:*” (when people suffer, śrī rāma suffers more than they do ), I do not have anyone as my lord other than you, who are unable to tolerate the sorrow that I suffer from. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) asks him “Is there any compulsion on my part to grace you since you do not have any refuge?”. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) responds…

**nāraṇanē nī ennai anṟi ilai** – you, who are the owner, cannot attain completeness unless you accept me, your property. The magnanimous person will attain completeness only if he attains a mendicant. In the same way, you will attain completeness only if you accept me who has shortcomings.

**nāraṇanē** – your characteristic of *nārāyaṇatvam* (being nārāyana) will show life only if you shower your mercy on me. Thus, my saying that your grace will fall on me, today or tomorrow or after sometime, is totally true. It is pertinent to recall the verse of [śrī āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) in [stōtra ratnam 51](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/sthothra-rathnam-51/) “*tadaham tvadrutē na nātavān madrutē tvam dayanīyavān na ca* I *vidi nirmitam ētad anvayam bhagavan pālaya mā sma jīhapa:* īī” (thus, I do not have anyone other than you as my lord. You too do not have anyone other than me to shower your grace. This relationship has been created purely by your mercy. Oh the omniscient! Please protect, do not give up).

## 8. Ilai tunai marru

*en nenjē – ōh my heart! īsanai venṟa – one vho defeated rudra silai koṇḍa – one vho donned te bov sem kaṇ māl – emperumān vho has lotus like eyes sērā – one vho did not bov dovn and get redeemed, but got annihilated kulai koṇḍa īraindu talaiyān – rāvaṇa vho had ten heads close togeter as if in a cluster ilangaiyai – lankā īdu aḻitta – one vho annihilated kūr amban allāl – oter tan (tat) śrī rāma vho has śarp arrovs kuṟai – during te times vhen (ve) have difficulties maṟṟu tuṇai – support oter tan him ilai – ve do not have*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**ilai tuṇai maṟṟu – it is not important to know that emperumān is our protector. It is more important to know that others are not protectors. Wouldn’t vaiṇavatvam (quality of being a follower of viṣṇu) be attained if people do not worship other deities, forgetting the divine feet of śrīman nārāyaṇa? Did not piḷḷai lōkācāryar (one of the preceptors of our sampradāyam) say in his mumukśuppadi treatise “adilum anya sēśatvam kaḻigaiyē pradānam” (giving up being a servitor to other deities is primary) in line with this!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “There are some ignorant people who will eat only if there is smoke (in the kitchen) – meaning that they will eat only if something is cooked in their homes. Otherwise they will go hungry. In the same way could you say nān unnai anṟi ilēn kaṇḍāy nāraṇanē? Could you not carry out your task through someone else?”. āḻvār says that there is none who is a protector other than emperumān. āḻvār tells his heart that the others (who are supposed to be protectors) are not only incapable of protecting, they will also pose hurdles in the path of emperumān when he protects us. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: ilai tuṇai maṟṟen nenjē īsanai venṟa silai koṇḍa sengaṇmāl sērā kulai koṇḍa īraindalaiyān ilangaiyai īdaḻitta kūrambanallāl kuṟai*

**en nenjē** – Oh my heart who is favourable to listen to my discourse on this!

**en nenjē** – aren’t you different from other hearts which follow the saying “*cancalam hi mana:*” (heart is fickle minded)!

What is the reason for saying that there is no other protector? This is because only śrīman nārāyaṇa is the supreme deity. When asked “others such as rudra et al are also said to be supreme entities. Why then are you emphasising that only nārāyaṇa is supreme?”. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says derisively in the next verse as to how rudra et al were called as supreme.

**īsanai venṟa silai koṇḍa sengaṇmāl** – the lord of all with lotus-like eyes, who dons the bow which defeated rudra. This incident has been described in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam by paraśurāma to śrī rāma (paraśurāma tells śrī rāma that the two bows (one belonging to Siva and the other to viṣṇu), belonging to the dēvalōkam (outer world), famous throughout the world, great, firm, strong and eminent, were created by viśvakarma, the celestial carpenter. One was given to rudra during the war with the three towns (*tiripuram, tripura*) and it was that bow which later was broken by śrī rāma at the court of King janaka. The other bow, which cannot be approached by anyone, was given by the great ones among the celestial entities to viṣṇu. After the war on the three towns, the dēvas wanted to know who among viṣṇu and rudra had greater ability. Hence viṣṇu and rudra engaged  in a fight. During the fight (with the two eminent bows) when viṣṇu made a menacing sound (*hūnkāra*) Siva’s bow cracked a bit and Siva too became unconscious, standing like a pillar. The riśis and celestial entities, seeing the cracking of Siva’s bow, realised the greatness of viṣṇu).

**īsanai venṟa silai** – the bow which defeated Siva who had the vain feeling that he is īśvara (one who controls everything). We can also construe this as *sengaṇmāl*  who defeated rudra.

**silai koṇḍa** – all that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) did was to don the bow. The victorious act was carried out by the bow itself.

**sem kaṇ māl** – supreme being who has reddish eyes, which manifest his ability to control everything. The supreme being was hailed as *puṇḍarīkākśan* based on the saying of vēdas “*kapyāsam puṇḍarīkam ēvam akśiṇī*” (the two lotus-like eyes which were blossomed by sun).

**sem kaṇ māl** – we can also construe this as śrī rāma who has reddish eyes, showing affection for his followers. Wasn’t śrī rāma hailed as *rāmō rājīvalōcana:* and *rāma: kamalapatrākśa*:, denoting his lotus-like eyes! The words *sem kaṇ* would denote both wealth and affection. The word *māl* would mean one who is great as well as one who is affectionate.

**īlai tuṇai maṟṟen nenjē īsanai venṟa silai koṇḍa sengaṇmāl** – Is it not apt to enquire whether the one who is victorious is the protector or the one who was defeated!

**sengaṇmāl sērā** – rāvaṇa said that he would rather fall back even if he is split into two than falling at the feet of rāma, as that is his nature. This is brought out by in the ślōkam 36-11 of śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam “*dvidā bhajyēyamapyēvam na namēyantu kasyacit* I *ēśa mē sahajō dōśa: svabhāvō duratikrama:* īī”

**sengaṇmāl sērā** – how is that an entity can remain without tilting his head at the divine feet of one even after seeing his lotus like eyes?

**īsanai venṟa….. sengaṇmāl sērā** – do we need to talk about unfavourable entities \[such as rāvaṇa\] stringing arrow in offence when favourable entities \[such as rudra\], under the influence of *tāmasa* *guṇa* (ignorant state) string an arrow in offence?

Who is that entity who is so incongruent with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)?

**kulai koṇḍa īraindalaiyān** – rāvaṇa who has ten heads, like a bunch.

**īraindalaiyān ilangaiyai īdaḻitta** – lankā was protected by rāvaṇa with all his might, as said in “*lankām rāvaṇa pālitām*” (lankā protected by rāvaṇa. śrī rāma destabilised such lankā).

**kūrambanallāl kuṟai illai tuṇai** – there is none like śrī rāma, who has sharp arrows, as support for us to rid us of our difficulties. cētana (sentient) entities such as rudra et al or acētana (insentient) entities such as beads cannot protect us. Only the sharp arrows of cakravarty tirumagan (son of emperor daśarata) will be a refuge for us, similar to how they were for sītāppirātti.

We will take up the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 9. Kurai kondu nanmugan

*nānmugan – brahmā kuṟai koṇḍu – reminiscing his śortcomings kuṇḍigai nīr – te vater in kamaṇḍalam \[vater pot used by ascetics\] peydu – pouring maṟai koṇḍa – present in te vēdas \[sacred texts\] mandirattāl – vit hymns suc as puruśa sūKtam etc vāḻtti – praising āngu – during tat time (vhen emperumān measured te vorlds) aṇḍattān sēvadiyai – te divine feet of tat supreme entity \[emperumān\] kaṟai koṇḍa kaṇḍattāṇ senni mēl ēṟa – ensuring tat (te divine vater from emperumān’s divine feet\] fell on te head of rudra vho has poison in his troat kaḻuvinān – cleansed*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**kuṟai koṇḍu – two meanings are given: 1. Reminiscing shortcomings; 2. Getting expectations fulfilled. When mahābali took away the kingdom of indra et al after troubling them, brahmā and the celestial entities went to emperumān and pleaded with him to restore the kingdom that they had lost. emperumān took the form of vāmana (dwarf) and took alms from mahābali. Once his request (for three steps) was granted, he took the form of trivikrama and measured all the worlds with his divine feet. āḻvār reminisces this incident. In this context, the word kuṟai refers to expectations. It can also refer to shortcomings in the person as seen in the explanatory notes of vyākhyānam of appiḷḷai.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned that there is no refuge (other than emperumān) when we are in difficulties. In this pāsuram, by saying that even for brahmā, rudra et al who are thought of as being complete in all respects, the refuge is emperumān only, he emphasises the meaning mentioned in the previous pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: kuṟai koṇḍu nānmugan kuṇḍigai nīr peydu maṟai koṇḍa mandirattāl vāḻtti kaṟaikoṇḍa kaṇḍattān senni mēl ēṟak kaḻuvinān aṇḍattān sēvadiyai āngu*

**kuṇḍigai nīr peydu** – brahmā cleansed the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with the water from his *kamaṇḍalam* (water pot) where deity for righteousness, looking at the divine feet of emperumān reaching *satyalōka* (the world of brahmā) melted and filled up brahmā’s *kamaṇḍalam* as water. brahmā cleansed out of his gratitude for seeing his expectation \[of getting the kingdom back for indra\] fulfilled as well as out of his ordained duty in paying obeisance to emperumān.

**nānmugan maṟai koṇḍa mandirattāl vāḻtti** – praising the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) out of concern for their having gone over mounds and craters \[while measuring the worlds\] despite being so soft, by reciting hymns such as puruśa sūktam etc from vēdas. By reciting the hymns, brahmā also accrued benefit for his four faces.

**kaṟai koṇḍa kaṇḍattān senni mēl ēṟak kaḻuvinān** – brahmā cleansed he divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) so that the divine water will fall on the head of his son, rudra, who had incurred *brahmahatti dōśam* (a sin which results due to harming a brāhmaṇa since he had severed one of brahmā’s heads).

**kaṟai koṇḍa kaṇḍattān** – rudra with blue throat, who was vain after swallowing the poison *hālahāla* since he saved the celestial entities by his act.

**senni mēl ēṟak kaḻuvinān** – brahmā cleansed [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine feet in such a way that the water would fall on rudra’s head.

**aṇḍattān sēvadiyai** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who grew in such a way that he filled the entire universe with his divine form. Alternatively, brahmā, who is the head of one universe, cleansed the divine feet of one who is the lord of all universes, in such a way that the divine water would fall on the head of his son (rudra) who lives within one of the universes, so that his son’s sin would get removed. This act of brahmā is similar to that of a father who sprinkles his *śrīpādatīrtam*  (water from cleaning his foot) on his habituated errant sons in order to correct them. It is pertinent here to recollect the sayings of brahmā to riśis on this incident when he cleansed the divine feet of emperumān with the water generated by *darmadēvatai* (deity for righteousness) so that the divine water would fall on his son rudra to cleanse him of his sins \[rudra was willing to take the water on his head and keep it within his head for thousands of years\].

We will take up the 10<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 10. Angaravaram adukettu

*\[mistaking tat sound for asuras creating commotion\] aḻal umiḻum – spitting fire of poison pū kār aravu aṇaiyān – emperumān has te furious anantāḻvān (ādiśēśa) as his mattress. ṭhat emperumān’s ponmēni – te divine form nām – ve (vho are ananyabhaktas \[not vorśipping oter deities\]) kāṇa – to vorśip vallam allamē – do ve not have te ability? (Yes, ve have te ability) mā malarān – brahmā vho  vas born in a great (lotus) flover vār sadaiyān – śiva, vho has long matted hair vāḻttu – in praising (emperumān) vallar allarē – tey are not capable*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**āngu – during that time of trivikrama avatāram, “kaḻuvinān……āngu” as mentioned in the previous pāsuram.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “brahmā, rudra et al as well as you take refuge under emperumān with your faults, without any distinction. Does this mean that there is no difference between them and you?” to which āḻvār responds in this pāsuram, saying “there is a lot of difference between us. Since I have been granted emperumān’s mercy, I am capable of seeing him too. brahmā, rudra et al, who try to see emperumān through their efforts, are not capable of even praising him”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: āngāravāram adu kēttu aḻal umiḻum pūngāraravaṇaiyān ponmēni yāngāṇa vallamē allamē? māmalarān vār sadaiyān vallarē allarē vāḻttu āngu – during that time of trivikrama avatāram āravāram kettu – hearing the loud sound emanating from brahmā et al as they praised emperumān*

**āravāram adu kēttu** – hearing the words of worship (1) by various entities such as brahmā who, as mentioned in the previous pāsuram “*maṟai koṇḍa mandirattāl vāḻtti*” (praising through hymns from vēdas; (2) by people who were referred to by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his tiruvāimoḻi 7.4.1 “*disai vāḻi eḻa*” (words of praise emanating from all directions), (3) by various people in the worlds who said “*sangais surāṇām divi bhūtalastais tatā manuśyair gaganē ca kēsarai:* I *stuta: kramān ya: prasakāra sarvadā mamāstu māngaḷya vivruddayē hari:* īī ” (let that hari, who was praised by entities such as dēva, manuśya et al (celestial, human et al) and who measured the worlds, expand my auspiciousness)

**aḻal umiḻum** – spitting fire, mistaking the sounds emanating from various directions which were actually words of praise on trivikrama, for attempts being made by some \[demons\] for harming [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).  [bhūdattāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/), in his [iraṇḍām tiruvandādi 71](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/01/irandam-thiruvandhadhi-71/), had mercifully mentioned “*vidangālum tīvāyaravaṇai mēl tōnṟal disaiyaḷappān pūvāradi nimirttapōdu*” (when emperumān who reclines on the mattress of ādiśēśan who spits poison from his frightening mouths….). The same meaning can be seen in vēda “*jāgruvāmsas samindatē…*”(nityasūris remain alert …); by [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) in his [tiruppallāṇḍu 12](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/11/thiruppallandu-12-pallandu-enru/) “*sūḻndirundēttuvar pallāṇḍē*” (emperumān will be praised by nityasūris from all sides) and by [parāśara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) “*snēhādastānakśā vyasanibhi* ” (those who make an effort to protect \[emperumān\] out of misplaced fear).

**pūngār aravaṇaiyān** – one who has the furious ādiśēśan as his mattress.

**pūngār aravu** – since ādiśēśan’s fury is arising out his love towards [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), even that fury appears beautiful to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) who is praying that this fury should remain unchanged.

**ponmēni yāngāṇa vallamē allamē** – are we, who do not consider that there can be anything beyond emperumān’s divine form as the means and goal, not capable of seeing his divine form? It is implied that they are very well capable of seeing. Even for bhagavān, his *rūpam* (divine form) is dearer than his *svarūpam* (basic nature) as mercifully mentioned by śrī [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) in his [catu: ślōkī](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/12/chathu-sloki-slokams/) 4 “*mūrtam brahma tatō’pi tatpriyataram rūpam yadadbutam*”.

**māmalarān** – nānmugan who resides in the lotus, shooting from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s navel.

**vār sadaiyān** – rudra who has long matted locks.

**vāḻttu vallarē allarē** – they are not capable of even praising that emperumān.

**māmalrān …** – brahmā, who has the vanity that he was born in the lotus like divine navel of emperumān directly, and rudra, who is vain that he carried out penance, by letting his matted hair fall to the ground, are incapable of praising emperumān. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that people who consider emperumān’s divine form as both means and goal are capable of seeing him.

**vallamē allamē …..vallarē allarē** – only those like āḻvār who praise those who are affectionate towards [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) are capable of seeing him whereas those who consider that they have great power are incapable of even vorśipping him.

There is another interpretation for this pāsuram. *āngu* is considered to be śrīvaikuṇṭam . *āravāram adu kēttu* – hearing the *sāma vēda* (one of the four vēdas, which is sung with intonations) of nityasūris in śrīvaikuṇṭam. *aḻal umiḻum* – ādiśēśan spits fire of poison thinking mistakenly that demons have descended on śrīvaikuṇṭam and are shouting. The rest of the interpretation is similar to the earlier one.

We will take up the 11<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 11. Valttuga vay

*sūḻtta – encircling tuḻāy – divine tuḷasi garland mannu – aptly fitting nīḷ mudi – one vho has te long divine crovn en tollai māḷ tannai – sarvēśvaran (supreme being) vho has been having affection tovards me for a long time vaḻā – vitout even a moment’s break vaṇ kai kūppi madittu – joining te hands togeter in salutation and meditating magudam tāḻtti – boving te head dovn taṇ malarāl – vit cool flovers vaṇangumin – attain him vāy – (your) mout vāḻttuga – let it praise (emperumān) kaṇ – eyes kāṇga – let tem see( only him) sevi – ears kētka – let tem hear (only his narratives)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vāḻttuga vāy – praise him with the mouth which has been created only for praising him, as mentioned in the saying “sā jihvā yā harīm stauti” (only that which praises hari (emperumān) is termed as tongue)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār instructs the others to engage their sensory organs, which were given in order to carry out service for emperumān, in the matter of that sulabhan (simple person). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: vāḻtuga vāy kāṇga kaṇ kētka sevi magudam tāḻtti vaṇangumingaḷ taṇmalarāl sūḻtta tuḻāy mannu nīṇmudi en tollai māl tannai vaḻā vaṇ kai kūppi madittu*

**kāṇga kaṇ** – see him with the eyes which have been created only for seeing his divine form.

**kētka sevi** – hear his divine qualities, names and activities with the ears which have been created only for hearing his auspicious qualities.

**magudam tāḻtti vaṇangumingaḷ** – lower the head which has been created to bow down to his divine feet, at his divine feet and worship him.

**vaṇangumingaḷ taṇmalarāl** – worship him with the apt flowers which are cool enough for his soft divine form.

**malarāl** – among flowers, there is no rule that only such and such flowers are apt for him and others are not. Has he not mercifully mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 9.26](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-26/) “*patram palam puśpam tōyam yō mē bhaktyā prayaccati* I *tadaham bhaktyupahrutam aśnāmi prayatātmana:*  īī” (I take with full happiness with whatever leaf or flower or fruit or water has been offered to me by one with devotion) and hasn’t [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too mercifully mentioned in his [tiruvāimoḻi 1.6.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/08/thiruvaimozhi-1-6-1-parivathil-isanai/) “*purivadum pugai pūvē*” (whatever fragrance or flower is offered to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)…)!

āḻvār next describes the distinguished feature of emperumān for our bowing our head to him.

**sūḻtta tuḻāy mannu** – donning tuḷasi garland in itself is a distinguished feature for his being the supreme entity.

**sūḻtta tuḻāy mannu** – decorated with the coiled tuḷasi garland.

**tuḻāy mannu nīṇmudi** – isn’t he the entity who has “*tōḷiṇai mēlum nanmārbin mēlum sudar mudi mēlum tāḷiṇai mēlum punainda taṇṇanduḻāyudai ammān*  ” (the lord who is decorated with cool tuḷasi garland on his divine shoulders, on the great chest, on the radiant lock and on his divine feet)! ḍidn’t [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/) (a primary disciple of [bhagavat śrī rāmānuja](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/)) also mercifully say in atimānuśa stavam “*ādirājyamadikam bhuvanānām īsa! tē piśunayan kila mauli:*” (Oh Lord! That you are the controller of all the worlds is indicated by your divine crown)!

**en tollai māl tannai** – we can construe this as one who has showered his affection on me from time immemorial. *māl* – an affectionate person.

**vaḻā** – unchanged.

**vaṇ kai kūppi** – joining the magnanimous hands together. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) quotes the adjective (*vaṇ*) based on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s recitation in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7.18](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-18/) “*udārās sarva ēvaitē*” (those who worship me are magnanimous persons). It can also be taken as āḻvār celebrating since those who bow down to emperumān in samsāram (materialistic realm) are indeed rare.

**tollai māḷ tannai madittu vaṇangumingaḷ** – worship the supreme being with the heart which thinks of him. Since [periyavāccāṇ piḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyavachchan-pillai/) has said in his vyākhyānam for this pāsuram “thinking, without a break, of the divine names of supreme being ” it could be construed that the words in the text could also be *tollai māl nāmam*.

**magudam tāḻtti vaṇangumingaḷ** – since you are thinking in vain that you are someone great, get rid of that vanity and worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with bowed head.

**vāḻttuga vāy…..vaṇangumingaḷ** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) is recalling the verse of riśis who said “*vicitrā dēhasampattirīśvarāya nivēditum* I *pūrvamēva krutā brahmaṇ hastapādādi samyutā* īī” (Oh brahmaṇa! This body with its strange hands and legs has been created at the beginning only for offering to the supreme being) and the divine hymn of mudal āḻvār ([poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/)) who mercifully said in [mudal tiruvandādi 11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-11/) “*vāy avanai alladu vāḻtādu kaiyulagam tāyavanai alladu tām toḻā…*” (the mouth will not praise anyone except emperumān and the hands will not worship anyone other than [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is like a mother to the worlds..).

We will go on to the 12<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 12. Madittay poy nangin

*pōy – analysing all te śāstras (sacred texts) madiyār – tose vho do not knov you nāngil – among te four major species (celestial, human, animal and plant) pōy vīḻa – falling \[into one of te four species\] madittāy – you had taken a vov madi – moon’s kōḷ – curse viduttāy – ōh one vho caused it to disappear! madu – in a pond kidanda – having arrived at (due to a curse) mā mudalai – a huge crocodile kōḷ – grasping (te elephant gajēndra) viduppān – to liberate it āḻi – te divine disc vidaṟku – to propel madittāy – you tought of iraṇḍum – bot te elephant and te crocodile pōy – leaving teir earlier stage iraṇḍin – for te tvo creatures vīdu – for liberation from teir curse and for attaining śrīvaikuṇṭam madittāy – you took a vov*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**nāngil pōy madiyār vīḻa madittāy – you had vowed that those who do not know you despite analysing the four vēdas will bite the dust.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār implores people to analyse the hardships which those who do not know emperumān face and the benefit that gajēndrāḻvān (the elephant gajēndra) accrued by thinking about him as mentioned in the ślōkam “sa tu nāgavara: śrīmān manasā’cintayat harīm” (that gajēndrāḻvān who has the wealth of kainkaryam (service to emperumān) thought of hari (emperumān) in his mind). While the pāsuram has been addressed to emperumān, in line with the earlier and the succeeding pāsurams, the people of the world are the real target. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: madittāy pōy nāngin madiyādār pōy vīḻa madittāy madikōḷ viduttāy madittāy madukkidanda mā mudalai kōḷ viduppān āḻi vidaṟkiraṇḍum pōyiraṇḍin vīdu*

**nāngil madiyādār** – “*catur vēdadarō viprō vāsudēvam na vindadi* I *vēdabhārabharākrāntas sa vai brāhmaṇagardabha*:  īī” (the brāhmaṇa who studies the four vēdas and who does not know vāsudēvan ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)), is like a donkey which carries the loaded vēdas (which is like invaluable saffron) ). These people (who, despite analysing vēdas do not know emperumān) are despised like this (like the example given here).

**madiyār nāngil pōy vīḻa madittāy** – you vowed that such people will be born in the four major species (of celestial, human, animal and plant) repeatedly. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has mercifully mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 16-19](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/16-19/) “*tānaham dviśata: krūrān samsāreśu narādamān* I *kśipāmyajasramaśubhānāsurīśvēva yōniśu*  īī” (I push those who hate me, who are cruel, who are the worst among people  and sinners, into the demonic species repeatedly).

**madi kōḷ viduttāy** – you removed the affliction of candra (moon) who knew you. Is it not well known that those who do not know you suffer and those who know you are joyous!

Has this been seen elsewhere before?

**madukkidanda māmudalai kōḷ viduppān āḻividaṟku madittāy** – you decided to release the divine disc (to allow the elephant to be liberated) when the crocodile in the pond grasped firmly.

**māmudalai** – both in terms of physical form and strength, the crocodile was bigger than the elephant.

**kōḷ** – the strong grasp (of the crocodile)

**āḻi vidaṟku madittāy** – while you could have killed the crocodile through your vow/will, you came to the pond and released the disc so that you would sustain gajēndra who wanted to have your darśan (seeing) with the divine hand and divine disc. While removing the enemy could have been achieved with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s vow, pleasing the follower can be done only through releasing the disc. āren’t emperumān’s divine, auspicious form and armaments/ornaments meant only for his followers, as quoted in jitantē ślōkam 5 “*na tē rūpam na cākārō nāyudāni nā cāspadam*”  (your basic nature, your divine form, your weapons, your dwelling place are not meant for you; they are for your devotees)!

**iraṇḍum pōy iraṇḍin vīdu madittāy** – you vowed that both crocodile and elephant would get rid of their forms and return to their original forms – crocodile regained its old gandarva (a class of celestial entity) form and the elephant went to śrīvaikuṇṭam. *vīdu* – position. This word can also mean liberation. Hence [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) vowed that crocodile will get liberated from its curse and elephant, liberation from samsāram.

Legend has it that a sage dēvala was carrying out penance in a water body. At that time, a gandarva by name *hūhū* grasped the sage’s leg due to which the sage cursed him that the gandarva will be born as a crocodile and eat by grasping its victims. Similarly, *indradyumna*, a *viṣṇubhakta* (devotee of viṣṇu) was meditating when he did not see the arrival of sage agastya. The sage cursed him that he will become an elephant due to his vanity. Though born as an elephant, indradyumna was devoted to viṣṇu. One day, when he entered a pond to pluck a flower and offer it to emperumān, he was grasped by the crocodile (gandarva) and the two fought for a very long time. Ultimately, when the elephant cried out to emperumān *ādimūlam* (the prime cause) he was saved by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and both crocodile and elephant were liberated from their curse.

We will take up the 13<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.
