## 13. Vidakkum perriyariyadu

*vīdu ākkum peṟṟī aṟiyādu – not knoving te pat to attain mōkśam (liberation) mey – physical body varutti – creating distress trough fasting etc kūdu ākki – making it very muc like a skeleton, vitout any strengt ninṟu – carrying out penance like tis for a long time uṇḍu koṇḍu – eating tereafter uḻalvīr – ōh tose vho are toiling! vīdu ākkum – one vho can grant mōkśam (liberation) mey poruḷdān – he is certainly a means vēdam mudal poruḷdān – he has been śovn as te supreme being by sacred texts viṇṇavarkku – for nityasūris (permanent dvellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam) nal poruḷdān – te enjoyable entity nārāyaṇan – it is only śrīman nārāyaṇa*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vīdākkum peṟṟi aṟiyādu – without knowing which (type of) means will grant liberation. āḻvār feels amazed that people are considering the insentient type of means such as karmayōgam (path of carrying out ordained deeds) for attaining liberation instead of knowing that the supreme being himself is the means.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After āḻvār mercifully stated that those who know emperumān attain him, he was asked ‘it is said that attaining him is very difficult as mentioned in vēdas “yajñēna dānēna tapasā’nāsanēna brāhmaṇa vividiśanti” (brāhmaṇas desire to know emperumān through rituals, donations, penance and fasting). This being the case, could you say that attaining him is simple?’ by those who are following other means to attain him. āḻvār responds to them through this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: vīdākkum peṟṟīyaṟiyādu mey varuttik kūdākki ninṟuṇḍu koṇḍuḻalvīr! vīdākkum meypporuḷdān vēda mudaṟporuḷdān viṇṇavarkku naṟporuḷdān nārāyaṇan*

**mey varuttikkūdākki** – punishing the body, while carrying out penance, and making it appear like a skeleton. āren’t they the people who believe as said in “*tapasā’nāśakēna*” (through penance, by fasting)!

**ninṟu** – remaining like this for a long time.

**uṇḍu** – enjoying the benefit of the penance for sometime.

**koṇḍu uḻalvīr** –Oh those who wander about thinking that by carrying out karmayōgam for some more time, you can attain mōkśam! If the text is *konṟu uḻalvīr* the meaning will change as those who wander by killing yourself!

**aṟiyādu mey varutti . . . .uḻalvīr** – As mentioned in “*vammin pulavīr! ṇum mey varuttik kai seydu uymiinō*” for carrying out kainkaryam (service to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)), if one should exert oneself through physical efforts it will be a means for uplifting oneself as well as being sweet to carry out as said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 9-2](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-2/) “*susukam karttum*”.  [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that it is foolhardy to starve the body and make it into a skeleton, thinking that it is the means to attain liberation.

When told “Alright, we do not know what is the means for liberation. Since you know it, why don’t you mercifully mention it to us”, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says….

**vīdākkum mey poruḷdān nārāyaṇan** – his name will announce that he is the means for liberation. Haven’t you heard of his name! āḻvār is asking whether they have not heard of the authentic source “*nāraśabdēna jīvānām samūha: prōcyatē bhudai*: I *gatirālambanam tasya tēna nārāyaṇa; smruta*: īī”(the sound \[word\] nārāyaṇa denotes the collection of [jīvātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/). Since he is the means as well as the end result for those jīvātmās, he is called as [nārāyaṇa](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)).

**vīdākkum mey poruḷdān** – even the karmas (deeds carried out as per sacred texts) will bear fruit for those who carry them only if they make nārāyaṇa happy. Thus these deeds are not the direct means to attain him. Only nārāyaṇa is the direct means who grants the end results. Thus, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says, get rid of the thought that these deeds are the means and carry out service to him.

When asked if there is any other proof that he is the means for liberation, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says…

**vēda mudaṟporuḷdān** – quotations from puruśa sūktam and nārāyaṇa sūktam are given, as mentioned below:

nārāyaṇa sūktam “*nārāyaṇam …… pārāyaṇam*” (nārāyaṇa is the highest means)

puruśa sūktam “*utāmrutatvasyēśāna:*” (only the supreme being is the lord for mōkśam)

“*amrutasyaiśa sētu*” (he is the bridge for reaching mōkśam ).

Thus, it is nārāyaṇa who is mentioned in vēdas as the primary means for attaining mōkśa.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) is further asked, “If he is the means, is there any other object which is attained and enjoyed?”  He says…

**viṇṇavrakkum naṟporuḷdān** – he is the entity who is enjoyed by the nityasūris in śrīvaikuṇṭam.

**vēda mudaṟporuḷdān viṇṇavarkku naṟporuḷdān** – vēdas clearly state, as in śrī viṣṇu sūktam “*tad viṣṇō*: *paramam padam sadā paśyanti sūraya:*  ” (that is śrīvaikuṇṭam; the nityasūris keep looking at him constantly); as in puruśa sūktam uttara bhāgam (end part) “*yō dēvēbya ātapati* I *yō dēvānām purōhita*: ” (one who remains for nityasūris and one who is the object of attainment from time immemorial…); and as said in subhālō’paniśat “S*araṇam gatir nārāyaṇa*: ” (it is nārāyaṇa who is both means and the object of attainment).

When asked who is he who remains as both means and object of attainment, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says …

**nārāyaṇan** – it is nārāyaṇa. The implied meaning is that this name shows as to who is the means and object of attainment.

We will take up 14<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 14. Narayanan ennai ali

*nārāyaṇan – one vho has te divine name of nārāyaṇa ennai āḷi – one vho has made me as his servitor naragattu sērāmal kāKkum – one vho protects (his follovers) from coming to samsāram (materialistic realm) tirumāḷ tan – tirumāl’s pēr āna – divine names pēsa peṟāda – tose vho are unfortunate in not reciting (suc divine names) piṇam – valking like a corpse samayar – tose vho do not believe in vēdas or vho misinterpret vēdas pēsa – babbling kēttu – hearing suc vords palar – many people āsaippattu – desiring to be suc disbelievers or misinterpreters of vēdas āḻvār – tey vill also fall into tat rut.*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**nārayaṇan ennai āḷi – āḻvār says that he is not bothered about the others. śrīman nārayaṇa has made him his servitor.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “You say that nārāyaṇa is both the means and object of attainment. But many in the world do not believe this. What is the reason for this?”. āḻvār responds “This is the handiwork of people who belong to contrary philosophies who are unfit even to talk about him”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: nārāyanan ennai āḷi naragattuc cērāmal kākkum tirumāḷ than pērānā pēsappeṟāda piṇaccamayar pēsakkēttu āsaippattāḻvār palar*

**nārayaṇan ennai āḷi** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is the lord of all the worlds, has such deep affection for āḻvār that he kept āḻvār on one side of a weighing balance and all the others on the other side. When such is the case, why should āḻvār bother about the others!

**nārāyaṇan ennai āḷi** – [emperumānār (bhagavat rāmānujar)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/) in line with this verse, wrote in his [śaraṇāgati gadyam](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/12/saranagathi-gadhyam-5-part-5/) “*akila jagat svāmin asmat svāmin*” (emperumān is the lord of all the worlds; he is also my lord)

When asked as to how [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) protected him, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says..

**naragattuc cērāmal kākkum** – by ensuring that I do not get involved with samsāram (materialistic realm) which is like cruel hell, emperumān protected me. This samsāram is more cruel than the other hells such as rūru, etc. Those hells are very well known for their cruelty and hence one would be wary of them. In the case of samsāram, superficially it would appear that it is very good and hence people would think that they need not fear it and indulge in their desires. Thus this samsāram is very cruel. Both in the previous pāsuram and in this pāsuram, it has been mentioned that nārāyaṇa is both the means and the object of attainment. There is a small difference between the two pāsurams. Means would mean both granting of what is desired and removal of hurdles. The previous pāsuram stressed on granting of what is desired in the verse *vīdākkum meypporuḷdān nārāyaṇan* while in this pāsuram the stress is on the removal of hurdle *nārāyaṇan ennai āḷi naragattuc cērāmal kākkum*.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) was asked as to why [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is coming now to protect him from hell when he had allowed āḻvār to be in this hell from time immemorial. āḻvār says..

**tirumāḷ** – since he is the consort of *puruśakārabhūtai* (one who is playing the recommendatory role) śrī mahālakśmi, due to that relationship he has made āḻvār as his servitor.

**tirumāḷ** – one who is infatuated with śrī mahālakśmi. Even though he is nārāyaṇa \[supreme being\] since he is also totally affectionate towards her, could he bear if she frowns? How can he look up her face if he disregards [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) who is her servitor?

**nārāyaṇan than pērāna** – the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) which denote his supremacy and his infatuation with her.

**pērāna** – his thousand names as denoted by the verses “*dēvō nāmasahasravān*” (lord with a thousand names), “*pērāyiram koṇḍadōr pīdudaiyan nārāyaṇan*” (nārāyaṇa who has a thousand names due to which he has greatness), “*pērum ōrāyiram piṟa pala udaiya nārāyaṇan*” (nārāyaṇa who has a thousand names and many other qualities).

**pēsappeṟāda piṇaccamayar** – those belonging to other philosophies, who do not have the good fortune as a result of which they are like corpses.

**piṇaccamayar** – this could be construed as those belonging to decaying philosophies (like a corpse)

**nārāyaṇan …..pērāna pēsappeṟāda piṇaccamayar** –  isn’t it apt to call those who do not know him who is the only life for all the worlds and those philosophies which they belong to, as being like corpse!

**pēsakkēttu** – hearing that whatever has been said in vēdas is not correct and what has been said outside the vēdas only is true.

**āsaippattu** – being drawn towards them \[who speak against vēdas\], thinking that only these are true

**āḻvār palar** – There are many who get drawn towards the samsāram, in this way.

We will move on to the 15<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 15. Pala devar etta

*pala dēvar – many dēvatās (deities vho are attained by samsāris) ētta – to be praised padi – eart kadandān – emperumān vho measured, his pādam – divine feet malar – flovers ēra vittu – offering iṟainji – vorśipping vāḻtta valar āgil – if you are able to praise him nīrkkaṇḍan kaṇḍa nilai – te position of rudra vho has poisoned neck mārkkaṇḍan kaṇḍa vagaiyē – te vay (sage) mārkkaṇḍēya sav directly varum kaṇḍīr – you vould see tat tis happens*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**pala dēvar ēttap padi kadandān – emperumān measured the worlds in such a way that all the celestial entities praised him. Hasn’t it been celebrated in the māngaḷyastavam of śrī viṣṇu darmam “śanghais surāṇām divi bhūtalastais tatā manuśyair kaganē ca kēcarai: I stuta: kramān ya: prackāra sarvadā mamāstu māngaḷya vivrutdayē hari:  īī ” (let that hari (emperumān) who was praised when he kept his divine feet (during trivikrama incarnation) by the celestial entities in heaven, by the people on earth and by those who keep wandering in the skies, enlarge my auspiciousness)!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said that many people (in samsāram, the materialistic realm) get into the muddle of samsāram repeatedly. Instead of suffering like that, āḻvār asks people to know that emperumān is the apt entity to be attained. Many deities, who are attained by samsāris, carry out kainkaryam (service) to him and there are some people who carry out such kainkaryam directly to emperumān. āḻvār tells samsāris to be like these people and carry out kainkaryam directly to emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: pala dēvar ēttap padi kadandān pādam malarēṟa vittiṟainji vāḻtta valar āgil mārkkaṇḍan kaṇḍa vagaiyē varum kaṇḍīr nīrkkaṇḍan kaṇḍa nilai*

**pala dēvar ētta** – when pitāmaha (grandfather) brahmā himself praised [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with apt hymns from vēdas and washed the divine feet of emperumān when he took the incarnation of trivikrama, is it required to tell about other celestial entities!

**pala dēvar ēttap padi kadandān** – even those who considered themselves as *īśvarōham (*they were the Lords), praised [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) after coming under his divine feet.

**pādam malar ēṟa vittiṟainji vāḻtta valarāgil** – if you are capable of praising him after offering tuḷasi, water lily, lotus, bael (a type of citrus flower) etc on his divine lotus feet.

**padi kadandāṇ pādam ….. vāḻtta valarāgil** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says “you are doing this even now, but not at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)”.  You are doing this at the feet of those celestial entities who praised emperumān earlier (during his trivikrama incarnation). Instead of praising *śarīri* (one who dwells inside the soul), you are praising other *śarīrabhūtas* (those who are a part of his divine body).

**pala dēvar ēttap padikadandān pādam vāḻtta valarāgil** – why don’t you people carry out the service at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) instead of at the feet of those entities who are serving emperumān?

**valar āgil** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) feels that people of this type are rare. While in the previous pāsuram he said *āḻvār palar* (those who repeatedly get entrenched in samsāram are many), here he says that those who carry out service directly to emperumān are very few.

**mārkkaṇḍan…..** – the words should be rephrased as *nīrkkaṇḍan kaṇḍa nilai mārkkaṇḍan kaṇḍa vagaiyē varum kaṇḍīr* **–** you can also see the way that mārkkaṇḍēya (sage) saw in rudra’s position. When mārkkaṇḍēya asked rudra for lengthy longevity and mōkśam thereafter, rudra showed him that it is only janārdana ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) who is capable of granting mōkśa.  [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that we can also see the same situation that mārkkandēya saw in rudra.

This narrative (the discourse between rudra and mārkkaṇḍēya) is explained in the 12<sup>th</sup> skanda (volume) of śrimad bhāgavatam (treatise on the incarnations of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) as well as in the āraṇya parvam of mahābhāratam. It has been widely quoted by many [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/), in praise of emperumān.

We will take up the 16<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 16. Nilai mannum

*annānṟu – during te period vhen te mahābhārata var vas being fought dēvar talaimannar tāmē – (emperumān) himself, vho is te head of all celestial entities māṟṟu āga – positioning as an enemy pala mannar – many kings pōr – in te battlefield māḷa – to be killed vem kadirōn – sūriya (sun) māya – to get set (at an unnatural time) poḻil maṟaiya – te entire eart to be engulfed in darkness tēr āḻiyāl – vit te divine disc maṟaittārāl – by emperumān vho hid (sūrya) en nenjam – my mind (vhic is fickle) nilai mannum – vill hold on in a steadfast manner*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**nilai mannum en nenjam – just as arjuna told kriṣṇa in śrī bhagavat gītā 6-34 “cancalam hi mana: kriṣṇa pramādi bhavat drudam I tasyāham nigraham manyē vāyōriva suduśkaram īī ” (Oh kriṣṇa! The  mind (in good matters) is unstable, makes me to grieve, and is very strong; it is (in lowly matters) very stable. I feel that it is very difficult to control it, similar to controlling wind) and as mentioned by tirumangai āḻvār in periya tirumoḻi 1.1.4 “ninṟavā nillā nenjam” (the mind keeps shifting), my mind, which is very unstable, became stable, says the āḻvār.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about those, who, when someone attains them, instead of protecting them, point them out to another entity (emperumān) who will protect them \[the incidence of mārkaṇḍēya with rudra\]. In this pāsuram, āḻvār speaks about the one who would protect his followers, even if he has to change his basic nature. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: nilai mannum en nenjam annānṟu dēvar talai mannar tāmē māṟṟāga pala mannar pōrmāḷa vengadirōn māyappoḻil maṟaiya tērāḻiyāl maṟaittārāl*

**tērāḻiyāl maṟaittārāl … nilai mannum en nenjam** – just as jitantē ślōkam 5 says “*na tē rūpam na cākārō nāyutāni na cāspatam* I *tatāpi puruśākārō bhaktānām tvam prakāśasē* īī ” (your divine basic nature, divine form, divine weapons and divine place (śrīvaikuṇṭam) are not meant for you; they are meant for your devotees. Thus, even though you are completely under the control of your followers, you shine radiantly as the supreme being) and as mentioned by [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/) in varadarāja stavam “*parijana paribarhā bhūśaṇānyāyudāni pravara guṇagaṇāśca jñānaśaktyā dayastē* I *paramapadamadāṇḍānyātmadēhas tadatmā varada! sakalamētat samśritārtam cakarta:* īī” (Oh varada! You created your servitors (such as [viśvaksēnar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/vishwaksenar/), ādiśēśan et al), your attendants (such as the divine umbrella etc), ornaments, weapons, auspicious qualities such as knowledge, power etc, śrīvaikuṇṭam, the egg-shaped universe, your divine form and your divine nature etc only for your followers), once my heart knew that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has created his divine nature, divine form, auspicious qualities and wealth for the sake of his followers, it became very steady, just like a floating cotton flower gets stabilised once a weight is kept over it.

**nilai mannum en nenjam** – the status which comes on reaching paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) was acquired while being in this world itself, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/).

āḻvār explains the reason for this, in the lines to follow.

**annānṟu** – during the time of mahābhārata war. āḻvār celebrates that period saying that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) came as a *sulabhan* (an easy-to-approach person) who could be seen by even simple-minded women and ignorant people.

**dēvar talai mannar tāmē** – vasn’t it the Lord of nityasūris who did this! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) melts down thinking of the supreme being , who is praised by vēdas and mahābhāratam and who came down as a very simple person, and wonders at this simplicity.

**tāmē māṟṟāga** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) did not consider duryōdana et al as the enemies of pāṇḍavas. He considered them as his enemies. In mahābhāratam uttara parvam 24-27, kriṣṇa tells duryōdana “*dviśadannam na bhōktavyam dviśantam naiva bhōjayēt* I *pāṇḍavān dviśasē rājan mama prāṇā hi pāṇḍavā:* īī” (it is not apt to consume the food offered by one who hates; it is not correct to offer food to one who hates. Oh King! You hate the pāṇḍavas. āren’t pāṇḍavas my life-line!)

**tāmē māṟṟāga** – isn’t the one who hates the body, an enemy to the one who occupies (pervades) that body too? \[here the reference is to pāṇḍavas and to kriṣṇa who has pervaded their bodies and soul\]. Hasn’t [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) mentioned mercifully in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7.18](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-18/) “*jñāni tvātmaiva mēmatam* ” (jñāni (knowledgeable person) is my sustenance; this is my opinion) implying that they are his followers.

**pala mannar pōr māḷa** – ensuring that the egoistical kings died in the war.

**vengadirōn māya** – even sūrya, who cannot be hidden by any object due to his powerful rays, was hidden.

**venga dirōn māya** – sūrya, who carries out the task of rising and setting as per [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s orders, as mentioned by taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 7 “*bhīśōdēti sūrya:*” (sūrya rises out of fear of emperumān), disappeared at an unnatural time.

**poḻil maṟaiya** – ensuring that the entire earth was in darkness. In normal times, when sun sets in one place, he may be still shining in some other place on earth. In order to prevent jayadrada’s (a King and staunch supporter of duryōdana) friends from claiming that sun had not set in the place where they were hiding, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) made the entire earth dark by making the sun set.

**tērāḻiyāl maṟaittārāl** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that his heart will get stabilised by the one who hid sūrya with his divine disc which has thousand times the resplendence of sūrya. *tērāḻi* – a part of chariot; here it refers to the wheel \[and thus to emperumān’s divine disc\]. pūrvācāryars (preceptors in our philosophy) will aver that the divine disc, being thousand times brighter than sūrya, blunted the eyes and hid sūrya.

The narrative here is that arjuna had avowed that he would kill jayadrata, who had killed arjuna’s son abhimanyu, before the sun set that day and if he did not carry out his vow, he would kill himself by entering a pyre. duryōdana et al hid jayadrata so that arjuna would not be able to kill him and thus face death himself, as he had avowed. It is very famously mentioned in mahābhāratam that kriṣṇa, who had given his word that he will not take to arms (take part in the war with his weapons) had taken out his divine disc, thus falsifying his word, and hidden sūrya to enable arjuna to kill jayadrata.

**dēvar talai mannar tāmē tērāḻiyāl maṟaittārāl – nilai mannum en nenjam** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that his mind calmed down after knowing that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is described by vēdas (sacred texts) as *satyakāma* (one who has truthful qualities) and *satyasankalpa* (one whose vows are always fulfilled), broke his world in order to fulfil the words of his follower \[arjuna\]. After knowing that emperumān, who speaks only truth, broke his word in order to protect his follower, āḻvār’s mind which was lamenting that it did not have means such as truthfulness, penance, control of self (both internal and external), ability to donate, follow dharma (righteous path) diligently etc which are mentioned in the vēdas as apt for reaching paramapadam, calmed down and became stable.

We will go on to the 17<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 17. Ala nilarkil

*mey tavattōn – rudra, vho has true knovledge about te metods of penance jñālam aḷandānai – one vho measured te vorlds āḻi kidandānai – one vho reclined in te milky ocean (kśīrābdi) āl mēl vaḷarndānai – emperumān vho lay on te tender banyan leaf tān vaṇangum āṟu – te pat by vhic he vorśips aṟam neṟiyai – te righteous pat mēlai yugattu – in earlier yuga (period of time) āla niḻal kīḻ – in te śadov of a banyan tree nālvarkku – to four great sages uraittān – discoursed (instructed)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**ālaniḻaṟ kīḻ aṟaneṟiyai nālvarkku mēlai yugatturaittān – rudra gave a discourse on the path of devotion (as a means of attaining emperumān) to four great sages, agastya, dakśa, pulastiya and kaśyapa, in the shadow of a banyan tree, in a previous yuga (a period of time) and thus protected them.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When some people asked āḻvār “did not rudra protect those who attained him?”, āḻvār mercifully responded saying ”isn’t it by speaking about the auspicious qualities of emperumān and telling them that he worships emperumān, that rudra, who is distinguished in his knowledge protects those who attain him?”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: Ala niḻaṟkīḻ aṟaneṟiyai nālvarkku mēlai yugatturaittān meyttavattōn jñālam aḷandānai āḻikkidandānai ālmēl vaḷarndānait tān vaṇangumāṟu*

**meyttavattōn** – rudra, who had observed rare to practice penances, to the extent that one would say “this is the true penance; there is no penance beyond this”

If one were to ask as to by saying what, did rudra protect the four great sages…

**āḻikkidandānai** – one who reclined in the *tiruppāṟkadal* (milky ocean) in order to take many incarnations \[to protect his devotees\]

**jñālam aḷandānai** – later taking the incarnation of trivikrama and stroking all the people of the world with his divine feet, without distinguishing between a vaśiśta (a great sage) and a caṇḍāḷa (a person belonging to a very lowly birth)

**Al mēl vaḷarndānai** – the amazing entity who lay on a tender banyan leaf, after swallowing and protecting all the cētana (sentient) and acētana (insentient) entities during the time of deluge.

**tān vaṇangumāṟu** – rudra instructed the four great riśis, the way he was vorśipping [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), and thus protected them.

## 18. Maraya danavanai

*māṟu āya – standing as an opponent dānavanai – te demon hiraṇya kaśyap’s mārvu – cest vaḷ ugirāl – vit śarp finger nails iraṇḍu kūṟagak kīṟiya – splitting it into tvo kōḷ ariyai – narasimha mūrty, vho is very strong vēṟu āga – as a dinstinguiśed entity ētti iruppārai – tose vho vorśip him maṟṟu avarai sātti iruppār tavam vellumē – te good deed of te ultimate devotees vho attain suc devotees of emperumān \[as mentioned in te previous vord\] vill defeat.*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**māṟāya dānavanai – the demon hiraṇyāsuran who opposed emperumān ; one who thought of himself as being the supreme being and who opposed emperumān himself.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After āḻvār said “ālmēl vaḷarndānait tān vaṇangumāṟu …..uraittān” (as mentioned in the previous pāsuram), some people asked him “would you say that attaining emperumān is the most superior means?”. āḻvār responds in this pāsuram saying “attaining his followers is superior to attaining emperumān”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: māṟāya dānavanai vaḷḷugirāl mārviraṇḍu kūṟākak kīṟiya kōḷariyai vēṟāga ēttiyiruppārai vellumē maṟṟavaraic cāttiyiruppar tavam*

**māṟāya dānavanai** – one who remained until the end as an opponent to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). ḍoesn’t emperumān annihilate only those who remain as an opponent even after he says as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 59-114 “*gaccānujānāmi*” (you may go; I allow you) \[this was said by śrī rāma to rāvaṇa after destroying his chariot and weapons, allowing him to ponder in his quarters\].

**māṟāya dānavanai** – hiraṇya kaśyap who remained as an opponent with all his three faculties of mind, speech and deed. emperumān does not kill if his opponent opposes him with only two out of the three faculties. He kills only those who oppose him with their minds. Hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mentioned in his [tiruvāimoḻi 2-6-6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/04/thiruvaimozhi-2-6-6-unnaich-chindhai/) “*unnaic cindaiyināl igaḻnda iraṇiyan agalmārvam kīṇḍa en munnaik kōḷariyē* ” (Oh the powerful lion! You tore the heart of hiraṇya who despised you with his thoughts)! For emperumān to accept someone, it is enough if that person displays a mere intention. But he does not give up a person unless that person hates him completely, with his heart.

**māṟāya dānavanai** – the demon who slid down towards his lifeline itself. Just as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself had said as in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7.18](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-18/) “*jñānī tvātmaiva mē matam*” (my philosophy is that the knowledgeable person is like my soul) and in mahābhāratam uttara parvam 24-23 “*mama pṟāṇā hi pāṇḍavā*: ” (the pāṇḍavas are like my life), did not hiraṇya kaśyap carry out offence towards prahlāda, who emperumān considered as his life?

**māṟāya dānavanai** – emperumān does not consider someone who opposes him as his foe. But if someone opposes his followers, he considers such a person as his foe. Just as it is said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 24-34 “*krōdamāhārayat tīvram*” (śrī rāma made himself very angry), śrī rāma took on an angry makeup when arrows \[from rāvaṇa’s bow\] fell on him. When the arrows fell on *tiruvadi* (hanuman), as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 59-136 “*ata rāmō mahātējā rāvaṇēna krutavraṇam* I *druśtva plavagaśārtdūlam kōpasya vaśamēyivān*  īī” (the radiant śrī rāma, on seeing the injuries suffered by the great monkey hanuman due to rāvaṇa, became anger personified), did not [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) become anger personified?

**vaḷḷugirāl kīṟiya** – emperumān effortlessly carried out the task with his fingernails.

**mārviraṇḍu kūṟāgak kīṟiya kōḷariyai** – Is it required to split hiraṇya kaśyap’s chest into two in order to kill him? Wouldn’t an angry snort (*hūnkāra*) have been sufficient for this? It is said that emperumān wanted to see if there was some wetness (indicative of affection) in hiraṇya’s heart and this is why he used his fingernails to check for this. ḍidn’t [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) too mercifully mention in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 1-6-9 “*uḷandottu iraṇiyan oṇmārvagalam piḷanditta kai*” ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) touched the heart of hiraṇya with his hand and tore it)!

**vaḷḷugirāl kīṟiya** – emperumān carried out the task by fulfilling brahmā’s boon that hiraṇya will not be killed by weapon.

**kīṟiya** – emperumān tore the heart effortlessly, similar to tearing a dry blade of grass.

**kōḷariyai** – the mightily strong narasimha.

**vēṟāga ēttiyiruppārai** – those who vorśipped in a distinguished manner. The distinction comes out because the one who is protected \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s follower\] meditates on the divine activities of emperumān in earlier times, worries about what will happen, accepts the protection and praises emperumān. These are people who are similar to the ones mentioned by [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) in his [tiruppallāṇḍu 6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/10/thiruppallandu-6-enthai-thanthai/) “*andiyam pōdil ari uruvāgi ariyai aḻittavanai pandanai tīrap pallāṇḍu pallāndu enṟu pādudumē* ” (we will praise emperumān who killed he demon hiraṇya, the enemy of emperumān’s follower prahlāda, during the time of dusk, in the form of narasimha (lion head and human body) so that his pain will be relieved).

**maṟṟavaraic cāttiyiruppār** – those who hold on to such śrīvaiṣṇavas who sing pallāṇḍu to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) (those who praise emperumān, as seen in the previous verse), keeping them as the means as well as the object of attainment.

**maṟṟu** – just as it is mentioned by [madurakavi āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/madhurakavi-azhwar/) in his [kaṇṇinuṇ ciṟuttāmbu 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/kanninun-chiru-thambu-2-navinal/) “*dēvu maṟṟaṟiyēn*” (I do not know of any other God…), these people will not have emperumān as the means. Since he is vorśipped by those whom they have held on to, they will look at emperumān. These are people who are mentioned as in [āṇḍāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/andal/)’s nācciyār tirumoḻi 10.10 “*villi puduvai vittu sittar tangaḷ dēvarai vallaparisu varuvipparēl adu kāṇḍumē*” (if vittucittan (periyāḻvār) calls out to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), then we will see and worship emperumān) \[this implies that on our won, we will not worship emperumān, but will worship him if the person that we have attained, shows him to us and asks us to worship him\].

**ēttiyiruppārai vellumē maṟṟavaraic cāttiyiruppār tavam** – the virtues earned by those who hold on to the followers of emperumān will win over the virtues earned by the followers of emperumān. If one holds on to the hands of another person and asks for help, that may be rejected; but if he holds on to the legs of the person and asks for help, can it be denied? In the same way, those who hold on to emperumān may have doubts on the end result. But in the case of those who hold on to the followers of emperumān, the result is guaranteed. The superior meaning of this pāsuram is that engaging with śrīvaiṣṇavas (followers of emperumān) is better than engaging directly with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Here, the word *tavam* refers to that which is liked by emperumān.

We will take up the 19<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 19. Tavam seydu

*tavam seydu – carrying out penance nānmuganāl – from brahmā peṟṟa – (tose entities suc as hiraṇyakaśyap vho) obtained varattai – boons avam seyda – one vho ruined tem āḻiyān anṟē – aren’t you te emperumān vit divine disc in your divine hand! emmai – us uvandu – vit a happy divine mind kāppāy nī – you are te only one vho protects kāppadanai āvāy nī – te vov to protect is also yours only evvuyirkkum – for all cētanas (vho attained you) vaikundam – paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) ippāyum – one vho grants nī – it  is only you*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**tavam seydu nānmuganāl peṟṟa varattai avam seyda āḻiyān anṟē – considering himself as the creator of worlds, brahmā, while being born from your navel, out of vanity, wanted to be a grantor of boons, without analysing the results of granting such boons. Is he the entity to be attained or are you \[emperumān\] who, without making such boons ineffective, transformed yourself as half-lion, half human, and killed the demon hiraṇya at dusk with your finger nails, the entity to be attained? Only you have to respond, says āḻvār.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asks emperumān to fulfil his desires too just as he protects the celestial entities such as brahmā et al, who have limited knowledge. While brahmā et al become devotees of emperumān when the quality of satvam (goodness) becomes predominant in them, they think that they are the lords when they are under the control of rajas (emotional; aggressive) and tamas (lazy; ignorant) qualities and grant boons to demons such as hiraṇya kaśyap, only to suffer later. Such people are not apt to be attained ever. āḻvār requests emperumān to become his protector too just as he saves brahmā et al from various dangers. One can consider from this pāsuram until the 26th as a dialogue between emperumān and āḻvār as well as experiencing of emperumān’s auspicious qualities by āḻvār. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: tavam seydu nānmuganāl peṟṟa varattai avam seyda āḻiyān anṟe uvandemmaik kāppāy nī kāppadanai āvāy nī vaikundam īppāyum evvuyirkkum nī Word for Word Meanings*

**tavam seydu** – this could be construed as referring to rāvaṇa. brahmā et al granted boons to rāvaṇa and suffered. Are such entities apt to be attained or are you, in order to fulfil their boons, took the form of a human, and killed rāvaṇa et al and gave back their residences, the apt entity to be attained?

**āḻiyānanṟē** – the implied meaning is that entities such as brahmā et al who cannot protect themselves are unfit to be attained while [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who constantly has the divine disc in his hand in order to protect his followers, is the protector. When protecting, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) does not consider that he is carrying out something for the sake of his followers; he feels elated that he is carrying out something for his sake. Did not vālmīki mention in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam “*abhiśicya ca lankāyām rākśasēndram vibhīśaṇam* I *krutakrutyas tadā rāmō vijvara*: *pramumōda*:  īī ” (after performing the coronation of vibhīśaṇa, the head of demons, in lankā, śrī rāma felt as if he had carried out a task for himself and felt joyous)!

**uvandemmaik kāppāy nī ….. tavam seydu nānmuganāl peṟṟa** – is it required to spell out as to whether entities such as brahmā et al who granted boons which will get destroyed, after carrying out hard penance, are apt to be attained or emperumān who carries out tasks for his followers as his own and feels happy about it?

**kāppadanaiāvāy nī** – you \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] are the protector; the *sankalpa* (vow) to protect is also yours. This implies that on a few occasions when brahmā et al protect their followers, it is emperumān who, as their *antaryāmi* (indvelling soul) protects them.  Hasn’t emperumān himself mercifully said this in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7-21](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-21/), [22](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-22/) “*yō yō yām yām tanum bhakti: śraddayārccidum iccati* I ……*labhātē ca tata kāmān mayaiva vihitān hi tān* īī” (whoever earnestly worships whichever deities, who are all part of my physical form, obtain whatever they had asked for from those deities, which are given to those deities by me)!

**kāppadanaiyāvāy nī** – could one expect the rakśaṇa dharma (the duty to protect) from anyone else? parāśara maharishi in viṣṇu purāṇam ślōkam 1-22-21  says “*na hi pālana sāmartyam rutē sarvēśvaram harim* I *stitau stitam mahābhāga bhavatyanyasya kasyacit* īī” (is there anyone other than the supreme being hari, who is fully engaged in protecting, who has the capacity to protect others?)

**vaikuṇḍham ippāyum evvuyirkkum nī** – let the other capabilities remain. Is there anyone who has the capability to grant mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) so that one need not return to earth and suffer again?  Haven’t śāstras loudly proclaimed, as in puruśa sūktam, nārāyaṇa sūktam etc “*utāmrutatvasyēśāna:* ” (only he is the supreme lord for paramapadam too); “*tamēvam vidvānamruta iha bhavati nānya: pantā ayanāya vidyatē*  “ (only he who knows [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) like this attains mōkśam; there is no other way); “*amrutasyaiśya sētu:*” (he is the bridge to reach mōkśam); “*mōkśamiccēt janārdanāt*” (mōkśam should be desired only from janārdana (emperumān)).

Thus in this pāsuram, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) has mercifully stated that brahmā et al, who are  not capable of protecting themselves, are not the apt entities to be attained and it is only emperumān, who protects every creature, who is apt to be attained.

We will take up the 20<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 20. Niye ulagellam

*ulagu ellām nīyē – you are te controller of all te vorlds niṟpanavum – te stable, puruśārtam (end benefit) of mōkśam (liberation / śrīvaikuṇṭam) nin aruḷē – due to your mercy alone tavam dēvar dēvanum  nīyē – you are te head of celestial entities suc as brahmā et al vho became celestial entities after carrying out penance. eri sudarum – te splendorous agni (fire) māl varaiyum – te huge mountains vhic sustain eart eṇ disaiyum – all te entities present in te eight directions aṇḍattu – present in te universe iru sudarum – candra sūrya (moon and sun) āya ivai – all tese nīyē – are only you*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**nīyē ulagellām – many pramāṇams (authentic sources of information) have been given to substantiate that emperumān is all pervading.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After āḻvār had said “kāppāy nī kāppadanai āvāy nī vaikundam īppāyum evvuyirkkum nī” \[as in the previous pāsuram\], emperumān became very happy. Just as it is said “kim mruśtam sutavacanam” (Which is the sweetest to hear? It is hearing one’s child speaking) emperumān, desirous of hearing more sweet words from āḻvār, tells āḻvār “āḻvīr! Your pāsurams are very sweet to hear. Compose one more pāsuram”. āḻvār immediately composes another pāsuram (this) dipping the all pervading quality of emperumān in his mellifluous words and offers to emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: nīyē ulagellām ninnaruḷē niṟpanavum nīyē tavattēva dēvanum nīyē erisudarum māl varaiyum endisaiyum aṇḍattu irusudarum āya ivai*

nārāyaṇa anuvākam – “*viśvam nārāyaṇam harim*” (he is the indvelling soul of all the worlds)

[śrī bhagavat gītā 11-40](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/11-40/): “*sarvam samāpnōśi tatō’Si sarva:*” (since you pervade everything you are everything)

mahābhāratam – “*tatstatvādanupaśyanti hyēka ēvēti sādava:*” (since [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has pervaded the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), sādus (ascetics) see them \[emperumān and jīvātmā\] as one)

brahma sūtram – “*avastitēriti kāsakrutsna*”

Since no creation can exist if separated from him and since such a separated state cannot be seen, there is nothing wrong in saying that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the world.

**ninnaruḷē niṟpanavum** – the pruruśārtam mōkśam (liberation or śrīvaikuṇṭam), which is stable and permanent, happens due to your mercy. This can also be construed as – all creations exist in a stable way, due to your mercy. But since this meaning is conveyed by the previous verse *nīyē ulagellām* periyavāccān piḷḷai, the vyākyātā (commentator) for this prabandam supported the meaning relating to puruśārtam. Since the goal in paramapadam is unlimited, plural number *niṟpana* has been used.

**nīyē tavattēva dēvanum** – you are the lord of those who became deities after carrying out penance. svētasvatara upaniśat says “*tam dēvanām paramanca daivatam*” (emperumān is the supreme deity for all those deities). mahābhāratam sabā parvam 14-8 says “*yugakōti sahasrāṇi viṣṇumārādya padmabhū:* I *punas trailōkyadātrutvam prāpatavāniti śuśruma:* īī ” (we hear that by carrying out penance towards viṣṇu for thousands of crores of yugas, brahmā attained the position of being the head of the three worlds once again). nārasimha purāṇam says “*brahmādayas surās sarvē viṣṇumāradya tē purā* I *svam svam padamanuprāptā: kēśavasya prasādata:* īī ” (dēvas such as brahmā et al vorśipped viṣṇu and with his mercy attained their respective position). mahābhāratam says “*mahādēvas sarvamēdē mahātmā hutvā’tmānam dēvadēvō babhūva*” (by sacrificing himself in the sarvamēda yāgam (a ritual), the great soul mahādēva (rudra) became a deity). Thus in all these cases, it is clearly established that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the supreme deity for all those who became deities after carrying out penance towards him.

**nīyē erisudarum māl varaiyum …** – is it required to say these separately? You are the burning fire, the huge mountains which sustain earth, the eight directions, sūrya and candra (sun and moon) which are within the universe. All these are under your control. This is in line with the śrī viṣṇu purāṇa ślōkam 2-12-38 “*jyōtimśi viṣṇur bhuvanāni viṣṇur vanāni viṣṇur girayō diśaśca*” (all the radiant objects are viṣṇu; all the worlds are viṣṇu; all the forests are viṣṇu; all the mountains and directions are viṣṇu).

We will move on to the 21<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 21. Ivaiya pilavay

*tiṟandu – spread out eri kāṇṟa – spitting fire pilavāy – mout as huge as a cave ivaiyā – is it tis? erivatta – like a huge ball of fire, being round and gloving kaṇgaḷ – divine eyes ivaiyā – are tese? eri – like fire pongi – tumultuous kāttum – having a divine form imaiyōr perumān – lord of nityasūris, vho controls tem ari – as lion faced pongi – tumultuous kāttum – śovn aḻagu – beauty iviayā – is it tis?*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**ivaiyā pilavāy – Could one sing in a pāsuram how an entity, who is spoken of as in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-6 “avākyanātara:” (he will not engage in conversation with anyone and he does not have any likes or dislikes) and as in muṇḍanīka upaniśats 1-1-7  “vrukśa iva stabdō divi tiśtati” (he stands like an unbending tree in paramapada), suffers for the sake of his followers?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After hearing the sweetened words of āḻvār, emperumān became very happy. His divine mouth, divine eyes and divine form blossomed and became radiant. āḻvār became wondrous at the change in the form of emperumān, a change that emperumān brought on himself for the sake of his follower (āḻvār) even though vēdas (sacred texts) refer to him as one who is unchanged in his form (avikārāya). Immediately emperumān’s form as narasimha, a combination of human head with lion’s body, which he took when his devotee prahlāda was tortured, came to his mind. āḻvār engages with this form of narasimha, wondering as to how emperumān transformed himself for the sake of a follower. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: ivaiyā pilavāy tiṟanderikāṇṟa ivaiyā eri vattak kaṇgaḷ ivaiyā eri pongik kāttum imaiyōr perumān ari pongik kāttum aḻagu*

**ivaiyā pilavāy tiṟanderi kānṟa** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine mouth is described by [tiruppāṇāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thiruppanazhwar/) in his amalanādipirān 7 as “*māyanār seyya vāy aiyō ennaic cindai kavarndaduvē*”  (the divine, reddish mouth of the amazing entity engaged my mind. How is this?) and by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his tiruvāimoḻi 7-7-3 “*vāliyadōr kanikol vinaiyāttiyēn valvinaikol kōlattiraḷ pavaḷat tuṇḍangolō aṟiyēn neelanedumugil pōl tirumēniyammān toṇḍai vāy*” (emperumān’s divine mouth appears like a ripened fruit fit to be enjoyed, but [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/), in the guise of parāngusa nāyaki, is sorrowful that her cruel sins have taken a form to prevent her from enjoying that). How amazing that such a divine, beautiful mouth could spit fire after opening out like a cave!

**ivaiyā** – this could be construed as *ivai Ah* – here,  *Ah* would mean that this is very wondrous. Alternatively, this could be taken as one word *ivaiyā* which would mean is this (same as the other one)? \[in other words, such a divine, fruit like mouth of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is spitting fire after opening out like a cave; are the two, the same or are they different?\] [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) is wondering whether the mouth that he is seeing, looked different during emperumān’s incarnation as narasimha by opening out like the mouth of a cave and spitting fire from it.

**ivaiyā erivattak kaṇgaḷ** – would the divine eyes, which were sung about by [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) (1) as in periya tirumoḻi 7-1-9 “*sudar māmadi pōl uyirkkellām tāyāyaḷikkinṟa taṇḍāmaraikkaṇṇā*” (Oh one with cool, comforting lotus eyes! Your eyes are like the bright, huge moon looking at the people of this world like a mother), (2) as in tiruvāimoḻi 7-7-1 “*sūḻavum tāmarai nāṇmalar poḷ vandu tōnṟum kaṇḍīr*” ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s eyes will appear from all four sides like lotus which has just then blossomed) and (3) as in amalanādippirān 8 “*kariyavāgip pudai parandu miḷirndu sevvariyōdi nīṇḍa apperiyavāya kaṇgaḷ ennaip pēdamai seydanavē*” (those black, expansive, bright, with reddish lines, long eyes made me lose my control), which attract his followers and engage with them, become inimical in the case of the enemies of followers and spit fire? How amazing that these eyes have become balls of fire and are spitting fire?

**ivaiyā eripongik kāttum imaiyōr perumān ari pongik kāttum aḻagu** – emperumān, whose divine form is radiant and who is the lord of nityasūris, transformed himself, for the sake of his follower, into a lion and how beautiful is this!

**eri pongik kāttum** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine form will outwit all the radiant, glowing objects. His divine form is described in muṇḍakōpaniśat 2-2-11 as “*na tatra sūryō bhāti na candratārakam nēmā vidyutō bhāti kutō’yamagni*” (in the presence of that paramātmā (supreme being) sun and moon will not shine; lightning too will not look bright; is there a need to talk about agni (fire)?)

**imaiyōr perumān** – It is implied that since he is the lord of celestial entities, he carried out this task (of transforming himself into a lion, spitting fire) for  their sake.  It also implies that they vilfully became his servitors after seeing this beauty and losing out to it.

**ari pongik kāttum aḻagu** – even though he is the supreme being, his beauty in the form of a furious narasimha was superior to that of his original form (as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)). Wasn’t it to experience and enjoy this beauty of narasimha, which was like sugar mixed well with milk, that [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) mercifully composed a whole decad in periya tirumoḻi 1.7 “*angaṇ jñālam*”!

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) thus mercifully mentioned as to how an entity \[emperumān\], who does not change at all, transforms himself for the same of its followers.

We will take up the 22<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 22. Alagiyan tane

*vittu tān – he is te cause for te entire universe ēḻ ulagukkum – for te seven vorlds tanmaikkum – qualities (suc as rūpa, rasa, ganda etc (form, taste, smell etc) of te five senses) tanmaiyan – being te repository kuḻavi āy – being in te form of an infant (during praḷaya, deluge) mīn āy – being in te form of fiś (at some point of time) uyir aḷikkum – one vho protects te souls tānē – emperumān himself ari uruvan – has te form of narasimha (lion face and human body) tānē aḻagiyān – he himself is te boundary for beauty paḻagiyān – tat emperumān, vho is tere from time immemorial, his tāḷē – divine feet paṇimin – attain*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**aḻagiyān tānē . . . . – the narrative on narasimha avatāram (incarnation as Lord narasimha) which āḻvār enjoyed in the previous pāsuram, continues in this pāsuram.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*With the joy of experiencing the beauty of narasimha, āḻvār thinks that some people are missing out on this great experience and instructs them “uplift yourselves by vorśipping this beautiful form of emperumān”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: aḻagiyān tānē ari uruvan tānē paḻagiyān tāḷē  paṇimin kuḻaviyāyt tānēḻ ulagukkum tanmaikkum tanmaiyanē mīnāy uyiraḷikkum vittu*

**aḻagiyān tānē** – he ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) is the only one with beauty in him. When asked whether it is paramapadanātan (the Lord of śrīvaikuṇṭam) who is beautiful, āḻvār responds …

**ariyuruvan tānē** – it is only he who took incarnation as *narasimha mūrti* (in the form of lion head and human body) who is beautiful. If emperumān’s beauty in paramapadam is similar to lighting a lamp in daylight , his beauty in the form of narasimha is like lighting a lamp in darkness. There (in parapadam) one can only see his mercy. Here (on earth) once can see his mercy along with his fury. There he shows his beauty to those who are as knowledgeable as he is while here he shows his beauty to the ignorant. That beauty is something that we only hear while this beauty is something which as [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) has mercifully said in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4.1.1 “*iraṇiyanadāgam piḷandariyāy udiram aḷainda kaiyōdirundānai uḷḷavā kaṇḍār uḷar*” (there are people who have seen [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) truly, splitting the chest of the demon iraṇiya and with blood stained hands, rubbing his hands on iraṇiyan’s chest).

**aḻagiyān tānē ariyuruvan tānē** – isn’t he the one who has been praised the world over as *aḻagiya singar* (the beautiful lion)!

**aḻagiyān tānē ariyuruvan tānē** – these words appear to be the translation for the verse in śrī viṣṇu sahasranāman “*nārasimhavapu: śrīmān*” .

**aḻagiyān tānē …..paṇimin** – once a person looks at this beauty of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), śouldn’t he bow down to his divine feet?

Is it only beauty? Is there no valour?

**ariyuruvan tānē …. paṇimin** – śouldn’t one worship him who is the boundary for valour? When one, who is the refuge in times of danger, is himself beautiful, could one let go of him?

Even though he may be the refuge during times of danger and may be beautiful,  śouldn’t he also have known our disease and our desire?

**paḻagiyāntāḷē paṇimin** – he is the *purāṇa puruśa* (an entity who could be termed as ancient) who knows that the disease for you people is this body (physical form) which should be got rid of and that the desire is getting an *aprākruta śarīra* (a body which is not made of the primordial matter), which you may be not be aware of, yourselves. Attain such an entity.

**paḻagiyāntāḷē paṇimin** – if you bow down to his divine feet, holding them as your means as well as object of attainment, then he, who knows that it is your past deeds which make you keep getting this physical form for a long time and that the antidote for it is only his mercy, will protect you.

**paḻagiyāntāḷē paṇimin** – just as people would hold on to a physician who is renowned for a long time and who knows the nature of the body as well as of the medicines, attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is variously said as in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-3-6 “*maruttuvanāy ninṟa māmaṇivaṇṇā* ” (Oh one with the glitter of gemstones and who is like a physician!), as in vēdam “*vaidyō nārāyaṇō hari:*” (śrīman nārāyaṇa, who is the physician), as in śrī viṣṇu sahasranāmam “*bhēśajam bhiśak*” (he is both the medicine and the physician) instead of attaining other deities.

How can it be said that he knows this body which is apt to be given up and the body which is apt to be taken up \[to attain śrīvaikuṇṭam\]?

**tānēḻulagukkum tanmaikkum tanmaiyanē** – he is the indvelling soul for all the worlds. Who else, apart from him, would know the disease, the patient and the medicine?

**tānēḻulagukkum tanmaikkum tanmaiyanē** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is *prakruti śarīra viśiśtabhūtan* (one who is conjoined with primordial matter, he being its indvelling soul and the primordial matter forming a part of his body), is the repository for *mahān* (great matter), *ahankāram* (ego matter) and *pancabhūtam* (five elements of ether, wind, fire, water and earth); thus he is the causative entity for the seven resultant factors. He is also the repository for the five causative factors for these five elements, namely *śabda, sparśa, rūpa*, rasa and *ganda* (sound, feel, form, taste and smell respectively). *tanmai* is ‘tanmātrai’ viz. subtle element. In other words, the previous state for each of the five elements, which is the respective subtle or rudimentary element. Thus, for example, for sky, the subtle element is sound and the quality of sky will be implicitly held in sound; the same is true for the other four elements too. There are huge differences between the quality and subtle element. The subtle elements will be matter and be the causative factors for each of the five elements. Quality is not like this. The meaning for *tānēḻulagukkum ….. tanmaiyanē* is that he is the causative entity for all the mutually causative and effective (resultant) entities. The word *ēḻulagu* refers to the resultant factors of five elements, *mahān* and *ahankāram*.

Alternatively, **tānēḻulugakkum tanmaikkum tanmaiyanē** – *ēḻulagu* would mean the seven upper worlds of bhū:, bhuva:, suva: etc up to satya lōkam and the seven lower worlds of atala, vitala, sutala etc down to pātāla lōkam with all the sentient and insentient entities living in these worlds. The word *tanmaikkum* would refer to the qualities of the insentient entities such as śabda, sparśa etc \[seen previously\] and the qualities of sentient entities. Thus, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the substrate for all the sentient and insentient entities and their qualities. When they (the worlds and the entities living in them) are his attributes, there is no difference between the *guṇa* (qualities) and the *guṇi* (one who or which has the qualities). If the text is *than vaikkum tanmaiyanē* it would mean that he has the seven worlds within himself as a natural phenomenon.

If asked whether emperumān protects us ……

**kuḻaviyāy mīnāy uyiraḷikkum vittu** – is there any protection that he has not given us? During deluge, he kept us in his stomach and protected us. As mercifully mentioned by [parāśara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) (son of [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/) and disciple of [embār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/embar/)) in śrī rangarāja stavam 2-60 “*mīnatanus tvam nāvi nidāya stiracaraparikaramanu manubhagavān* I *vēdasanābi svōkti vinōdai ragalitalaya bhayalavamamumavaha:* īī” (Oh [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)! Taking the form of a fish, you kept representatives of animal and plant species in a boat, near manu (a King) and with the strange words of vēdas you protected them without any danger from deluge), during deluge, emperumān took representatives of animal and plant species along with their leader manu on a boat with his will and, taking the form of a fish, navigated the boat to safety from deluge and protected them. In the same way, he returned the vēdas which are like eyes for brahmā, to brahmā and aided him in the act of creation. In this manner, he acted like a fish and gave life to species.

**vittu** – apart from these, as mentioned by cāndōjña upaniśat 6-2-1 “*sadēva sōmya*” he remained as the causative, materialistic and accessorial reason for all of us.

We will take up the 23<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 23. Vittumida

*\[īn tat case, hov does one spend te rest of his life?\] moyttu eḻunda – rising togeter kār mēgam anna – like rain bearing clouds karu māl – te dark coloured tirumāl’s (emperumān’s) tirumēni – divine form nīr vānam – te clouds vit plenty of vater nigaḻndu – standing right in front kāttum – vill śov (by looking at tis simile for emperumān, ve can sustain ourselves)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**pattiyuḻavan paḻampunattu vittumida vēndungolō – is it required of us to sow the seed of puṇya (virtuous deeds) on this old land (world) of paramapuruśa (supreme being, emperumān) who is toiling to raise the produce of bhakti (devotion) in the cētana (sentient entity)?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After āḻvār mentioned in the previous pāsuram “paḻagiyān tāḷē paṇimim” (the way to get rid of samsāram (materialistic realm) is to attain the divine feet of the supreme entity who has the complexion of gem stones and who stands like a physician to cure us of samsāram), he was asked by some people “One has to toil hard even to obtain small benefits. When such is the case, is it enough if one were to bow down at the divine feet of emperumān in order to attain mōkśam”, āḻvār says “is anything expected from the cētana (sentient entity) when emperumān protects all by incarnating as lion or fish when the cētana takes a birth in this world and by keeping all the entities in his stomach during deluge when the cētana has no name or form?”. He says that if the cētana develops a desire to attain emperumān through his natural knowledge, emperumān will take care of all the activities which are required to be carried out to attain him. What is there to be done by the cētana beyond having that desire? Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: vittum ida vēṇḍungolō vidaiyadartta patti uḻavan panam puḻattu moytteḻunda kār mēgam anna karumāl tirumēni nīr vānam kāttum nigaḻndu vidai adartta – one who controlled the bulls (for winning the hand of nappinnaip pirātti) patti uḻavan – emperumān who takes all the efforts for the cētana to develop devotion towards him paḻam punattu – in this land (samsāram, the materialistic realm) which is very old vittum ida vēṇḍunm kolō – is it required to sow the seed (of making one’s own efforts to attain emperumān)? \[No, it is not required\]*

**pattiyuḻavan** – are the efforts put in by this farmer (emperumān) in order to germinate the plant of bhakti in the mind of cētana, only a little bit? Just as a farmer ploughs the land, waters it, plants the seeds, germinates them, removes the weed, applies manure to the plants etc, the efforts put in by emperumān to cultivate the plant of bhakti (in the samsāris) cannot be contained in any measure. While *tripād vibhūti*  (śrīvaikuṇṭam which is three times the size of samsāram) is very fertile, emperumān is unable to bear the barrenness of *leelā vibhūti*  (samsāram). Thus he creates the worlds, the cētanas (sentient entities), gives them body and sensory perceptions, remains as their antaryāmi (indvelling soul) and protects them constantly. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) reminisces such activities with these words. Hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too spoken of him as the farmer in his [tiruvāimoḻi 5.3.4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thiruvaimozhi-5-3-4-uravar-kavvai/) “*uṟavar kavvai eruvittu annai sol nīr maduttu Ira nel vitti muḷaitta nenjapperunjeyyuḷ pēramar kādal kadal puraiya viḷaivitta kāramar mēni nam kaṇṇan*” (emperumān is the farmer who planted the seed of deep love in the mind of parāngusa nāyaki (nammāḻvār) and nurtured it by watering it with the words of her mother and applied manure for it with the words of the townspeople)!

**paḻampunattu** – it is not a new field where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is carrying out farming activities. This world is the old land of emperumān’s who has been repeatedly ploughing, sowing and reaping (the produce of cētanas). This world has been in existence for a very long time, as a continuous flow of action \[like flood\].

**paḻampunattu vittumida vēndungolō** – just as in a fertile land, even if the seeds are not sown in a proper way but strewn in a haphazard way, they will germinate properly and grow, in this old land of the farmer emperumān, the cētanas do not need anything in them more than a mere desire (to be with emperumān). He takes care of all the activities of farming.

**paḻampunattu vittumida vēndungolō** – in the land sown by farmer, should anyone else sow the seed? Just as manu has said in his manusmruti 8-92 “*tēna cēdavivādastē mā gangām mā kurūn gama:*” (if there is no argument with emperumān, there is no need to go to holy rivers such as gangā or holy places such as kurukśētram), [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) forbids any action from us for our benevolence.

What are the activities of the farmer [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) for nurturing the land?

**vidaiyadartta pattiyuḻavan** – the incarnations carried out by emperumān are the activities for nurturing the field of *bhakti* (devotion). His action of killing seven bulls to win the hands of nappinnaip pirātti is superficially meant for marrying her but the real intention is to win over the hearts of followers and grow *bhakti* in them.

Just as kriṣṇa has mercifully mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 3-5](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/3-5/) “*na hi kaścit kśaṇamapi jātu tiśtatyakarmakrut*” (no one remains in this world even for a moment without carrying out any activity), how will the cētana pass his time? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) responds saying ….

**moytteḻunda kārmēgamanna karumāl tirumēni nīrvānam kāttum nigaḻndu** – the rain bearing clouds will show the black complexion of kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) which is like closely held monsoon clouds which rise together. āḻvār says that after developing affection towards [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with the activities of emperumān, one can pass time until one attains emperumān by sustaining himself looking at such similes of emperumān and developing further taste for him. Here, the term *nīrvānam* (water bearing cloud) is quoted as an example of various similes for emperumān.

**nigaḻndu kāttum** – emperumān will reveal to us his divine form, which he does not reveal even to brahmā et al.

We will move on to the 24<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 24. Nigalnday pal pon

*pāl pon pasuppu kār nāngu vaṇṇamum – having te four colours of vhite, red, green and black nigaḻndāy – glittered (in te four yugas) \[yuga is a period of time\] iruvaraiyum – te tvo demons madu and kaitabha vīya – to be destroyed igaḻndāy – you removed sēnāpatiyāy – being te protector of arjuna’s army manam – having te desire (to enable draupadi to plait her hair) pōr – te mahābhārata var mudikkum vagai – te vay by vhic it vill be ended sinappōr suvēdanai – arjuna, vho has vhite horse as his vehicle and vho fought furiously in te var pugaḻndāy – you praised*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**nigaḻndāy pāl pon pasuppu kār vaṇṇam nāngum – śāstras say that emperumān changes his dark complexion (which normally is similar to rain bearing clouds), in each yuga (a period of time) based on the quality of the people living in that yuga. Since it is through his divine form that he develops bhakti (devotion) among his followers, as brought by nammāḻvār’s saying “kādal kadal puraiya viḷaivitta kāramarmēni” (emperumān’s form is such that it creates affection in his devotees equalling the great ocean), emperumān involves in changing his complexion in line with the qualities of his followers.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār mercifully describes the efforts put in by the pattiyuḻavan (farmer emperumān) in this as well as the next pāsuram. In this pāsuram, he melts while describing how emperumān transforms himself for the sake of his followers. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: nigaḻndāy pāl pon pasuppuk kār vaṇṇam nāngum igaḻndāy iruvaraiyum vīyap pugaḻndāy sinappōrc cuvēdanaic cēnāpatiyāy manappōr mudikkum vagai*

**niganḻdāy pāl vaṇṇam** – As mentioned in the pramāṇam “*krutē śuklaścadurbhāhu*” people living in kruta yugam (the first of four yugams) are full of *satva guṇa* (purely good qualities), they will have pure hearts and would desire white complexion in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) too. Thus emperumān displays white complexion for them. The word white refers not only to the complexion of the divine form but also to the qualities.

**nigaḻndāy pon vaṇṇam** – As mentioned in the pramāṇam “*trētāyām rakta varṇōsau*”, since people in trētā yugam have *rajō guṇa* (quality of passion and aggressiveness) mixed with *satva guṇa*, emperumān displays a complexion of red mixed with white, a hue of golden colour.

**nigaḻndāy pasuppu vaṇṇam** – As mentioned in the pramāṇam “*dvāparē śyāma:*” people in dvāpara yugam have *rajastamō guṇa* (mixture of rajas and tamas qualities; we have already seen rajas  quality; tamas gives rise to laziness, ignorance, bewilderment etc in a person) along with *satva guṇa*. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) thus displays a combination of white, red and black, a kind of greenish tinge similar to that of algae.

**nigaḻndāy kār vaṇṇam** – As seen in the pramāṇam “*kalau kriṣṇam tviśā kriṣṇam*” since no one attains emperumān in kali yuga, he displays his natural colour of black clouds.

Thus, apart from displaying different complexions at different ages in time based on the qualities of the people in the different ages, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) next mentions about the efforts that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) puts in, to remove the enemies of his followers, thus paving the way for the irrigation of bhakti in his followers. ….

**igaḻndāy iruvariyum vīya** – you took a vow to destroy two demons madu and kaitabha, who were enemies of your follower brahmā, from whom they stole vēdas (sacred texts). Alternatively, this can be construed to refer to the egoistic kings who were on the two sides of pāṇḍavas and kauravas when the two sides engaged in the mahābhārata war and how [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), as kriṣṇa, destroyed all such egoistic kings.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) now cites another example of how [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) transforms himself in order to complete the task for his followers ….

**pugaḻndāY sinappōr suvētanai** – you praised arjuna, who has the chariot drawn by white coloured horses as his vehicle and who can fight furiously.

**pugaḻndāy** – not paying heed to arjuna’s addressing him (emperumān) as “*hē kriṣṇa, hē yādava*” (Oh kriṣṇa! Oh covherd!), you called him as “*parantapa*” (one who singes his enemies in the battlefield), “*anagha*” (one who is faultless) and in different such ways. īsn’t it amazing that one who is praised constantly by vēdas, riśis, [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) et al, praises another entity!

**sinappōr suvētanai** – (praising) arjuna, who is a warrior in the battlefield and not a coward who runs away from it. Among the many names for arjuna is *suvētan (śvēta).* This refers to his chariot being drawn by white coloured horses.

**sēnāpatiyāy** – as the charioteer for arjuna, it was kriṣṇa who was for all practical purposes, the head of pāṇḍavas’ army. He conducted many amazing activities during the war and brought it to a close. ḍidn’t sanjaya (charioteer of King drutarāśtra and who was giving constant updates on the happenings in the battlefield to the King) say in [śrī bhagavat gītā ślōka 18-78](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/18-78/) “*yatra yōgēśvara: kriṣṇa: yatra pārtō danurdara: tatra śrīr vijayōbhūtir…* ”, (where kriṣṇa and where arjuna with his bow are present, wealth and victory will go only there) giving prominence to kriṣṇa by referring to him first and only then, arjuna!  While referring to pāṇḍavas, it used to be said “*kriṣṇāśraya: kriṣṇa balā: kriṣṇa nātāśca pāṇḍavā:*” (for the pāṇḍavas, kriṣṇa is the final goal to be attained, kriṣṇa is their strength and kriṣṇa is their Lord). Is it required to be said that he is the head of their army!

What is the purpose behind praising arjuna and engaging him in the war? ….

**manappōr mudikkum vagai** – in order to bring to an end, the war that he had vowed to fight in his divine mind. The meaning implied is that he wanted to bring the war to an end to enable draupadi to plait her diśevelled hair, to enable her husband yudiśtra to ascend the throne, and to destroy duryōdana and others  who had insulted her. But why carry out so much for her? Did she not call out to him when she was being disrobed by duryōdana’s brother duśśāśana “*gōvindēti yatā grandat kriṣṇa mām dūravāsiṇam* I *ruṇam pravaruttamiva mē hrydayān nāpasarppaty* īī” (draupadi’s calling out to me when I was in a far-off land has not left my heart, just as interest keeps building up on a loan taken).  Thus, to ensure that the war is brought to a close, he praised arjuna. The war had to be brought to an end for the sake of darupadi who had surrendered to him earlier. All his activities are centred around his followers.

We will take up the 25<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 25. Vagaiyal madiyadu

*madiyādu – not considering tis as a huge task vagaiyāl – in te pat of begging for alms maṇ koṇḍāy – you took te vorlds from mahābali (a king vho had  seized all te vorlds from indra) maṟṟum – moreover vayiram – gem like diamond vagaiyāl – vit te help of medicines kuḻaittu – softening it uṇṇum – one vho eats it (adanāl) tān ennum – one vho has ego due to tat and tinks of himself as bhagavān māvali – mahābali’s vayiram vaḻakku – te inimical relationśip oḻittāy – you removed vagaiyāl – due to suc activities of yours varuvadu onṟu uṇḍē – is tere any resultant benefit for you? (you are doing all tese for te sake of your follovers)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vagaiyāl maṇ koṇḍāy – since mahābali had the quality of magnanimity, he should not be destroyed; at the same time, indra (head of celestial entities) has to get back his lost kingdoms. After analysing the situation, you transformed yourself to the form of a mendicant, measured the worlds through a good means and returned the redeemed worlds to indra, so that he could reside in his dwelling place.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that all his \[emperumān‘s\] activities will be only for the sake of his followers and none for himself. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: vagaiyāl madiyādu maṇkoṇḍāy maṟṟum vagaiyāḷ varuvadu onṟuṇḍē vagaiyāl vayiram kuḻaittuṇṇum māvali tānennum vayira vaḻakkoḻittāy maṟṟu*

**vagailyāl** – this refers to the form of the dwarf, vāmana, his sweet talk etc which softened the heart of the asura mahābali so that he would give the three steps of land asked for by the dwarf.

**madiyādu maṇ koṇḍāy** – instead of considering it as a huge task, you performed the activity of measuring of the worlds in an effortless manner. There are some who construe this as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) not thinking about his greatness and transforming himself into a dwarf. appiḷḷai, one of the vyākyātās (commentators) of this prabandam considered that emperumān did not think about the demonic nature of mahābali as something to be considered at all.

**maṟṟu** – was it only to do something good for indra that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) transformed himself into a dwarf? Was it not for doing a benefit for mahābali? Did he not do good for mahābali considering the demon as his follower?

**vagaiyāl vayiram kuḻaittuṇṇum māvali** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) refers to the decorations that mahābali carried out to boost his ego. If someone were to take diamond directly, it would lead to death. Hence, mahābali mixed diamond in small portion with several medicinal materials and consumed it to nurture his physical body.

**māvali tān ennum vayira vaḻakku oḻittāy** – you removed the ego of such mahābali, making him to bow down his head to you, kept your divine foot on his head and stabilised him. *vayiram* – diamond. v*ayira vaḻakku* – the way by which one becomes an enemy.

**tān ennum vayira vaḻakku** – it is implied here that while outwardly mahābali appeared to be a devotee \[of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\], since he had lot of ego, he behaved like an enemy of emperumān. Hasn’t emperumān himself mercifully mentioned “*ājñāccēdī mama drōhi madbhaktōpi na vaiṣṇava:*” (one who goes against my order is one who commits treachery against me even if he is my devotee, he is not a vaiṣṇava)!

**maṟṟum vagaiyāl varuvadu onṟu uṇḍē** – apart from granting benefits to your followers, is there any benefit for you \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] by carrying out all these deeds? It is implied by this that the activities of emperumān are only for the sake of his followers and there is nothing in them for him.

We will take up the 26<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.
