## 26. Marrut toluvar

*kadalvaṇṇā – ōh emperumān vho has te complexion of an ocean! yān toḻuvār maṟṟu oruvaraiyum inmai – tere is none in te matter of my attaining any deity (oter tan you) kaṟṟuccadaiyān – rudra vho has matted hair karik kaṇḍāy – see tat he is te vitness yān – tis servitor (me) vho vorśips only you unnai – you eṟṟaikkum – forever kaṇḍukoḷkiṟkum āṟu – to keep on vorśipping you kaṇḍu koḷ – you have to bless me.*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**maṟṟuttoḻuvār oruvaraiyum yāninmai kaṟṟaiccadaiyān karikkaṇḍāy – Siva is the witness for the fact that I do not worship any other deity.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān, though knowing the steadfastness of āḻvār \[in emperumān\], wanted to hear this from āḻvār himself. Hence he asks him “We hear that there are other deities too who carry out deeds for the sake of their followers. You could attain them”. āḻvār responds saying “rudra is the witness for the fact that I do not worship other deities and that they are not capable of granting mōkśam”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: maṟṟut toḻuvār oruvarum yān inmai kaṟṟaiccadaiyān karikkaṇḍāy eṟṟaikkum kaṇḍukoḷ kaṇḍāy kadalvaṇṇā yānunnaik kaṇḍukoḷkiṟkumāṟu*

**kaṟṟaic cadaiyān** – the witness is rudra who has matted hair, which is indicative of the fact that knowledgeable people will not attain him as a supreme being and that he will go in search of ignorant people and make them worship him.

**kaṟṟaiccadaiyān karikkaṇḍāy** – it is not unknown to you ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)), the omniscient. If at all you need a witness for it, you can ask your grandson rudra \[emperumān created brahmā, who becomes his son, and brahmā created rudra who becomes his son, thus rudra is emperumān’s grandson\]. kari – witness; one who saw in person.

It can be said that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) reminisces an incident that occurred in his life. One day he was sitting in [tirumaḻisai](http://thirumazhisai.koyil.org/), meditating on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), when rudra, along with his consort pārvati, was going atop his vehicle, bull, in the skies. Looking at tirumaḻisai āḻvār pārvati asked rudra as to who he was. rudra said that it is tirumaḻisai āḻvār who had left their philosophy and gone over to śrīman nārāyaṇa. pārvati said that in that case they should grant him their darśan (audience) and boon. They thus descended to earth and appeared in front of āḻvār. After looking at them, āḻvār did not bother and started sewing a torn cloth. Siva asked him “I came to shower our mercy on you. Why are you indifferent?”. āḻvār responded “There is nothing that you can do for me. Hence I am not bothered”. rudra continued saying “our coming here should not go waste. We have to grant you something. Hence, ask for whatever you want”. āḻvār said “In that case, grant me mōkśam”. rudra said “mōkśam can be granted only by śrīman nārāyaṇa. Hence ask for something else”. āḻvār said “In that case, grant me a long life so that I can stay on in this earth for a long time, carrying out penance towards śrīman nārāyaṇa and attaining mōkśam”. rudra said “Long life is dependent on your karma (past deeds). I cannot grant that. Ask for something else” to which [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) responded with a derisive smile “grant me a boon that the thread will come behind this needle when I sew this cloth”. On hearing this, Siva became very angry and vowed “I will make this egoistic person to lose his form and burn him”, opening his third eye in the forehead. Fire started spitting from his third eye. tirumaḻisai āḻvār opened his third eye which was below the big toe on his right foot and the fire emanating from his third eye started putting out the fire from rudra’s third eye. rudra unlocked dark clouds from his matted hair so that they will put out the fire created by āḻvār. Despite a deluge like appearance, nothing else happened, with āḻvār, not bothered about anything, carrying on with his meditation. Looking at this, rudra was amazed, gave āḻvār the title of *bhaktisārar* (essence of bhakti), praised āḻvār and returned to his place. Reminiscing all these, āḻvār said *kaṟṟaiccadaiyān karikkaṇḍāy*.

[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) asks āḻvār “What should I do for your having total devotion towards me?” . . .

**eṟṟaikkum kaṇḍukoḷ kaṇḍāy kadal vaṇṇa yānunnaik kaṇḍu koḷkiṟkumāṟu** – all that I want is that I should be like this, without any contact with other deities and being totally devoted to you.

**eṟṟaikkum** – it is not only in this birth, but in all the births hereafter, I should be  totally devoted to you, and not worship anyone else even by mistake.

**eṟṟaikkum yānunnaik kaṇḍukoḷ kiṟkumāṟu kaṇḍukoḷ** – Just as it is mercifully mentioned in jitantē ślōkam 12 “*durgatāvapi jātāyām tvad gatē mē manōrata:* I *yadi nāśam na vindēta tāvatāsmi krutī sadā* īī” (even if my focus towards you goes into a very low level, if it is not destroyed totally, I will be very much thankful to you) and in jitantē ślōkam 13 “*na kāma kaluśam cittam mama tē padayō: stitam* I *kāmayē vaiṣṇavam tu sarvajanmasu kēvalam*  īī ” (my mind which is engaged with your divine feet is not disturbed by desire in other matters. I desire only for the quality of being a vaiṣṇavan in all births), I desire only this benefit of not forgetting you ever and being your follower forever. There is no compulsion to grant me mōkśam.

**eṟṟaikkum kaṇḍukoḷkiṟkumāṟu** – if I get to see you here itself what nityasūris see in śrīvaikuṇṭam “*sadā paśyanti*” (constantly seeing), what is the compulsion to go there?

**kadalvaṇṇā kaṇḍukoḷ** – you \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] who are capable of removing all the hurdles, and who showed your divine form and created unwavering devotion towards you, should establish this quality of being totally dedicated towards you.

We will take up the 27<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 27. Mal tan pugunda

*pēdaigāḷ – ōh fooliś people! nīṟādi tān – rudra vho has his body covered vit aś kāṇa māttāda – not vorśipped tār – donned vit garland agalam – being expansive sēvadiyai – te divine foot kāṇa vallēṟku – for me vho is fortunate to vorśip tat māl – emperumān tān pugunda – tat vhic he entered on his volition mada nenjam idu – more tan tis obedient heart (of mine) maṟṟaduvum – anyting else pēṟu āga – as a benefit yān koḷvanō – vill ī accept? \[ ṇo. ī vill not\]*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**māl tān pugunda mada nenjam – just as it is mentioned in pramāṇams such as in taittirīya upaniśat bhruguvalli “yat …..abhisam visanti tat vijijyāsasva tat brahmēti” (know that as brahmam (supreme being) into which those who attain liberation enter), in śrī bhagavat gītā 11.54 “bhaktyā tavananyayā śakya: ahamēvam vidōrjuna I jñātum draśtum ca tatvēna pravēśtunca parantapa īī” (Oh arjuna who singes enemies in battlefield! Only with totally focussed bhakti could I be known, seen and entered upon), “ēśa brahmā praviśtōsmi grīśmē śītamiva hradam” (I enter brahmam just as one enters a cool, comfortable pond in hot summer time), emperumān is such an entity who is apt to be entered into by all. āḻvār says that such an entity has entered his heart on his \[emperumān’s\] own volition!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After āḻvār prayed in the previous pāsuram “eṟṟaikkum kaṇḍukoḷ kaṇḍāy kadal vaṇṇa yān unnaik kaṇḍukoḷkirkumāṟu”, emperumān blessed him significantly, saying let it be so. Some people who heard about āḻvār’s prayer asked him “Is it enough if you are always a vaiṣṇava? Is it not required to reach mōkśa?”. āḻvār says “Oh ignorant people! When emperumān who usually remains in mōkśa discards that and enters my heart, is there any possibility for my desiring anything other than my heart?”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: māl tān pugunda mada nenjam maṟṟaduvum pēṟāgak koḷvanō pēdaigāḷ nīṟādi tān kāṇa māttāda tāragalac cēvadiyai yān kāṇa vallēṟkidu*

**māl tān pugunda** – did not the great supreme being enter my heart! māl – great person.

**māl tān pugunda** – did not one who is the epitome of affection enter my heart! māl – one with affection.

**maṟṟaduvum pēṟāgak koḷvanō pēdaigāḷ** – leaving this \[heart, which he has entered\] could I consider anything else \[śrīvaikuṇṭam\] as a benefit? Don’t the words of great persons reflect this opinion – [tiruppāṇāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thiruppanazhwar/)’s amalanādipirān 10 “*en amudinikaik kaṇḍa kaṇgaḷ maṟṟonṟinai kāṇāvē*” (my eyes which have seen the divine form of periya perumāḷ will not see anything else), [toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/)’s [tirumālai 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/07/thirumalai-2-part-2/) “*accuvai perinum vēṇḍēn*” (I will not desire even if offered śrīvaikuṇṭam, after seeing periya perumāḷ), tiruvadi’s saying in śrī rāmāyaṇam “*bhāvō nānyatra gaccti*” (I will not relish any other benefit elsewhere)?

**madanenjam maṟṟaduvum pēṟāgak koḷvanō** – hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully stated similar meaning in his periya tiruvandādi 53 “*nin pugaḻil vaigum tam sindaiyilummaṟṟinidō nīyavarkku vaigundam enṟaḷum vān* ” (is your offering of śrīvaikuṇṭam \[to your followers\] greater than your being in their minds) and in [tiruvāsiriyam 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/thiruvasiriyam-2/) “*māyāp peruviṟalulagam mūnṟinodu nalveedu perinum koḷvadeṇṇumō teḷḷiyōr kuṟippē* ” (will the knowledgeable ones desire even mōkśam apart from the three worlds…..)?

**māltān pugunda mada nenjam maṟṟaduvum pēṟāgak koḷvanō pēdaigāḷ** – Just as mercifully mentioned  by [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-4-10 “*vadatadamum vaikuntamum madiḷ tuvarāpadiyum idavagaigaḷ igaḻnditttu enpāl idavagai koṇḍanaiyē*” (discarding your great dwelling places of banyan leaf, śrīvaikuṇṭam, dvārakā etc, you took residence inside my heart), when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has discarded śrīvaikuṇṭam in favour of my heart, should I discard my heart and go to śrīvaikuṇṭam?

**pēdahigāḷ** – you people who don’t know this, are indeed fools only.

When asked as how he got such firm faith, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says…..

**nīṟādi tān kāṇamāttāda tāragalaccēvadiyai yānkāṇa vallēṟkidu** – am I not the one who has been able to see the divine foot which is expansive and which is donned with garland, and which could not be seen by rudra who has smeared ash all over his physical body! What is surprising in my having firm faith in such supreme being!

**nīṟādi tān kāṇa māttāda** – it is the divine foot which rudra who is claimed to bestow knowledge on others as per the saying “*īśvarāt jñānamanviccēd*” (let the cētana obtain knowledge from rudra), could not see. It is something which the one who is claimed to be omniscient could not know.

**nīṟādi tān** kāṇamāttāda – just as mentioned by brahmā in mahābhāratam brahmarudra samvādam “*na sa śakyas tvayā draśtum mayānyair vāpi sattama* ” (that supreme being cannot be seen by me (brahmā) or by you (rudra)), and in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam “*yanna dēvā na munaya: na cāham na ca Sankara:* I *jānanti paramēśasya tad viṣṇō: paramam padam* īī” (that which the dēvas, sages, I (brahmā), Sankara do not know, that is the supreme dwelling place of viṣṇu), this is not known to many celestial entities.

**nīṟādi tān kāṇāmāttāda tār** – it is not that rudra does not know the divine foot. He does not know even the garland which is decorating that divine foot.

**tāralagalac cēvadiyai** – [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) has mercifully mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-9-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/10/thiruvaimozhi-1-9-7-tholinai-melum/) “*tāḷiṇaimēlum punainda taṇṇanduḻāyudai ammān*” ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has the cool and comfortable tuḷasi garland on his divine foot).

**nīṟādi tān kāṇamāttāda tāragalac cēvadiyai** – when emperumān incarnated as trivikrama, brahmā washed emeprumān’s divine foot which reached satyalōkam (brahmā’s dwelling place) with water and rudra took that purified water (which had the connection with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine foot) on his head. Thus, rudra saw only the water but not emperumān’s divine foot. It was the divine foot that even brahmā hadn’t seen as mentioned by [poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) in [mudal tiruvandādi 56](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-56/) “*kadikkamalattuḷḷirundum kāṇgilān kaṇṇan adikkamalandannai ayan*” (through residing in the fragrant lotus emanating from emperumān’s navel, brahmā has not seen the divine foot of emperumān).  Thus, when brahmā, who resides in such close proximity to emperumān has not seen his divine foot, is there any surprise that rudra has not seen the divine foot?

**kāṇavallēṟku** – with causeless mercy, when emperumān displays his divine foot, I am able to see it, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/). Thus, āḻvār says that he cannot discard the heart in which [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has taken residence and go elsewhere.

We will take up the 28<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 28. Iduvilangai idaliya

*ilangai – te country of lankā īdu aḻiya – to be ruined kattiya – built (vit te help of te army of monkeys) sētu – te divine bridge idu – tis too vilangu – one vho vas born in te animal species vāliyai – vāli (king of monkeys) vīḻttaduvum – te activity by vhic emperumān killed him ilangaidān – te country of lankā odunga – to be destroyed vil nudanga – to bend te bov sārngam taṇ tār rāvaṇanin ūnodunga – making te back of rāvaṇa vho vas donning cool, comfortable garland, to be arced eydān – rāma vho śot te arrovs idu – tis activity too ugappu – is a playting for him*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**idu ilangai īdaḻiyak kattiya sētu – the bridge which emperumān \[as śrī rāma\] built across the ocean in order to ruin lankā so that it lost its pristine structure, is appearing right in front of my eyes now.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When some people asked āḻvār “You say that you had seen the divine feet of emperumān which had not been seen by even Siva. Where did you see them?”  āḻvār responds saying “Did I see only his divine feet? I am seeing all the amazing activities that he undertook for the sake of his followers, now itself, inside my heart where he has entered”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: iduvilangai īdaḻiyakkattiya sētu iduvilangu vāliyai vīḻttadu(vum) iduvilangai tānodunga vilnudangat taṇḍārirāvaṇanai ūnodunga eydān ugappu*

**idu ilangai īdaḻiyak kattiya sētu** – in the previous pāsuram, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) said that he saw the divine feet of emperumān. In this pāsuram he talks of the bridge where emperumān’s divine feet left their prints.

**īdaḻiyak kattiya** – the ocean was a natural protective guard for lankā. Even as the bridge was being built, it appeared that lankā was lost. Even as dēvas (celestial entities) such as indra et al heard about the bridge, their hearts melted.

**idu vilangu vāḷiyai vīḻttaduvum** – the incident in which the animal vāli was killed with an arrow is also appearing in front of my eyes.

**vilangu vāliyai** – he was an animal not only by birth but through his activities too. The opinion here is that the action of śrī rāma in killing vāli while hiding himself cannot be faulted. Has it not been mentioned mercifully by śrī rāma himself in śrīrāmāyaṇam “*vāgurābiśca pāśaiśca gūdaiśca vividair narā:* I *praticcannāśca*  *druśyāśca gruhṇanti subahūn mrugān* īī” (human beings catch animals through nets, ropes, various devious methods, by hiding themselves and by being in front of them)?

**ilangai tān odunga vil nudangat taṇṭhār rāvaṇanai eydān idu** – the activity of śrī rāma by which he created stress for lankā, drew his bow and made rāvaṇa to bend his back.

**ilangai tān odunga** – I can see lankā being destroyed right in front of my eyes.

**vil nudanga** – I can see \[śrī rāma\] drawing his bow \[to shoot the arrows\]

**taṇ tār … eydān idu** – I can also see śrī rāma shooting arrows at rāvaṇa such that the demon, who is donning a cool, comfortable garland, who is vain, and who will not bow down to anyone, is shrinking.

**idu idu idu ugappu** –  these activities, which are appearing in front of my eyes, are those which were engaged in by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), for the sake of his followers.

Since these activities are running now in his heart, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) is referring to them as *idu, idu, idu*.

We will now move on to the 29<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 29. Ugappuruvam tane

*madikkil – if one vere to tink ugappu uruvam tānē – he himself has te divine form vhic is desired by everyone oḷi uruvam tānē – he himself has te divine form vhic is radiant magappu uruvam tānē – he himself has te form vhic is amazing avan – tat śrī rāma miga – huge puruvam onṟukkonṟu ōsanaiyān – kumbhakarṇa vho is so huge tat his eye brovs are separated by a huge distance vīḻa – to be destroyed anṟikkoṇḍu – coming furiously (at kumbhakarṇa) oru kaṇaiyāl – vit one arrov eydān – śot and killed him*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**ugappuruvam tānē – it  is emperumān alone who has a divine form which is much desired by everyone. parāsara maharishi in his śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-5-84 says “samasta kalyāṇa guṇātmakō’sau svaśaktilēśōdrutabhūtasarga: I iccāgruhītāmatōrudēhas samsāditā’śēśajagaddititō’śau  īī” (this emperumān has all auspicious qualities as his nature; with just a fraction of his power, he sustains all the creations; he has several forms which he takes of his own volition and desires; he is benevolent to all the worlds).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When āḻvār was asked “Do you then know the divine form of emperumān completely?” he responds saying “īsn’t he one whose divine form cannot be known by anyone!” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: ugappuruvam tānē oḷiyuruvam tānē magappuruvam tānē madikkil migappuruvam onṟukkonṟu ōsanaiyānvīḻa oru kaṇaiyāl anṟikkoṇḍu eydān avan*

**ugappuruvam tānē** – apart from others desiring his divine form, his divine form is superior to his auspicious qualities. Hasn’t āḷavandār (one of our primary preceptors) mentioned in his [catu:ślōki 4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/12/chathu-sloki-slokams/) “*śāntānanda mahāvibhūti paramam yadbrahma rūpam harē:* I *mūrtam brahma tatō’pi priyataram rūpam yadatyadbutam*  īī” (the rūpam of hari (divine form of emperumān) is without variations, joyous, has many wealth, is huge, makes others huge, due to all of which it is denoted by the word *brahmam*. Is there anything superior to it which is desired by emperumān, which is seen by the eyes, which is denoted by the word brahmam and which is amazing!)

**ugappuruvam tānē** – since it is the goal of his followers, his divine form is a matter of exhilaration for them.  Since his followers desire it, his divine form will be pleasing for emperumān too.

**ugappuruvam tānē** – his *rūpam* and *svarūpam* (divine form and basic qualities) will be intertwined to such an extent that they will appear same. The text could also be *ugappuruvan tānē*.

The reason for being desired by everyone is mentioned next.

**oḷiyuruvam tānē** – it is he who is spoken of as having a radiant form at many places in vēdas (puruśa sūktam, nārāyaṇa sūktam etc) such as “*param jyōti:*” (superior radiance), “*nārāyaṇa parō jyōti:*” (śrīman nārāyaṇa has superior radiance), “*ādityavarṇam*” (he has the complexion of sūrya), “*vidyuta: puruśāt*” (his divine form is like lightning).

**oḷiyuruvam tānē** – īsn’t emperumān referred to as “*tējasām rāśimūrjitam*” (he is a huge pile of splendour)!

**oḷiyuruvam tānē** – just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) has mercifully declared in his [tiruvāimoḻi 3.1.3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/07/thiruvaimozhi-3-1-3-paranjodhi/) “*paranjōti nī paramāy ninnigaḻndu pin maṟṟōr paranjōtiyinmaiyin padiyōvi nigaḻginṟa paranjōti* ” his splendour is such that apart from him no one else has any quality of radiance. His splendour is such that it will make the rays of sūrya and candra (sun and moon respectively) appear dark as mentioned in the vēdic saying “*na tatra sūryōbhāti*”. In paramapadam, his radiance will be such splendorous that the radiance of nityasūris will appear similar to that of fireflies in front of sun.

**magappuruvam tānē** – he has a huge form himself. The greatness of his form has been spoken of in śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 6-7-70 “*samasta: śaktyaścaitā nrupa yatra pratiśtitā:* I *tat viśvarūpavairūpyam rūpamanyat harēr mahat* īī” (Oh King! That hari’s (emperumān’s) form, in which all these powers get established, is superior to and different from all the other forms). The greatness here is derived due to the form and qualities such as vondrousness etc. The Sanskrit word *mahat* (large, great, lustre etc) is written here is *magappu*.

Is the one who has such great qualities the Lord of śrīvaikuṇṭam? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says in the second half of the pāsuram that it was one who considered himself as a human being as mentioned in the śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam ślōkam “*ātmānam mānuśam manyē*”….

**puruvam onṟukkonṟu ōsanaiyān vīḻa** – kumbhakarṇa, who had a *yōjana* (distance equivalent to ten miles) between his eyebrows and who was huge,  fell like a heap.

**miga anṟikkoṇḍu eydān** – śrī rāma shot the arrow in a state of anger as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam ślōkam “*kōpamāhārayat tīvram*” while he is normally said to be very calm as mentioned in the ślōkam “*kśamayā: pritvīmai:*” (being like earth in his calmness). Instead of being calm considering that he was carrying the activity for his followers, he became angry as if he were carrying out the task for himself.

**kaṇaiyāl eydān** – instead of killing through his vow (this would appear similar to a suicide) and thinking that it would not befit the insult heaped on his followers, emperumān came in person and shot the arrow from the bow held in his hand. His posture was described by [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) in siṟiya tirumadal “*vennaragam sērāvagaiyē silaigunittān*” (there is no hellish experience greater than seeing him holding his bow).

Since the enemy was so huge (ten miles between his eyebrows), did he have to put in lot of efforts to destroy kumbhakarṇa?

**oru kaṇaiyāḷ eydān** – he killed kumbhakarṇa effortlessly with just one arrow.

**madikkil** – if one were to analyse such śrī rāma he will appear to be with *ugappuruvam, oḷiyuruvam* and *magappuruvam* (as we have just seen). Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) has used the term *madikkil* (if one were to think) it is implied that it is not possible for anyone to estimate his divine form.

**eydānavan ugappuruvam tānē** – śrī rāma had a divine form which was desired by sages and trees, as brought out by the ślōkam where the sentient sages say “*tē tam sōmamivōdyantam*” (śrī rama who has a form similar to the rising moon) and the ślōkam where the insentient trees felt “*api vrukśā: parimlānā:* ” (trees withered, not seeing him).

**eydānavan oḷiyuruvam tānē** – Just as it is mercifully mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam ślōkam “*śōbhayan dandakārānyam dīptēna svēna tējasā* ” (śrī rāma reached the forest area of daṇḍakāraṇyam, emitting radiance which made the entire forest area to glow), he is the one who is capable of making even a dark forest to glow.

**eydānavan magappuruvam tānē** – just as it is mentioned in the ślōkam “*pumsām druśiticittapahāriṇam*” śrī rāma’s form was so great that it could steal the hearts and eyes of even men.

We will take up the 30<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 30. Avan ennai ali

*(tus, hencefort) avan – tat emperumān veḷḷattu – in te milky ocean aravu aṇaiyin mēl – on te mattress of ādiśēśan kidakkumē – vill he reside aptly (no, he vill not)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**arangattu avan ennai āḷi – the one who has taken residence in śrīrangam and who is referred to by all as “he is śrī rāma” is the one who rules over me. It is implied that emperumān is lying in śrīrangam only to protect āḻvār.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “If he is ugappuruvan, oḷiyuruvan and magappuruvan (as seen in the previous pāsuram), how did you see him?” He responds “When he has discarded śrīvaikuṇṭam and taken residence in my heart, what is the difficulty in seeing him!” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: avan ennai āḷi arangattu arangil avan ennai eydāmal kāppān avan ennadu uḷḷattu ninṟān irundān kidakkumē veḷḷat taravaṇaiyin mēl ennai āḷi – he rules over me and showers his grace on me arangattu avan – śrī ranganātan ennai arangil – in the stage of samsāram (materialistic realm) eydāmal – without entering kāppān – will protect avan – that emperumān ennadu – my uḷḷattu – in \[my\] heart ninṟān irundān – carried out the activities of standing and sitting*

What did he do lying in śrīrangam? ….

**arangil avan ennai eydāmal kāppān** – it is he who protects me from entering the stage called as *samsāram*. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too mentioned of samsāram as a teatre \[play or dance\] in his tiruvāimoḻi 8-4-11 “*piṟavi māmāyak kūttu*” Just as a person dons several roles and comes on stage, an [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) takes many physical forms and comes on the stage of samsāram \[in multitude of births\].

Thus, until now [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) spoke of the means that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had observed to obtain one āḻvār. Now he speaks of the benefits that emperumān accrued from āḻvār.

**avan ennadu uḷḷattu ninṟān irundān** – āḻvār implies that emperumān reclined in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) and tiruvarangam (śrīrangam) in order to enter [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/)’s heart. Just as it is mentioned “*rakśyāpēkśām pratīkśatē*” (looking forward to the desire of the protected), emperumān keeps waiting for a person to be protected and once he obtained a foothold to enter āḻvār’s heart, he was overjoyed and carried out the activities of standing, sitting and lying down in āḻvār’s heart as if he had got something which he could never have got.

**kidakkumē veḷḷattaravaṇaimēl** – hereafter, will he ever lie down on top of ādiśēśan in śrīvaikuṇṭam? Once a person gets the readily available means, will he/she observe the rituals to get to the means? Here, the reference made to ādiśēśan in tiruppāṟkadal is also applicable to his postures of standing, sitting or lying down in other [divyadēśams](https://koyil.org/) (divine abodes) such as paramapadam, tirumalai, tiruvarangam etc.

We will go on to the 31<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 31. Mel nanmugan

*(experienced by you people) māyam – te amazing sorrovs pēsil – if one vere to talk kadai – it vill be as expansive as mahābhārata narrative.*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**mēl nānmugan aranai itta vidu sābam – the curse which the father brahmā gave his son rudra when rudra plucked out one of brahmā’s heads**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had mercifully mentioned that it is emperumān who removes all his sorrows. In this pāsuram he says “Is it only for me that he removes sorrows? Even for brahmā, rudra et al who you people consider as deities, it is only he who removes the sorrows; attain him”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: mēl nānmugan aranaiyitta vidu sābam tān nāraṇan oḻittāṇ tāragaiyuḷ vānōr perumānai ēttāda pēygāḷ piṟakkum karumāyam pēsil kadai mēl – once upon a time nānmugan – brahmā aranai – rudra itta – given vidusābam – curse tāragaiyuḷ – on this earth (for everyone to know) nāraṇan tān – nārayaṇa himself oḻittān – mercifully got rid of (such) vānōr perumānai – śrīman nārayana, the lord of nityasūris ēttāda – not praising him pēygāḷ – Oh foolish people! piṟakkum karu – inside the womb which is the basis for birth*

**tān nārāṇanoḻittān tāragaiyuḷ** – it was nārāyaṇa who got rid of that curse in such a way that all the people on earth knew about it. It is pertinent here to reminisce the ślōkam in mātsya purāṇam:

*dēvīm prati rudra: tata: krōda parītēna samraktanayanēna ca I*  
*vāmānguśtānakāgrēṇa cinnan tasya śirō mayā īī*

*brahmā – yasmādanaparādasya śirascitan tvayā mama I*  
*tasmāccāpasamāviśta: kapālī tvam bhaviśyasi īī*

*rudra: brahmahākulitō bhūtvā caram tīrtdāni bhūtalē I*  
*tatō’ham katavaṇ dēvi! Himavantam śilōccayamīī*

*tatra nārāyaṇaś śrīmān mayā bhikśām prayācita: I*  
*tatastēna svakam pārśvam nākāgrēṇa vidāritam īī*  
*mahatī sravatī dārā tasya raktasya nissrutā īī*

*viṣṇu prasādāt suśrōṇi! kapālam tatsahasradā I*  
*spuditam bahūdā yātam svapnalabhdam danam yadā īī*

(rudran informing dēvi – ‘later the head of that brahmā was plucked by me, who has a lot of anger and reddish eyes, with a corner of my left thumb nail. brahmā cursed me saying “Since my faultless head was severed by you, you will roam around due to my curse as a kapāli (one who is with a skull). Oh devi! As one who caused hurt to a brāhmaṇa, and with saddened heart, I went to many holy waters  and then I went to himālayas. There, I begged śrīman nārāyaṇa to rid me of my curse. Then, a part of his body was scratched by him with his fingernail and from that blood poured out like flood waters. … Oh supreme beauty! With the mercy of viṣṇu, that skull broke into a thousand pieces and disappeared like wealth that one obtained in his dream)”

**tāragai** – derived from the root “*dāratīti dārakā*” (earth is called as dārakā since it bears everything), this refers to earth.

**tāragaiyuḷ** – he \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] got rid \[of the curse\]  in such a way that everyone on earth knew about it. He removed the curse so that rudra got the famous names of *kapāli* and *bikśāṇḍa* and emperumān got the famous name *harapāpahara* (one who removed the sin of rudra).

**vānōr perumānai** – son stood like a sinner since he severed the father’s head; father stood sorrowfully having lost his head; [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) rid the sorrows of both. nityasūris, who saw this happening, ceded themselves to emperumān. Alternatively, *vānōr* would refer to the celestial entities who on seeing their leaders brahmā and rudra being rid of their sorrows, affirmed that emperumān is the supreme being.

**ēttāda pēygāḷ** – Oh foolish people who do not praise such a distinguished entity!

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says next as to what will befall such foolish persons since they do not praise [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**piṟakkum karumāyam pēsil kadai** – if one were to talk about the sufferings that you people, who do not praise emperumān, have to endure in the wombs ( in all these births) it will be as expansive as mahābhārata narrative.

We will take up the 32<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 32. Kadaip poruldan

*kadaip poruḷdān – smruti, itihāsa, purānas (narratives vhic are quoted as autentic texts) kaṇṇan tiruvayiṟṟinuḷḷa – ove teir existence to te supreme being’s sankalpa (vov) udaippu aḷavu – every moment pōdu pōkkinṟi – vitout spending (in any oter manner) vāynda guṇattup padādadu – vitout involving in te amazing auspicious qualities (of emperumān) vadaipporuḷdān – is only hurting oters āynda guṇattān – emperumān vho has faultless qualities, his adi – divine feet adaiminō – attain*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**kadaipporuḷdān – itihāsas (epics)and purānas (old, yet remaining fresh, treatises) which have narration as the primary focus and smrutis (explanatory texts for vēdas) which have philosophy as the primary focus. In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had mentioned about karumāyam pēsil kadai (if one were to talk about the sufferings of people in their mothers’ wombs, it would be as expansive as mahābhārata), hence he is referring to it here.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had spoken ill of those who are ignorant of matters relating to emperumān by calling them as “perumānai ēttāta pēygāḷ” (the foolish people who do not praise emperumān). Some people asked him “You are full of satva guṇa (purely good qualities). This being the case, could you use lowly words such as pēy (foolish people) or nāy (dog) and talk ill of others? Don’t elders say that we should avoid abusing others?” to which āḻvār responds in this pāsuram saying “It is not out of taste in abuse that I spoke that way. Those who do not involve themselves with matters relating to emperumān not only destroy themselves but are fools who destroy the basic nature of others too. If those who I had abused as fools become followers of emperumān, they would become acceptable people to me too!” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: kadaip poruḷdān kaṇṇan tiruvayiṟṟinuḷḷa udaippaḷavu pōdu pōkkinṟi vadaipporuḷdān vāynda guṇattup padādadu adaiminō āynda guṇattān adi*

**kadaipporuḷdān kaṇṇan tiruvayiṟṟinuḷḷa** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that the itihāsas and purāṇas which talk about our *karumāyam* (what we endure in our mothers’ wombs) are dependent on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s vow. The implied meaning is that only emperumān is capable of removing them.

**tiruvayiṟṟinuḷḷa** – the term *vayīru* actually refers to the inner seat of mind and thus to its activity of *sankalpa* or vow. From authentic sources such as (1) āḷavandār’s catu:ślōkī  “*aravinda lōcanamana: kāntā*” (he is dear to the entity with lotus-like eyes); (2) śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam ślōkam “*asyā dēvyā manastanmin tasyas cāsyām pratiśtitam*” (this dēvi’s mind is established in his ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s) divine mind, and his divine mind is fixed in hers); (3) tiruvāimoḻi 10-3-9 “*undan tiruvuḷḷam idarkedundōṟum*” (whenever your divine mind is pleased), it is clear that emperumān has an *aprākruta manas* (mind, not made of primordial matter). *pramāṇams* (authentic sources of information) such as “*aprāṇō hyamanā:*” (lifeless; mindless), “*paśyatyacakśu: ya śruṇōtyakarṇa:*” (even though he is without eyes, he sees; even though he is without ears, he hears) affirm that emperumān does not have primordial mind, eyes, ears etc and that he does not have to see with eyes, does not have to hear with ears and does not have to carry out a vow with his mind. Thus the term *vayiṟu* would refer to his mind and to its activity of carrying out *sankalpa.*

**kadaipporuḷ …… pōdu pōkkinṟi** – vyākyātā [appiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/appillai/) construed this to mean that all the [jīvātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) who are referred to in itihāsas and purāṇas sustain their existence every moment, through [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s *sankalpa*.

**udaippaḷavu pōdupōkkinṟi vāynda guṇattup padādadu vadaipporuḷdān** – instead of continuously involving with the amazing qualities of emperumān, being engaged with other matters is considered as causing hurt to the world. How can such people be called other than as *pēygāḷ* (ignorant fools)? If one were to ask whether calling such people as fools is not abusing others, all that one can say is that such people, apart from losing their existence (*asannēva bhavati*), will make those who follow them also as non-existing. Thus they will be the leaders of hurting others.

**udaippaḷavu …. vadaipporuḷdān** – it will be termed as hurting others if they do not involve with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s matters even for a moment. Haven’t riśis too mercifully said “*yanmuhūrtam kśaṇam vāpi vāsudēvō na cintyatē* I *sā hānis tanmahaccidram sā brāntis sā ca vikriyā* īī” (if vāsudēva (emperumān, referred to here as son of vasudēva) is not thought of for a muhūrttam (a time frame equivalent to 48 minutes) or even for a moment, that itself is cause for lowliness; that is a great trouble; that is bewilderment; that is distortion or ugliness)! *udaippu* – moment.

**vadaipporuḷdān vāynda guṇattup padādadu** – jīvātmās who engage with worldly matters and get destroyed \[of their svarūpam\] will not involve with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s auspicious qualities.

**adaiminō āynda guṇattān adi** – attain the divine feet of emperumān who has faultless qualities. When he has qualities which are the exact opposite of lowliness, is it proper not to attain his divine feet?

**adaiminō** – implied meaning here is that those who were called as *pēygāḷ* would be referred to by a dignified name if they become favourable to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

We will take up the 33<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 33. Adiccagadam sadi

*adi sagadam sādi – kicking te demon cakatāsuran vit his divine foot aravu ātti – śaking up te  snake kāḷiyan ānai pidittu osittu – catcing hold of te elephant kuvalayāpīdam and breaking its tusks pēy mulai nanju uṇḍu – drinking te poisoned milk of te demon pūtanā vadi pavaḷa vāy pinnai tōḷikkā – for te sake of nappinnai pirātti vho had beautiful coral-like lips and beautiful śoulders val ēṟu eruttu iṟuttu – breaking te humps of poverful bulls pinnum – moreover kuṟippu – trough his divine mind kō ānān – he became te lord*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**adiccagadam sādi – did he not give himself to his followers by killing the demon cakatāsuran with his divine foot, just seven months after being born!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had implored samsāris (dwellers of materialistic realm) to attain emperumān by saying adaiminō. Would the hurdles which have been chasing samsāris from time immemorial allow them to attain emperumān? āḻvār says in this pāsuram that emperumān has the quality of removing hurdles for nappinnaip pirātti, who is very dear to him, and for others. Hence there is no need to fear for enemies. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: adiccagadam sādi aravātti ānai pidittosittup pēymulai nanjuṇḍu vadippavaḷa vāippinnait tōḷikkā vallēṟṟeruttiṟuttu kōppinnumānān kuṟippu*

**aravātti** – the word *adi* can be added here too. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) shook up the snake *kāḷiya* through his divine foot, chased it (out of the river yamunā) and protected the cows and covherds. Since the word “chased” has been used in the vyākhyānam, it is quite possible that the text is *aravōtti* (chasing the snake) and not *aravātti* (shaking up the snake).

**ānai pidittu osittu** – taking the elephant kuvalayāpīdam (which was engaged by kamsan to kill kriṣṇa) by his hand and breaking its tusks.

**pēy mulai nanju uṇḍu** – even when kriṣṇa was an infant, the demon pūtanā came in the garb of his mother to kill him by applying poison to her bosom. kriṣṇa drank that poison as if it were nectar. ḍidn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully say in his [tiruvāimoḻi 1-5-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/08/thiruvaimozhi-1-5-9-mayom-thiya/) “*perumāvanjappēy vīya tūya kuḻaviyāy vidappāl amudā amudu seyditta māyan* ” (the amazing entity who, even as an infant drank the poisoned milk  as if it were nectar, from the deceitful pūtanā, such that she was destroyed)!

**vadippavaḷa vāyppinnait tōḷikkā vallēṟṟeruttiṟuttu** – destroying the necks of seven bulls which were as strong as he himself was, by hugging them with his divine form, for the sake of nappinnai who has beautiful lips looking like coral and beautiful shoulders.

**adiccagam sādi aravātti** – destroying enemies through his divine feet.

**ānai pidittosittu** – destroying enemy through his divine hand

**pēymulai nanju uṇḍu** – destroying enemy through his divine mouth

**vallēṟṟeruttiṟuttu** – destroying enemies through his complete divine form

He used his entire divine form for nappinnai who is a distinguished entity to attain. For the other enemies he used only one or other divine part of his form. There is no difference in the activity, as far as destroying the enemy was concerned. As far as means for destroying the enemy is concerned, anything is accepted, whether hand or leg or mouth.

**vadippavaḷa vāyppinnait tōḷikkā** – śouldn’t the means applied for a distinguished entity be appropriate for that entity? Hence [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) used his entire divine form for destroying the seven bulls by hugging them.

**pinnum kuṟippu kōvānān** – after the duration of incarnation was over he carried on with the task of destroying enemies of his followers through his internal implement of sankalpa (vow) instead of using external implements of divine hand, foot, mouth etc. He remained the lord of the worlds once again.

**pinnum** – when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is there to take care of enemies irrespective of whether he incarnates or not, and doing it as a profession, why do you people bother about enemies, asks [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/).

We will take up the 34<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 34. Kurippenakku

*(When suc is te case) vēngadattu mēyānai veṟuppanō – vill ī dislike emperumān vho has taken residence at tiruvēngadam? mey vinai nōy eydāmal tān kadattum tanmaiyān tāḷ veṟuppanō – vill ī forget and ignore te divine feet of emperumān vho (protects and) prevents diseases and deeds vhic come about on account of physical form?*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**kuṟippu enakku kōttiyūr mēyānai ētta – my opinion is that emperumān who has taken residence at tirukkōttiyūr should be constantly praised.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “You had said that one should involve with the faultless qualities of emperumān without any break. Are you involved like that?” to which he responds in this pāsuram saying “when he has taken residence in divine abodes for my sake, will I turn away from him?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: kuṟippenakku kōttiyūr mēyānai ētta kuṟipppenakku nanmai payakka veṟuppanō vēngadattu mēyānai mey vinai nōy eydāmal tāngadattum tanmaiyān tāḷ kōttiyūr mēyānai ētta kuṟippu – my opinion is to keep praising emperumān who is aptly residing at tirukkōttiyūr. enakku nanmai payakka kuṟippu – my opinion is that I should derive some benefit.*

**kōttiyūr mēyānai** – he is residing at tirukkōttiyūr since he could not stay at paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) on account of not having got me.

**kuṟippu enakku nanmai payakka** – is there any [cētana (sentient entity)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) who does  not want any benefit for himself? Since I desire to have benefit, I am continuously praising [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

*kōttiyūr mēyānai* – If I look at his basic nature (of being the supreme being) I have to praise him. *enakku* – If I look at my basic nature (of being a servitor) I have to praise him.

**nanmai payakka** – the benefit for this cētana is the greatness accrued out of being together with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). I am praising emperumān for getting this benefit.

**kuṟippu enakku nanmai payakka** – Has it not been stated mercifully “*svōjjīvanēccāyadi tē svasattāyām spruhā yadi* I *ātmadāsyam harē: svāmyam svabhāvanca sadā smara* īī” (if you are interested in uplifting yourself and if you have a desire to exist, keep meditating constantly on the fact that [ātmā (soul)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is a servitor and hari ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) is the master and keep thinking of their natures)?

**veṟuppanō vēngadattu mēyānai** – will I ignore the one who has descended from śrīvaikuṇṭam and is standing at tiruvēngadam? Is there anyone who will kick aside the wealth that one is getting?

**meyvinainōy eydāmal tān kadattum tanmaiyān tāḷ veṟuppanō** – will I hate the divine feet of that supreme being who protects us by removing the diseases which affect our (physical) forms and the deeds which are the causative factors for these diseases? Will I forget such divine feet even for a moment? The words should be arranged in this manner – mey nōy vinai eydāmal (ensuring that the diseases of the body and the deeds responsible for these diseases do not approach…). Alternatively if it is taken as *meyvinai*  it would refer to the deeds (vices and virtues) which are the result for attaining a physical form. If the text is *vevvinai* it would mean cruel deeds. The word *nōy* would refer to the sufferings that one undergoes due to these deeds.

**meyvinai nōy eydāmal tān kadattum tanmaiyān** – his very name *vēngadattān* would imply that he removes the deeds which cause taking repeated births.

We will move on to the 35<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, next.

## 35. Talal ulagam

*nīḷ ōdam vandu alaikkum māmayilai māvallikkēniyān – one vho has taken residence at te great tiruvallikkēṇi vhic is near te great tirumayilai vhere vaves come and lap aindalai vāy nāgattu aṇai vāḷā kidandaruḷum – lying still atop te bed of serpent vhic has five heads and mouts vāy tiṟavān – he is not even speaking tāḷāl ulagam aḷanda asaivē kol – (is  tis) because of te tiredness after measuring te vorlds?*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**tāḷāl ulagam aḷanda asaivē kol – Oh emperumān! Are you lying down without moving or speaking because your divine form is tired, having to measure forests, tall peaks, pits, rocks etc with your divine feet, the feet which would turn red the moment śrī mahālakśmi touches them \[in order to  carry out kainkaryam to you\] as mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 9.2.10 “vadiviṇaiyillā malarmagaḷ maṟṟai nilamagaḷ pidikkum melladiyāi”? (the soft divine feet which is gently stroked by śrī mahālakśmi who has unparalleled beauty and śrī bhūmippirātti who is also like that)?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the 31st pāsuram, āḻvār despised people who do not praise emperumān as perumānai ēttāda pēygāḷ. The subsequent pāsurams until the previous one were corollaries of that. In this pāsuram he says “When I do not have enough time to feel for the great activities that emperumān undertook, what is the point in despising others for their nature?” and takes efforts to sympatise with emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: tāḷāl ulagam aḷanda asaivē kol vāḷā kidandaruḷum vāy tiṟavān nīḷōdam vandalaikkum māmayilai māvallikkēṇiyān aindalai vāy nāgattaṇai*

**tāḷāl** – it appears that you saw only the connection (between you and the [jīvātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), the sentient entities in materialistic realm) when you stroked them with your divine foot in order to uplift them! Did you have to physically carry out this deed (of stroking them) in order to uplift them? Were you not all alone at one point of time \[carrying all the worlds in your divine stomach\] as mentioned in the pramāṇam “*ēkō ha vai nārāyaṇa āsīt* I *na  brahmā nēśāna: nēmē dyāvāpritivī* īī” (only nārāyaṇa was present; not brahmā; not Siva; not the lower or higher worlds) when all these entities were without a form or name, lying inside your stomach, when you decided to create the world with your *sankalpa* (divine vow) “*bahusyām ….*” (let me multiply….)? Could you not have made another sankalpa in order to uplift these jīvātmās? Did you have to exert yourself by physically measuring the worlds and in the process, uplifting these jīvātmās?

**ulagam** – did you have to make such efforts for the sake of this cruel world?

**aḷanda asaivē kol** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/), out of his deep affection for emperumān, thinks that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is the lord of the indefatigable nityasūris, has become tired on measuring the worlds. And, emperumān has the quality of *avikāra* (does not undergo transformation) as per pramāṇas. This is termed as *astānē bhayaśanka* (misplaced fear).

**vāḷā kidandu aruḷum vāy tirṟavān** – he is remaining motionless, not even turning from left to right…. For us, who have seen the beauty of his reclining posture, in order to enable us to get rid of our deep desire for seeing the beauty of his measuring the worlds, he is displaying the beauty of being motionless.

**vāy tiṟavān** – just as sumantra, minister of emperor daśarata mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam “*āśayā yadi vā rāma: puna: śabdāyayēditi*” (I was waiting there for a long time in the hope that śrī rāma will come there and call me again…), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is not pronouncing one word which will enslave his followers.

**nīḷōdam vandalaikkum māmayilai māvallikkēṇiyān** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has taken permanent residence in tiruvallikkēṇi, which is a part of the tirumayilai region, a place where the waves from the ocean will gently lap on the thighs of people who are lying there, putting them to sleep. The waves are doing this because ocean and shore are places which are very much pleasing to emperumān and the waves come on their own to touch emperumān with tiny droplets.

**nīḷōdam** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) thinks that just as those who have toiled in a day come to the shore in order to get a fresh breath of air, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has also come to the ocean-side in order to rid himself of the tiredness of having measured the world.

**aindalaivāy nāgattaṇai** – it appeared that emperumān was lying on the soft mattress of ādiśēśan, who, with his five heads, protects emperumān like an umbrella from wind and sun, and praises emperumān with his five mouths, in such a way that emperumān could overcome the tiredness of having to walk across rough terrain and forests, while measuring the worlds.

We will move on to the 36<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 36. Nagattanai

*(for vhat purpose?) aṇaippār karuttan āvān – in order to enter te hearts of follovers*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**nāgattaṇaik kudandai …… nāgattaṇaip pāṟkadal kidakkum – sarvēśvaran is reclining in the divyadēsams of tirukkudandai, tiruvehkā, tiruvevvuḷūr, tiruvarangam, tiruppēr, tiruvanbil and tiruppāṟkadal, at close proximity to his followers.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār wonders whether it was in only one place where he had reclined. The reasons for reclining in many places were due to the tiredness of measuring the worlds and for entering the minds of devotees who have a desire for emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: nāgattaṇaik kudandai vehkāt tiruvevvuḷ nāgattaṇai arangam pēranbil nāgat taṇaip pāṟkadal kidakkum ādi nedumāl aṇaippār karuttan āvān nāgattu aṇai – on top of the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) kudandai – at tirukkudandai (present day kumbakōṇam) vehkā – at tiruvekka (in kāncīpuram) tiru evvuḷ – at tiruvevvuḷūr (present day tiruvaḷḷūr) (in the same way) nāgattaṇai – on top of the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān arangam – at tiruvarangam (śrīrangam) pēr – at tiruppēr (divyadēsam kōviladi, near tirucci) anbil – at tiruvanbil (near tirucci) (in the same way) nāgattu aṇai – atop ādiśēśan pāṟkadal – at tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) ādi nedumāl – sarvēśvaran (lord of all) who is the cause for the worlds kidakkum – is reclining*

**aṇaippār karuttanāvān kidakkum** – he is reclining in all these divine abodes in order to enter the hearts of those followers who say “we will embrace you in our hearts” instead of saying “we will not allow you to enter our hearts”.

What is the reason for his waiting to enter the hearts of his followers at the appropriate time?

**ādinedumāl** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that it is because he is like a mother to all creations.

**ādi** – he is the cause for the universe, just like a mother is the cause for a child. At one point of time (during deluge) he had sustained all the creations in his stomach and protected them.

**nedumāl** – he has deep affection, just like a mother.

We will take up the 37<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 37. Vanulavu tivali

*vān – sky ulavu – moving tī – fire (ulkavu) vaḷi – moving vind mā kadal – huge ocean mā poruppu – huge mountains sustaining eart ulavu – roaming vem kadir tānum – sūrya vit hot rays taṇ madiyum – candra vho is cool mēl nilavu – moving above koṇḍal – clouds peyarum – cētana (sentient) entities disai ettum – eight directions sūḻcciyum – layers of various materials vhic surround aṇḍam – universe vhic comprises all tese tirumāḷ – emperumā’s agaippu – formed due to emperumān’s sankalpa (vov)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vān – sky (here it refers to ether)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked whether emperumān is the causative entity for all. This pāsuram is his response. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: vānulavau tīvaḷi mākadal māporuppu tānulavu vengadirum taṇmadiyum mēnilavu koṇḍal peyarum disaiyettum sūḻcciyum aṇḍandirumāl agaippu*

**ulavu tī vaḷi mākadal** – the entities which keep moving constantly, namely fire, wind and water. The word *kadal* (ocean) is indicative of the entire water entity (water in various forms such as ocean, river, stream, lake etc)

**mā poruppu** – the huge mountains which sustain earth. This refers to the entity of earth \[thus, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) has mentioned about the five elements of ether, air, fire, water and earth\].

**tānulavu vengdirum taṇmadiyum** – sūrya (sun) and candra (moon) who keep moving around. u*lavu* – moving. Just as it is mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli “*bhīśōdēti sūrya:*”, sun (and moon) would rise and set at appropriate times out of fear for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**mēl nilavu koṇḍal** – clouds which keep moving above, in the sky.

**peyarum** – *cētana varga* (sentient entities)

**koṇḍal peyarum** – could also be construed as that which is called by the name of cloud.

**disai ettum** – the eight directions as mentioned in rig vēda “*diśasca nārāyaṇa…*” (nārāyaṇa is also the eight directions…)

**sūḻcciyum** – the seven *āvaraṇas* (fortified layers) surrounding universe

**aṇḍam** – the universe made up of all these

**tirumāḷ agaippu** – these are controlled by the thoughts rising in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine mind. Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) has used the term *tirumāḷ agaippu* it is implied that the purpose of creation carried out by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is for the happiness of [pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) and that in this activity of creation, pirātti has a role to play, even if it is not primary.

We will take up the 38<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 38. Agaippil manisarai

*porukadalneer vaṇṇan – sarvēśvaran vho has te complexion of ocean vater vit vaves lapping agaippu il manisarai – people vho do not have distinction of being knovledgeable āṟu samayam – in te (lovly) six philosophies pugaittān – made tem get caught ugaikkum ēl – if he remains indifferent (tovards tem) appōdu – at tat time itself ettēvar vālāttum – te ego of vhicever deity evvāṟu seygaiyum – te various deeds carried out tovards tem as vorśip (suc  as rituals etc) aḻaippu – te invocations of tese deities (in suc rituals) oḻiyum – vill disappear*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**agaippil manisarai – jīvātmās (sentient entities) like you, with low level of knowledge, who do not understand how much ever is instructed. agaippu – rising; greatness in knowledge.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “You say that it is only because everything is dependent on sarvēśvaran’s sankalpa (vow of emperumān) that he is the causative factor for all. But in vēdas prevalent in samsāram, it is very well known that brahmā is the creator, rudra is the destroyer and various other deities are carrying out very important tasks. Will they not be causative entities?” to which he says in this pāsuram “if emperumān is not their antaryāmi (indvelling soul) all their greatness will disappear without a trace”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: agaippil manisarai āṟu samayam pugaittān porukadal neervaṇṇan ugaikkumēl ettēvar vālāttum evvāṟu seygaiyum appōdu oḻiyum aḻaippu*

**agaippil manisarai** – jīvātmās who have no distinction about their knowledge – those who do not have any high level of intelligence about them.

**āṟu samayam pugaittān** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) ensured that they were trapped in the six different philosophies. *āṟu samayam* – *bhaudda, naiyāyika, vaiśēśika, jaina, sānkya, yōga* philosophies established by *sākya, ulūkya, akśapāda, kśapaṇa, kapila* and *patanjali* respectively. Instead of *naiyāyika* and *yōga* philosophies, *cārvāka* and *pāsupata* philosophies are included by some.

**pugaittān** – just as hunters, in forests, while trying to get animals which have hidden themselves in dense bushes, will set fire to trash so that the frightened animals will come out of bush and be caught by the hunters, emperumān sets up the smokescreen of other philosophies and traps jīvātmās in samsāram.

**agaippil pugaittān** – For those caught in smoke, will the path be visible? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that it is because of this reason that samsāris are still holding on to other deities despite being told repeatedly about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**porukadal neervaṇṇan ugaikkumēl** – if emperumān, who has a complexion which will comfort the eyes of those who see him, turns out to be indifferent… it is well nigh impossible for someone who, like the ocean, keeps giving away whatever is asked for by the needy. However, if water were to turn into fire; if emperumān becomes indifferent…

**ettēvar vālāttum oḻiyum** – the vanity of all those dēvas (celestial entities) who roam around with the pride of a group of monkeys will disappear. Dogs wag their tail (vālāttum) when they feel proud about themselves; thus, the term wagging the tail implies pride. Don’t these dēvatas also feel proud saying *īśvarōham* (I am īśvaran, the controller of everyone else)?

**evvāṟu aḻaippucceygaiyum oḻiyum** – the invitations extended to these deities when a ritual is performed, such as *indramāvahayāmi, sōmamāvahayāmi* (I invoke the presence of indra, I invoke the presence of sōma) etc and the offerings made to them such as *indrāyasvāhā, varunāyasvāhā* etc will disappear. Alternatively…

**evvāṟu seygaiyum oḻiyum** – the rituals which the jīvātmās carry out to worship these dēvatās will themselves disappear.

**aḻaippu oḻiyum** – The invitations extended to the dēvas during rituals to partake the offerings made with the words *svāhā* will disappear.

**ettēvar vālāttum evvāṟu seygaiyum aḻaippum oḻiyum** – the pride of these dēvatās, the activities indulged in by these dēvatās such as *tripuradahanam* (burning of the three towns) etc and the invitations extended to them by jīvātmās in their rituals will disappear.

**appōdu oḻiyum** – instead of waiting for some time, all these will disappear the moment [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) becomes indifferent to these deities.

We will take up the 39<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 39. Alaippan tiruvengadattanai

*(mistaking te splendour of tose gemstones for fire) yānai – elephant veruvi – standing in fear aravu – pyton odungum – (mistaking tose gemstones for lightning) vill hide inside antills veṟpu – te divine hills of tirumalai kūda – to join suc hills tirukkūdal kūda iḻaippan – ī vill call out in a special vay*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**aḻaippan tiruvēngadattānai – I will call out to emperumān at tiruvēngadam. When asked whether he is calling out to emperumān in order to desire money, grains, sire etc similar to the samsāris or to desire mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) similar to wise and knowledgeable people, āḻvār says …**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār steps back from parōpadēśam (instructing others) and starts enjoying tiruvēngadamalai (tirumalai divya dēśam) and the emperumān who has taken residence there, in several pāsurams starting from this. For this āḻvār, alternating between parōpadēśam and svānubhavam (enjoying the experience of emperumān) is a regular feature. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: aḻaippan tiruvēngadattānaik kāṇa iḻaippan tirukkūdal kūda maḻaippēr aruvi maṇivaranṟi vandiḻiya yānai veruvi aravondungum veṟpu tiruvēngadattānai – tiruvēngadamudaiyān (emperumān) who resides in tiruvēngadam kāṇa – to worship him with my eyes aḻaippan – I call out maḻai – during rainy season pēr aruvi – the huge streams maṇi – gemstones (which are scattered at various places) varanṟi vandu iḻiya – gathering them and falling*

**kāṇa** – it is for neither. I just want to see him and enjoy him. Just as it is said in the old saying \[nācciyār tirumoḻi\] “*gōvindaṟkōr* *kuṟṟēval immaippiṟavi seyyāmē inippōyc ceyyum tavandānen* ” (what is the purpose in carrying out penance instead of carrying out kainkaryam to gōvinda (one of the names for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) at tiruvēngadam) in this birth, right now?) when I am deeply desirous of seeing him now, where is the question of seeing him in śrīvaikuṇṭam at some other point of time? When I am not interested even in śrīvaikuṇṭam, the question of desiring wealth (*aiśvaryam*) does not arise at all!

**iḻaippan tirukkūdal kūda** – I will call out to emperumān to be with him. The word *tirukkūdal* has a special significance. In olden days, in order to guess (similar to foretelling future) whether what one wishes to attain will fructify or not, people will draw a circle and keep drawing concentric circles within the first circle. If the count of circles so drawn is an even number, then it is a positive sign (that their wish will be fulfilled) while if the count is an odd number, then it is a negative sign. This has been referred to by āṇḍāḷ too, in nācciyār tirumoḻi 4<sup>th</sup> decad.

For desiring what, is [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) calling out ? He says in the subsequent lines that it is to be with tiruvēngadamalai.

**maṇippēraruvi maḻi varanṟi vandiḻiya yānai veruvi aravodungum veṟpu kūdat tirukkudal iḻaippan** – during monsoon, the huge streams will bring forth gemstones from various places to tirumalai. Seeing those gemstones, the elephants will mistake them for fire and pythons will mistake them for lightning and cower due to that. It is well known that elephants fear firewood and pythons fear lightning. Alternatively, it could be construed that elephant, mistaking the gemstones for fire will step into the open mouth of python thinking that it is the opening of a cave.

We will move on to the 40<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 40. Verpenru vengadam

*(by tis mere vord) vīdu ākki niṟkinṟēn – ī remained confident tat mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) is certain for me ninṟu ninaikkinṟēn – ī am tinking vit amazement tat for suc a small vord tat ī uttered, ī vas fortunate to get a huge benefit kaṟkinṟa – being recited nūl – among te vēdas (sacred texts) valaiyil pattirunda – standing firmly, as if caught in a net nūlātti kēḷvanār – mahālakśmi’s consort, emperumān’s kāl valaiyil pattirundēn kāṇ – ī got caught in te net of his divine feet and remained firm.*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**veṟpenṟu vēngadam pādinēn –without carrying any significant deed towards the means, I mentioned, unknowingly, the name of tiruvēngadamalai just as one mentions names of other mountains such as koccimalai, kudagumalai, imayamalai etc. emperumān accounted this as my having sung about tiruvēngadamalai. Thus I too became one who has sung about tiruvēngadamalai.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that the deed that he carried out was very small while the benefit he obtained was huge. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: veṟpenṟu vēngadam pādinēn vīdākki niṟkinṟēn ninṟu ninaikkinṟēn kaṟkinṟa nūlvalaiyil pattirunda nūlāttik kēḷvanār kālvalaiyil pattirundēn kāṇ veṟpu enṟu – while mentioning about several mountains vēngadam pādinēn – I sang about tirumalai also*

**vīdākki niṟkinṟēn** – The benefit obtained due to saying this was the paramapuruśārtam (highest benefit) of śrīvaikuṇṭam. Just as it is mentioned in śrīviṣṇu purāṇam “*yan na dēvā na munayō na cāham na Sankara:* I *jānanti paramēśasya tad viṣṇō: paramam padam* īī ” (dēvas, sages, I (brahmā) or rudra do not know about paramapadam, the residence of that supreme īśan (controller of all), viṣṇu) and in mahābhāratam “*atyarkānaladīptam tad stānam viṣṇōr mahātmana:*  I *svayaiva prabhayā rājan duśprēkśam dēvadānavai:* īī” (that dwelling place of mahātmā (supreme being) viṣṇu is superior to sūrya and agni (fire) in terms of its radiance; it cannot be seen by dēvas (celestial entities) and asuras (demons) with their splendour), I attained that śrīvaikuṇṭam which is unattainable by even people who carry out great penance. This I attained, the moment I mentioned the name of tiruvēngadamalai.

**ninṟu ninaikkinṟēn** – [śrī bhagavat gītā 6.34](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/6-34/) says that mind is fickle “*cancalam hi mana:*” periya tirumoḻi 1-1-4 says “*ninṟavānillā nenju*” (mind is wavering). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that he is analysing as to what happened with his mind, in a firm way. Alternatively it can be said to be thinking about what happened and standing. In other words, āḻvār is amazed that such a thing could happen and has become paralysed.

Once he analysed as to what happened, he knew that this has happened due to the causeless mercy of [pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) who is the *puruśakārabhūtai* (one who recommends to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) and that of the most merciful emperumān. He further says how he became fully engaged with them…

**kaṟkinṟa nūl valaiyil pattirunda . . . kēḷvanār** – emperumān who is firmly and inescapably caught within the net of vēdas (sacred texts) which are recited in a regular manner. If it is a net, there will be lot of interwoven threads……

**nūl valaiyil** – this is also a net of threads only, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/). Since he is caught well in the vēdas, it is clearly established that it is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) alone who is the supreme being and that no one can dispute this.

**nūlātti** – (feminine gender) one who controls the threads. In other words, just like emperumān, pirātti is also referred to in vēdas such as śrī sūktam etc. This implies that just as emperumān is said to be as mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 15.15](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/15-15/) “*vēdaiścasarvairahamēva vēda*” (only I am known throughout the vēdas), pirātti is also known through the vēdas since she is the *svarūpanirūpakabhūtā* (the entity who proves the basic nature of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)). pirātti herself mentioned in lakśmītantram “*miyē’khilair mānai:*” (I am mentioned about in all vēdas) and vēda says the same “*asyēśāna jagatō viṣṇupatni*” (she is the controller of the worlds and consort of viṣṇu). nammāḻvār too substantiated the same meaning in his [tiruvāimoḻi 3.1.6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/07/thiruvaimozhi-3-1-6-odhuvar-oththellam/) “*ōduvār ōttellām evvulagatevvevaiyum sāduvāy ninpugaḻin tagaiyallāl piṟidillai . . . .pūvinmēl mādu vāḻ mārbināy*”. In all the vēdas, emperumān is mentioned as the significant and different from all other sentient and insentient entities and pirātti is mentioned as his confidential distinguished entity. If anything is mentioned about her, it refers as well to him who is her *antaryāmi* (indvelling soul) and if anything is mentioned about him, it refers as well to pirātti, his *svarūpanirūpaka bhūtai*. It is this that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) emphasises in his words *kaṟkinṟa nūlvalaiyil pattirunda hūlattik kēḷvanār*. The implied meaning here is that it was due to her merciful recommendatory role that āḻvār attained this means.

**nūlātti kēḷvanār kālvalaiyil pattirundēn kāṇ** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) tells us to see how he was caught in the merciful net of the divine feet of *divyadampatis* (divine couple) both of whom wanted to protect him with utmost care. Alternatively, this can be construed as āḻvār telling his heart “*Oh heart! You also try to fall at their divine feet and see if you can stand firmly*”.

We will take up the 41<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 41. Kanal uruginren

*(even tough you have left tiruvēngadam and entered my heart) tiruvēngadam adanai  senṟu kāṇal uṟuginṟēn – ī am desirous of going to tiruvēngadam and having your darśan tere*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**kallaruvi muttudira – the streams make a sound “kal” and pearls fall down from them. Alternatively, aruvi kal muttu udira – the stream will spill out gemstones like rubies, pearls etc.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that since emperumān’s standing posture at tiruvēngadamalai is more distinguished than his stature as antaryāmi  (indvelling soul) inside his heart, he desires to go to tiruvēngadam and have darśan (seeing) of emperumān there. Just as emperumān desires to shift from his residences at various places to āḻvār’s heart as mentioned in pāsurams such as periya tiruvandādi 65 “kallum kanaikadalum vaigunda vānādum pullenṟoḻindana kol …. adiyēnadu uḷḷattu agam” (emperumān has entered my heart and all his earlier residences such as tiruvēngadam (kal – tiruvēngadam), tiruppāṟkadal, śrīvaikuṇṭam etc have become bushy areas since no one goes there as a result of emperumān having shifted from there…), āḻvār desires to go to tiruvēngadam and see emperumān instead of seeing him residing inside his heart. Hasn’t nammāḻvār prayed to emperumān not to leave his residence by mercifully mentioning in his periya tiruvandādi pāsuram “iḷam kōyil kaividēl” (do not give up the makeshift sannidi \[which is a temporary residence used during renovation of the main sannidi\])! Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: kāṇal uṟuginṟēn kallaruvi muttudira ōṇa viḻavil oli adira pēṇi varu vēngadavā en uḷḷam pugundāy tiruvēngadam adanaic cenṟu kal aruvi – through the streams which make a sound muttu udira – pearls ōṇam viḻavil – in the tiruvōṇam festival \[the star of tiruvēngadattān is tirovōṇam\] oli adira – with the sounds of praising \[emperumān\], reverberating pēṇi varu – (devotees) desirous of coming to vēngadavā – Oh one who has taken residence at tiruvēngadam! en uḷḷam pugundāy – you have entered my heart*

**ōṇa viḻavil oli adira** – during the festival time of tiruvōṇam (which is very famous in tiruvēngadam), the sweet voice of those who sing with lot of affection will reverberate through the hills.

**ōṇa viḻavil oliyadira, kallaruvi muttidira** – the sound of devotees praising [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) at tiruvēngadam will melt stones and they will shed tears. The word *oli* will refer to the sound raised by those who sing praises on emperumān, those who chant vēdas, those who sing and dance etc.

**pēṇi varu vēngadavā** – Oh emperumān at tiruvēngadam! You are residing in such a hill where devotees would always keep praising you with lot of affection, where even the insentient entities would lie as if they are in a peaceful state undergone by sentient entities, and which is frequented by your devotees.

**pēṇi varu vēngadavā** – Oh [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) at tiruvēngadam who would neglect all such wealth and desire to enter my heart!

**ennuḷḷam pugundāy** – you, who are carrying out *sādanam* ( an activity carried out as a means to attain a goal), entered the *sādyam* (readily available) heart of mine as soon as you obtained it!

**tiruvēngadam adanaic cenṟu kāṇal uṟuginṟēn** – (while you are leaving aside tiruvēngadam and entering my heart) I want to come to tiruvēngadam which is more distinguished than my mind and want to see you there.

What is such a distinguishing feature in tiruvēngadam compared to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/)’s heart? āḻvār says “My heart is the originating source for sins while tiruvēngadam removes sins” as mentioned by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his [tiruvāimoḻi 3-3-6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/08/thiruvaimozhi-3-3-6-venkatangal/) “*vēngadangaḷ mey mēl vinai muṟṟavum*”.

We will take up the 42<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 42. Senru vanangumino

*(tat tirumalai) nīrmaiyāl – due to its nature ninṟu vinai kedukkum – vill stand firm in removing sins angu – in tat tirumalai kadi kamalam nānmuganum – brahmā vho vas born in a fragrant lotus flover kaṇ mūnṟattānum – śiva vit tree eyes enṟum – at all times adi – at te divine feet (of emperumān) kamalam – lotus flovers ittu – offering ēttum – vill vorśip*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**senṟu vaṇanguminō sēṇuyar vēngadattai – go and worship tiruvēngadam which is very great in terms of its height and fame.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Some people asked āḻvār “What we need is attainment of desirables and removal of undesirables; will these fructify if we travel to tiruvēngadam?” āḻvār responds “There is no need to climb the hills and worship the father (emperumān) there. It is enough if one goes near the hills and worships the hills. Removal of undesirables and attainment of desirables will happen on their own” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: senṟu vaṇanguminō sēṇuyar vēngadattai ninṟu vinaikedukkum nīrmaiyāl enṟum kadikkamala nānmuganum kaṇ mūnṟattānum adikkamalam ittēttum angu sēṇ uyar vēngadattai – tirumalai which has tall peak senṟu vaṇangumin – go and worship*

**sēṇuyar vēngadattai** – you don’t have to walk a long distance. Just as mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-4-1 “*senniyōngu taṇ tiruvēngadam*” (cool hills of tirumalai whose peaks rise to a great height), even as you leave your town, does not this mountain make itself visible to you, occupying a huge space in the sky, to remove your sins? The word *ninṟu* could be added to both vaṇanguminō and vinaikedukkum by the *madyamaṇi nyāyam* (a provision in poetical grammar). Thus, we can have

**ninṟu vaṇanguminō** – without hurrying yourself, stand calmly and worship.

**ninṟu vinai kedukkum nīrmaiyāl** – the word *vēngadam* means “that which removes sins”. Since the hill has this quality as its identity, it naturally has the quality of removing sins. This implies that one need not cry for removal of undesirables.

What is to be done for getting the desirables? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says  next that the deities who you all think as granting your desires, get their positions by vorśipping [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) at tiruvēngadam….

**enṟum** – it is not that this happens once in a lifetime. This happens always, constantly.

**kadikkamala nānmuganum** – Since brahmā was born out of the entity who is termed as “*sarvaganda:*” (repository of all fragrances) \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\], the lotus on which brahmā sits is also full of fragrance.

**kaṇmūnṟattānum** – Siva who has three eyes indicating that he has high level of knowledge.

**adikkamalam ittēttum angu** – both brahmā and Siva would worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) at tiruvēngadam hills, by offering lotus flowers at his divine feet, and get their positions etc. Thus, the meaning implied here is that samsāris need not worry about attaining their desirables.

We will take up the 43<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.
