## 43. Mangultoy senni

*mangul tōy senni – having peaks vhic reac up to te clouds vadavēngadattānai – for te perumān vho has taken residence at tiruvēngadam kāppu aṇivān – in order to vard off evils tingaḷ sadai yēṟa vaittānum – śiva vho has candra (moon) on his matted hair tāmarai mēlānum – brahmā vho vas born on a lotus flover tām – tese tvo entities kudai ēra kuvittuk koṇḍu – gatering materials suc as pearl umbrella kangul – during te joining time (of night and morning) pugundārgaḷ – vill go to tirumalai*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**mangul tōy senni vadavēngadattānai – the one who has taken residence in tiruvēngadam whose peaks reach the skies. Residing as a simple entity at tirumalai where dēvas (celestial entities) whose legs will not touch the earth, will touch the hills.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*It is not that brahmā, Siva et al come to tiruvēngadam and worship emperumān there for obtaining their desirables and removal of their undesirables. This pāsuram says that even for carrying out the daily worship (nitya tiruvārādanam) of emperumān, they will bring all the implements such as umbrella etc and worship him there. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: mangul tōy senni vadavēngadattānai kangul pugundārgaḷ kāppaṇivān tingaḷ sadaiyēṟa vaittānum tāmarai mēlānum kudaiyēṟat tānguvittuk koṇḍu*

**kāppaṇivān kangul pugundārgaḷ** – in order to carry out the ritual of warding off evils, they will enter the hills twice, at dawn and at dusk.

Who are these entities who enter at dawn and at dusk?

**tingaḷ sadaiyēṟa vaittānum** – rudra who kept the accursed candra on his head. candra was cursed that he would lose his rays gradually by dakśa prajāpati and surrendered to rudra. The meaning implied here is that even those who are famous as protectors get their quality of protecting others by entering tirumalai only.

**tāmarai mēlānum** – brahmā who was born on the lotus shooting from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine navel and who is engaged in the activity of creation. Without any distinction, both brahmā and Siva will enter tirumalai hills to worship emperumān.

**kudaiyēṟat tām kuvittuk koṇḍu pugundārgaḷ** – they will enter tirumalai carrying the divine umbrella made of pearls and other necessary implements in order to carry out *tiruvārādanam* (daily divine worship of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)). Reference to the divine umbrella applies to other implements such as divine fan, mirror etc.

**tām kuvittuk koṇḍu** – bearing them. Feeling happy about carrying them to great heights in order to worship emperumān.

**kangul pugundārgaḷ** – they will enter during every *sandyā kālam* (the time when day and night meet, once at dawn and once at dusk). Just as brahmā had told vālmīki that he would be able to see every activity of śrī rāma through his boon, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) too saw brahmā and Siva carrying out [*tiruvāradanam*](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-thiruvaaraadhanam.html)  to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) due to the unhindered knowledge granted by emperumān.

We will take up the 44<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 44. Kondu kudangalmel

*kumarar uḷḷīr – ōh tose vitout senility! paṇḍu – once upon a time kudangāl mēl – on te lap koṇḍu vaitta kuḻaviyāy – vit te infant kept daṇḍam arakkan – rāvaṇa, apt to be puniśed talai – ten heads tāḷāl – vit divine foot kīṟi – śoving by scratcing and counting pōm – one vho disappeared kumaran – emperumān vho is alvays young niṟkum – te place vhere he stands poḻil vēngadam malaikkē – to tiruvēngadam vhic is surrounded by orcards purindu pōm – go vit desire*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**koṇḍu . . .  kumaran – This narrative has already been mentioned by poygai āḻvār in mudal tiruvandādi 45 “āmē amararkku aṟiyavaduniṟka ….vāḷarakkan nīṇmudiyai pādamattāḷ eṇṇinān paṇbu” and by pēyāḻvār in mūnṟām tiruvandādi 77 “āynda arumaṟaiyōn nānmugattōn nanguṟangil vāynda kuḻaviyāy vāḷarakkan ēynda mudippōdu mūnṟēḻenṟu eṇṇinān ārnda adippōdu nangatkaraṇ”. When the demon rāvaṇa was observing stringent penance toward brahmā, emperumān took the form of an infant and lay in brahmā’s lap as if he were sleeping. In order to indicate to brahmā that this demon has ten heads and that he  will become a very cruel person on earth, emperumān counted his ten heads by scratching rāvaṇa’s ten heads with his divine foot. He also indicated to brahmā that he would incarnate later as śrī rāma and pluck his heads out. There is no reference to this narrative in itihāsa (epic) or purāṇa (ancient sacred text which appears new every time) but since āḻvārs were granted faultless knowledge by emperumān himself, they would have seen this.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When āḻvār was asked “Why do brahmā and rudra worship him like this?” āḻvār responds saying “When these knowledgeable entities lose their knowledge, is it not emperumān who protects them by carrying out benefits for them?” He further says “ Even when you have the strength, go to tirumalai where he stands and worship him” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: koṇḍu kudangāḷ mēl vaitta kuḻaviyāy daṇḍavarakkan talai tāḷāl paṇḍeṇṇip pōm kumaran niṟkum poḻil vēngada malaikkē pōm kumarar uḷḷīr purindu*

**koṇḍu kudangāl mēl vaitta kuḻaviyāy** – becoming an infant who will be kept on the lap

**daṇḍa arakkan . . .** – he is the demon who is going to be punished. The meaning implied here is that emperumān is hinting to brahmā that he is going to later sever the heads of the demon and punish him, by scratching his heads and counting them.

**paṇḍeṇṇippōm kumaran niṟkum** – the place where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who had at an earlier point in time counted the heads, shown to brahmā in order to help him and disappeared, took residence.

**poḻil vēngdamalaikkē** – to tiruvēngadamalai which is surrounded by orchards.

**kumararuḷḷīr purindu pōm** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) implores samsāris to go to tiruvēngadam hills before they reach the age of senility when their bodies would bend forward and they would need a stick to move. He is asking samsāris to go there and worship when they still have strength in them, as it will become very difficult for them to climb the hills when they grow old.

**kumararuḷḷīr** – Just as it is mentioned in śrūti (vēdas) “*bālyēna tiśtāsēt*” (let the *brahmajñāni* (most knowledgeable) be like a child), *kumararuḷḷīr* could be construed to mean that those with knowledge would have the heart of a child.

**pōm** – go immediately.

We will take up the 45<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 45. Purindu malarittu

*puṇḍaraīgappādam – at te divine lotus-like feet purindu – vit affection malarittu – offering flovers parindu – praise emperumān padu kādu niṟpa – prostrating before emperumān like a tree lies after being felled engum – at all places terindu – being seen tān ōngi ninṟān – emperumān vho has taken residence, vit his great auspicious qualities, suc emperumān’s taṇ aruvi vēngadamē – only tirumalai vit cool streams vānōrkkum – for nityasūris (permanent dvellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam) maṇṇōrkkum – for people of tis vorld too vaippu – like a treasure*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**puṇḍarīgappādam – Just as it is mentioned in śrī viṣṇu suktam “viṣṇōr padē paramē madva utsa: ” (streams of honey are dripping from the supremely divine feet of viṣṇu) and in tiruvāimoḻi 1-5-8 “tēnē malarum tiruppādam” (the divine feet from which honey will drip), the divine feet of emperumān which are very sweet like lotus from which honey is dripping.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsurams, āḻvār had mentioned that  tiruvēngadamalai (tirumalai) is the goal for samsāris by saying “senṟu vaṇanguminō sēṇuyar vēngadattai” and “poḻil vēngadamalaikkē pōm”. In this pāsuram he says that tirumalai is the goal for not only the ordinary samsāris but it is so for the distinguished nityasūris too. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: purindu malarittup puṇḍarīgappādam parindu padu kādu niṟpa terindengum tānōngi niṟkinṟān taṇṇaruvi vēngadamē vānōrkkum maṇṇōrkkum vaippu*

**purindu malar ittu** – offering flower etc with lot of affection. Reference to flower is equivalent to inclusion of water, lamp, smoke (incense) etc \[which are used in *tiruvārādanam*, the daily worship of emperumān\].

**parindu padu kādu niṟpa** – devotees will be worried about the place, saying “it is an area with lot of danger. What will happen?” They will prostrate before [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) there, like trees which have been felled in dense forests.

**terindu engum tānōngi niṟkinṟān** – one who is standing majestically atop the hill, displaying his auspicious qualities such as simplicity etc so that they can be seen by all. Just as it is mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-5-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/thiruvaimozhi-4-5-10-undum-umizhndhum/) “*kaṇḍavāṟṟāl tanadē ulagena ninṟān* ” (he stood in such a manner that it was evident to those who saw him that the world is his).

**padukādu niṟpat terindengum tānōngi niṟkinṟān** – amidst the fallen trees, he stands tall like a huge tree which is standing firmly. īsn’t he praised by vēdas, such as “*vrukśa iva stabda:*” (supreme being is standing like the tree which does not bow)!

**taṇṇaruvi vēngadamē** – it is a place which has cool streams which remove the heat from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as well as his followers.

**vānōrkkum maṇṇōrkkum vaippu** – it will be like a huge benefit, similar to a treasure, for both nityasūris and samsāris.

**maṇṇōr** – this could be construed as śrīvaiṣṇavas from samsāram.

We will take up the 46<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 46. Vaippan manivilakka

*(to catc vhic) eppādum vēdu – te hunters from outer peripheries vaḷaikka – to surround kuṟavar – te hilly people of tiruvēngadam vil edukkum – taking to teir bovs (to oppose suc hunters) vēngadamē – only tirumalai nādu – all te people in te  vorld vaḷaittu – to carry out pradikśaṇa (circum-ambulation) ādudum ēl – if tey dance (to express teir happiness) nanṟu – it is good*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vaippan maṇiviḷakkā māmadiyai mālukku enṟu eppōdum kai nīttum yānaiyai – this is the nature of animals in tiruvēngadam. An elephant which is living in tiruvēngadam happens to see the moon one night. Since it is as mentioned in this tiruvandādi 43rd pāsuram “mangultōy senni vadavēngadam” (vēngadam whose hills reach up to the skies), the elephant stretches out its trunk in order to keep the moon as nandāviḷakku (perpetual lamp which is lit inside sanctum sanctorum) for sarvēśvaran (lord of all). Though it could not catch when it stretched its trunk once, since it is a service being rendered for emperumān, out of deep desire, it keeps its truck aloft to somehow catch the moon. It appears that due to the distinction of the place \[being a divine abode\] even animals in the forest have a deep affection for emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asks in this pāsuram as to whether the entire country śouldn’t worship the hills whose ignorant animals and the lowly knowledgeable hilly people carry out service to emperumān and his followers. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: vaippan maṇiviḷakkā māmadiyai mālukkenṟu eppoḻudum kai nīttum yānaiyai eppādum vēduvaḷaikkak kuṟavar villedukkum vēngadamē nādu vaḷaittu ādudumēl nanṟu mā madiyai – (this) distinguished  candra (moon) mālukku – for tiruvēngadamudaiyān maṇiviḷakkā – as an auspicious lamp vaippan enṟu – vowing to keep it (before emperumān) (to catch that candra) eppoḻudum – always kai nīttum – holding its raised trunk, aloft yānai – one elephant*

**eppādum vēdu vaḷaikka** – since they do not live in this divine abode, the hunters who come from other places (peripheral areas) who have rajas and tamas qualities only (passionate and ignorant respectively) and who have come here to catch the elephant, surround it on seeing it. The elephant, which is engaged in rendering service to emperumān, being devoted to something else, does not see the hunters around.

What will happen to the elephant?

**kuṟavar villedukkum** – the hilly people (tribals) who live in tiruvēngadam, in order to oppose the hunters and drive them out, take their bows.

**eppādum vēdu … kuṟavar** – since the hunters have been identified with the adjective *eppādum* and the hilly people have not been identified with any such adjective, it is implied that the hilly people are from tiruvēngadam. Since the hunters are not residing in the divine abode, they carry out a cruel deed which is in line with their clan. While the hilly people are also from a cruel clan only, since they live in a divine abode, they have become *sātvika* (those who have only good qualities). They exhibit their cruel nature towards the hunters, instead.

**eppādurm vēdu … kuṟavar** – there is no difference in the cruel nature of the hunters and hilly people, coming from their clans. Living \[and not living\] in the divine abode creates a huge difference in their qualities.

**kuṟavar villedukkum vēngadamē nāduvaḷaittu ādudumēl nanṟu** – if ignorant as well as knowledgeable people  can go and dance around the tiruvēngadamalai, which gives knowledge even to the ignorant people, it will do the country a lot of good.

**vēdu vaḷaikkak kuṟavar villedukkum vēngadamē … nanṟu** – when there is protection for even animals in the divine abode, should one talk about it for people?

We will take up the 47<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 47. Nanmani vannanur

*āḷiyum – yāḷis (an extinct animal vhic is like a lion, vit an additional trunk) kōḷ ariyum – te poverful lions pon – gold maṇi – carbuncles muttamum – pearls pū maaramum – trees vit blossomed flovers pal maṇi nīrodu porudu uruḷum kānamum – forests vit streams vherein many different types of gemstones vill mix togeter and roll dovn vānaramum – monkeys vēdum – hunters’ tribes udai – having (all te aforementioned entities) vēngadam – tirumalai nal maṇivaṇṇan ūr – residing place of sarvēśvaran (lord of all) vho has te form of a blue coloured good gemstone*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**nanmaṇi vaṇṇanūr –  residential place of one who has a complexion and basic nature similar to a good, blue gemstone. vaṇṇam – complexion; basic nature. In terms of his complexion, he is invaluable like a gemstone. emperumān has a simplicity which is similar to folding the gemstone in a piece of cloth and putting it inside a pocket. If it is lost, life will be worthless and if it is present, one can sustain oneself. Since emperumān has qualities seeing which everyone will fall at his divine feet, he is compared to the gemstone and hence is called as maṇivaṇṇan.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about an elephant. In this pāsuram, he mentions about many different types of materials which have no knowledge at all, getting knowledge similar to nityasūris and carrying out service to emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: nanmaṇi vaṇṇanūr āḷiyum kōḷariyum ponmaṇiyum muttamum pūmaramum panmaṇi nīrodu poruduruḷum kānamum vānaramum vēdumudai vēngadam*

**nanmaṇivaṇṇan** – actually, the gemstones which are seen in samsāram are not even a patch on emperumān. Hence [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) calls him as *nanmaṇivaṇṇan* thus qualifying the gemstone; emperumān doesn’t have the faults of gemstones such as being hard, being small etc.

**Ur** – When āḻvār is subsequently going to say “*kānamum vānaramum vēdum udai vēngadam*”, is it apt to refer to tirumalai as *Ur* (town)? īsn’t it apt to be referred to as mountain or forest? *Ur* is defined as one which is fit for protection by the king. śrī rāma left ayōdyā and went to the forest only because the sages living in the forest were to be saved from the fear of demons. Thus forest became country. sumitrā tells lakśmaṇa even as he is leaving for the forest along with śrī rāma and sītāppirātti, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 40-8 “*ayōdyā adavīm viddi*” (know the forest as ayōdyā). Thus whatever is to be protected becomes *Ur*. As per this description, tirumalai which welcomes lowly people too and gives them protection is fit to be called as *Ur*.

The pāsuram talks now about those who are protected . . .

**āḷiyum kōḷariyum** – yāḷis and lions, which are always inimical to each other, forget their enmity and carry out service to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) together at tirumalai.

**ponnum maṇiyum muttamum pūmaramum panmaṇi nīrodu poruduruḷum kānamum vānaramum vēdum udai vēngadam** – even [acētana (insentient) entities](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/) such as gold, carbuncles etc which have no knowledge, acquire knowledge due to the greatness of emperumān and carry out service to him. The pride of this place is that even those with knowledge pray that they would like to be born as a stork in the pond at tiruvēngadam or as a fish in the river there or a flower or a pillar there (reference here is to [kulaśēkara āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kulasekarazhwar/)).

**ponnum ….vēdumudai vēngadam nanmaṇi vaṇṇanūr** – This is the place which welcomes even those who have lower levels of knowledge and protects them, where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) resides with joy. īsn’t this place, which makes even samsāris to become nityasūris, better than paramapadam where he protects nityasūris (eternal residents of paramapadam) and muktas (liberated souls who have attained paramapadam)!

**āḷiyūm ….** – periyavāccān piḷḷai, the commentator for this divyaprabandam, has written “*tirumalaiyil varttikkum nityasūrigaḷaic collugiṟadu*” ((āḷiyum) refers to nityasūris who dwell in tirumalai). There are two ways in which this could be interpreted. One is that the animals etc which live in tirumalai, after getting an interest in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) related matters, carry out service to emperumān similar to the way nityasūris carry out. Another is that nityasūris, in order to enjoy the simplicity of emperumān at tirumalai, take the forms of acētana as well as cētana entities (insentient and sentient) such as gold, carbuncles, yāḷis, lions etc and carry out service to him here. This second interpretation is based on śrī parāśara bhattar’s śrī rangarāja stavam 1-33 ślōkam “*ahrutasahajagāsyās sūraya: srastabandā: vimalacaramadēhā ityamī rangadāma:*  I *mahita manujatiryak stāvaratvā: śrayantē sunīyatamiti ha sma prāhūrēbyō nama: stāt* īī ” (elders have said that nityasūris who do not ever let go of their basic nature of carrying out service to emperumān, muktas who have severed their connection with samsāram and mumukśus who have got rid of their sins and are having their final physical form , willingly take the forms of human species, animal species such as cows and birds and botanical species and attain śrīrangam always. My salutations to them!)

We will go on to the 48<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 48. Vengadame

*viṇṇōr – nityasūris (permanent dvellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam) meymmaiyāl – vit true devotion toḻuvaduvum – vorśip vēngadamē – only tirumalai mey vinai – indelible sins (vhic cannot be got rid of, vitout experiencing) mey nōy – ills of te body tīrppaduvum – gets rid of vēngadamē – only tirumalai dānavar vīḻa – to destroy te demons tan āḻi padai tottu – vielding his cakrāyudam (te veapon of divine disc) vānavarai – dēvas (celestial entities) kāppān – emperumān vho protects, his malai – tirumalai vēngadamē – it is only tiruvēngadam*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vēngadamē viṇṇōr toḻuvaduvum meymmaiyāl – Since āḻvār says “meymmaiyāl toḻuvaduvum” and later in the pāsuram says “vānavraikkāppān”, the term viṇṇōr here refers to nityasūris.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it is tirumalai alone which nityasūris worship and which removes the ills of samsāris. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: vēngadamē viṇṇōr toḻuvaduvum meymmaiyāl vēngadamē mey vinai nōy tīrppaduvum vēngadamē dānavarai  vīḻat tannāḻip padai tottu vānavaraik kāppān malai*

**meymmaiyāl toḻuvaduvum** – it appears that there are two different types of worship – a true worship and a false one. When dēvas (including brahmā) worship him on account of their karma (effect of past deeds) as mentioned in the [42](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/07/nanmugan-thiruvandhadhi-42/)[<sup>nd</sup>](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/07/nanmugan-thiruvandhadhi-42/) [pāsuram](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/07/nanmugan-thiruvandhadhi-42/) of this prabandam “*kadikkamala nānmuganum kaṇmūnṟattānum adikkamalam ittēttum angu*”, even though they consider [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as the means, they will worship him with their position, wealth etc as their goal. This is falsified worship. On the other hand, nityasūris will consider tirumalai, his dwelling place, as the means and goal and worship him. This is true worship. For those who worship him without a purity of mind, since their goal is different, the worship is false while for those who worship him considering him as everything, this natural worship becomes true worship.

**vēngadamē meyvinai nōy tīrppaduvum** – apart from being the goal for nityasūris, tirumalai also removes the undesirables of samsāris (dwellers of materialistic world).

**meyvinai** – strong sins which cannot be got rid of, without experiencing. Just as it is mentioned in brahmavaivarttam prakruti kaṇḍam 26-70 “*avaśyanubōktavyam krutam karma śubhāśubham*  I *nābhuktam kśīyatē karma kalpakōtiśatairapi*  īī” (jīvan (sentient entity) has to experience the results of virtuous and viceful sins. Even if it takes a hundred crore kalpam (one kalpam is brahmā’s one day), the karmas will not disappear without experiencing), it is only tirumalai which removes sins.

**nōy tīrppaduvum vēngadamē** – it  is only tirumalai which removes the ills which result as a consequence of such sins.

**meyvinai nōy …** – it is only tirumalai which removes sins and ills which come due to  connection with *śarīra* (physical form).

**vēngadamē meyvinai nōy tirppaduvum** – doesn’t the word *vēngadam,* which means  “burning sins” indicate that it is capable of removing bodily ills? v*inai* would refer to sins and *nōy* would refer to the physical ills which come as a result of the sins.

**vēngadamē dānavarai vīḻat …… vānavarai kāppān malai** – it is only tirumalai which is the residence of the one who dons the divine disc to annihilate the demons and provide dwelling place for celestial entities, thus protecting them.

**āḻippadai tottu** – there is no need even to release cakrattāḻvān (divine disc). It is enough if [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) just touches the disc in order to annihilate the gang of demons.

We will move on to the 49<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 49. Malaiyamaimel

*malai – mantara parvam (te mountain mantara, a celestial mountain) āmai mēl vaittu – atop te form of kūrma (tortoise ) i.e. on himself vhen he took incarnation in te form of tortoise vāsugiyai – te serpent vāsuki suṟṟi – coiling around te mountain (like a rope for curning) āmai tān – he himself, vho incarnated as kūrmāvatāram talai oro kai paṟṟī – pressing te top of te mountain vit one hand alaiyāmal – ensuring tat te vater did not go out pīṟa – (nectar) to come out kadainda – curning (te ocean) perumān – emperumān’s tirunāmam – divine names kūṟuvadē – reciting aloud yāvarkkum – for all kūṟṟu – let it become te norm for speaking*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**malai āmai mēl vaittu – keeping the mountain mantara atop himself, in the form of kūrma (tortoise). In order to protect the celestial entities who were without strength, emperumān wanted to give them nectar by churning the milky ocean, tiruppāṟkadal. For this, he uprooted the mountain mantara and planted it in the ocean as churning staff and started churning. When it appeared that due to the churning motion, the mountain would sink in the ocean, emperumān took the incarnation of kūrma (tortoise) and positioned himself below the mountain. āḻvār is referring to this.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār spoke about the simplicity of emperumān in standing atop the hill and welcoming his followers. In this pāsuram, he speaks and feels joyous about the simplicity of emperumān in lying below the hill and sustaining it. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: malaiyāmaimēl vaittu vāsugiyaiccuṟṟi talaiyāmai tānoru kai paṟṟi alaiyāmal pīṟak kadainda perumāḷ tirunāmam kūṟuvadē yāvarkkum kūṟṟu*

**vāsugiyaic cuṟṟi** – coiling the huge snake vāsuki as the rope for cunring the ocean through the mountain.

**āmai tān oru kai paṟṟi** – while the mountain was supported from sinking in the ocean, during the process of churning, it would move sideways or go to the top. To prevent such motions, emperumān held the top of the mountain with his own hand.

**alaiyāmal pīṟak kadainda** – even after doing all these, dēvas (celestial entities) and asuras (demonic entities) were not capable of churning the ocean. Hence emperumān carried out this task too, himself.

**alaiyāmal pīṟak kadainda** – ensuring that the ocean did not become rageful, as well  as ensuring that the waters of the ocean did not spill over since a huge mountain was dropped into  it as the churning staff, emperumān churned the ocean such that nectar came out of the ocean as its essence. By this act of churning the inner sides of the ocean, emperumān proved that he is the Lord of dēvas too.

**kadainda perumān** – we can say that by this one act, emperumān made the celestial entities as his servitors.

**perumāḷ tirunāmam kūṟuvadē yāvarkkum kūṟṟu** – reciting the divine names of emperumān who transformed himself in order to carry out the task for *anyaprayōjanars* (those who seek him for other benefits) is the most apt act for both *anyaprayōjanars* and *ananyaprayōjanars* (those who seek emperumān himself as the benefit), without any distinction.

We will take up the 50<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 50. Kurramum sara

*Hence kūṟṟamum sārā – fear of yama (deity of righteousness) not approacing kodu vinaiyum sārā – cruel sins not approacing tī mārṟamum sārā  – bad reputation not approacing vagai – means aṟindēn – ī knev*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**kūṟṟamum sārā vagai aṟindēn – I knew the means by which yama will not come near me. It is apt to recall here some pāsurams from divya prabandam such as nānmugan tiruvandādi 68 “tiṟambēnmin kaṇḍīr” (yama tells his messengers “What I now instruct you, do not go against”), tiruvāimoḻi 10-2-1 “kedum idarāyavellām kēsavā enna nāḷum koduvinai seyyum kūṟṟin tamargaḷum kuṟugakillār” (by reciting the divine name kēśava, all the hurdles will disappear; the messengers of yama who indulge in cruel punishments every moment, will not come anywhere near and will run away), tirumalai 1 “nāvalittu uḻitaruginṟōm namantamar talaigaḷmīdē ” (we are walking on top of the heads of yama’s messengers, raising svictorious slogans), periya tirumoḻi 8-10-7 “vaḷḷalē! un tamarkkenṟum namantamar kaḷḷarpōl” (Oh the magnanimous one! the messengers of  yama will hide from your followers just as thieves would hide themselves ) and śrīviṣṇu purāṇam ślōkam 3-7-14 “svapuruśamabivīkśya” (having observed the supreme being).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār instructed others to recite the divine names of sarvēśvaran (lord of all) to reap the benefit of having been given a tongue. In this pāsuram he says that he has no ills since he has taken the divine names of emperumān in his heart and sheds all his worries. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: kūṟṟamum sārā kodu vinaiyum sāra tī māṟṟamum sārā vagai aṟindēn āṟṟang karaikkidakkum kaṇṇan kadal kidakkum māyan uraikkidakkum uḷḷattenakku kadal – in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) kidakkum – reclining māyan – amazing entity āṟu karai – at kapistalam on the banks of river kāvēri kidakkum – reclining kaṇṇan – kriṣṇa’s urai – the divine carama ślōka (ultimate hymn) enakku – for me uḷḷattu – heart kidakkum – is registered*

**koduvinaiyum sārā vagai aṟindēn** – I also knew the means by which terrible sins, which cause yama to come near me, will not approach me.

**tī māṟṟamum sārā vagai aṟindēn** – I also knew the means by which ill reputation such as “he is a *dēhābhimāni* (one who mistakes physical body for soul); he is a *svatantrābhimāni* (he thinks that he is independent); he is a *dēvataṇṭhara paran* (he worships other deities); he is *viśayāntaraparan* (he is after worldly pursuits); he is *upāyāntaraparan* (he goes after other means to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)))” will not approach me. *tīmāṟṟam* – calamity; evil

What is the basis for knowing such means?

**kadal kidakkum māyan āṟṟangaraik kidakkum kaṇṇan uraikkidakkum uḷḷattu enakku** – kaṇṇam (kriṣṇa) lies in kapistalam on the shores of the river kāvēri in order to be accessible to all those who cannot approach him at tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) where he normally reclines as an amazing entity who is approached by brahmā et al; such kriṣṇa, during the mahābhārata war had said, as mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/18-66/) “*sarva darmān parityajya māmēkam śaraṇam vraja* I *aham tvā sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi mā śuca:* īī” (leaving aside all the means for attaining mōkśam, along with their traces, hold on to me alone, as the means; I will liberate you from all sins; don’t feel sad). These words will always be in my heart. Thus I knew the means by which yama, cruel sins and ill reputation will not approach me.

**kadal kidakkum māyan** – he is an amazing entity who reclines in the milky ocean where he can be approached only by a few people such as those living in śvētadvīpam (another planet like ours).

**āṟṟangaraikkidakkum kaṇṇan** – just as in a town, when a person goes in search of another and doesn’t find him easily, he goes to the riverfront and waits for him there (since everyone comes to the river for their needs), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) also reclines near the river bed, waiting for those who have desire in him to come there to worship him.

**kaṇṇan** – kriṣṇa, who becomes the matter for everyone’s eyes.

**kaṇṇan urai** – the carama ślōkam recited by kriṣṇa. Alternatively, we can construe this as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine names which refer to his *svarūpa* (basic nature), *rūpa* (divine form), *vibhūti* (wealth) and *cēśtita* (divine activities).

**uraikkidakkum** – the heart where his divine names rest. In other words, to me who has the knowledge about him.

**kaṇṇan uraikkidakkum uḷḷattenakku kūṟṟamum sārā** – for me, who has his instructions “*sarva darmān parityajya*” (letting go of all means) in my heart, there is no fear about yama which haunts those who are engaged only in their *varṇāśrama darmam* (doing whatever is ordained in the vēdas for one’s classification and status).

**kaṇṇan uraikkidakkum ….. koduvinaiyum sārā** – there is no scope for me who is fully engaged in kriṣṇa’s instructions, to have any connection with *pāpa / puṇya* (vice / virtue) which would befall those who observe certain deeds desirous of end results, which those deeds would give them.

**āṟṟangaraikkidakkum** – this implies certain qualities such as simplicity which are referred to in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s utterance of the word *mām* (in the carama ślōkam), which are known as *āśrayaṇa saukaryāpātaka guṇas* (those which facilitate his followers to attain him).

**kadal kidakkum māyan** – this implies certain qualities such as jñāna sakti which are referred to in his utterance of the word *aham* (in the carama ślōkam) which are known as *āśritakāryāpātaka guṇas* (qualities which facilitate his carrying out the activities of his followers).

We will move on to the 51<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 51. Enakkavar ar oruvare

*punam – groving on its ovn land kāyā – te flover kāyāmpū’s (a purple coloured flover from te plant kāyā) vaṇṇanē – ōh emperumān vho has te same complexion! enakku – for me vho remains tat you are te only protector āvār ār oruvarē – is tere anyone vho is an equal? (ṭhere is none) emperumān – te sarvēśvaran (lord of all) tānē tankkāvān allāl – vill be equivalent to himself. ṭhere is noting else. maṟṟu – vill you be my equal? unnai piṟar aṟiyār – oter tan me, no one else knovs about you en madikku – for my intellect (vhic knovs you fully) vinnellām uṇḍō vilai – even if all entities in śrīvaikuṇṭam get togeter, can tey compare vit me?*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**enakkāvār Ar oruvarē – Oh benefactor! In both vibhūtis (nityavibhūti – śrīvaikuṇṭam and leelā vibhūti – materialistic realm) is there anyone who is comparable to me?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram āḻvār said that he has no ills. In this pāsuram he says that he has no equal. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: enakkāvār Ar oruvarē emperumān tanakkāvān tānē maṟṟallāl punakkāyā vaṇṇanē unnaip piṟar aṟiyār enmadikku viṇṇellām uṇḍo vilai*

All the vēdas and those who follow these vēdas uniformly say that he ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) is the most superior and that there is none anywhere near him, as brought out by (1)  svētasvatara upaniśat “*na tvatsamaścāpyadigaśca druśyatē*” (there is none who is equivalent or superior to that paramapuruśa (superior entity)) 2) [śrī bhagavat gītā 11.43](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/11-43/) “*na tvatsamōstyabhyadika kutōnyō lōkatrayē’pyapratimaprabhāva*” (Oh the incomparable entity! There is none in the three worlds who is your equivalent. Could there be one who is superior to you?) [sarvēśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) says “When such is the case, why are you proclaiming about yourself?”

**emperumān tanakkāvān tānē maṟṟallāl** – you, the [sarvēśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/), are equivalent to yourself. Even you cannot be my equivalent. Just as yourself, I am also without anyone comparable to me. Just as there is none who is your equivalent in protecting others, there is none who is my equivalent among those who are protected. You, who would say “there is none who protects me”, cannot be compared to me who would say “only [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the protector”

Let it be so. There are others in this world who are in your state, who consider only me as the protector. Will those people not be comparable to you?

**punakkāyā vaṇṇanē unnaip piṟar aṟiyār** – there is none who knows you other than me, who will not leave your beautiful divine form even if you turn out to be one who tortures instead of one who protects.

**punakkāyāvaṇṇanē** – Oh one who has the complexion of kāyāmpū (a type of flower with purple colour) which blossoms in its own land and who has its qualities of softness, coolness, fragrance etc!

**punakkāyāvaṇṇanē! unnaip piṟar aṟiyār** – will those who consider their physical forms as their souls, know you? Will those who feel that they are totally independent, know you? Will those who worship other deities, know you? Will those who worship you for obtaining other benefits, know you? Will those who observe other means to attain you, know you? ōr will riśis, who meditate on your basic nature, know you?

[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) then asks him “in nityavibhūti, there are great entities such as [viśvaksēnar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/vishwaksenar/), anantāḻvān, garudan et al who know me. Will they, at the least, be comparable to you?”

**en madikku viṇṇellāl uṇḍō vilai** – in this samsāram where everyone puts in his efforts to attain anything, I am vorśipping your divine feet as the means for attaining you. Do all the entities in śrīvaikuṇṭam (viśvasksēnar et al) have the total belief that my intellect has in you? The implied meaning here is that since they (entities in śrīvaikuṇṭam) have already attained the goal, the firmness that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) has in emperumān as the means will be lacking in them. āḻvār says “I have no objection to the fact that they will know your svarūpam, rūpam etc (basic nature, divine form etc). But even if they all get together, they cannot equal me. They dwell in a place where enemy (karma, deeds from past) is non-existent whereas I am staying in samsāram where karma rules in a cruel way with the firm belief that ‘you are the protector’. Will they be comparable to me? Could the lotus in pond be compared to one which blossoms from a clay oven? Could daylight be comparaed to lamp in the night?” Hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too mercifully mentioned in tiruviruttam 79 “*sīdanaiyē toḻuvār viṇṇuḷārilum sīriyarē*” (those who worship the cool [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) without any break are superior to those in śrīvaikuṇṭam)!

**enakkāvār Ar oruvarē** – thus, since there is no one in both vibhūtis who could be compared to me, who is there who could be compared to me? Since this pride comes not out of *ahankāram* (vanity) but due to connection with emperumān, this is not considered as a fault but is acceptable completely.

We will take up the 52<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 52. Vilaikkatpaduvar

*takkōr – lovly people vilaikku āḷ paduvar – tey vill become slaves in order to eke out a living visādi ēṟṟu uṇbar – villingly take on oters diseases and enjoy tem (as part of accepting mrutyudān (donation of deat) talaiiku āḷ bali tirivar – tey vill roam around offering teir heads for someone else to live, indulging in human sacrifice. mulaikkāl vidam uṇḍa vēndanaiyē – kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa vho drank te poisonous milk from pūtanā’s bosom vēṟu ā ēttādār – not vorśipping pointedly kallādavar – being vitout knovledge kadam uṇḍār – enjoying only sins*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vilaikkātpaduvar – people who will sell their souls to others for very low values. Their souls are expected to be servitors only to emperumān, which are only his possession and which are sweet to him like śrī kaustubam \[śrī kaustubam is a divine jewel decorating the divine chest of emperumān and is said to represent all ātmās (souls)\]**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “Could you say that there is none who is equal to you in this world?” he responds in this pāsuram that world is full of these types of people (as explained in the vyākhyānam). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: vilaikkātpaduvar visādiyēṟṟuṇbar talaikkātbali tirivar takkōr mulaikkāl vidamuṇḍa vēndanaiyē vēṟā ēttādār kadamuṇḍār kallādavar*

**viśadiyēṟṟuṇbar** – when an affluent person suffers from a disease, for the sake of money that he offers, these people will not hesitate to take on his disease and will be willing to enjoy that too. *visādi* – vyādi (disease)

**uṇbar** – while they enjoy the money that the affluent person offers, these people will be willing to enjoy his disease too.

Is it only this much?  …..

**talaikkāt bali tirivar** – when a person who is suffering from terminal disease escapes from it, these people will be willing to offer their heads as a sacrifice to lowly deities; they will go around town, looking for such ‘diseased’ people.

**takkōr** – very intelligent people \[mentioned with sarcasm\]. What sort of knowledge do these people have that they will be willing to sell themselves or take up someone else’s disease or offer their heads, all for the sake of money, without realising that one can but paint only if the canvas is there!

What is the reason for their knowledge to get blunted like this?

**mulaikkāl vidamuṇḍa vēndanaiyē vēṟā ēttēdār kallādavar** – not vorśipping with their intelligence, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who drank not  only the poison that the demon pūtanā who came in the guise of yaśōdā had applied on her bosom, but also the poison of her deceit and killed her. The implied meaning is that since they are not knowingly vorśipping emperumān, who removes enemies of his followers as his profession, their enemy of ignorance is still around.

**vēndanaiyē vēṟā ēttādār** – those who do not worship emperumān specifically. Since they are people who will do anything for money, there will be no shortcoming in their praising the dogs and ghosts of this word as indra and candra (i.e. as great people). But they seem to have taken a vow that they will not worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) even once, due to their ignorance.

**vēṟā ēttēdār** – *vēṟā*  would mean pointing out specifically. There are some who would construe the meaning of *vēṟā ēttēdār* as those who do not worship in a special way.

**kadamuṇḍār** – they swallow their sins, just as it has been mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā ślōkam 3-13](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/3-13/) “*tē tvagham bhunjate pāpā:*” (those sinners eat their sins). *kadam* – sin.

**vēndanaiyē vēṟā ēttēdār kadamuṇḍār kallādavar** – this follows the meaning expressed in [śrī bhagavat gītā ślōkam 7-15](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-15/) “*na mām dukritinō mūdā: prapadyantē narādamā:* I *māyayā’pahruta jñānā: āsuram bhāvamāśritā:* īī” (fools, lowest among men, those with their knowledge blunted by illusion and demonic people do not attain me as they are all sinners). If the text is taken as *kadanuṇḍār* the meaning will be that those who keep borrowing in order to sustain themselves.

**kallādavar** – even after knowing that they cannot repay, these people will go on borrowing due to their blunted knowledge.

We will move on to the 53<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 53. Kalladavar ilangai

*kallādavar ilangai – te country of lankā populated by te ignorant demons kattu aḻitta – destroying its fortress kāguttan allāl – oter tan śrī rāma oru deyvam – anoter deity yān ilēn – ī vill not accept as a deity pollāda dēvarai – deities vit deformities dēvar allārai – (in trut) lacking in te qualities of being a deity tiru illā dēvarai – vitout having any connection vit pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi), tose vho bear te name as deity dēvu – as deities tēṟēlmin – please do not tink of*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**kallādavar ilangai – lankā which is polulated like the hive of wasps, with ignorant demons. Just as sītāppirātti  doubted, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 21-9 “iha santō na vā santi” (does this place not have learned people?) It is the habitat of ignorant people who could not tolerate the presence of the only intelligent person vibhīśaṇa and drove him out of the country. Here, the term kalvi refers to knowledge relating to mōkśam as mentioned in śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1-19-41 “sā vidyā yā vimuktayē” (learning is that which is used for attaining mōkśam). īsn’t it well known that those in lankā lack that learning!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Some samsāris asked āḻvār “What is the reason for your position in saying enakkāvār Ar oruvarē and our position vilaikkātpaduvar? Also, please mercifully tell us the way in which we can redeem ourselves”. āḻvār responds saying “being not connected with other deities is the reason for my greatness and the same is the reason for your lowliness. If you wish to redeem yourselves, sever your connection with other deities”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: kallādavar ilangai kattaḻitta kāguttan allāl oru deyvam yānilēn pollāda dēvarait tēvarallārait tiruvillāt tēvarai tēṟēlmin dēvu*

**kattaḻitta kāguttan** – the divine son of emperor daśarata who destroyed the fortress of lankā.

**kāguttanallāl oru deyvam yānilēn** – I will not think of  anyone other than that benefactor who is totally devoted to the huge *yāgam* (a ritual with agni, fire) for protection through surrendering, as being fit enough for being attained. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) implies that this is the reason for his greatness.

**yān ilēn** – āḻvār tells samsāris that not having this quality of not vorśipping other deities, even through forgetfulness, is the reason for their lowliness. This āḻvār is similar to hanuman who said as in śrī rāmāyaṇam uttara kāṇḍam 40-15 “*snēhō mē paramō rājan tvayi nityam pratitiśtita:* I  *bhaktiśca niyatā vīra bhāvō nānyatra gaccati* īī” (Oh emperumān! The affection that I have towards you is huge. This affection is firmly established in you).

When asked by the samsāris as to the way by which they can redeem themselves, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) tells them the nature of other deities and tells the samsāris to sever their connection with such deities…..

**pollāda devarai** – unlike emperumān who attracts the heart and eyes of his followers through his divine form, nature, qualities and activities, these other deities do not have the kind of form to see or fame which is sweet to hear or do not have the capability to grant boons which will be everlasting. Their forms will be crude and grotesque with deformed eyes, eyes which emit fire and with matted hair, with powdered ash applied all over the body, unlike the lotus like eyes of emperumān who is like a mother to the world. Their activities too will be crude such as plucking the head of one’s own father, begging all over the world in order to remove the curse that got stuck on removing the head of father, destroying holy rituals, running away out of fear from a demon called basmāsura and helping another demon bāṇāsura and getting defeated in the battle in the bargain etc. These are in direct contrast to the sweet activities of emperumān who killed the wicked ones, protected the good ones, redeemed dead sons and granted mōkśam to bird, demon and pot. Instead of asking for *patram puśpam* … ([śrī bhagavat gītā 9.26](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-26/)) for vorśipping, these deities will ask for the flesh of devotee’s son. Thus their qualities will also be crude. Since they are crude in various ways, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) calls them as crude deities.

Next āḻvār realises that he has called them as *dēva* by mistake….

**dēvarallārai** –  if one were to disregard such cruelties in them and analyse their basic nature in order to worship them they will be such that they will look up to emperumān for alms for their sustenance and be without any greatness. In other words, they will not be apt to be vorśipped.

**dēvarallārai** – just as it is mentioned in nācciyār tirumoḻi “*ayanmudalāttanda naḷirmā malarundi vīttaippaṇṇi viḷaiyādum vimalan*”, is it correct to call those who were created by emperumān for his pastime, as deities? Is it apt to call those who run away from battlefield (in the war with bāṇāsura which they indulged in and lost) or those who borrow radiance and decorate themselves or those who learn vēdas from emperumān or those who carry a skull in their hands in order to get rid of a curse or those who run away when troubled by demons? With what feature or quality in them can they be called as dēvas?

What is the reason for their not being dēvas? …

**tiruvillāttēvarai** – since they do not have the grace of śrī mahālakśmi, they are not dēvas. Since they do not have the connection with [periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) who even the vēdas praised by saying “*śraddayā dēvō dēvatvamaśnutē*” (only due to periya pirātti, even śrīman nārāyaṇa qualifies to be a dēva), they do not qualify to be dēvas.

**tiruvaillāttēvarai** – when referring to lowly persons in the world, they are called as *tiruvilār* (lacking in wealth).

**tiruvillāttēvarai** – did not puruśa sūktam which spoke about emperumān “*utāmrutatvasya īśāna:*” as the one who grants mōkśam, say “*hrīśca tē lakśmīśca patnyau… sarvam maniśāṇa*” (śrīdēvi and bhūdēvi are your consorts… we will obtain everything from you) concluding that it is due to his being śrīmān that he grants mōkśam!

**pollādadēvarai dēvarallārai tiruvillāttēvarai** – since they do not have any connection with pirātti, even though they are not really dēvas, they are called as dēvas as they carry the name dēvas. Hence [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) is calling them as *pollāda dēvar* and *tiruvillāttēvar*.

**tēṟēnmin dēvu** – āḻvār tells us not to respect them as deities. If you follow this \[instruction\] you can also say “*enakkāvār Ar oruvarē*” (who is equal to me, as āḻvār had sung in the earlier pāsuram).

**tēṟēnmin dēvu** – since these deities also grant materials such as son, cow, food etc they may be called as deities. Hence you may also think that these deities are fit to be attained. Since the materials which they give are transient (they do not last long), are lowly and are granted ultimately by emperumān as mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7.22](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-22/) “*mayaiva vihitān hi tān*” through these deities, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) tells us not to think of them as deities.

We will take up the 54<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 54. Devaray nirkum

*dēvarāy niṟkum attēvum – among (āditya, indra et al) dēvatās (celestial entities) standing as viṣṇu and as upēndra attēvaril – among tose dēvas mūvar āy niṟkum – standing as one among te tree mūrti (forms) mudu puṇarppum – te first incarnation yāvar āy niṟkinṟadu ellām – standing as all cit (sentient) and acit (insentient) entities suc as dēvas et al nedumāl enṟu ōrādār – tose vho do not analyse and knov tat he is nārāyaṇa alone kaṟkinṟadu ellām kadai – all te education tat tey have had is very lovly.*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**dēvarāy niṟkum attēvum – Making the people of this world astonished saying “He (emperumān) is one among the twelve ādityas and indra”, he remained as one among the ādityas and as upēndra, the younger brother of indra (lord of celestial entities), also with the name of vāmana (dwarf). āḻvār reminisces the way emperumān incarnated as one among dēvas. There are also those who construe the meaning for this as “Being the antaryāmi (indvelling soul) of dēvas”. However, since later in the pāsuram āḻvār is going to say “yāvarāy niṟkinṟadellām” and since mentioning about upēndra ahead of viṣṇu as in the line “mūvarāy niṟkum mudupuṇarppum” is more apt, and since the flow is better this way, it is better to construe this as referring to upēndra.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Some people asked āḻvār “You are abusing rudra and other celestial entities by calling them as pollāda dēvarai. But śāstras consider your emperumān also as a dēva, upēndra, the brother of indra and as one among brahmā, viṣṇu and rudra, again as a dēva among the other two primary dēvas and as being controlled by other dēvas. Wouldn’t these faults encumber him?” āḻvār ridicules them saying “All the śāstras which you people learnt are a waste since you do not know that the one who is in the form of upēndra and as viṣṇu is none other than śrīman nārāyaṇa who pervades everyone and everything as sarvāntaryāmi”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: dēvarāy niṟkum attēvum attēvaril mūvarāy niṟkum mudupuṇarppum yāvarāy niṟkinṟadellām nedumāl enṟōrādār kaṟkinṟadellām kadai*

**attēvaril mūvarāy niṟkum mudupuṇarppum** – Among the dēvas, standing along with brahmā and rudra, giving the impression to the people of the world that he is equal to the other two so as to be addressed as mummūrti (the three forms) thus taking the first incarnation as viṣṇu.

**mūvarāy niṟkum** – standing as one among the three. Alternatively it could be construed as – standing as three. Even though he stands between brahmā and rudra to be thought of as one among them, did not knowledgeable people affirm, as mentioned in śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1-2-66 “*sruśti stityantakaraṇīm brahmaviṣṇuśivātmikām* I *sa samjñām yāti bhagavānēka Eva janārdana:*  īī” (it is nārāyaṇa who is also known as janārdana who creates, sustains and annihilates, attaining the names of brahmā, viṣṇu and Siva respectively )!

**mudu puṇarppum** – the ancient incarnation with the name viṣṇu. m*udumai* – ancient; *puṇarppu* – *sankalpa* (will or vow). (Here) It refers to the incarnation which came about out of emperumān’s vow. Just as emperumān had mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 4.6](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/4-6/) “*sambhavāmyātmamāyayā*” (I am born out of the knowledge of my vow), doesn’t his birth take place out of his will rather than out of karma?  Since his birth is not lowly, āḻvār refers to it with the word *puṇarppu* (incarnation out of his vow).

**mudu puṇarppum** – Just as it is mentioned in puruśa sūktam “*pādōsyēhābhavāt puna:*” (one of his incarnations anirudda incarnated himself again as viṣṇu), since the incarnation of viṣṇu is the first incarnation in this world, āḻvār refers to this as *mudupuṇarppu*.

**mūvarāy niṟkum mudu puṇarppum** – just as [parāśara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) had mercifully mentioned in śrī rangarāja stavam 2-5 “*madyē virincagiriśam pratamāvatāra: tat sāmyata: stagayitum tava cēt svarūpam* I *kim tē paratva piśunairapi rangadāman satvapravartana krupāparipālanādyai:*  īī” (Oh śrīranga! If the purpose of taking incarnation as viṣṇu and standing amidst brahmā and rudra is to show equality with them and to hide your basic nature, why did you carry out the deeds of(1) exhibiting your *satva guṇa* (purely auspicious qualities) which manifests your supreme nature and (2) protecting brahmā and rudra in that same incarnation?) Is it not well known that he (viṣṇu) is the incarnation of the supreme puruśa?

Is it that he only remained as viṣṇu, controlling brahmā and rudra as well as their physical forms? āḻvār says next that he also controls all entities which are sentient . . .

**yāvarāy niṟkinṟadu ellām** – controlling the physical forms of dēva (celestial), manuśya (human), tiryak (animal) and stāvara (botanical) species and all the sentients who control these forms. It is implied here that there is no insentient entity which is not controlled by a sentient entity. Has it not been mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 2-17](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/2-17/) “*avināśi tu tat viddi yēna sarvamidam tatam*” (know that those ātmās which control all these insentient entities are indestructible)!

**dēvarāy . . . . ellām nedumāl** – one of the commentators for this prabandam, [śrī appiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/appillai/) construed this to mean that all the sentients such as dēvas who are totally devoted to karma yōga, brahmā and rudra who are considered as primary amongst them, other species such as manuśya (human) etc are all the attributes of emperumān and they are all his servitors.

**yāvarāy niṟkinṟadellām nedumāl enṟu ōrādār** – those who do not know that it is only śrīman nārāyaṇa, who pervades all the entities which are different from himself through sentient entities giving them physical forms and names, incarnates as upēndra and viṣṇu and remains as one among dēvas and as one among the three primary deities.

**niṟkinṟadellām nedumāl** – it is only nārāyaṇa who stands as all entities and who stands amongs dēvas and with brahmā and rudra.

**nedumāl enṟu ōrādār kaṟkinṟadeḷḷam kadai** – it is not only that whatever people learn goes as wasted if they do not know that it is only the supreme being who incarnates amongst dēvas and between brahmā and rudra; it becomes the cause for their continuing to struggle in samsāram. It is similar to what has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam “*vidyā’nyā śilpanaipuṇam*” (all learning other than about emperumān is equivalent to repairing one’s sandals).

We will move on to the 55<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 55. Kadai ninru amarar

*amarar kadai ninṟu – standing at te doorsteps of oter deities nāḷum – everyday kaḻal toḻudu – falling at (teir) feet idai ninṟa inbattar āvar – attaining lovly comforts  (suc as svarga etc) vhic come in te middle (betveen samsāram and mōkśam) pudai ninṟa nīr ōdam mēni nedumālē – ōh lord of all, vho has a divine form similar to te ocean vhic is surrounding (eart) nin adiyai – your divine feet ōda vallār avar yār – is tere anyone vho vill meditate? (tere is none)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**amarar kadai ninṟu – standing at the gates of those deities who are difficult to worship. Those deities are not like emperumān who  is easy to worship with some flower, some water, some fragrance and some lamp \[in other words, emperumān is very easy to worship while those other deities are not\]**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār, finding that no one corrected himself even after his parōpadēśam (instructions to others), turns to emperumān and complains to him “Oh benefactor! In this world where people fall at the feet of other deities for lowly pleasures, who is there to worship your divine feet?” In the 52nd pāsuram of this prabandam, he felt sad seeing samsāris searching for comfort in samsāram (vilaikkātpaduvar). In this pāsuram he feels sad for those who neither engage with the readily available superior comforts in samsāram (vorśipping emperumān at various divine abodes) nor with mōkśam, which even though has to be attained through some means, is a very great goal to attain but instead engage with svarga (heaven) etc which are to be attained for comforts by observing yajña (rituals) etc and falling at the feet of other deities. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: kadai ninṟu amarar kaḻal toḻudu nāḷum idai ninṟa inbattarāvar pudai ninṟa nīrōda mēni nedumālē nin adiyai yārōda vallār avar*

**kaḻal toḻudu nāḷum** – falling at their feet every day. Those deities are not like emperumān who will come running head over heels on hearing the words *gōvindā* as if a great weight has been placed on his chest or seeing the *anjali* (vorśipping with cupped palms) as if it is also a great weight on him.

**kaḻal toḻudu nāḷum** – carrying out deeds at the feet of the other deities, deeds which are to be done at [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s feet without thinking of them as means to attain him but as goals in themselves.

What is the benefit of carrying out such activities?

**idai ninṟa inbattar āvar** – they will attain insignificant, temporary comforts such as svarga etc which are neither like the readily available comforts in samsāram (divine abodes of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) where one can worship him) nor the great mōkśam which is attained through observing certain means. These people will suffer a great deal in attaining even such lowly comforts.

**pudai ninṟa nīrōda mēni nedumālē** –  Oh emperumān who has a divine form similar to the ocean surrounding earth! The implied meaning is that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine form is easy to see since (1) it is neither like the lowly and temporary comforts of samsāram nor invisible like svarga and mōkśa but visible to the followers since emperumān is in [*arcā*](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/p/archavathara-anubhavam.html) [(idol) form](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/p/archavathara-anubhavam.html); (2) like the ocean it removes all types of heat from samsāram and gives great comfort.

**nīrōda mēni nedumālē ninnadiyai yārōdavallār avar** – who is there in this world who praises your divine form, a form which is such great that it is apt to be praised even if that praising does not yield any benefit? Are the samsāris who sell themselves for lowly comforts capable of praising you? ōr, are those who attain lowly goals such as svarga etc capable of praising you?

We will take up the 56<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 56. Avar ivar enrillai

*avar ivar enṟu illai – tere is no distinction betveen knovledgeable and ignorant people anangavēḷ tādaikku – kaṇṇapirān (kriṣṇa), te fater of manmata (cupid) evarum – no one edir illai kaṇḍīr – see, no capability to oppose him uvarikkadal nanjam uṇḍān – śiva, vho svalloved te poison vhic emanated from salty ocean vāṇaṟku – to bāṇāsura kadan enṟu – saying “it is my duty to protect you” udan ninṟu – standing togeter vit him in te battle (against emperumān) orungu tōṟṟān – lost and fled*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**anangavēḷ tādaikku avar ivar enṟillai evarum edirillai kaṇḍīr – Have you seen that there is none, among both the knowledgeable and the ignorant without any distinction, who can be called as comparable to sarvēśvaran who is praised as sākśāt manmata manmata: (he is the most beautiful among all the beautiful persons) and who is the father of manmata who is considered as the most beautiful?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After āḻvār, with tears in his eyes, withdrew from parōpadēśam (instructing others) by telling emperumān “nedumālē ninnadiyai yārōdavallār avar” some devotees of rudra asked him “It is alright that you are praising bhagavān’s greatness. But rudra should be considered on a par with viṣṇu. During the time of amrutamadanam (churning of ocean for nectar) was it not Siva who swallowed the ālahāla poison which would, otherwise, have killed the dēvas, thus protecting them?” āḻvār responds in this pāsuram saying “would you consider as protector someone like Siva who, after having solemnly vowed to bāṇāsura when he approached Siva to protect him, ran away from the battlefield when war broke out with kriṣṇa?” and brings clarity in them. Let us go torugh the pāsuram and its meanings: avar ivar enṟillai anangavēḷ tādaikku evarum edirillai kaṇḍīr uvarik kadal nanjamuṇḍān kadanenṟu vāṇaṟku udaninṟu tōṟṟān orungu*

**anagavēḷ tādaikku evarum edirillai** – just as there is none comparable to emperumān in beauty, have you noticed that there is none comparable to him in maniliness too?

**avar ivar** – the terms *avar* and *ivar* are normally used to refer to someone who is at a distance and who is closeby, respectively. Here, the term *avar* refers to brahmā, rudra et al who are at a great distance compared to the ordinary cētanas (sentient entities) in terms of knowledge and *ivar* refers to normal cētanas. The meaning implied is that just as the normal cētanas cannot be compared to emperumān in terms of *jñāna, śakti* etc (knowledge, ability etc), in the same way brahmā, rudra et al who have abundance of these qualities cannot be compared to emperumān.

**evarum edirillai kaṇḍīr** – rudra, who you consider to be superior, as well as other deities who are under him, cannot match emperumān, have you seen this? The implied opinion here is that in case they have not seen, they can see, even as āḻvār shows them.

When they (the devotees of Siva) ask him “How can you say that there is none? During the churning of ocean for extracting nectar, Siva had consumed the *ālahāla* poison and thus protected the dēvas; vouldn’t he be comparable?” āḻvār mercifully explains further . . . .

**uvarikkadal nanjam uṇḍān** – śāstras say that it was because  of emperumān’s significant presence at that time (as *antaryāmi* of Siva), that Siva was able to consume that poison.

**kadan enṟu vāṇaṟku** – in front of all the people in the court assembly, Siva had solemnly told bāṇāsura  “protecting you is my duty”

**udan ninṟu** – standing together with bāṇāsura and engaging in battle against emperumān.

**udan ninṟu tōṟṟān** –  Siva lost the war, in the presence of bāṇāsura as the witness.

**orungu tōṟṟān** – he lost without any distinction between himself and bāṇāsura. Just as mentioned by tirumaḻisai āḻvār himself in tiruccanda viruttam 53 “*mōdiyōdu ilaccaiyāya sābameydi mukkaṇān kūdu sēnai makkaḷōdu koṇḍu maṇḍi venjamattu ōda*” (rudra ,who had a curse which vil make him feel ashamed, along with kāḷi (durgā), his children and the huge army, ran away from the battlefield) and in pāsuram 71 of tiruccanda viruttam “*muṇḍan nīṟan makkaḷ veppumōdi angi ōdida*” (mundan nīṟan – Siva; veppu – fever; mōdi – kāḷi; angi – agni (deity for fire); all these deities ran away from the battlefield) and as mentioned by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in  [tiruvāimoḻi 3-10-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/11/thiruvaimozhi-3-10-4-parivinri/) “*parivinṟi vāṇanaikkāttumenṟu anṟu padiyodum vanderinda tiripuram seṟṟavanum maganum pinnum angiyum pōr tolaiya* ” (putting Siva, who had burnt the three towns and who had taken a vow that he would protect bāṇāsura  without any effort, along with his son āṟumugan (skanda), and agni to shame in the battlefiled), Siva lost in the battle with emperumān, along with his family members.

We will take up the 57<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 57. Orungirunda nalvinaiyum

*pēsil – if one vere to talk te trut peru kurundam sāyttavanē – ōh kaṇṇapirān vho uprooted te kurunda tree (a type of  vildlime)! orungirunda nalvinaiyum tīvinaiyum āvān – he is te executor for te puṇya (virtues) and pāpa (vices) vhic are vit jīvas (souls). marungu irunda – staying close-by vānavardām – celestial entities dānavardām – demonic entities tāragaidān – eart en nenjam ānavardām – te sarvēśvaran vho stays in my heart allādadu en – vhat entity is tere vitout tat sarvēśvaran?*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**pēsil – if one were to talk with vēda vākyams (verses from sacred texts) such as “brahmā nārāyaṇa:  śivaśca nārāyaṇa:”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Starting with the first pāsuram, until the previous pāsuram those who had spoken of supremacy to brahmā and rudra as well as those who said that these two entities are equal to emperumān were rejected. People who argued that all the three deities (brahmā, viṣṇu and rudra) are one and the same, now started the argument, giving the following strategy: vēdas (nārāyaṇōpaniśat etc ) say “sa brahmā sa Siva:” and “brahmā nārāyaṇa: śivaśca nārāyaṇa:” (brahmā is nārāyaṇa; Siva is nārāyaṇa). Hence brahmā, rudra and nārāyaṇa are the same entity. When such is the case, arguing whether this entity is superior or that entity is superior is against vēdas. āḻvār rubbishes this argument in this pāsuram. He tells them “you will go to horrible ways! Along with “brahmā nārāyaṇa: śivaśca nārāyaṇa” there are also other verses such as “śakraśca nārāyaṇa:, kālaśca nārāyaṇa:, diśaśca nārāyaṇa:, vidiśaśca nārāyaṇa:, ūrdvanca nārāyaṇa:, adaśca nārāyaṇa:, antarbahiśca nārāyaṇa:, nārāyaṇa ēvēdam sarvam yadbhūtam yacca bhavyam” (indra is nārāyaṇa, time is nārāyaṇa, directions are nārāyaṇa, intermediate regions are nārāyaṇa, above (upper world) is nārāyaṇa, below (lower world) is nārāyaṇa, inside and outside are nārāyaṇa, what was in the past, what is there now and what will be are all nārāyaṇa ) which will tell you that apart from sentient entities such as indra et al, insentient entities such as directions, time etc are also referred to in the same breath. Will you then bring about equality between the supreme entity and insentient entity? Understand that all these entities are emperumān’s wealth, physical body, effect, being sustained, being controlled, being servitors, being attribute etc. Thus, one can say that everything is him and in the same breath, vēdas say that brahmā and rudra are also him. Is there any entity which does not have him \[as indvelling soul\]? Thus, with the help of vēdas, āḻvār rejects those who try to establish trimūrti (oneness among three forms) philosophy. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: orungirunda nalvinaiyum tīvinaiyum āvān perungurundam sāyttavanē pēsil marungirunda vānavardām dānavardām tāragaidān ennenjam ānavardān allādaden*

**orungirunda nalvinaiyum tīvinaiyum āvān** – he himself is the pāpa and puṇya which are always with us. Just as the vēda vākyam says “*ēśa Eva sādu karma kārayati* I *ēśa Eva asādu karma kārayati* īī” (this *paramapuruśa* (supreme being) makes the jīva to carry out good deeds; this *paramapuruśa* instigates the jīva to carry out bad deeds too), since it is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who executes the virtues and vices, he is mentioned in the vēdas as virtues and vices themselves. Is it correct, because of this, to equate karmas, which exist for a transient time, with him?

**orungirunda nalvainaiyum tīvinaiyum** – just as it is mentioned in brahmavaivarttam prakruti kaṇḍam 26-90 “*avaśyanubhōktavyam krutam karma śubhāśubham*  I *nā bhuktam kśīyatē karma kalpakōtiśatairapi* īī”(the \[results of\] good and deed bad deeds carried out (by jīvātmās) have to be necessarily experienced. Even if it takes hundreds of crores of kalpams (a very long period related to the life cycle of brahmā), these cannot be got rid of unless experienced), the puṇya and pāpa that we earn will not leave us unless we experience them compulsorily.

**perungurundam sāittavanē nalvinaiyum tīvinaiyum āvān** – when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) incarnated as kriṣṇa, kamsa had set upon kriṣṇa many demons in order to kill him. One of them entered a kurundam tree on which kriṣṇa used to climb often. kriṣṇa broke that tree, killing that demon, thus eliminating one of the enemies of the herdsmen. Just as he effortlessly eliminated the enemy of herdsmen, emperumān will remove our enemies too (our sins) through his vow as he had mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/18-66/) “*sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi*”. Since karma is controlled by him, he is referred to as virtuous and viceful deeds.

Is this all?

**marungirunda vānavardām** – ([āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) tells samsāris that) since brahmā, rudra et al are at a far away distance, there may be a bewilderment in your minds that they are one with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). But in śāstras, aren’t celestial entities such as indra, agni (deity for fire), vāyu (deity for wind), sūrya (sun) et al, who are [cētanas](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) like us and are near us, also mentioned as emperumān? Do not vēdams say “*śakraśca nārāyaṇa*” “*tatēvāgnis tad vāyu: tad sūryas tadu candramā:*” (that brahmam itself is agni, that itself is vāyu, that itself is sūrya and that itself is candra (moon)). Can we say *svarūpaikyam* (oneness with emperumān) for these celestial entities?

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) further mercifully says, when told “what if we say that they have *svarūpaikyam*?”

**dānavar tām** – isn’t it said that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is also the sinful demons? Can we say oneness for these demons with him?

When told “What if we say that there is *svarūpaikyam* between emperumān and cētanas (sentient entities)?”  āḻvār responds saying . . . .

**tāragaidān** – even insentient entity earth is nārāyaṇa. Do not vēdas proclaim that insentient entities are also nārāyaṇa as in “*diśaśca nārāyaṇa: vidiśaśca nārāyaṇa: nārāyaṇa ēvēdam sarvam* ” (directions are nārāyaṇa, intermediate regions are nārāyaṇa, all these are nārāyaṇa)! Can one say oneness for [acētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/) (insentient) entities which are full of faults with the supreme entity who is the opposite of any fault?

Who is that entity who is all of these?

**en nenjam ānavardān** – just as it is mentioned in subālōpaniśat “*manaśca nārāyaṇa*” (mind is nārāyaṇa), [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that the one who is in his heart is all of these too.

Is this all? āḻvār further clarifies . . .

**allādaden** – what entity is there among those mentioned in śāstras which is not nārāyaṇa? vēdas say “*nārāyaṇa idam sarvam*” (nārāyaṇa is all these), “*sarvam kalvidam brahma tajjalān*” (since all these (entities) appear, live and fade away in brahmam, these are all brahmam only). Thus, if the argument that brahmā, rudra et al are to be considered as one with nārāyaṇa is to be accepted as valid, then *svarūpaikyam* (oneness in form) has to be accepted for all sentient and insentient entities which is totally faulty. Since all entities such as brahmā, rudra et al cannot sustain if separated from him and sustain themselves due to him, we have to consider that everything is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself. Thus the opinion is that concepts such as *ātmaikyam, dēvaikyam, trimūrtyaikyam* (oneness with all sentient entities, oneness with all celestial entities, oneness with the three mūrtis) will not be valid.

We will take up the 58<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 58. Ennenjameyan

*en nenjamēyān – one vho resides permanently in my heart iruḷ nīkki empirān – one vho bestoved a favour on me by removing te darkness of ignorance (from my heart) man anja – for te kings to be terrified mun oru nāḷ – once upon a time maṇ aḷandān – measuring te eart en nenjam mēyānai – emperumān vho(vit tat divine form) does not ever leave my heart illā – one vho did not take emperumān in te heart vidai ēṟṟān – rudra, vho has bull as his vehicle vem vinai – te huge sin tīttu – making it disappear āyānukku – emperumān vho existed (due to tat) anbu ākkinēn – created affection (in te vorld)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**en nenjamēyān – emperumān remained aptly in my heart. After attaining āḻvār’s heart, even paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) was not apt for him. The same meaning has been conveyed  by several āḻvārs, including this āḻvār who has sung in tiruccandaviruttam 65 “niṟpadum ōr veṟpagattu” (emperumān stood in the distinguished tiruvēngadamalai ) and in tiruccandaviruttam 64 “ninṟadendai ūragattu” (stood in tiru ūragam), and by nammāḻvār in periya tiruvandādi 68 “kallum kanaikadalum vaikundavānādum pullenṟoḻindanakol ēpāvam vella nediyān niṟam kariyān uḷpugundu nīngān adiyēnaduḷḷattagam” (emperumān left tiruvēngadam, tiruppāṟkadal and śrīvaikuṇṭam due to which these places lost their grandeour and grass started sprouting in these places due to want of attention. emperumān, who is tall and dark, entered my mind and will never leave it) and in tiruvāimoḻi 3-8-2 “nenjamē neeḷnagarāga irunda en tanjanē” (Oh emperumān, my refuge, who remained in my heart, considering it as a huge town) and by periyāḻvār in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-4-9 “panikkadalil paḷḷigōḷaippaḻagavittu ōdivanden manakkadalil vāḻavalla māyamaṇāḷa nambi”(Oh the consort of śrī mahālakśmi , who is with amazing activities and who is  complete in all aspects! You left your mattress in tiruppāṟkadal and resided in the ocean of my mind).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After āḻvār chastised those who were arguing for oneness between emperumān and other deities, they became followers of emperumān. Looking at this, āḻvār is elated and says “to emperumān who granted me benefits in many ways, I made the world to show its affection”. With the previous pāsuram, the parōpadēśam (instructing others) of this āḻvār has ended. The pāsurams from here onvards will reflect āḻvār’s experience, his instruction to those who have corrected themselves and have become followers of emperumān, on how to spend their time purposefully and his dejection on seeing how some people are still losing this experience. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: ennenjamēyān iruḷ nīkki empirān mannanja munnorunāḷ maṇṇaḷandān ennenja mēyānai illā vidai ēṟṟān vevvinai tīrttu āyānukkākkinēn anbu Word for Word Meanings*

What is the reason for taking roots in [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/)’s heart?

**iruḷ nīkki** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had taken residence in āḻvār’s heart in order to remove the darkness of  ignorance, initially in āḻvār’s mind and later on, from the minds of other people.

**ennenjamēyān iruḷ nīkki** – if he stays in paramapadam where there is no darkness, he cannot remove any darkness. Hence he decided to enter my heart which was full of darkness and got the famous name of *iruḷnīkki* (one who removes darkness).

**iruḷ nīkki empirān** – he favoured me by removing my ignorance and ignorance of other people.

Who is this entity? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) explains further . . . .

**man anja munnorunāḷ maṇṇaḷandān** – it was [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who measured the worlds with his feet when the egoistic kings on earth were terrified looking at the status of mahābali who was ruling all the three worlds.

**iruḷnīkki empirān maṇṇaḷandān** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that the purpose of emperumān’s measuring the worlds was only to occupy āḻvār’s mind and remove his ignorance.

**maṇṇaḷandān en nenjamēyānai** – since [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) was not satisfied even after measuring and seizing the three worlds, he entered the mind of āḻvār with lot of affection.

**en nenjamēyānai illā vidai ēṟṟān** – Siva, who has bull as his vehicle, did not have [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in his heart. On a few occasions when his *satva guṇa* (purely good quality) is at its peak, Siva will tell emperumān “*ahamapi tē dāsa:*” (I too am your servitor) but otherwise he will oppose emperumān, being prominent with tamas and rajas guṇa (passion and ignorance).

**vidai ēṟṟān vevvinai tirittu** – even though Siva did not take emperumān in his heart due to his bad qualities, when he was cursed by brahmā and started roaming all over the world to get rid of the curse, emperumān took pity on him and removed his curse.

**vevvinai tīrttu āyānukku** – did not emperumān think that he had lost his existence when Siva roamed all over the place to get rid of the brahmakapāla (severed cranium of brahmā) and that he regained his existence only after removing the curse with his own hands by filling up that cranium! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) wonders at [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s simplicity.

**āyānukku ākkinēn anbu** – For such an entity who became a benefactor even for one who opposed him, apart from rendering him affection, I made the others in the world to render affection. īsn’t keeping me in such a position the greatest benefit that emperumān endowed on me!

We will move on to the 59<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
