## 59. Anbavay

*anbu āvāy – one vho is te epitome of affection (tovards me)! ār amudam āvāy – ōne vho is like complete nectar! adiyēnukku inbu āvāy – one vho created for me (te experiencing of your) enjoyment! ellāmum nī āvāy – one vho is all oter enjoyment for me! pon pāvai kēḷvā – ōh te consort of mahālakśmi! kiḷar oḷi – (due to te union vit mahālakśmi) having radiant splendour en kēsavanē – ōh emperumān (my ḷord)! kēdu inṟi – vitout any fault āḷvāykku – for you vho vould take in a servitor adiyēn nān – ī, te servitor, āḷ – am a servitor*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**anbāvāy – you are the epitome of affection. Starting from “nānmuganai” (1st pāsuram), āḻvār had been emphasising on emperumān’s supremacy. Once he starts expressing his experience, he concludes that affection is emperumān’s form. This is similar to those who said “nārāyaṇanē namakkē paṟai taruvān” (he will provide us with kainkaryam towards him) in the beginning, called him as “mālē” (one who is affectionate) when they approached him. Alternatively, this could be construed as āḻvār who told earlier ākkinēn anbu (making his as well as others’ affection towards emperumān) realises that it is emperumān who created affection in him, and hence calls him ennudaiya anbāgavum ānavanē (one who became by affection). Thus this could be interpreted as āḻvār saying (1) one who created your love towards me and (2) one who created affection in me towards you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*acyutan (emperumān), seeing the affection of āḻvār who said ākkinēn anbu, was readying to serve āḻvār by massaging his hands and legs. āḻvār indicates to him in this pāsuram, the proper order. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: anbāvāy āramudamāvāy adiyēnukku inbāvāy ellāmum nīyāvāy pon pāvai kēḷvā kiḷaroḷi en kēsavanē kēdinṟi āḷvāyk kadiyēn nān āḷ*

**āramudamāvāy** – you are the supremely enjoyable entity for me. As epitome of affection, only you are the most complete nectar for me. Apart from creating affection in me, you offered me yourself as an outlet for that affection.

**Ar amudamāvāy** – Complete nectar. This is nectar which does not have any admixture with salt, which was earlier given to the celestial entities!

**adiyēnukku inbāvāy** – For me, who is your servitor and a unique entity of enjoyment for you, you offered me the scope of enjoying *brahmānandam* (supreme bliss), which is enjoyment in your matter. The word *inbu* refers to the supreme bliss of enjoying [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**ellāmum nī āvāy** – this refers to emperumān granting *nityamuktānubhavam* (enjoyment of nityasūris and muktars) and *ubhayavibhūti anubhavam* (enjoyment of both spiritual realm and materialistic realm). Just as [śrī bhagavat gītā ślōkam 7.19](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-19/) says “*vāsudēvas sarvam*” (everything is vāsudēvan) and [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)’s [tiruvāimoḻi pāsuram 6-7-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/03/thiruvaimozhi-6-7-1-unnum-soru/) “*uṇṇum sōṟu parugu nīr tinnum veṟṟilaiyum ellām kaṇṇan*” it is emperumān who is the sustaining, nourishing and enjoying entities for āḻvār.

**pon pāvaik kēḷvā** – Oh the consort of lakhsmi who is of golden complexion! The implied meaning here is that emperumān bestowed all the above because of the proximity of [mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/).

**anbāvāy – pon pāvai kēḷvā** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who normally says as in [śrī bhagavat gītā 16.19](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/16-19/) “*kśipāmi*” (I push them (into demonic births)) and as in varāha purāṇam “*na kśamāmi*” (I do not forgive them), and who is hot similar to fire, became the epitome of affection due to connection with mahālakśmi, just as hot water with cold water. Did not nammāḻvār too mercifully say in tiruvāimoḻi 10-10-7 “*kōlamalarpāvaikku anbāgiya en anbēyō*” (Oh one, who is the love of mahālakśmi who sits on a beautiful flower,  who became affection personified for me!)

**āramudamāvāy  – pon pāvai kēḷvā** – just as [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) says in periya tirumoḻi 6-1-2 “*amudil varum peṇṇamudu uṇḍa emperumānē*”  (Oh my emperumān who took the nectar of woman who came from the nectar of tiruppāṟkadal!) emperumān became āramudu (complete nectar) after consuming [mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) who is the epitome of the essence of nectar.

**adiyēnukku inbāvāy – pon pāvai kēḷvā** – it is her nearness which gives him his own experience. Hasn’t nammāḻvār too mercifully declared in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-6-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/08/thiruvaimozhi-1-6-9-tharuma-arum/) “*taruma varum payanāya tirumagaḷār kēḷvan*” ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) bestows the supreme benefit as the consort of periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi))!

**adiyēnukku inbāvāy – pon pāvai kēḷvā** – it won’t appear to be appealing if given by brahmacāri (bachelor) nārāyaṇa! The meaning conveyed here is that emperumān is sweet to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) just as he is to periya pirāttiyār. nammāḻvār too mercifully mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-5-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/thiruvaimozhi-4-5-8-namakkum/) “*namakkum pūvinmisai nangaikkum inbanai*”(the entity who is sweet to us and to śrī mahālakśmi who resides on a flower).

**ellāmum nīyāvāy – pon pāvai kēḷvā** – āḻvār implies that emperumān’s ability to sustain, nurture and to be an enjoyable entity for us is due to her connection. *pāvai* – the best among women as mentioned in the saying “*nārīṇām uttamāvadū:*” *kēḷvan* – one who is sweet to her.

**pon pāvai kēḷvā kiḷar oḷi** – due to his union with periya pirātti, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) attained resplendent radiance. vēdas said “*śraddayā dēvō dēvatvamaśnutē* ” (due to pirātti, the supreme entity attains radiance). vālmīki said in śrī rāmāyaṇam “*atīva rāma: śuśubhē’dikāmayā vibhu: śriyā viṣṇuriva* ” (just as the all-pervading viṣṇu is manifested by śrīdēvi, śrī rāma is manifested by pirātti). [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/)’ pāsurams also bring out the same meaning, such as in [tiruppallāṇḍu 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu-2-adiyomodum/) “*vadivāy nin valamārbinil vāḻginṟa mangai*” and tiruvāimoḻi 10-6-9  “*tigaḻginṟa tirumārbil tirumangai tannōdum tigaḻginṟa tirumālār*”.

**kiḷar oḷi en kēsavanē** – attaining radiance from śrī mahālakśmi and being my Lord. One who has the bunch of locks which she likes and which is mentioned by vēdam as “*hiraṇya kēśa*” (one with golden coloured locks). *kēsavan* ***–*** one who has excellent locks – the locks which are praised by vēdas and by those who follow vēdas.

**kēdinṟi āḷvāykku adiyēn nān āḷ** – one who makes me as a servitor without any hurdle for kainkaryam (service) and experience. adiyēn, who is a servitor, has to carry out service. The implied meaning is that you do not have to massage my limbs and carry out service (to me).

Wouldn’t *punarukti* (the fault of repeating a word) be mentioned for *adiyēn* and *āḷ* since both refer to the same person ([āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/))? The word *adiyēn* refers to *śēśatvasvarūpam* (the basic nature of being a servitor) while *āḷ* refers to being the entity for whom servitorśip is the refuge. Hence there is no fault of repetition. Is it required of him to indicate this to the omnisicient emperumān? āḻvār is indicating to him that it is not proper on the part of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is the epitome of love, to bring himself to a lowly level and carry out service to him \[āḻvār\].

We will take up the 60<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 60. Al parttu

*kētpārkku – for tose vho vould like to knov (vit teir ovn efforts) aru poruḷāy ninṟa aranganē – ōh tiruvarangā vho became impossible to knov supreme entity! āḷ pārttu uḻi taruvāy – one vho goes searcing “vill ī get anyone vho vill be under my control?” nin tāḷ pārttu uḻi taruvēn tanmaiyai – my basic nature of seeking out your divine feet enṟum kaṇḍukoḷ – you śould mercifully śover your glance on me so tat tis lasts forever unnai virumbuvadē – my nature of desiring you manam – in my heart viḷḷēn – ī am unable to avoid*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**ātpārttu uḻitaruvāy – Oh one who is going out, searching “Is there anyone who is willing to be my servitor?”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells emperumān to ensure that he (āḻvār) retains the quality of being a servitor always. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: āḷ pārttu uḻitaruvāy kaṇḍukoḷ enṟu nin tāḷ pārttu uḻitaruvēn tanmaiyai kētpārkku arum poruḷāy ninṟa aranganē unnai virumbuvadē viḷḷēn manam*

**kaṇḍukoḷ enṟum tātpārttu uḻi taruvēn tanmaiyai** – make sure that my quality of moving around with the desire of attaining your divine feet is always present. The duty of stabilising my quality of being under your control is yours.

**kētpārkku arum poruḷāy** – For those who desire to attain through their efforts of listening (to śāstrams), memorising them and meditating on them, you are impossible to attain, except through your mercy. The word *kētparkku* referring to listening is applicable equally to memorising and meditating. Has it not been said in kata upaniśat 3-23 “*nāyamātmā pravacanēna labhyō na mēdayā na bahunā śrutēna*” (he is difficult to attain through different modes of listening, memorising and meditating)!

**ninṟa aranganē** – Just as it is mentioned in kata upaniśats 3-23 “*yamēvaiśa vruṇutē tēna labhyas tasyaiśa ātmā vivruṇutē tanūm svām*” (whoever this supreme entity desires, such person attains this supreme entity through him. This supreme entity shows such person his divine form), oh tiruvarangā, you have come and reclined here in order to become simple to those of your devotees to whom you wish to show yourself!

**unnai virumbuvadē viḷḷēn manam** – Even if I do, my mind will not stop desiring you who would go in search of servitors. The opinion here is that you should ensure that I always remain with this quality \[of desiring to be your servitor\].

We will move on to the 61<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 61. Manakkedam sara

*madusūdan tannai – emperumān vho killed te demon madu tanakkē tanjam ā tān koḷḷil – if one takes(emperumān) as his refuge manak kēdam – troubles of te mind sārā – vill not reac onṟi ninṟu – (vitout letting go) standing firmly ēḻ ulagai – in all te seven vorlds āṇai ōttinān – emperumān vho rules vit his sceptre inṟu – today tān – he himself senṟu – coming (to me) onṟi ninṟa tiru – te vealt vhic is fitting (in me) enakkē – is only for me and for no one else.*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**manakkēdam – if we consider this as a derivative of the Sanskrit word mana: kēda:, it will mean sorrows of the mind. Alternatively, this word can be split into manattukku ēdam which will mean faults of the mind such as ignorance etc.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Once āḻvār mercifully said “virumbuvadē viḷḷēn manam” emperumān established himself firmly in āḻvār’s mind saying “even if you and your mind forget me, I will not” just as nammāḻvār said mercifully in tiruvāimoḻi 1-10-10 “maṟappanenṟu sendāmaraikkaṇṇodu maṟappaṟa ennuḷḷē manninān” (thinking that I will forget, emperumān, with his lotus-like eyes, came and established himself firmly in my mind so that I will not forget). Looking at this, āḻvār is elated saying “if others think of emperumān, the supreme being will merely remove the sorrows of their minds. However in my case, he establishes firmly in my mind, without any cause, falling head over heels, like a firmly rooted plant. No one else has this wealth”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: manakkēdam sārā madusūdan tannai tanakēdān tanjamākkoḷḷil enakkēdān inṟonṟi ninṟulagai ēḻāṇai ōttinān senṟonṟi ninṟa tiru*

**manakkēdam sārā madusūdan tannait tanakkē tān tanjamāk koḷḷil** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had killed the demons madu and kaitabha when they stole the vēdas and spread ignorance in the world, and protected brahmā, rudra et al. If one takes such emperumān as the means, emperumān will destroy ignorance in his mind just as he destroyed madu / kaitabha and shower his grace on him.

**tanakkē tān tanjamāk koḷḷil** – this implies that this is the status of *svagata svīkāra niśtā* – one who holds on to emperumān on his own efforts. Those who surrender are of two types – those who are *svagata svīkāra niśtā* \[just now explained\] and *paragata* *svīkāra niśtā* – one who looks up to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to enable him to hold on to emperumān, thinking “the owner will hold on to his possession when he wants”.

Thus [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) spoke of the status of *svagata svīkāra niśta* in the first two lines. In the next two lines, āḻvār speaks about the way emperumān distinctly treats *paragata svīkāra niśtā* – himself.

**enakkēdān inṟonṟi ninṟulagai ēḻāṇai ōttināṇ senṟonṟi ninṟa tiru** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is referred to as one who pervades all the worlds both inside and outside, as mentioned in nārāyaṇa sūktam “*antarbahiśca tatsarvam nārāyaṇastita:”* āḻvār says that he got the great wealth of such emperumān who is like a well fitting great emperor of all the worlds, who came on his own into this mind and stayed firmly.

**enakkēdān** – the affection which emperumān has towards those who look up to him is many times more than the affection that emperumān has towards those who seek him out on their own. Thus his affection towards herdsmen, herdsvomen, herdsgirls, cows, calves is ten times deeper than with others. This is the greatest treasure which is hidden in such pramāṇams (authentic sources of information) such as [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/)’ pāsurams “*tivattilum pasunirai mēyppu uvatti*” (you are more joyous tending to cows than you are in paramapadam), “*kanṟu mēyttu iniduganda*” (one who is the most joyous tending to calves), “*kāli mēykka vallān*” (one who is strong in grazing cattle), “*kanṟu mēyttu viḷaiyāda vallān*” (one who is strong in grazing and playing with calves) etc.

This pāsuram explains clearly the meaning of the *vēda vākyam* (saying of vēda) “*prajāpatis tvam vēda* I *prajāpatim tvō vēda* I *yam prajāpatir vēda sa puṇyō bhavati*” (supreme being attains one (on his own). One attains supreme being, seeking him out, on his own. Of these, whoever supreme being attains on his own, is the best).

We will move on to the 62<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 62. Tiru ninra pakkam

*(due to tat) sirīdaran tan – emperumān vho got te divine name of śrīdara vaṇḍu ulavu taṇ tuḻāy tār tannai – te cool divine tuḷasi garland tovards vhic beetles come sūdi – donning it (on te head) tarittu – getting to exist because of tat tiru ninṟa pakkam tiṟavidu enṟu ōrār – tose vho could not ascertain tat nārāyaṇa, vho has pirātti aptly fitting, is te supreme being karu ninṟa – brahmā, rudra et al vho also stayed in a vomb (just like temselves) kallārkku uraippar – vill instruct fools  (tat brahmā, rudra et al are supreme beings)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**tiru ninṟa pakkam tiṟavidu – without any restriction on the type of entity, whichever entity that pirātti chooses to manifest herself in, becomes the supreme entity. Did not vēdas also come to the same conclusion as mentioned in the ślōkam “śraddayā dēvō dēvatvam aśnutē” (due to pirātti, emperumān attains the quality of being dēva)! parāśara bhattar has also mercifully mentioned in śrī guṇaratna kōśam “apāngā bhūyāmsō yadupari param brahma tadabhūt ” (the entity on which pirātti’s grace fell became parabrahmam (supreme entity)), and “vēdāntās tatvacintām murabhiduraśi yatpādacihnais taranti ” (on looking at whose chest on which the footprints of pirātti’s divine feet have been implanted, the vēdāntas concluded their debate on supremacy)!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After becoming happy with his eminence, āḻvār saw the lowliness of others. In this pāsuram, he feels sad that the reason for this is their bewilderment, as well as making others to be bewildered, with other deities who are born from a womb just as they themselves were, and still thinking of them as supreme beings. The identity for śrīvaiṣṇava is the inability to tolerate sorrows of others. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: tiru ninṟa pakkam tiṟavidenṟu ōrār karu ninṟa kallārkku uraippar tiruvirunda mārvan sirīdaran than vaṇḍulavu taṇḍuḻāy tār tannaic cūdit tarittu tiru irunda mārvan – having divine chest in which periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) has taken residence*

**tiṟavidenṟu** – The word *tiṟavidu* means having all entities under its control and not comprehendable by anyone unless it reveals itself.

**ōrār** – if people had the knowledge, they would have known that ‘this entity is like this’. But they do not have that knowledge! There is no sign that they have knowledge.

**karu ninṟa** – dēvatās such as rudra et al, who are also born like samsāris, out of a womb. ḍidn’t vēda, while speaking about the birth of rudra, say “*uśasi rētō’sinjat*” (brahmā deposited his semen with the deity of dawn), confirming that rudra too stayed in a womb and was born?

**karu ninṟa kallārkku uraippar** – these samsāris will instruct other samsāris, who are as foolish as they are, without any knowledge about śāstras, that these deities (born out of a womb) are supreme entities.

**tiruvirunda mārvan sirīdaran** – there is a worm which does not have any connection with mango fruit; however it is called as *māmbaḻa uṇṇi* (mango-worm). It is in the same way that devotees of other deities, who do not have any connection with pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) call their deities with the prefix śrīmān. In the case of the *paramapuruśa* (supreme being) on whose chest pirātti has taken residence saying as in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/06/thiruvaimozhi-6-10-10-agalagillen-part-1/) “*agalagillēn iṟaiyumenṟu*” (I am not capable of separating from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) even for a moment), the name *śrīdaran* is a very apt name. It is only to show emperumān’s unique greatness that sage vyāsa brought out in 12 divine names the connection with pirātti as mentioned in śrī viṣṇu sahasranāmam  608-619 “*śrīvatsavakśā: śrīvāsa: śrīpati: śrīmatām vara: śrīda: śrīśa: śrīnivāsa: śrīnidi: śrīvibhāvana: śrīdara: śrīkara: śrēya: śrīmān*” (one who has the divine mole on his chest; the dwelling place of lakshmi; the consort of lakshmi; the head of those blessed by lakshmi; one who grants wealth; wealth of lakshmi; husband of lakshmi; treasure of lakshmi; one who praises lakshmi; one who sustains lakshmi; one who is born with lakshmi and one who has lakshmi). There are some texts which give this verse as **tiruvirunda mārbil sirīdaran**. The meaning is identical.

**sirīdaran than vaṇḍulavu taṇ tuḻāyt tār tannaic cūdittarittu** – the garland of tuḷasi which sirīdaran (consort of lakshmi) is wearing is dripping with honey, to seek which beetles throng the garland. Followers of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will wear the divine garland which has been removed from emperumān, in line with periyāḻvār’s [tiruppallāṇḍu pāsuram 9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/11/thiruppallandu-9-uduththuk-kalaintha/) “*sūdik kaḷaindana sūdum ittoṇḍargaḷōm*” (these servitors who would wear the garland, clothes etc which have been removed from emperumān). They will realise their existence once they wear this garland.

We will go on to the 63<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 63. Tarittirunden

*tāragaṇam –  group of stars’ pōr – (teir) movement virittu uraitta – one vho let it be knovn videly (trough te śāstram of astrolojñ) vem nāgattu unnai – you (emperumān) vho are te antarātmā (indvelling soul) of tiruvananantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) vho is very fiery for enemies terittu – meditating upon eḻudi – vriting about vāsittum – reading (vhat had been vritten) kēttum – hearing (from many people) vaṇangi – boving dovn vaḻipattum – carrying out tiruvārādanam (elaborate vorśip) pūsittum – vorśipping pōdu pōkkinēn – ī am spending my time āgavē – since ī did like tis tarittirundēn – ī vas existing*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**tāragaṇappōr viritu uraitta vem nāgattu unnai – you tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) who explained elaborately on the movement of groups of starts with the help of astrology. Here the term unnai (you) refers to emperumān who is the indvelling soul of ādiśēśan.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, looking at the lowly state of others, āḻvār felt very sad. In this pāsuram, āḻvār redeems his heart and is overjoyed at the benefit that he had obtained, just as nammāḻvār had mentioned mercifully in periya tiruvandādi pāsuram 25 “ārānum ādānum seyya agalidattai ārāyndu adu tiruttal āvadē” (let others do whatever they want. Is it possible for me to analyse what everyone is doing on this expansive earth and try to correct them?) Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: tarittirundēnāgavē tārā gaṇappōr viritturaitta vennāgattu unnai teritteḻudi vāsittum kēttum vaṇangi vaḻipattum pūsittum pōkkinēn pōdu*

**terittu** – knowing; understanding; meditating. Here, meditating  is the most apt meaning since it gels with the words which follow.

**terittu** – initially meditating with the mind

**eḻudi** – writing what has been meditated upon, through books

**vāsittum** – reading what has been written by self as well as by others.

**kēttum** – hearing what has been told by great personalities such as mudal āḻvārgaḷ (who had preceded this āḻvār); also instructing disciples and making them to hear.

**vaṇangi** – without having any pride thinking “I am so knowledgeable”, thinking that this is also due to his grace, bowing down to his divine feet, without any possessiveness.

**vaḻipattum pūsittum** – vorśipping him through the three faculties of mind, speech and body. Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) has used two words with similar meanings, āḻvār hints that vorśipping [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is his prime method of spending time.

**pōkkinēn pōdu** – there is nothing further to be done for uplifting the ātmā (soul). Others have been corrected to the extent possible. Now all that remains to be done for the time to be spent here is to engage in useful way. I did that by following the above, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/).

**āgavē tarittirundēn** – Thus, despite living in this cruel samsāram, I was able to exist. Alternatively, if asked as to the purpose for which he did this, āḻvār would say, for the purpose of existing.

We will take up the 64<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 64. Podana vittirainji

*pon magaram kādānai – one vho is donning beautiful earrings in te śape of fiś ādi – te causative entity for te vorld perumānai – one vit greatness nādānai – being te lord (of all) nallānai – having te quality of vātsalyam (moterly love) nāraṇanai – one vho is famously knovn as nārāyaṇa nam ēḻ piṟappu aṟukkum sollānai – about te puruśōttama (best among all sentient entities) vhose divine names vill sever our connection vit repeated birts pōdu āna ittu iṟainji ēttuminō – offering anyting vhic goes in te name of flover, boving dovn to him and praising him solluvadē – reciting (his divine names vholeheartedly) sūdu – apt activity for us*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**pōdāna ittu iṟainji ēttuminō – offer whatever goes in the name of flower at his divine feet; bow down to him with your mind and praise him with your mouth. By using the word pōdāna āḻvār is hinting that if offered with devotion, there is no flower which is unacceptable to emperumān. kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) had mercifully said in śrī bhagavat gītā 9.26 “patram puśpam ….. bhaktyupahrutam aśnāmi ” (I accept with affection, whatever leaf or flower has been offered to me with devotion). tirumangai āḻvār too considered that there is no flower which is unacceptable to emperumān in his pāsuram “kaḷḷār tuḻāyum kaṇavalarum kūviḷaiyum muḷḷār muḷariyum āmbalum …. puḷḷāy ōrēnamumāy pukkidandān ponnadikku “. nammāḻvār also mercifully mentioned in his tiruvāimoḻi 1.6.1 “purivadum pugai pūvē”. parāśara bhattar who was one day giving a discourse on this subject, mentioned that even if trash is burnt and smoke is generated, emperumān will accept that; even if kaṇḍagālippū (a type of flower) is offered,he will accept that. nanjīyar, his disciple, interjected and said that śāstras do not accept kaṇḍagālippū “na kaṇḍakārikā puśpam dēvāya vinivēdayēt” to which bhattar responded “it is not that śāstram had rejected kaṇḍagālippū. It is only because the thorns in the plant will pierce the hands of his devotees that this has been avoided”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mercifully mentioned about how he spent his time purposefully. In this pāsuram he instructs others the way in which they can spend their time purposefully. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: pōdāna vittiṟainji ēttuminō ponmagarak kādānai ādipperumānai nādānai nallānai nāraṇanai nammēḻ piṟappaṟūkkum sollānaic colluvadē sūdu*

**ittu iṟainji ēttuminō** – by using the word *ittu* the service through physical faculty has been stressed; *iṟainji* refers to service through the faculty of mind and *ēttuminō* refers to service through the faculty of mouth.

**pon magarak kādānai** – one who has divine ears which are decorated with desirable golden ear-rings in the shape of fish. āḻvār used the word *poṟ kādānai* to hint that emperumān is very precious and is to be vorśipped by offering him gifts, just as one buys gold and protects it.

**ādip perumānai** – unlike some materials, which though looking beautiful, will not be apt for us, he is most apt for us and has the greatness of creating us all.

When asked whether he will accept us, āḻvār says . . .

**nādānai** – one who will accept us because he is our *svāmy* (lord)

Will he despise us because of our faults?

**nallānai** – one who does not see any faults in us. One who considers our faults as our auspicious qualities due to his auspicious quality of *vātsalyam* (showing motherly love).

What is the proof that he has such greatness?

**nāraṇanai** – āḻvār says that the name nārāyaṇa itself announces to the world his greatness.

**ādipperumānai nāraṇanai** – At the time of creation, since the *nārā:* (the clusters of souls) take shelter in him, the word nārāyaṇa shows his ability to create all beings.

**nādānai nāraṇanai** –  emperumān sustains and pervades all souls and is their means as well as goal. The word nārāyaṇa also indicates his being the lord of all. nammāḻvār mercifully said in his [tiruvāimoḻi 2-7-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/04/thiruvaimozhi-2-7-2-naranan/) “*nāraṇan muḻuvēḻulagukkum nādan*”.

**nallānai nāraṇanai** – instead of despising the lowly cluster of souls, since he pervades them completely, the word nārāyaṇa also indicates *vātsalya guṇa* (motherly love). tirumangai āḻvār mercifully said in his periya tirumoḻi 6-10-4 “*nallānudaya nāmami sollil namō nārāyaṇamē*”.

**nāraṇanai nammēḻ piṟappaṟukkum sollānai** – one who is referred to by the divine word of nārāyaṇa which will sever us from all our births. Has it not been said in vēda “*sankīrtya nārāyaṇa śabdamātram vimukta du:khā: sukinōbhavanti* ” (the moment people recite the name of nārāyaṇa their sorrows are removed and they attain mōkśa)?  tirumangai āḻvār too mercifully said in his periya tirumoḻi 1-1-9 “*adiyār padutuyarāyinavellām nilandaranjeyyum nārāyaṇa ennum nāmam*” (reciting the divine name of nārāyaṇa will destroy all the sorrows of followers). Alternatively *nammēḻ piṟappaṟukkum sollānai* can be construed as referring to the greatness of emperumān’s divine names. ruśis also spoke of this meaning when they said “*yannāma sankīrttanō vimucyatē*”, “*yannāma sankīrttanatō mahābhayāt vimōkśamāpnōti* ” (by reciting whose divine names sentient entity gets rid of the fear of samsāram and attains mōkśa).

**solluvadē sūdu** – reciting his divine names is apt for us, the jīvas (souls).

We will take up the 65<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 65. Sudavadu

*mādu āya – one vho is te epitome of beauty mālavanai – one vho is infatuated vit follovers mādavanai – about tirumāl (śriman nārāyaṇa) solmālai – tese garlands of vords yādānum valla ā sindittu iruppēṟku – me vho has te capability to meditate until ī have te strengt vaigundattu idam illaiyō – is tere no place in śrīvaikuṇṭam? solleer – please say sūdu āvadu – tis is te most apt for us en nenjattu eṇṇinēn – ī affirmed in my heart*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**sūdāvadu ennenjatu eṇṇinēn – I meditated in my mind about the deed that is apt to be carried out. Just as nammāḻvār had mercifully mentioned in periya tiruvandādi 53 “nin pugaḻil vaigum tam sindaiyilum maṟṟinidō nī avarkku vaigundam enṟaruḷum vān” (is the śrīvaikuṇṭam that you are granting to your devotees greater than the thoughts that they have about your greatness?) since thinking about emperumān gives supreme bliss I kept thinking about this continuously in my mind.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār, who instructed others in the previous pāsuram solluvadē sūdu, tells in this pāsuram as to how he practiced it himself and the benefit that he is going to get out of it. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: sūdāvadu ennenjattu eṇṇinēn sol mālai mādāya mālavanai mādavanai yādānum vallavā sindittu iruppēṟku vaigundattu illaiyō solleer idam*

What is that?

**solmālai vallavā sindittu….** – meditating on the *prabandams* (divine hymns) which speak about the supreme being.

**mādāya** – the epitome of beauty. *mādu* – beauty.

Would he be beautiful outside, but very cruel inside?

**mālavanai** – one who is the epitome of affection.

What is the basis for this beauty and affection?

**mādavanai** – the consort of [lakshmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/); *mā* – lakshmi; *davan* – lord. The opinion here is that it is her connection which makes him beautiful and affectionate.

**yādānum vallavā sinddittu iruppēṟku** – to me who keeps thinking about the *śrīsuktis* (divine hymns) about *tirumāl* ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) in one way or the other, to the extent possible.

**vaigundattu illaiyō solleer idam** – is there no place in śrīvaikuṇṭam? Please tell.

**sindittu iruppēṟku vaigundattu illaiyō idam** – Just as it is mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-17-78 “*āyāsa: smaraṇena kō’sya smrutō yaccati śōbanam*” (What is the difficulty in thinking about this supreme being? He grants benefits the moment one thinks about him) when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is with the simplicity mentioned, is there anything required to attain him other than thinking about him?

**mādāya** – being affectionate like a mother \[alternative meaning\].

If the text is *māyavanai* instead of *mālavanai*, the meaning would be – one who has amazing activities.

We will move on to the 66<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 66. Idamavadu

*paṇḍu – before (my tinking) padam nāga aṇai nediya māṟku – to te supreme being vho is reclining on śēśasayanam (mattress of ādiśēśan) vho has hoods inṟu – from nov onvards ellām – for all times to come idam āvadu – dvelling place en nenjam – my heart madi sūdi tannōdu – rudra vho carries candra on his head ayanai – brahmā too didam āga – as supreme beings vaiyēn – ī vill not keep tem in my heart vaiyēn nān – ī vho am having subtle knovledge (to discern vho is te supreme being) valam – vit te strengt (acquired due to nearness to emperumān) āḷ seyyēn – ī vill not carry out service to tose dēvatāntarams (oter deities)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**idam āvadu en nenjam inṟu ellām – after I kept emperumān in my heart, nowadays my heart has become his dwelling place.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār mercifully said in the previous pāsuram en nenjattu eṇṇinēn. As soon as he thought (about emperumān) he mentions about the hardships that emperumān underwent and looking at them, how he became engaged, in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: idam āvadu ennenjam inṟellām paṇḍu padanāgaṇai nediya māṟku didamāga vaiyēn madi sūdi tannōdu ayanai nān vaiyēn ātceyyēn valam*

Where was he before this?

**paṇḍu pada nāgaṇai** – before this, it was atop ādiśēśan whose hoods became puffed up due to proximity with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), that he was reclining.

**nediya māṟku** –  for the supreme being who is superior to everyone else.

**idamāvadu en . . . nediya māṟku** – since emperumān has deep affection, he established himself firmly in my lowly heart. The same meaning has been explained by this [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) himself in tiruccanda viruttam 64 “*anṟu vehkaṇaik kidandadennilāda munnelām*” (before reaching my heart, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) was dwelling in tiruvehkā) and in tiruccanda viruttam 65 “*kidappadum naṟperum tiraikkadaluḷ nān ilāda munnelām*” (before I came, emperumān used to recline on the milky ocean) and by [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) in his periyār tirumoḻi 5-4-9 “*panikkadalil paḷḷigōḷaip paḻagavittu ōdi venden manakkadalil vāḻavalla māya maṇāḷa nambi* ”(leaving aside the cool tiruppāṟkadal, emperumān, who has amazing powers, who is the consort of pirātti and who is complete in auspicious qualities, is capable of entering my mind and living there).

**nediya māṟku** – one who has affection bettering mine. *māl* – one who is great; one who is affectionate. Just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) had mercifully said in tiruvāimoḻi 9-6-10 “*vārikkoṇḍu unnai viḻunguvan kāṇilenṟu ārvuṟṟa ennai oḻiya ennin munnam pārittut tān ennai muṟṟum paruginān*” (before I could enjoy him, he, with greater desire than mine, ate me up completely), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who has a greater affection towards me than I have towards him, entered my lowly heart and established himself there.

When asked “What did you do?” [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says, in the second part of this pāsuram that he made his heart suitable for emperumān.

**didamāga vaiyēn** – I will not keep other deities as stable entities in my heart.

Who are those deities?

**madi sūdi tannōdu ayanai** – rudra, who gave shelter for candra when he was in a precarious position, thus protecting him, due to which he thought vainly that he is the protector for all and, brahmā, who, because he was born in a lotus, thought that he was an *ayōnija* (born without staying in a womb). I will not think of rudra and brahmā as supreme entities.

**nān vaiyēn** – this is because I have subtle knowledge, capable of distinguishing between good and bad. *vaiyēn* – one who has sharp knowledge.

**vaiyēṇ ātceyyēn** – Since I have sharp knowledge, I will not become a servitor to rudra, brahmā et al.

Has it not been said in mahābhāratam mōkśa parvam 116 “*brahmāṇam śidikaṇṭanca yāścānyā dēvatā: smrutā:* I *pratibhuddā na sēvantē yasmāt parimitam palam* īī ” (those with sharp brain will not worship brahmā, Siva and other deities since the benefit which can be obtained from them is limited)!

**valam** – the reason for all this greatness is due to the strength of having [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in proximity.

**valam vaiyēn** – it could also be construed that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) is saying that he will not circumambulate these deities.

We will take up the 67<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 67. Valamaga

*nāraṇai – being lord of all nāpadiyai – controller of my tongue jñānam perumānai – being great in knovledge sīr aṇanai – supreme being vho has all te auspicious qualities fitting vell vaigal nalamāga ēttum tiṟam – tis nature of praising him all te time valam āga – vheter it gives good benefits māttāmai tāṇ āga – or it does not give good benefits kulam āga – vheter it leads to birt in good clan kuṟṟam tān āga – or it leads to birt in bad clan (ī vill not give up praising emperumān)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**nalamāga nāraṇanai – being the supreme lord of all in a great way. Even while he pervades all the souls and sustains them, emperumān is such that their faults will not affect him. The opinion here is that praising such emperumān will not be the reason for any lowliness.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “You had mentioned earlier, nenjattu eṇṇinēn and didamāga vaiyēn madisūdi tannōdu ayanai. In this world, inimical people will celebrate and friendly people will be saddened when something terrible occurs. Will you have this firmness in you even when misfortune comes your way? ” He responds saying “whether joy comes or sorrow comes, there will be no shortcoming in the way I worship emperumān” thus mercifully affirming his deep faith. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: valamāga māttāmai tānāga vaigal kulamāga kuṟṟandānāga nalamāga nāraṇanai nāpadiyai jñānapperumānai sīraṇanai ēttum tiṟam*

**nāpadiyai** – the  one who controls my tongue such that he  made my tongue to look in his direction from the lowly matters in which it had got immersed earlier.

The text is also *nampadiyai* in which case the meaning will be that he is our lord to such an extent that even if all the other clusters of souls get together, they cannot equal us.

**jñānap perumānai** – just as it is said “*yassarvajña: sarvavit*” (one who knows the basic nature and basic qualities of all entities) he is the repository of all knowledge. Just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) had said in periya tiruvandādi 53 “*unnadiyārkku enseyvan enṟē nī irutti*” (you are constantly contemplating on what further you could do for your followers), he is great in terms of knowledge too.

**sīraṇanai** – he is the reservoir of all auspicious qualities.

**ēttum tiṟam** – the way in which he is praised. We can prefix the word *nalamāga* to this word and say “praising him well”.

**valamāga** – whether the act of praising him gives good results or

**māttāmaidān āga** – does not give good results.

**kulamāga** – whether praising him leads to birth in a good clan or

**kuṟṟandān āga** – it leads to birth in a bad clan

**vaigal** – whether this becomes a daily event or not. Since praising him constantly is what is acceptable to me and not the benefit out of it, I will never let go of the two firm resolves *en nenjattu eṇṇinēn* and *didamāga vaiyēn*.

**nāraṇanai nāpadiyai jñānapperumānai sīraṇanai ēttum tiṟam** – The opinion here is that since [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the supreme being who is praised, since he made my tongue to recite this prabandam (divine hymn), since he is omniscient in order to protect me, only good things will happen to me. It is pertinent here to recall the firmness of [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) when he said in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-3-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thiruvaimozhi-5-3-5-kadiyan-kodiyan/) “*kadiyan kodiyan nediya māl ulagam koṇḍa adiyan aṟivaru mēni māyattan āgilum kodiya ennenjam avanenṟē kidakkum ellē* ” (even though emperumān will be intent on completing his task, will not look back once the task is completed, has a superior nature even if he starts leaving after the task in such a way that it is difficult to reject him, has divine feet with which he brought all the worlds under his control, bewilders the beholders with his beauty in such a way that they cannot find out whether he is good or not, my cruel heart has totally surrendered to him).

We will take up the 68<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 68. Tirambenmin kandir

*namanum – yama tan tūduvarai – his servitors kūvi – calling sevikku – (in teir) ears tiṟambēlmin kaṇḍīr – ḍo not miss (te order tat ī am going to issue you nov) tiruvaditan – sarvēśvara’s (supreme being’s) nāmam – divine names maṟandum – even if tey forget puṟam toḻā māndar – (if you see) people vho do not vorśip oter deities iṟainji – boving (to tem) sāduvar āy pōdumingaḷ enṟān – he said to behave like an ascetic (avoiding any cruelty)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**tiṟambēnmin kaṇḍīr – do not cross the words which I am going to tell you now. Since yama is giving his order (to his messengers) with such a preface, it is clear that this order (which he is about to give) is more important than all his other orders. The hint here is that yama will not mind if they go against some of his other orders, but they should not go against this. If yama gives a note in writing to bring the soul of a person to his messenger, the messenger can refuse to obey it if the person happens to be a śrīvaiṣṇava.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār had said that vorśipping of emperumān and avoidance of other deities are acceptable even if they give troubles. To state the fact correctly, in śrīviṣṇu purāṇam, ślōkam 3-7-14 states clearly that connection with emperumān alone will give all the benefits, during the course of a conversation between yama and his messenger. “svapuruśam abhivīkśya pāśahastam vadati yama kila tasya karṇa mūlē I parihara madusūdanapprapannān prabhur ahamanyanruṇām na vaiṣṇavānām  īī” (yama (the dēvatā for righteousness), looking at his messenger, who has a rope in his hand (with which to fetch souls),tells him  “Messenger! Go away from those who have surrendered to madusūdana (emperumān). I am the lord for others but not for vaiṣṇavas”. This 68th pāsuram is the translation and vyākhyānam (commentary) for this śrīviṣṇu purāṇa ślōkam. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: tiṟambēnmin kaṇḍīr tiruvadi than nāmam maṟandum puṟam toḻā māndar iṟainjiyum sāduvarāyppōmingal enṟān namanum than tūduvaraik kūvic cevikku*

**tiruvadi than nāmam maṟandum puṟam toḻā māndar** – āḻvār is explaining clearly the meaning of the verse *madusūdana prapannān* in śrī viṣṇu purāṇa ślōkam (seen in the introduction) without any fault of non-comprehension. āḻvār explains the term which has been generally stated as “those who had surrendered to madusūdana” as “maṟandum puṟam toḻā māndar” (followers of emperumān, even if they forget the divine names of emperumān, will not worship other deities), keeping in his divine mind the *svabhāvam* (the basic nature) of the person who surrenders.  Once a person surrenders to emperumān, it will not be a grave mistake if he forgets emperumān completely. It is enough if (s)he does not worship other deities. This is similar to a lady not having affection for her husband. Since affection will gradually creep back in her, no loss occurs to her *patnītvam* (the quality of being a chaste wife). However, will she not lose her basic nature and become ineligible for being taken by her husband if she goes to another person?  In the same way, even if they forget the divine names of emperumān, his followers will remain as śrīvaiṣṇavas if they do not worship other deities. yama’s messengers should be wary of them too is the meaning hinted here. *tiruvadi* –svāmy (lord).

**māndar** – people. Even if they do not have any distinguishing qualities of followers of emperumān and are like normal people, it is acceptable. The only guiding principle to be observed strictly is that they should not worship any other deity.

**iṟainjiyum sāduvarāyp pōdumingaḷ enṟān** – this explains the term *parihara* in the śrī viṣṇu purāṇam ślōkam. Yama tells his messengers that it is not enough if you (messengers) say “We will not harm them”. You should discard the cruel form of *pāśahastam* (having a rope in the hand to drag the soul away from its body), carry out *anjali* (salutation with cupped palms), bow to them and behave like an ascetic; *sāduvar* – ascetics.

**namanum** – yama is famous as being impartial as brought by the śrīviṣṇu purāṇa ślōkam 3-7-15 “*ahamamaravarārcitēna dātrā sama iti lōkahitāhitē niyukta:*” (I have been ordered by the *paramapuruśa* who is vorśipped by the head of dēvas, to carry out good deeds to and remove the bad deeds from the worlds since I am equal to all (without showing any distinction between the superior and the lovl y people)). It is that yama who told his messengers not to disturb śrīvaiṣṇavas. It is only towards other people, for whom he is *prabhurahamanyanruṇām* (I am head for other people), that he behaves as equal and yama. Can the king who hands down justice to his citizens carry out similar role towards his queen? āḻvār says that in the same manner, yama, who is famous for being impartial, says a few words which seemingly appear partial. poygai āḻvār also mercifully said in his mudal tiruvandādi 55 “*avantamar evvinaiyarāgilum engōn avan tamarē enṟoḻivadallāl naman tamarāl ārāyappattaṟiyār kaṇḍīr aravaṇai mēl pērāyarkku ātpattār pēr*” (you would see that the followers of emperumān, irrespective of the deeds that they carry out, will not be questioned by the messengers of yama as they have been told by their leader yama that they are followers of his lord (emperumān)).

**than tūduvarai** – this is translation for the world *svapuruśam* in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam ślōkam. The opinion here is that these are his confidential servitors who have the capability to reject many of his messages.

**kūvi** – Calling them out aloud to show the importance of the task on hand. This word explains the meaning of *abhivīkśya vadati* in the śrīviṣṇu purāṇam ślōkam.

**sevikku** – meaning for the word *karṇa mūlē* (within the ears).  If a person tells another person aloud ”don’t desire the Queen” and if this message reaches the King’s ears, the person who said this would get beheaded first. In the same way, even if yama has to tell something to his confidential messenger, considering the importance of the message, fearing the result that this would have if the secretive message gets leaked, with pounding heart, yama tells his messenger into his ears.

We will take up the 69<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 69. Sevikkinbamavaduvum

*sevikku inbamāvaduvum – being sveet to te ears sem kaṇ māl nāmam – te divine name of puṇḍarīkākśan (one vit lotus like eyes) puvikku – for all te persons on eart puviyum aduvē – it is only te divine name vhic is te place (for providing śade, so tat people can take rest under it) kavikku niṟai poruḷāy ninṟānai – emperumān vho is te subject matter for poets nērppattēn – by cance ī attained pārkkil – if one vere to analyse maṟai poruḷum attanaiyē tān – it is te essence of vēdas (sacred texts)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**sevikkinbamāvaduvum sengaṇmāl nāmam – the um in this word (sevikkinbamāduvum) refers to the agony undergone by yama in the previous pāsuram. It is not only that emperumān’s divine name removes our being under the control of yama. The divine name of the lotus eyed emperumān is also very sweet to the ears. It will immerse the person who hears it in the flood of nectar.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that bhagavat viśayam (matter relating to emperumān) not only has the greatness to avoid any agony to yama. It is also sweet to hear, it is the refuge for earth (people living on earth), complete subject matter for poets (to compose poems) and the meaning of vēdas (sacred texts). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: sevikkinabamāvadum sengaṇmāl nāmam puvikkum puviyaduvē kaṇḍīr kavikku niṟaiporuḷāy ninṟānai nērppattēn paṟkkil maṟaipporuḷum attanaiyē kāṇ*

**māl** – one who is affectionate towards his followers; supreme being; *sem kaṇ* indicates both meanings.

**puvikkum puviyaduvē kaṇḍīr** – Is it that his divine name is sweet only for me? For all the people on this earth, isn’t that divine name the refuge? Has it not been said in the vēdas “*ārtā viśaṇṇā: śitilāśca bhītā: ghōrēśu ca vyādiśu varttamānā:* I *sankīrtya nārāyaṇa śabdamātram vimukta dukkā: sukinō bhavanti* īī” (if those who have been troubled by the *tāpatrayam* (ills brought on by self, ills brought on by nature and ills brought on by past deeds), if those who fear whether they will be troubled by them and those who are inflicted with terrible diseases, hear the word *nārāyaṇa* they will be liberated from their troubles and will be in bliss)?

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) explained the greatness of the divine name so far. Now he explains the greatness of the one who has the name.

**kavikku niṟai poruḷāy ninṟānai** – when one has to praise others, since their faults are more, one has to fill up their faults with non-existent abundance in qualities**.** [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/) (one of the primary disciples of [bhagavad śrī rāmānuja](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/)) has mercifully said in śrīstavam 3 “*anyadīyān guṇān anyatra tvasatō’dirōpya paṇiti:*” (praise is defined as taking the good qualities of one person, thrusting them on another person who doesn’t have them and singing about the second person). On the  other hand, with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the situation is that however much one has sung his praises, his *svarūpa rūpa guṇa vibhūti* (basic nature, divine form, auspicious qualities and wealth) are so much that what has been sung will be very little and what remains to be sung, so huge. Thus he is apt to be called as complete in all respect. Has it not been said in jitantē stōtram 1-7 “*vacasām vācyamuttamam*” (supreme being is beyond words)!

**nērppattēn** – I attained him by chance, without any efforts from me. In other words, I attained him due to his causeless mercy. It is emperumān’s causeless mercy which is spoken about as *vidi* in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-1-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/05/thiruvaimozhi-5-1-1-kaiyar-chakkaraththu/) “*vidivāykkinṟu kāppārār*” (who can prevent when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s mercy takes effect ) and in [stōtra ratnam 51](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/sthothra-rathnam-51/) “*vidinirmitam ētadanvayam*” (this connection came about due to *vidi* (destiny))

**pārkkil** – I did not remain complacent because I obtained it due to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s grace. Just as one examines a gemstone, I too analysed and what I found is that …

**maṟaipporuḷum attanaiyēdān** – the deep-rooted meaning of vēdāntam is also the same. I realised that when I tripped, what I got to lay my hands on was indeed an invaluable carbuncle.

We will move on to the 70<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 70. Tan oruvanagi

*(īmmersing in te deluge) daraṇi – eart idandu eduttu – digging out (from te valls of universe) eyiṟṟil – on his tusks tāngiyadu – vay sustained yān oruvan – only ī inṟā – until nov aṟiginṟēn allēn – am not knoving (many people knev tis for a long time)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**tān oruvanāgi – being the lord of all, emperumān reaches a unique state. For a great person, unique state is to be at the outermost limit of qualities such as sauśeelyam (simple), saulabhyam (easy to approach) and vātsalyam (having motherly love). That is the state of vāmana (incarnation of vāmana). nammāḻvār too said in tiruvāimoḻi 1-10-1 “oru māṇikkuṟaḷāgi” (being a unique dwarf)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “You said that this is the substance of vēdas. How did you find it?” āḻvār responds saying “I am the one who did not know this till today. Knowledgeable persons knew this all along”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: tān oruvanāgit taraṇi idandeduttu ēnoruvanāy eyiṟṟil tāngiyadum yān oruvan inṟāvaṟiginṟēn allēn iru nilattaic cenṟāngu adippadutta sēy tān oruvan āgi – being uniquely beautiful senṟu – going (to mahābali as a mendicant) āngu – in that yāgasālai (place where ritual is conducted) iru nilattai – expansive earth adippadutta – one who measured sēy – the child ēn oruvan āy – as the distinguished form of varāha (wild boar)*

**oruvanāgi** – it appears that *oruvan* and *vāmana* are synonyms. A great entity stood in a lowly manner. One who grants everyone’s wish, stood like a mendicant, asking for alms. Without expecting anything, he stroked everyone with his divine foot. īsn’t this the limit for *sauśeelyam, saulabhyam, vātsalyam* etc! īsn’t this entity termed as *oruvan*! Due to the interest in this meaning and due to the fact that *ēn oruvanāgi* has been separately dealt with, this term *tānoruvanāgi* can be better combined with the last line *irunilattaic cenṟāngu adippadutta sēy* to get a complete meaning.

**āngu senṟu** – while [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) could have completed the task through his *sankalpam* (divine vow), he went to the place where mahābali was, by transforming himself, because mahābali had the quality of magnanimity.

**iru nilattai adippadutta sēy** – measuring the expansive earth in such a way that it came under his foot. Just as it is said “*ēka: prāseesaratpādamēka: prācikśaḷanmudā* I *aparōpyadaran mūrdnā kō’dikas tēśu gaṇyatām* īī” (one stretched his leg; one washed it joyfully; one bore that water on his head. Ponder on who among these three is the greatest), he is the child of whom it could be said that he is without any equal.

**daraṇi idandeduttu** – digging out the earth with his tusk when earth was sticking to the walls of the universe at the time of deluge.

**ēn oruvanāy eyiṟṟil tāngiyadum** – with a unique form of boar; despite being a great entity, since he incarnated as a boar without any pride, his qualities of *sauśeelyam, saulabhyam, vātsalyam* were manifested splendrously. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) calls him as *ēn oruvanāy* (unique boar). It could also be construed that āḻvār is calling him as *ēn oruvanāy* since he is a huge boar both in terms of form and beauty.

**eyiṟṟil tāngiyadum** – he bore earth in one corner of his tusk.

**yān oruvan inṟā aṟigillēn allēn** – I who am without equal in my ignorance, did not know this till now. *inṟā* – until today. But did this matter come to light only after I knew it now? The opinion is that many have known this already.

**yān oruvan inṟā aṟiginṟen** – Did I come to know of this matter only today?

**allēn** – No; for a long time, many have known it.

We will take up the 71<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 71. Seyan aniyan

*sēyan – being at a far avay distance migap periyan – being huge siṟiyan – incarnating in lovly forms (lover tan samsāris) aṇiyan – being very easy to approac āyan – being born in te clan of herdsmen tuvarai kōnāy ninṟa – having te greatness of being te head of dvārakāpuri (dvārka, in present day Gujarat śtate) māyan – emperumān anṟu – during tat time (vhen mahābhārata var vas fought) ōdiya – mercifully said (on te seat of his cariot, to arjuna) vākku adanai – tat divine vord (of carama ślōkam) ulagattil – in tis vorld kallār – not having learnt mey jñānam il – vitout having te inclination for true knovledge ēdilar ām – are inimical tovards emperumān*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**sēyan – emperumān is at such a far away distance that brahmā, rudra et al would not know about him. Did not brahmā say in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-55 “yanna dēvō na munayō na cāham na ca Sankara: I jānanti paramēśasya tat viṣṇō: paramam padam īī” (dēvas (celestial entities), munis (sages), I (brahmā), rudra do not know the superior nature of that paramēśvara (supreme being) viṣṇu) and in mahābhāratam mōkśa parvam “na sa śakyas tvayā draśtum mayānyair vāpi sattama I sarvēśām sākśibhūtō’sau na  grāhya kēnacit kvacit īī” (Oh child, rudra! That emperumān is not capable of being seen by you, me or anyone else. Even though he sees everyone, he cannot be seen by anyone at any time)! emperumān himself has said in varāha purāṇam “adyāpi mām na jānanti rudrēndrās sapitāmahā:” (rudra and indra, along with brahmā, do not know me even today) and in śrī bhagavat gītā 7.25 “nāham prakāśa: sarvasya” (I do not manifest to anyone)! This same meaning has been brought out by śrī viṣṇu darmam ślōkam “na yasya rūpam na balaprabhāvau na ca svabhāva: paramasya pumsa:  I vijñāyatē śarvapitāmahādyais tam vāsudēvam praṇamāmyacintyam īī” (I bow down to that supreme being vāsudēva whose divine form, greatness of strength and qualities are not known by brahmā, rudra et al). bahvruca śruti also said “na tē viśnō jāyamāṇō na jātō dēva mahimna: paramantamāpa” (Oh viṣṇu! No one who has been born earlier or is going to be born in future, knows the outer limit of your greatness).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*It is not that we do not know that emperumān protected us during the time of worldly deluge. āḻvār feels sad not only for this but also for the fact that we do not know till date the carama ślōka (ultimate verse) that emperumān gave us when he tried to lift us from the deluge of samsāram (materialistic realm). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: sēyan aṇiyan siṟiyan migapperiyan āyan tuvaraikkōnāy ninṟa māyan anṟu ōdiya vākkadanaik kallār ēdilarām mey jñānamil*

What is the reason for not being known?

**migapperiyan** – one who has humongous form, nature, qualities, wealth etc.  He is so huge that vēdas, which had set out to measure him, returned without completing the task as mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat “*yatō vācō nivartantē aprāpya manasā saha*” (whichever words with mind returned without attaining supreme being…)

**aṇiyan** – he will be close at hand for those who have just a little bit of desire to see him.

**sēyan – aṇiyan** – he is very far away from people such as duryōdana and those who attempt on their own to see him. He is close at hand for people such as yaśōdā and those who look up to him. Hasn’t nammāḻvār mercifully said in his [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-4-yarumor-nilaimaiyan/) “*yārumōr nilaimaiyan ena aṟivariya emperumān yārumōr nilaimaiyan ena aṟivu eḷiya emperumān*” (for those who attempt on their own, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is difficult to be estimated; for those who attain him through his grace, he is simple to be estimated)!

How is he so close at hand for the favourable ones?

**siṟiyan** – since he is born below the levels of his followers, due to his desire, he manifests himself as a very simple entity.

**sēyan aṇiyan siṟiyan migapperiyan** – [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/), one of the preceptors in the lineage of our ācārya gurupamparai, combined these four divine names in this ślōka from stōtra ratnam “*namō namō vāngmanasādi bhūmayē namō namō vāngmanasaika bhūmayē* I *namō namō’nanta mahāvibhūtayē namō namō’nanta dayaika sindavē*  īī”(salutations to you, who are difficult to attain for the words and minds of those who attempt to attain you on their own; salutations to you who are easy to attain for the words and minds of those who see you due to your grace; salutations to you who are having unlimited, huge wealth; salutations to you who are like an ocean of mercy). Since mercy is the reason for his incarnation as vāmana (dwarf), āḷavandār is calling [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as *ananta dayaika  sindavē* .

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) gives an example for emperumān’s quality of easy approacability..

**āyan** – herdsman who cannot distinguish between his right hand and left hand (he is such ignorant)

**tuvaraikkōnāy ninṟa māyan** – leaving aside his role as head of both vibhūtis (material and spiritual realms), he stood as the head of dvārakā.

**māyan** – one with amazing activities. Apart from being easily approachable by favourable entities, he earned the ill-fame of “hiding in dvārakā from the lowly jarāsanda after losing to him” only because he wanted to help his favourable person bhīma to kill jarāsanda.

periyavāccān piḷḷai, one of the commentators for this prabandam said that *āyan tuvaraikkōnāy ninṟa māyan* is the explanation for the terms *aṇiyan siṟiyan* while another commentator, appiḷḷai considered the term *āyan* as the explanation for *aṇiyan siṟiyan* and the words *tuvaraikkōnāy ninṟa māyan* as the explanation for the words *sēyan periyan*. As an example for these two, he says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had the simplicity of being born in the clan of herdsmen and, having the greatness of being the controller of dvārakā, he took multitudinous forms in order to dance with ten thousand women, thus exhibiting his amazing activity.

**māyan anṟu ōdiya vākkadanaik kallār** – those who did not learn the true words which kriṣṇa recited at the beginning of mahābhārata war. Those who did not learn through their ācāryan (teacher) the carama ślōkam (ultimate hymn) as said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/18-66/) “*sarva darmān parityajya māmēkam śaraṇam vraja* I *aham tvā sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi mā suca:* īī” (giving up all righteous activities along with their traces, attain me (who is having qualities such as easily approachable etc) as the means. I (who am having qualities of knowledge and power) will liberate you from all hurdles.  Do not grieve).

**aṇiyan siṟiyan ōdiya vākku** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) explains the qualities of easy to approach etc, as mentioned in the term *mām*.

**sēyan periyān ōdiya vākku** – āḻvār explains the qualities of knowledge, power etc as mentioned in the term *aham*.

**tuvarikkōn** – this explains that he is the svāmy (lord).

**māyan** – without distinguishing between his qualities of *saulabhyam* etc and his qualities of *jñāna, śakti* etc.

**kallār ulagattil** – is it that only one or two persons in this world do not know *carama ślōkam*? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that most of the people do not know the meanings of *carama ślōkam*.

What is the reason for not learning this?

**mey jñānam il** – the reason for not learning this is the absence of true knowledge about *tatva (knowledge about sentient, insentient and īśvara entities), hita (knowledge about the means to attain emperumān), and puruśārta* (knowledge about the goal to be enjoyed after attaining emperumān’s divine feet). What is the reason for not having this knowledge?

**ēdilarām** – being haters of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).  *ēdilar*  – enemies. As mentioned by emperumān himself in [śrī bhagavat gītā 16.19](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/16-19/) “*tānaham dviśata: krūrān samsārēśu narādamān* I *kśipāmyajasram aśubhān āsuriśvēva yōniśu* īī ” (I push those who hate me, who are cruel and the lowliest among men into *samsāram*, and there too, in the clan of demons) and in “*ājñāccēdī mama dvēśi*” (one who crosses my order is one who hates me), emperumān considers in his cool mind that these people are his haters. As mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 106-6 “*śatrō: prakyāta vīryasya*” he makes them declare that *he (*emperumān*) is their enemy*.

We will take up the 72<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 72. Illaram illel

*illaṟam il ennum sollum – tose autentic souces vhic say tat karma yōgam (pat of carrying out of deeds), vhic is te rule for gruhastam (vay of life for married people) and vhic is accepted by śāstras, is te means \[for attaining mōkśam\] sol alla – not autentic illēl – even if it is not (means) tuṟavaṟam il ennum sollum – te autentic sources vhic say tat jñāna yōgam (pat of knovledge) is te  means sol alla – not autentic illēl – even otervise allana aṟam il ennum sollum – autentic souces vhic say tat oters (bhakti yōgam (pat of devotion), dēśavāsam (residing in a divine abode), tirunāma sangīrtanam (reciting divine names of emperumān) etc) are te means sol alla – not autentic nal aṟam āvanavum – jñānam, bhakti etc vhic are righteous pats nāl vēda mā tavamum – karmayōgam vhic has been spoken of in te four vēdas nāraṇanē āvadu – give results due to te grace of śrīman nārāyaṇa īdu – tis trut anṟu enbār ār – vho is tere vho vill say tat tis is not correct?*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**illaṟam illennum sollum sollalla – those sayings which state that karmayōgam (path of carrying out deeds mentioned in śāstras) is the means \[to attain emperumān\] are not authentic. Will illaṟam (life of householder) refer to karmayōgam?  āḻvār equates this way because only gruhastan (one who is in a familial life) has control over dānam (donating alms), tapam (carrying out rituals) etc which are part of karma yōgam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*samsāris told āḻvār “What purpose is served by despising others saying – māyan anṟōdiya vākkadanaik kallādār ulagattil ēdilarām? Since you have learnt the teaching of emperumān, why do you not say the meaning yourself?” āḻvār gives the meaning of carama ślōkam in this pāsuram. Just as kriṣṇa carama ślōkam is for mahābhāratam, śrī rāma carama ślōkam for śrī rāmāyaṇam, “mēmporuḷ” pāsuram (38) for tirumālai divya prabandam, this pāsuram is the most important pāsuram for this tiruvandādi. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: illaṟam illēl tuṟavaṟam illennum sollaṟam allanavum sollalla nallaṟam āvanavum nāl vēda māttavaum nāraṇanē āvadu anṟu enbārār (for the souls who are roaming around, without knowing the means for attaining mōkśam)*

**illennum** – Just as it is mentioned in the vākyam “*upāyē gruha rakśitrō: śabda: śaraṇam ityayam varttatē*” (the word śaraṇam is used in the context of upāyam (means), gruha (house), rakśaka (protection)),the words śaraṇam and gruha are synonyms. *il* which refers to gruha thus refers to śaraṇam, which is the means.

**illaṟam illennum sollum sollalla** – [śrī bhagavat gītā 3-20](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/3-20/) says “*karmaṇaiva hi samsiddim āstitā janakādaya:*” (people like janaka et al attained mōkśam through karma yōgam only); same ślōkam “*asaktōhayācaran karma paramāpnōti pūruśa:*” (the sentient entity who observes karmayōgam without any attachment, attains his superior ātmā). [śrī bhagavat gītā 18-46](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/18-46/) ślōkam “*yata: pravrutti bhūtānām yēna sarvaidam tatam* I *svakarmaṇā tamabhyarcya siddim vindati mānava:* īī” (sentient entity, by carrying out karma, worships and attains me, from whom he is able to engage in activities such as creation, by whom he is pervaded and exists). All these texts which say that karmayōgam is the means for attaining mōkśam are not authentic.

**illēl** – if karmayōgam is not the means

**tuṟavaṟam il ennum sollum sollalla** – the sayings which mention that jñāna yōgam (path of knowledge) is the means are not authentic. Will *tuṟavaṟam* (life of ascetic) refer to jñānayōgam? Since *tuṟavaṟam* has to be undertaken after giving up illaṟam (the life of householder), we can say that *tuṟavaṟam* refers to jñānayōgam. In several ślōkas in śrī bhagavat gītā, it has been established that jñānayōgam is *sanyāsam* or *karma sanyāsam*.

**tuṟavaṟam il ennum sollum sollalla** – Texts such as śrī bhagavat gītā 4-37 which says that jñāna yōgam is the means for attaining mōkśam “*yataidāmsi samiddō’gnir bhasmāt kurutē’rjuna* I *jñānāgni sarvakarmāṇi bhasmāt kurutē tatā* īī” (Oh arjuna! Just as fire which is glowing well burns out the firewood, in the same way the fire of jñānayōgam will burn out all the karmas) are not authentic.

**allana aṟam il ennum sollum sollalla** – other righteous acts seen as means for mōkśam are also not authentic. Which are the other righteous acts which are said to be means for mōkśam? (a) śrī bhagavat gītā 11-54 “*bhaktyā tvananyayā śakya ahamēvamvidō’rjuna* I *jñātum draśtum ca tattvēna pravēśtum ca parantapa* īī” (I can be known of, seen and attained only through bhakti which is fully focussed on me) says that bhakti yōgam is a means for attaining him. (b) The vēda vākyam “*dēśōyam sarvakāmaduk:*” (this divine abode will grant all desires, including mōkśam) says that living in a divine abode will grant mōkśam. (c) viṣṇu sahasranāma ślōkam “*kim japan mucyatē jantu:*” (by vorśipping whom, jīvan attains mōkśam) says that reciting divine names is the means for attaining mōkśam. (d) śrī bhagavat gītā ślōkam 4-9 “*janma karma ca mē divya mēvam yō vētti tatvata:* I *tyaktvā dēham punar janma naiti māmēti sō’rjuna* īī” (Oh arjuna! One who knows my *aprākruta* birth (birth which does not take place due to karma) and my activities as they are, will not attain another birth once he gives up his body. He will attain me) says that knowing the secret of emperumān’s incarnations is the means to mōkśam. (e) śrī bhagavat gītā ślōkam 15-19 “*yō māmēvamasammūdō jānāti puruśōttamam* I *sa sarvavit bhajati mām sarva bhāvēna bhārata* īī” (one who, without bewilderment, knows that I am *puruśōttama* (the supreme being), knows all the means; he attains me through all means) says that *puruśōttama vidyai*(knowing that emperumān is the supreme being) is also a means of attaning mōkśam. These are referred to as *allana aṟam* here.

While these have been referred to as means for attaining mōkśam by emperumān himself in śrī bhagavat gītā and by various riśis in itihāsa (epics) and purāṇas (ancient narrations which appear new), can āḻvār say that these are not authentic for consideration of mōkśam? Since bhagavān himself has said in śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66 “*sarvadarmān parityajya*” (giving up all the paths that I have shown as means for attaining mōkśam), it could be said that way. In truth, that karma, jñāna and bhkti yōgas can be said to be means is only for the sake of saying. They cannot be taken as primary. Only that which gives results (on its own) can be taken as means. karma, jñāna and bhakti yōgams, which are evanescent and which are insentient, cannot grant results on their own. Only emperumān, who is pleased with them, grants the results. Hence it is nārāyaṇa who is primary for granting mōkśa. For this reason, āḻvār’s assertion that *illaṟam, tuṟavaṟam, allana aṟam* are not the means, is the essence of vēdāntams.

What is the reason for your (referring to āḻvār) saying that karma, jñāna, bhakti yōgam etc are not the means?

**nallaṟamāvnavum** – *aṟam* refers to deeds. *nallaṟam* refers to deeds which are carried out without any expectation in the results. In other words, karma, jñāna and bhakti yōgams, which are exclusive for brāhmaṇa, kśatriya and vaiśya menfolk are *aṟam*. *nāma sangīrttanam* (reciting divine names of emperumān) etc which can be sung by traivarṇikas (the first three classes of people) without any distinction, along with the fourth varṇa people and women are called as *nallaṟam.* The advantage of *nāma sangīrttanam* etc which are referred more by itihāsa and purāṇa over karma, jñāna, bhakti yōgams which are referred more by vēdas is that they are applicable to all.

**nālvēdamāttavamum** – karmayōgam which is spoken of extensively in the *pūrvabhāga* (earlier parts) of the four vēdas (rig, yajur, sāma and atarvaṇa). This is also applicable to jñāna and bhakti yōgas which are spoken of in the vēdāntams (latter parts of vēdas). Alternatively, *nālvēdamāttavam* would refer to gruhasta and sanyāsa darmas (paths of righteousness) which are accepted by vēdas. *nallaṟamāvanavum* would refer to *allana aṟam* (bhakti yōgam, tirunāma sangīrttanam etc) and *nālvēdamāttavamum* would refer to *illaṟam* and *tuṟavaṟam*. Both meanings are acceptable. periyavāccān piḷḷai considered in his divine mind that *tuṟavaṟam* refers to jñāna yōgam and bhakti yōgam while appiḷḷai included bhakti yōgam in *allana aṟam*.

**nārāyanēyāvadu** – all these give results due to nārāyaṇa. Hence, *illaṟam, tuṟavaṟam* and *allana aṟam* will not be means. Only nārāyaṇa who is the refuge for the cluster of nārās (souls) is the means. When he is the means, there is no need for their support.

**nallaṟamāvanavum nālvēda māttavamum nārāyanēyāvadu** – “*sarvē vēdās sarvavēdyā: saśāstrā: sarvē yajñās sarva ijyāśca kriṣṇa:* I *vidu: kriṣṇam brāhmaṇās tattvatō yē tēśām rājan sarvayajñās samāptā:* īī” (Oh King! All vēdas, śāstras, materials which are known (through them), all rituals, the dēvas who are vorśipped in those rituals are all only kriṣṇa. For those brāhmaṇās who know kriṣṇa truly, all rituals are completed).

**īdu anṟu enbār Ar** – Who can refute this meaning? Has this not been felt by experts in vēdas along with experts in bhagavat viśayam without any distinction? Has it not been said in  mahābhāratam vana parvam 71-123 “*yē ca vēdavidō viprā yē cādyātmavidō janā:* I *tē vidanti mahātmānam kriṣṇam darmam sanātanam* īī” (brāhmaṇas who know vēdas and elders who know the basic nature of souls know that kriṣṇa is the age-old dharma (righteousness))!

**īdu anṟu enbār Ar** – there are many authentic sources to claim that *paramapuruśa* (supreme being) is the means for mōkśam. Some of these are: “*nārāyaṇam – pārāyaṇam*”, “*mumukśurvai śaraṇam aham prapadyē*”, “*lōkānām tvam parōdarma*” (śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam120-15), “*rāmō vigravān dharma*”( śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 37-13), “*kriṣṇam darmam sanātanam*” (mahābhāratam vanaparvam 71-123), “*pāvanas sarvalōkānām tvamēva*” (śrī rāmāyaṇam uttara kāṇḍam 82-9), “*pavitrāṇām hi gōvinda: pavitram paramucyatē* I *puṇyānāmapi puṇyō’sau mangaḷānānca mangaḷam* īī” (mahābhāratam vanaparvam 88-27, among the pious entities, gōvinda is the most pious; among the virtuous, he is the most virtuous and among the auspicious, he is the most auspicious). “*māmēkam śaraṇam vraja*” (śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66), “*tamēva śaraṇam gacca*”, “*tamēva cādyam puruśam prapadya* ”, “*śaraṇam mām prapdyasva*”. āḻvārs too have sung several pāsurams conveying the same meaning “*aṟandānāyt tirivāy*”, “*nāgaṇaimisai nambirān caraṇē śaraṇ namakku*”, “*neṟivāsal tānēyāy ninṟānai*”, “*nārāyaṇanē namakkē paṟai taruvān*”, “*kaḷaigaṇ nīyē*”. Is there anyone who will deny this great meaning?

How is this pāsuram expounding caramaślōkam?

*illaṟamallēl tuṟavaṟam illenum sollaṟamallanavum sollalla* – sarvadarmān paridyajya (leaving aside all darmams completely)

*nāraṇan* – mām as well as aham (me, I)

*nāraṇanē­* – ēkam (the long syllable *E* in naraṇanē refers to exclusivity, viz. alone, or *ēkam*)

*nallaṟamāvanavum nālvēda māttavamum* – (with the meaning that getting rid of pāpas (sins) is due to nārāyaṇa) sarvapāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi (I will relieve you from all sins)

*īdanṟu enbār Ar* ***–*** mā suca: (do not grieve)

We will move on to the 73<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
