## 73. Are arivar

*anaittu ulagum – all te vorlds uṇḍu umiḻnda – svalloving (during deluge) and spitting out (later) pēr āḻiyān tan – emperumān vho resembles a huge ocean perumaiyai – greatness aṟivār ārē – vho vill nov (estimating it)? (ṭhere is none) avan vaitta – vhat tat emperumān had (earlier) kept paṇḍaittānattin padi – carama ślōkam vhic is te age-old means kār seṟinda kaṇḍattān – śiva vit bluiś coloured troat eṇkaṇṇān – nānmugan (brahmā) \[vit eight eyes\] kāṇān – do not knov*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**ārē aṟivār anaittulagum uṇḍumiḻnda pērāḻiyān than perumaiyai – who will know the greatness of the one \[emperumān\], who is like a great ocean which is referred to as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 19-31 “apramēyō mahōdadi:” (the ocean whose greatness cannot be measured), who keeps all the worlds in his stomach during deluge so that they are not injured, and who lets them out later?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mercifully mentioned the meaning of carama ślōkam. When asked as to what is the benefit for those who are deeply engaged with that, he says in this pāsuram the prāypya vaibhavam (distinctions of the goal – emperumān). He says that even brahmā, rudra et al do not know the greatness of emperumān and the greatness of his position. Alternatively, it can be said that āḻvār is saying that it is not that only the samsāris do not know the distinguishing features of carama ślōkam. Even knowledgeable entities such as brahmā, rudra et al do not know the greatness of carama ślōkam. periyavāccān piḷḷai, one of the commentators for this prabandam would say that bhattar (parāśara bhattar, son of śrī kūrattāḻvān) would give the meanings of both previous pāsuram, which are very difficult to know, and this pāsuram  with the quotation”bhattar aruḷicceyya nān kēttēn” enṟu (nam)piḷḷai aruḷicceyvar”  ((nam)piḷḷai would mercifully say that bhattar would mercifully state the meanings of these pāsurams). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: ārē aṟivār anaittulagum undumiḻnda pērāḻiyān than perumaiyai kār seṟinda kaṇḍattān eṇkaṇṇān kāṇān avan vaitta paṇḍaittānattin padi*

**pēr āḻiyān** – one who is like a great ocean. āḻi – ocean. This is as per [periyavāccān piḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyavachchan-pillai/)’s interpretation. [appiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/appillai/)’s interpretation is different – “kriṣṇa who is donning the great divine disc (*sudarśan chakra*, also known as *āḻi* in tamiḻ) in his divine hand”.

Would brahmā, rudra et al know?  . . .

**kār seṟinda . . . .**  – Let their knowing his greatness remain on one side. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) is wondering whether they know where paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) is.

**kār seṟinda kaṇḍattān** – *neelakaṇṭa* (Siva) who has the vanity of having protected the dēvas by swallowing the *ālahāla* poison and having he power which is the base for that.

**eṇ kaṇṇan** – brahmā who has eight eyes in order carry out his activities in all eight directions simultaneously and who has lot of knowledge.

**kāṇān** – do not see.

**kār seṟinda kaṇḍattān kāṇān** – even if one possesses lot of power, is it possible to know the dwelling place of one who is omnipotent?

**en kaṇṇan kāṇān** – even if one has lot of knowledge and is the head of the universe, is it possible to know paramapadam which is beyond the universe?

**avan vaitta paṇḍait tānattin padi** – they do not know the dwelling place which [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) created with his eternal desire, long time back. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-59 says “*yan na dēvā na munayō na cāham na Sankara:* I *jānanti paramēśasya tat viṣṇō: paramam padam* īī” (that is viṣṇu’s supreme dwelling place which is not known to dēvas, munis (sages), me (brahmā) and rudra); śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-55 says “*yannāyam bhagavān brahmā jānāti paramam padam*I *tannatā: sma jagaddāma tava sarvagadācyuta* īī” (Oh all pervading acyuta! We bow down to that supreme dwelling place of yours which even bhagavān brahmā does not know and which is the sustaining entity for the universe); mahābhāratam āraṇya parvam 136-18 says “*atyarkkālanadīptam tat stānam viṣṇōr mahātmana:*  I *svayaiva prabhayā rājan duśprēkśam dēvadānavai:*  īī” (the resplendence of the dwelling place of that supreme ātmā viṣṇu is much greater than the radiance of sūrya and agni. Oh King! Due to such resplendence, it cannot be seen by dēvas or demonic entities); mahābhāratam ślōkam “*divyam stānamajaram cāpramēyam durvijñēyam cāgamair gamyamādyam* I *gacca prabhō: rakśa cāsmān prapannān kalpē kalpē jāyamāna: svamūrtyā* īī” (Oh my lord! Please reach that dwelling place which is *aprākruta* (not made of primordial matter), indelible, inestimable, unknowable, that which can be known only by vēdas and which is ancient. During each kalpam (a period of time), please incarnate with your divine form and protect us, your servitors). Through all these authentic sources, it has been established firmly that paramapadam is not known to brahmā, rudra et al.

**paṇḍaittānattin padi** – it is the dwelling place \[of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] which is ancient.

The interpretation seen so far is based on *prāpya vaibhavam* (glory of emperumān as the goal). [appiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/appillai/) has given a second interpretation for this pāsuram, based on *caramaślōka vaibhavam* (glory of carama ślōkam). We will see it now.

**ārē aṟivār anaittulagaum uṇḍumiḻnda pērāḻiyān than perumaiyai** – who will know the *nirapēkśa upāya* (being means without any expectation) nature of emperumān who protects all the worlds during deluge so that they are not destroyed, who lets them out during the next cycle of creation, and who is as profound as the ocean?

**anaittulagum uṇḍumiḻnda pērāḻiyāṇ than perumaiyai** – Was [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s act of protecting the worlds during deluge carried out with any expectation? ōr, was his act of providing body and senses to sentient entities at the time of creation, done with any expectation?  [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully mentions in his [tiruvāimoḻi 1-10-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/10/thiruvaimozhi-1-10-5-kandaye-nenje/) “*kaṇḍāyē nenjē karumangaḷ vāykkinṟōr eṇḍhānum inṟīyē vandiyalumāṟu uṇḍānai ulagēḻum ōr mūvadi koṇḍānai kaṇḍukoṇḍanai nīyumē*” (Oh heart! Have you seen that without any thoughts from us, things are happening as they should? Did you not get to see the emperumān who protected all the worlds during deluge and who measured all the worlds with three steps?)

**pērāḻiyān than perumaiyai** – is it due to any expectation that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is always with the divine disc in his divine hand, in order to protect those who are in danger? *pērāḻiyān* – one who is donning divine disc which is great in protecting.

Wouldn’t brahmā, rudra et al know?

**kār seṟinda kaṇḍattān eṇ kaṇṇan avan kāṇān avan vaita paṇḍaittānanttin padi** – brahmā and rudra, who have abundance of knowledge and power will not know carama ślōkam which is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s ancient repository for *nirapēkśa* *upāyam* (means which does not expect anything). emperumān has kept this as a secret just as people gather wealth and keep it a secret. The meaning conveyed here is that they (brahmā, rudra et al) do not know the greatness of the means of emperumān which does not expect anything.

**paṇḍaittānanttin padi** – *padi* – repository. Just as it is mentioned in śrī rangarājastavam 2-87 “*upāyōpēyatva tadiha tava tatvam*” (means and goal are very important to you) and [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)’s [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-2-elivarum-iyalvinan/) “*vīdām teḷidaru nilaimaiyadu oḻivilan muḻuvadum iṟaiyōn*” ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is the lord of all, who always gives the clarity of mōkśam completely), being emperumān’s *svarūpam* (basic nature), being ancient, being the refuge for all, and being known as *stānam* (position), carama ślōkam is called as his dwelling place as it signifies his means without expecting anything.

We will move on to the 74<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 74. Padippagaijnarkku

*padi pagaijñarkku āṟṟādu – fearful of periya tiruvadi (garuda) , vho has been  enemy from te beginning. pāy tiṟai niṟp pāḻi – te divine bed vhic is like ocean having vater vit expansive vaves. madittu – believing (emperumān) as refuge adainda – coming and attaining vāḷ aravam tannai – sumukha, te resplendent snake madittu – supporting avan tan – tat (inimical) garuda val āgattu ēṟṟiya – one vho made him climb te poverful form of garuda māmēni māyavanai allādu – apart from sarvēśvaran (lord of all) vho has a great, auspicious divine form onṟu – anoter en nā – my tongue ēttādu – vill not praise*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**padippagaijñarkku – the word patin refers to lineage; hence this word refers to the enmity (between garuda and sumukha) for generations together. In other words, an enemy for the clan. Just as it is mentioned in tiruccanda viruttam “puḷḷin meyppagai” (the real enemy of birds), is it not the natural enmity which exists between snake and garuda?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned about carama ślōka vaibhavam (glory of carama ślōkam). In this pāsuram, through an incident relating to vainatēya (garuda) as mentioned in mahābhāratam, he says that sarvēśvara (lord of all) will carry out the śaraṇāgata rakśaṇam (protection of the surrendered) somehow or the other. The vainatēya vruddāntam (incident of garuda) has been explained in mahābhāratam udyōga parvam 103-105, as mentioned below: mādali, the charioteer of indra (head of celestial entities and the lord of three worlds bhū:, bhuva: and suva:) was looking for a suitable alliance for his daughter guṇakēśi. He went along with nārada muni (son of brahmā) to many places looking for suitors and finally desiring to select a snake sumukha in pātāla lōkam (nether world), asked sumukha’s grandfather āryaka. āryaka confided in mādali that garuda has already killed sumukha’s father and has vowed to kill and eat sumukha within one month.  Undeterred by this, mādali went to indra who was with upēndra (emperumān in the divine form of vāmana) and briefed him about this. upēndra bhagavān told indra that sumukha could be given nectar (elixir). However, indra, fearing the valour of garuda, granted sumukha long life, as a boon. Immediately, mādali gave his daughter in marriage to sumukha. On hearing this, garuda went to indra who was with upēndra and spoke ruthlessly to indra for having assisted his prey, preventing him from having his prey. sumukha, in the form of snake, coiled himself around upēndra’s seat \[as an act of surrendering\]. Looking at this, garuda told bhagavān “despite my being your vehicle and your flag, is this the way you repay? Think whether I, who am bearing you, am more powerful or you”. emperumān looking at garuda, said, “enough of your trumpeting. Don’t speak, ignorant of the fact that I bear you and all the worlds. See if you can bear my thumb alone” and pressed his thumb on garuda. Unable to bear the weight, garuda fell down. He then paid obeisance to emperumān and asked for forgivance. emperumān removed sumukha with his big toe, put him on garuda’s śouder and told him to protect him as his refuge. From then onvards, the two became good friends. This is the incident narrated in mahābhāratam. This incident has been spoken of by poygai āḻvār in mudal tiruvandādi 80 “adutta kadum pagaijñarkku” and by tirumangai āḻvār in periya tirumoḻi 5-8-4 “nanju sēravdōr”. Hasn’t sarvēśvaran (lord of all) mercifully stated in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-33 “abhayam sarva bhūtēbhyō dadāmi” (I give to one, who surrenders to me, protection from all). This pāsuram describes the way he gave refuge to sumukha who surrendered to him, from garuda who is principal among his followers. Since both are his followers, emperumān followed a distinguished way in protecting one from the other. In case he had not given protection to sumukha, his words in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-3 “mitrabhāvēna samprāptam na tyajēyam katancana I dōśō yatyapi tasya syāt satāmētadagarhitam īī” (Even if a person comes in the garb of a friend, I will never forego him. If he has faults, it is very good. This is not something to be ridiculed by good persons) would have been proved false. Even though he has been decreed as prey for garuda, since sumukha was desirous of living, he had committed fault against garuda. If emperumān had taken sumukha on himself and protected him, then his saying in varāha purāṇam “mad bhaktam śvapacam vāpi nindām kurvanti yē narā: I padmakōti śatēnāpi na kśamāi kadācana īī” (even if my devotee is a lowly person who eats dog’s meat, if anyone commits an offence against him, I will not forgive him for hundreds of crores of kalpams (a lengthy period of time)) would have been proved false. Thus, without any falsehood occurring for either of these two sayings, emperumān gave the responsibility of protecting sumukha to garuda himself. There could be a doubt as to whether garuda, a nityasūri (permanent dweller of śrīvaikuṇṭam), would kill and eat snakes and whether he would argue with emperumān. When emperumān took the incarnation of upēndra as the son of atiti and kasyapa, and as the younger brother of indra, garuda too incarnated as son of vinatā, as a bird, and stood as the vehicle for emperumān. Since he incarnated as a bird, in line with that nature, killing and eating snakes is justified for him. Arguing with emperumān and being shown his place was intended to tell the world as to what happens when one carries out bhagavatapacāram (misdeed against emperumān) and to manifest emperumān’s greatness. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: padippagaijñarkku āṟṟādu pāytirai nīrppāḻi madittadainda vāḷaravam tannai madittavantan vallāgat tēṟṟiya māmēni māyavanai allādonṟu ēttādennā*

**āṟṟādu** – fearful of garuda’s might

**pāytirai nīrppāḻi** – the divine mattress which is like the ocean containing water with expansive waves. Since in mahābhāratam it is mentioned that sumukha coiled around [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s cot, it is assumed as the mattress. Alternatively, since this incident could have happened after kalpam, it could be construed that this narrative had taken place in tiruppāṟkadal.

**madittadainda** – ascertaining and attaining emperumān who would protect the person who has surrendered just then, leaving aside even nityasūris such as garuda, ādiśēśa et al, just like a cow would protect the calf that it has given birth to on that day, and would drive away the calf which it had given birth to in the previous year with its horn and hoofs.

**vāḷ aravam tannai** – sumukha, the resplendent snake. Even in its state of being fearful of garuda, it was emitting radiance. It was having the radiance since it was confident after seeing emperumān’s auspicious qualities of *sauseelyam, saulabhyam* (simplicity, easy to approach) etc, even before emperumān approved of it, thinking “my task will fructify”. Did not vālmīki say that vibhīśaṇa looked radiant after ascertaining that śrī rāma will never reject him, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 16-17 “*antarikśagata: śrīmān*” (vibhīśaṇāḻvān who set out in the sky and was resplendent)?

**madittu** – emperumān decided that he would protect sumukha through garuda, who is sumukha’s enemy. Thinking of him, despite being lowly, as an entity. Thinking of sumukha as one who was surrendering, the moment he coiled around the cot. Hasn’t tirumangai āḻvār too mercifully stated in his periya tirumoḻi 5-8-4 “*nanju sērvadōr venjiinaravam veruvidandu nin śaraṇ enac caraṇāy nenjiṟkoṇḍu nin anjiṟaippaṟavaikku adaikkalam koduttu aruḷ seydadu aṟindu* ” (knowing your ability when the snake (sumukha) which spits poison and which was angry, became terrified of garuda and surrendered to you and you became its protector and, keeping in your mind its sorrowful state and his words, you gave that snake as garuda’s entity for protecting him)

**avan vallāgattu ēṟṟiya** – making garuda to joyously don the snake sumukha like a garland on his powerful śouders so that sumukha could live without fear of anyone.

**māmēni** – emperumān’s divine form overflowed with joy since the activity for his follower was completed successfully. This was similar to how śrī rāma had felt on coronating vibhīśaṇa prior to waging war with rāvaṇa, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇa yudda kāṇḍam “*abhiśicya ca lankāyām rākśasēndram vibhīśaṇam* I *krutakrutyas tadā rāmō vijvara: pramōda ha* īī” (śrī rāma, on completing the coronation of vibhīśaṇa as the king of demons in lankā, felt relieved of his fever, and was joyous that his task has been completed).

**māyavanai** – one who is with amazing qualities and amazing divine form

**allādu onṟu ēttādu en nā** – my tongue will not praise anyone other than emperumān; it will  not praise minor deities like rudra who ran away, leaving his follower stranded , as in bāṇāsura war. Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) does not consider other deities as important at all, he calls them as onṟu \[one, someone etc\].

We will go on to the 75<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 75. Nakkondu

*tīkkoṇḍa sem sadaiyān – rudra vho has reddiś matted hair vhic looks like fire nalam āga – aptly enṟum – every day pū koṇḍu – carrying flovers senṟu – (himself) going valla āru – vitin his pover ētta – praise magiḻāda – not feeling happy (considering it as someting great for him) vaigundam selvanār – śrī vaikuṇṭha nāta’s sē adi mēl pāttu – (vit) te pāsurams vhic are fit for his divine feet nākkoṇḍu – vit te tongue mānidam – human beings pādēn – ī vill not sing*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**nākkoṇḍu mānidam pādēn – Just as it is said “sā jihvā yā harim stauti” (only that which praises emperumān is tongue), I will not sing about lowly human beings with the tongue which is to be used only for singing about emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asks whether he, who would not praise brahmā, rudra et al who have abundant knowledge and power, will praise lowly human beings. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: nākkoṇḍu mānidam pādēn nalamāgat tīkkoṇḍa senjadaiyān senṟu enṟum pūkkoṇḍu vallavāṟētta  magiḻāda vaigundac celvanār sēvadimēl pāttu*

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) was asked “Then, who will you sing?” He responds “I will sing about him who is praised by rudra, who, you people think highly of”

**tīkkoṇḍa senjadaiyān** – one who has reddish matted hair which has agni, that irritates the eyes, in it.  Alternatively, just as [poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) had mentioned in [mudal tiruvandādi 97](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/09/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-97/) “*anaṟku angai ēṟṟān*” (held his beautiful hands for fire), one who has fire in his hands and reddish matted hair.

**senṟu enṟūm** – since it is ordained, going himself

**pūkkoṇḍu vallavāṟu  enṟum ētta** – taking soft flower, water, lamp, *dūpam* (fragrant smoke) etc,  which are the implements for carrying out [*tiruvārādanam*](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-thiruvaaraadhanam.html) [(divine worship)](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-thiruvaaraadhanam.html), appropriate for the soft divine form of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Worśipping emperumān every day, utilising the knowledge and power that he (rudra) has, to the extent that he can. Mentioning flower is representative of other materials required for carrying out vorhip.

**enṟum nalamāga ētta** – praising emperumān appropriate to his greatness, at all times.

**vallavāṟu ētta** – however much one praises him, it (the act of praising) can never be considered to have been completed. Hence, praising (vorśipping) him to the extent that one can. mahābhāratam karṇa parvam 83-65 says “*varśāyutair yasya guṇā na śakyā vaktum samētairapi sarvalōkai:* ” (whose qualities cannot be spoken of even if (people in) all the worlds get together and speak for ten thousand years….); [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his [tiruvāimoḻi 4-3-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/thiruvaimozhi-4-3-10-yanum-eththi/) mercifully says “*yānum ētti ēḻulagum muṟṟumēttip pinnaiyum tānumēttilum tannai ētta ētta engeydum* ” (even  after I (who had been given knowledge through [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s grace) praise him, even after all the (people in the) seven worlds praise him, even after he himself praises him, is it possible to complete praising him?) emperumān is of the kind where it is not possible to complete praising him.

**ētta magiḻāda** – There is scope for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) becoming happy only if he considers it great being praised by these people. In truth, the praising of such people creates only faults for him, as mentioned by nammāḻvār in periya tiruvandādi 2 “*pugaḻvōm paḻippōm*” (if we, who do not have any knowledge, attempt to praise you, it will end up as a fault only) and in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-1-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/07/thiruvaimozhi-3-1-7-vazhththuvar-palaraga/) “*kēḻtta sīr araṇ mudalāk kiḷar deyvamāyk kiḷarndu sūḻttamarargaḷ tudittāl un tol pugaḻ māsūṇādē* ” (if entities such as rudra et al attempt to praise all your qualities by taking on their roles as small deities, each taking one aspect to praise you, will that not end up as faulting you?)

What is the reason for emperumān not being happy despite a superior entity such as rudra praising him?

**vaigundac celvanār** – since he is the lord of all the opulence in śrīvaikuṇṭam, such praises by dēvas (celestial entities) will not bring about any change in his stature.

**magiḻāda vaigundac celvanār** – did not vēdam also say as in taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 10-20 “*vrukśa iva stabdō divi tiśtatyēka*” ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) remains as incomparable in paramapadam, without any change, like a tree)?

**magiḻāda vaigundac celvanār** – emperumān does not feel happy when entities such as rudra et al praise him as these entities, when qualities of rajas and tamas (passion and ignorance) are prominent, gear up to fight with him. He is happy being praised with deep affection, by *nityas* (nityasūris, the permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam) and muktātmās (those who have been liberated from samsāram and have attained śrīvaikuṇṭam)) in śrīvaikuṇṭam.

**selvanār sēvadimēl pāttu** – with the pāsurams which have been sung on the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is the wealth of [pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), I will not praise the lowly human beings, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/).

We will move on to the 76<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 76. Pattum muraiyum

*(tus, all tese) māyan tan – one vho has amazing pover, tat emperumān’s māyaiyil patta – trough his sankalpa (vill) daṟpu – have existence*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**pāttum muṟaiyum padu kadaiyum pal poruḷum – vyākyātā periyavāccān piḷḷai’s opinion is that these four words denote the meanings which are spoken of extensively in purāṇas (ancient narratives which would appear new each time they are read). According to śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-8-13 these are “sargaśca pratisargaśca vamśam manvantarāṇi ca I vamśanucaritam krutsnam mayātra tava kīrtitam īī” (creation, annihilation, lineage, period of manu and narratives of lineages were narrated by me to you).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “What is the reason for your not singing about others?” He responds saying that all the words and their meanings in the world have been created due to emperumān’s sankalpa (will), implying that only emperumān has the greatness to be sung about. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: pāttum muṟaiyum padu kadaiyum palporuḷum īttiya tīyum iruvisumbum kētta manuvum surudi maṟai nāngum māyan tanamāyaiyil pattadaṟpu pāttum muṟaiyum – musical and prosaic padu kadaiyum – itihāsas (epics) which came about to describe the old narrations pal poruḷum – purāṇas which describe many meanings īttiya tīyum – agni (fire) which was created to have all five elements within it due to pancīkaraṇam (homogenisation of the five elements) iru visumbum – expansive sky (thus the five elements) kētta manuvum – manu smruti which is spoken of in vēdas too surudi – the method of learning through uccāraṇa and anuccāraṇa (teacher saying once and disciples repeating the same three times) maṟai nāngum – the four vēdas*

**pāttum** – starting from kings and going up to sarvēśvara, the songs which were sung about the greatness of each. Don’t purāṇas sing extensively about celestial entities, *pitrus* (forefathers), kings as mentioned in mātsya purāṇam “*agnē: śivasya mahātmyam*”?

**muṟaiyum** – the order of lineages and *manvantaram* (manu’s period). parāśara bhagavān called this “*vamśamanvantarāṇi ca*” (lineage and manvantaram).

**padu kadaiyum** – *sruśti* (creation), *pralayam* (annihilation), history of lineages which happened earlier. parāśara maharishi called this “*sargaśca pratisargaśca vamśānucaritam krutsnam*”.

**palporuḷum** – apart from these, various other meanings which are explained in purāṇas regarding activities based on *varṇa* (four classifications based on qualities and profession) and *āśrama* (status in societal life), determination of terrestrial movement etc.

This is the interpretation of periyavāccān piḷḷai. The interpretation of appiḷḷai is:

**pāttum muṟaiyum** – prose and proper music which sings about prose.

**padu kadaiyum** – itihāsas (epics) which talk about old narratives describing how they happened.

**pal poruḷum** – various meanings described by purāṇas starting with *sargaśca pratisargaśca*.

**īttiya tīyum** – agni (fire) which was created in order to exhibit the other elements (ether, water, wind and earth) through the method of *pancīkaraṇam* (homogenisation). Homogenisation is the method by which [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) split each of the elements into eight parts, retaining four parts in the mother element and mixing each of the other four parts with each of the other four elements.

**iru visumbum** – sky (ether) which is bigger than the other four elements since it pervades them. Mentioning about these two elements (*agni* (fire) and *ākāśam* (sky)) is representative of the other three elements and the materials which are resultant from them.

In periyavāccān piḷḷai’s interpretation, all these are *vācyam* (meanings of words) and what follow are the *vācagams* (words). In appiḷḷai’s interpretation, *īttiya tīyum iruvisumbum* alone is meaning of words. Those which precede and succeed this are words.

**kētta manuvum . . . .daṟpu** – Just as mentioned in taittirīya samhitai 2-2 “*yadyai kinca manuavadat tat bhēśajam*” (whatever has been mentioned by manu is medicine), manusmruti which has been quoted in vēdas, and the four vēdas themselves which have been taught by *ācārya* (teacher) and heard by *śiśya* (due to which they are called as *śruti*) have the nature of total truth since they have been created by the *āścarya bhūta* (amazing entity, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) through his *sankalpa* (will). This implies that the connection between a word and its meaning has been created by emperumān’s *sankalpa*.

**māyai** – this refers to the knowledge through *sankalpa* (will) as brought out by *ārśa nigaṇḍu* (lexicon) saying “*māyā vayunam jñānam*” kaṇṇa (kriṣṇa) also mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 4-6](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/4-6/) “*ātma māyayā sambhavāmi*” (I am born out of my will). *daṟpu* – *tatvam*; truth.

We will take up the 77<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 77. Tarpennai

*(Hence) evvinaiyum – all tose vhic are knovn as sins māyum – vill be destroyed āl – hov vonderful*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**taṟpennait tānaṟīyānēlum – even if he did not know me, as I am \[ i.e. my true nature\]. This āḻvār has said of himself in tiruccanda viruttam 90 “kulangaḷāya īriraṇḍil onṟilum piṟandilēn, nalangalāya naṟkalaigaḷ nālilum navinṟilēn pulangaḷaindum venṟilēn poṟiyilēn” (I am not born in any of the four varṇas (classification of society based on qualities and activities), I have not learnt any of the four vēdas which show correct path to all, I have not won over the five senses and I am trapped in worldly pursuits such as śabdam (sound) etc ).  Even if emperumān does not know that I have such faults…..**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says “Let emperumān’s sankalpa, the words and their meanings etc remain. After seeing how he showered his distinguished grace on me, who is the epitome of all the faults, how can I praise the human beings with my tongue?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: taṟpennait tānaṟīyānēlum tadangadalaik kaṟkoṇḍu tūrtta kadal vaṇṇan eṟkoṇḍa vevvinaiyum nīnga vilangā manam vaittān evvinaiyum māyumāl kaṇḍu tadam kaladai – expansive ocean kal koṇḍu – with mountains tūrtta – one who built a dam and blocked it kadal vaṇṇan tān – emperumān who has a dark complexion similar to the ocean ennai – me (who is the epitome of all faults) taṟpu – real nature aṟiyānēlum – even if (he is) not aware of en koṇḍa – having stolen me vevvinaiyum – all the cruel sins nīnga – to leave kaṇḍu – thinking with his heart manam – his divine mind vilangā – not going elsewhere vaittān – mercifully kept (in a focussed way)*

**tānaṟiyānēlum** – Just as mentioned in “*yassarvajña: sarvavit*” even if he, who is the omniscient, does not know my faults. *ennait tānaṟīyānēlum* – Instead of saying ‘my faults’ why is [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) saying ‘me’? Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) feels that he is the epitome of all faults, he says ‘me’ instead of ‘my faults’. *aṟiyānēlum* – has it not been mentioned “*avijñāta hi bhaktānām āgassu kamalēkśaṇa:*” (when it comes to the faults of his devotees, the lotus-eyed ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) remains ignorant)?

**tadangadalaik kaṟkoṇḍu tūrtta kadal vaṇṇan** – he built a dam in the huge ocean with stones; he has dark and cool form, just like the ocean; he has qualities such as profundity etc similar to the ocean. He built a dam across the ocean with stones which would normally sink in the ocean, and achieved a feat which no one else can, in line with his quality of having *agaditagatanā* skill (achieving the impossible).

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) achieved such feats not only for [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) (sītāppirātti, for rescuing whom he built the dam) but also for me . . . .

**eṟkoṇḍa vevvinaiyum nīngak kaṇḍu** – when I was fully transformed like an insentient entity being enveloped by all the sins, he graced me such that all the sins ran helter skelter, just as mercifully mentioned by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in periya tiruvandādi “*vānō maṟi kadalō mārudamō tīyagamō kānō orungiṟṟum kaṇḍilamāl*” (How strange! After attaining emperumān, we have not seen where the sins covering us went. Did they disappear in the sky or in the ocean or in the wind or in fire or in the forest?) *vevvinai* – cruel sins; some people split this as *ev vinay* – all the sins.

**vilangā manam kaṇḍu vaittān** – he kept me in his mind through his *sankalpa* (divine will) and did not look at anything else. Just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) has mercifully mentioned in periya tiruvandādi 68 “*kallum kanaikadalum vaigunda vānādum pullenṟoḻindana*” and [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-4-9 “*panikkadalil paḷḷigōḷaip paḻagavittu ōdivandu* ” [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) left everything else and thought only of me.

**aṟiyānēlum – manam vaittān** – even if emperumān did not know my faults, he kept his mind on me. One who did not know, started knowing. One, who did not know my faults, multiplied my qualities ten times or hundred times and started engaging with me. One who is *avijñātā* (one who does not know faults) became *sahasrāmsu* (knowing (qualities) thousand times) \[from śrī viṣṇu sahasranāmam\].

**eēvvinaiyum māyumāl** – once he keeps his mind on me, is there any way by which the sins will not be destroyed? Thus, all sins such as *bhagavatapacāram* (disrespectful acts towards [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)), *bhāgavatapacāram* (disrespetful acts against emperumān’s followers) and *asahyāpacāram* (disrespectful acts against anyone without any reason) will get destroyed. The pathos behind this is that this is the *agaditagatanā* (doing the impossible) act carried out by emperumān towards me.

We will take up the 78<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 78. Kandu vananginarkku

*kāman – manmadan’s udal – body koṇḍa – one vho turned it into aśes tavattāṟku – śiva vho carries out te penance umai – (his vife) pārvati uṇartta – as śe indicated vaṇḍu alambum tār alangal nīṇ mudiyān tan peyarē kēttirundu – on hearing te divine names of emperumān vho dons garland on his long crovn, and on vhic beetles keep svarming angu – at tat moment itself ār alangal ānamai – becoming transformed āyndāl – if one vere to analyse kaṇḍu vanaginārkku – tose vho see and vorśip en ām kol – vhat sort of transformation vill take place?  (cannot be said easily)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**kāman udal koṇḍa tavattāṟku – to rudra who carries out penance, and who burnt the body of kāma (manmada) who created love (in him) towards his wife (pārvati). To rudra who has such a cruel nature that he would spend the strength of all his penance in destroying the person who created desire in him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Let the unseen benefit of removal of sins be there; āḻvār wonders at the joy that those who see him and worship him have. If we look at the transformation in rudra, who is full of tamō guṇam (ignorance, laziness etc) and who is always angry, on hearing the divine names of emperumān by  chance, should we say anything about those who see him directly and worship him? Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: kaṇḍu vaṇanginarkku ennāngol kāmanudal koṇḍa tavattāṟku umai uṇartta vaṇḍalambum tāralangal nīṇmudiyān than peyarē kēttirundu angu āralangal ānamaiyāl āyndu*

**udal koṇḍa tavattāṟku** – the benefit of carrying out penance is to destroy someone. One who is so hard hearted. The implied meaning is that such a hard hearted person melted on hearing the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**umai uṇartta** – pārvati says. This narrative is as follows: Once when rudra was meditating, pārvati asked him “*kam tvamarcayasē dēva!  kim daivamadikam tava* I *japahōma namaskārai: tam vadasva mahēśvara* īī” (Oh Lord! Who are you vorśipping through *japa* (prayers), *hōma* (ritual by growing fire), *namaskāra* (salutation)? Who is the deity who is superior to you? mahēśvara! Please tell me about him). She started singing the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in a way which was sweet to the ears. rudra asked her to sing the divine names more and more and became very emotionally weak.

**umai uṇartta** – hearing her recite the divine names of emperumān as pārvati was asking him doubts by chance, rudra started hearing from her, matter relating to emperumān, thus changing the order of teacher – student (the student became teacher and teacher became student).

**vaṇḍalambum tāralangal nīṇmudiyān than peyarē kēttirundu** – the moment he heard and meditated on the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), on whose long crown tuḷasi garlands were decorated and on which beetles came to swim in the nectar.

**vaṇḍalambum tāralangal nīṇmudiyān** – [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully said in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-9-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/10/thiruvaimozhi-1-9-7-tholinai-melum/) “*sudar mudi mēlum punainda taṇṇanduyāyudai ammān*” (my lord who has cool tuḷasi on his resplendent crown); *vaṇḍalambu* – some interpret this as beetles humming.

**nīṇ mudiyān** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has a long crown which manifests his supremacy, as mercifully mentioned by [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/) in śrī varadarāja stavam “*ādirājyamadikam bhuvanānāmīśa tē piśunayan kila mouli:*” (your crown bears the tale that you are the lord of all the worlds).

**angu āralangalānamai āyndāl kaṇḍu vaṇanginārkku ennāngol** – as soon as one hears emperumān’s divine name, just as mercifully sung by various [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) “*kālāḻum nenjaḻiyum kaṇ suḻalum*” (I will be unable to stand as legs will wobble, heart will sink, eyes will roll), “*uḷḷelāmurugi kural taḻuttoḻindēn*” (heart melted and voice stammered), “*āhlādaśīta nētrāmbū:  pulakīkruta gātravān sadā paraguṇāviśta:*” (the devotee on hearing the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), will have tears of joy and goose pimples, being enslaved by the superior qualities of emperumān). When one analyses these situations, in all of which the devotee hears the divine names of emperumān, what will be the state of those who see emperumān directly and worship him? *alangal* – weariness; faintness.

**āralangalānamai** – becoming very weary; alternatively, *alangal* could also mean garland, hence people would interpret this as – devotees withering and falling down like a garland which has dried up fully.

We will take up the 79<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 79. Ayndu kondu

*ādi – one vho is te cause for te universe perumānai – sarvēśvara (lord of all) anbināl – vit affection āyndu koṇḍu – meditating vāynda manattu – (teir) apt hearts irutta vallār tāmum – tose vho are capable of establiśing tam vaigundam – te paramapadam vhic is tere for tem viraindu – quickly kāṇbār – desirous of seeing ēynda – fitting vit te soul tam mey – teir bodies kundam āga – as disease virumbuvar – vill consider*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**āyndu koṇḍu ādippirānai anbināl – meditating on sarvēśvara, who is the cause for the universe, with love. Has it not been said in atarvaśikai “kāraṇam tu dēya:” (only the entity which created the universe is apt for meditation)?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār had mentioned that the joy of those who see him directly is huge. When asked if engagement with this śarīra ( body) will not be a hurdle to this joy, he says that those who have the zeal to reach paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) thinking of sarvēśvara constantly, will think of the śarīra as a disease as mentioned in the saying “śarīram vraṇavat paśyēt” (let him see the body as a wound) and hence engagement with this body will not be a hurdle to the joy born out of experiencing emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: āyndu koṇḍu ādipperumānai anbināl vāynda manattirutta vallārgaḷ ēynda tam meykundamāga virumbavarē tāmum tam vaigundam kāṇbār viraindu*

**vāynda manattirutta vallārgaḷ** – those who are capable of keeping in their hearts, which will not say “No” when they wish to keep [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in their hearts

**vāynda manam** – it is the mind which will be ahead of the person himself in attaining emperumān as mentioned in periya tiruvandādi 1 “*munduṟṟa nenjē*” (Oh heart which is ahead of me!) and in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-7-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/09/thiruvaimozhi-1-7-8-ennai-negizhkkilum/) “*ennai negiḻkkilum ennudaiya nannenjam tannai agalvikkat tānum killān ini*” (even if he could separate from me, he is not capable of separating from my heart). It is the heart on which emperumān falls head over heels, as mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 10-8-6 “*iruppēn enṟu en nenju niṟaiyap pugundān*” (he entered my heart completely saying that he would like to stay there).

**ādipperumānai vāynda manattirutta vallārgaḷ** – those who are capable of firmly establishing the causative entity in their hearts as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-6-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/04/thiruvaimozhi-2-6-7-mudiyadhadhu-en/) “*muḻuvēḻ ulagumuṇḍān ugandu vandu adiyēnuḷ pugundān agavalvānumallan ini*” (the one who swallowed all the worlds, entered my heart desirously and will not separate from it, henceforth).

**manattirutta vallārgaḷ** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) emphasises that it is very difficult to keep [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in the mind which always prevaricates as mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 6-34](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/6-34/) “*cancalam hi mana:*” (mind always prevaricates) and in periya tirumoḻi 1-1-4 “*ninṟavā nillā nenju*” (heart which is not steady).

**vallārgaḷ tāmum** – this describes the greatness of those who are capable of achieving this feat.

**vallārgaḷ tāmum** – these people who have the interest in matters relating to emperumān who is the means (to attain him).

**tam vaigundam viraindu kāṇbār** – those who are desirous of reaching soon that paramapadam wherein there will be no hurdle for experiencing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**tam vaigundam** – that paramapadam which [*sarvēśvara*](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/), the eternal prince of śrīvaikuṇṭam, has created for his followers as mentioned in puruśa sūktam “*marīcīnām padam*” and in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-9-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thiruvaimozhi-3-9-9-vay-kondu/) “*vānavar nādu*”.

**viraindu kāṇbār** – instead of patiently waiting, saying  “we will attain it when it comes”, being in a hurry to see, just like hurrying to see something which has been lost.

**ēynda  tam meygundamāgavē virumbuvar** – because of that desire (to see śrīvaikuṇṭam) they will consider their physical bodies, fitting with them, as disease only.

**ēynda tam mey** – even after getting knowledge, considering their bodies which are united together with souls

**kundamāgavē virumbuvar** – they will consider their bodies as hurdles in attaining *śrīvaikuṇṭam*. Instead of thinking of bodies as cause for joy or cause for joy and sorrow, they will consider their bodies as cause for only sorrow. *kundam* – disease.

We will move on to the 80<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 80. Viraindu adaimin

*parandu – pervading everyvhere ādina – dancing ulagam – distinguiśed follovers pādina – te divine names recited kēttu – since tey vere heard padunagaram vāsal – at te entrance of hell kadavu – doors vīdina – fell dovn mēl oru nāḷ – at an earlier point of time veḷḷam parakka – vhen te flood due to deluge spread ulagam – all te vorlds karandu – hiding (inside his stomac) kāttu aḷitta – removed te sorrov and protected kaṇṇan – kanna pirān (kriṣṇa) viraindu adaimin – go soon and bov dovn to him.*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**viraindadaimin – Looking at (the extent of) your sins, śouldn’t you attain him fast? Who should we attain? āḻvār says we should attain the one who has protection of those who are caught in deluge as his duty.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When āḻvār was told “Let the knowledgeable consider their bodies as disease. For us the connection with samsāram (materialistic realm) is not ending”, he responds “If you attain kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) who protected the world when it was under deluge, you can also uplift yourselves”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: virandadaimin mēlorunāḷ veḷḷam parakkak karandulagam kāttaḷitta kaṇṇan parandulagam pādina ādina kēttup padunaragam vīdina vāsaṟ kadavu*

**mēl oru nāḷ** – just as the flood of samsāram (materialistic realm) is surrounding you today, the flood of deluge had surrounded the worlds then.

**ulagam karandu kāttu** – hiding all the worlds inside his divine stomach; even if deluge came searching for a long time, hiding and protecting them inside his divine stomach so that deluge vouldn’t know their (worlds’) whereabouts.

**aḷitta kaṇṇan** – attain such kaṇṇan who protected, fast. Also, kaṇṇan who showered his mercy on hearing the worlds singing about him (which comes later in the pāsuram)

When the fear of hell is troubling us, how do we attain him?

**parandu ādina ulagam pādina kēttu** – hearing the divine names of emperumān from śrīvaiṣṇavas who have pervaded all over the world and who are dancing. As the saying goes “*ulagam enbadu uyarndōr māttē*” (world is with those who are distinguished), the word *ulagam* refers to followers of emperumān who are greater than nityasūris.

**parandu ādina ulagam ādina kēttu** – [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-2-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/05/thiruvaimozhi-5-2-4-idam-kol/) “*kidandum irundum eḻundum gīdam palapala pādi nadandum paṟandum kunittum nādagam seyginṟanavē*” ((followers of emperumān) were lying down, sitting, standing, walking around, singing many songs on emperumān, dancing without their legs touching the ground and joyfully going around) and in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-2-6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/05/thiruvaimozhi-5-2-6-konruyir-unnum/) “*nanṟisai pādiyum tuḷḷiyādiyum jñālam parandār*” ((followers of emperumān were) singing songs happily, jumping up and down, pervading all over the world ).

**kēttu padu naragam** – after hearing the divine names of emperumān \[through these songs etc\], hell became heaven as mentioned in [tirumālai 12](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-12/) “*namanum muṟgalanum pēsa naragil ninṟārgaḷ kētka naragamē suvargamāgum nāmangaḷudaiya nambi*” (emperumān. the benefactor, has such sweet names that once when yama and mudgala \[a soul which had reached hell\] were talking about the divine names of emperumān, hell got transformed into heaven).

**naragam vīdina vāsal kadavu** – the doors at the entrance to hell broke down completely. The implication is that there is no need for you (samsāris) to fear anything.

**kaṇṇan ulgam parandu pādina ādina kēttup padu naragam vīdina vāsaṟ kadavu** – [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) explained the meanings of this pāsuram in his [tiruvāimoḻi 5-2-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/05/thiruvaimozhi-5-2-1-poliga-poliga/) “*kadalvaṇṇan bhūdangaḷ maṇmēl maliyappugundu isaipādi ādi uḻidarak kaṇḍōm*” (we saw the followers of emperumān who has the complexion of ocean, pervading all over this world, singing sweet songs \[on emperumān\] and dancing here and there).

Alternatively, we can consider that (tirumaḻisai) [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) was mercifully telling this pāsuram to the messengers of yama.

**vīdina vāsal kadavu viraindu adaimin** – after hearing the divine names of emperumān, those who were inside hell, broke the doors of hell and went out. Oh messengers of yama! Come fast and bolt the doors of hell. [poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/), in his [mudal tiruvandādi 87](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/09/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-87/) mercifully said “*ini yār puguvār eḻu naraga vāsal muniyādu mūrittāḷ kōṇmin*” (Oh messengers of yama! Who will henceforth enter the gates of hell? Without getting angry, close the gates of hell with a strong latch).

We will move on to the 81<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 81. Kadavu manam enrum

*(due to tat) manam – mind kadavu enṟum – tinking ( on a fev occasions) to be te hurdle for attaining emperumān (manam) kāṇalām enṟum – tinking (te mind) to be te aid in seeing emperumān (on a fev oter occasions) kudaiyum vinai āvi tīrndēn – ī avoided te heart vhic had unsteadiness (in svinging betveen tese tvo extremes) as its business*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**kadavu manam enṟum . . . . tīrndēn – āḻvār says that he avoided something which was a huge hurdle for others in attaining emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*As soon as āḻvār mentioned “viraindadaimin” (attain emperumān soon), all the people in the world became inclined towards bhagavān. Becoming very pleased after looking at this rare deed of āḻvār, emperumān asked him “āḻvīr! Is there anything that I can do in return for what you have done?” āḻvār replies “When you, who are the source for my doing such deeds, are there and had rectified my mind to enable it to reap rich harvest, what else is there to be done for me?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: kadavu manam enṟum kāṇalām enṟum kudaiyum vinaiyāvi tīrndēn vidaiyāga naṟṟamiḻai vitti en uḷḷattai nī viḷaittāy kaṟṟa moḻiyāgik kalandu nī – you (who have no shortcoming in knowledge, power etc) kaṟṟa moḻiyāgi – being the meaning for the tamiḻ words that I have learnt kalandu – mingling with me, like water mingling with water nal tamiḻai – the distinguished language of tamiḻ vidaiyāga vitti – sowing it as seed en uḷḷattai – my heart viḷaittāy – you made (this prabandam \[divine hymn\]) to grow*

What is that hurdle?

**kadavu manam enṟum kāṇalām enrum kudaiyum vinai āvi** – Just as it is mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-7-28 “*mana Eva manuśyāṇām kāraṇam banda mōkśayō:* I *bandāya viśayāsangi muktyai nirviśayam mana:* īī” (it is the heart alone which is the reason for people getting chained to samsāram as well as getting liberated from it; if it is engaged with śabda (sensory objects such as sound) etc it is chained and if it is not engaged with śabda etc, it is liberated), it is heart alone which acts as a hurdle like a door in seeing the supreme being and it is heart alone which is the means for seeing him. āḻvār says that he avoided getting an unsteady heart.

**kadavu manam enṟum kāṇalām enṟum** – did not kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) say in [śrī bhagavat gītā 6-5](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/6-5/) “*ātmaiva hyātmanō bandurātmaiva ripurātmana:*” (it is the heart alone which is friendly towards ātmā (soul) and it is the heart alone which is inimical towards the ātmā)!

**kudaiyum vinai āvi tīrndēn** – I avoided getting a heart which was unsteady. The meaning behind this is that āḻvār obtained a heart which was fully focussed on matters relating to emperumān.

**āvi** – the word *āvi* which refers to *prāṇa* (breath) indicates heart which is the seat for breath. In the gītā ślōkam shown above (6-5) the word ātmā, as well as the word *uyir* in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-3-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/02/thiruvaimozhi-2-3-1-unil-vazh/) “*ūnil vāḻum uyirē*” refer to heart. In the same way, the word *āvi* refers to heart.

In the rest of the pāsuram āḻvār says the reason for his avoiding the unsteadiness which others are afflicted with.

**vidaiyāga nal tamiḻai vitti** – sowing the seed of beautiful tamiḻ language, without any fault in its literature and grammar, in my heart.

**kaṟṟa moḻiyāgik kalandu** – entering inside me as the meaning for the tamiḻ language that I am habituated with, from the time of my birth.

**moḻiyāgi** – as the meaning for the language

**en uḷḷattai nī viḷaittāy** – Just as it is mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-3-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thiruvaimozhi-5-3-4-uravar-kavvai/) “*nenjapperum sey*” in the heart which is the arable land, you, who are without any shortcoming in knowledge or power, sowed the seed of tamiḻ language as the cultivating farmer and made these pāsurams (hymns) which are like the crop, to grow.

We will move on to the 82<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 82. Kalandan en ullattu

*kāmavēḷ tādai – one vho is manmata’s (cupid’s) fater alarnda alargaḷ ittēttum īsanum – sivan vho praises emperumān after offering blossomed flovers nānmuganum – brahmā enṟa ivargaḷ – (distinguiśed) dēvas suc as tese vittu ētta māttāda – one vho cannot be praised completely vēndu – dēvādidēva (lord of all dēvas) \[emperumān\] en uḷḷattu – in my heart kalandān – mingled īdu oppadu nalam tānum uṇḍē – is tere any benefit equalling tis benefit? (no, tere is none).*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**kalandān en uḷḷattu – emperumān mingled, like water mingles with water, with my heart which has been engaged with lowly worldly matters.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār is wondering whether it was just that emperumān created a desire for āḻvār in tamiḻ language and prevented him from having a wavering heart. He says aloud to the entire world, in this pāsuram, that there cannot be a greater benefit, which emperumān did for him, than his mingling with āḻvār’s heart despite his being a supreme being. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: kalandān ennuḷḷattuk kāmavēḷ tādai nalandānum īdoppaduṇḍē alarndalargaḷ ittēttum īsanum nānmuganum enṟivargaḷ vittētta māttāda vēndu*

Who is such an entity?

**kāmavēḷ tādai** – one who is called as *manmata* (cupid) among *manmata* since he gave birth to *manmata* (lord of love) who attracts others towards him because of his beauty. Such manmata who is most beautiful desired a lowly entity such as I (āḻvār) and mingled. *kāmavēḷ tādai* – father of *manmata*.

**kāmavēḷ** – *kāman* – one who creates desire in others. *vēḷ* ***–*** beautiful person. *kāman* is samskruta word and *vēḷ* is tamiḻ word.

Is it that he has only beauty?

**alarndalargaḷ . . . vēndu** – Siva and brahmā who think of themselves as īśvara (one who controls all) offered freshly blossomed flowers at the divine feet of emperumān and praised him to their hearts’ content. Such emperumān who cannot be fully praised by brahmā, Siva et al mingled with me, says āḻvār.

**īsanum nānmuganum enṟivargaḷ** – this indicates the prominence of Siva and brahmā who are heads of dēvas.

**vittētta** – Praising handsomely. Praising with full throat \[loudly\]. Praising totally.

**vēndu kalandān en uḷḷattu** – one who is supreme, mingled with a very lowly person due to his desire.

**īdu oppadu nalandānum uṇḍē** – Is there any benefit which is equal to this?

**alarndalargaḷ . . .vittētta māttāda vēndu kalandān; īdoppadu nalandāṇum uṇḍē** – rudra, brahmā et al worship emperumān with just blossomed flowers and praise emperumān on their own with the aim of attaining emperumān through their efforts just like people pick up grains from fields with difficulty for their sustenance. On the other hand, emperumān, of his own accord, enters āḻvār’s heart, head over heels. āḻvār asks whether there could there be any equivalent to this experience.

We will move on to the 83<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 83. Vendaray vinnavaray

*(even after bestoving all tese benefits) tan āṟṟān – being not satisfied internally tān kodukkum – giving (everyting) himself pinnāl – despite giving all tese tān seyyum pidir – he vill give te distinguiśed mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vēndarāy – being like kings who will function such that the strong people do not harm the weaklings, so long as they are in this world. Has it not been said that kings are denomination of bhagavān, as in the saying “nāviṣṇu: pritivīpati:” (king is not different from viṣṇu) and in tiruvāimoḻi 4-4-8 “tiruvudai mannaraik kāṇil tirumālaik kaṇḍēnēyennum” (if she looks at the king, she will say that she has seen tirumāḷ (emperumān))?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār mentioned about a benefit which had no equivalent. Is this all that emperumān does for his followers? Till such time that they are here, without any regulations about what he does, emperumān carries out all the protective actions; if the follower leaves this body, he gives him svarga (heaven); more distinguished than these, he also grants paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam); even after granting all these, isn’t he the entity who feels sad that he has not done anything for his follower? āḻvār thus explains in this pāsuram, the meaning that he had given in the previous pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: vēndarāy viṇṇavarāy viṇṇagait taṇṇaḷiyāy māndarāy mādāy maṟṟellāmāy sārndavarkkut than āṟṟān nēmiyān mālvaṇṇan tān kodukkum pinnāl tān seyyum pidir nēmiyān – one who has the divine disc in his hand māl vaṇṇan – emperumān who is the epitome of affection sārndavarkku – towards those who attain him vēndar āy – being kings viṇṇavar āy – being dēvas (celestial entities, who bestow rain) viṇ āgi – being the svarga (heaven) which gives enjoyment taṇṇaḷi āy – being the one who grants his grace (in heaven) māndar āy – being relatives mādā āy – being the one who gives all the benefits given by a mother maṟṟu ellām āy – being the one who carries out all the other types of benefits*

**viṇṇavarāy** – Is it not true that even if kings rule the land properly, if there is no water resource, there will not be any benefit to the people? Hence, (emperumān functions as) being the celestial entities who are the cause for rain which provides water resources to people in the world. vyāsa said “*sarva ijyāśca kriṣṇa:*” (all celestial entities are kriṣṇa).

**viṇṇāgi** – being *svarga* (heaven) which is distinct from earth and which is the benefit for deeds carried out on earth such as *jyōtiśtōma* (a type of ritual) etc. nammāḻvār too mercifully said in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-10-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/11/thiruvaimozhi-3-10-7-thunbamum-inbamum/) “*iniya nalvān suvargangaḷumāy*” (being the sweet paramapadam and *svarga*)

**taṇṇaḷiyāy** – being the grace showered by indra et al celestial entities. The opinion here is that it is only emperumān who grants happiness through indra et al in *svarga*. emperumān said himself in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7-22](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-22/) “*labhatē ca tata: kāmān mayaiva vihitān hi tān*” ((ātmā) attains all the desires which are granted only by me through celestial entities).

**māndarāy** – being relatives and friends who provide help. subālōpaniśat says “*suhrut gatir nārāyaṇa:*” (attain nārāyaṇa who is good at heart).

**mādāy** – being the mother who gave birth to. This applies to father too. Just as it is mentioned in the saying “*sahasram hi pitur mātā*” (being thousand times greater than father and mother) mentioning mother is also applicable to father. periya tiruvandādi 5 says “*peṟṟa tāy nīyē piṟappitta tandai nī*” (you are the mother who gave birth to and the father who was responsible for the birth) and subālōpaniśat “*mātā pitā nārāyaṇa*” (nārāyaṇa is the mother and the father).

**mādāy** – also could  be construed as being beautiful. *mādu* – beauty. It is emperumān’s beauty which the ātmā enjoys among all the beautiful things enjoyed.Hasn’t emperumān himself said in bhagavat gītā “*yad yad vibhūtimat satvam śrīmadūrgitamēva vā* I *tattadēvāgacca tvam mama tējōmśa sambhavam*  īī ” (know that all the materials which are full of opulence, which are beautiful, which are firm are born out of my radiance) and nammāḻvār in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-2-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/05/thiruvaimozhi-1-2-7-adangu-ezhil/) “*adangeḻil sampattu adangakkaṇḍu īsan adangeḻil ahdenṟu adanguga uḷḷē*”  (after looking at all the beautiful wealth of emperumān, one should realise that he is also one among his wealth and become humble)!

**maṟṟellāmāy** – being other relatives, means and benefit as mentioned in “*bhrātā nivāsa: śaraṇam*” (surrendering to my elder brother) and periya tiruvandādi 5 “*maṟṟaiyārāvārum nī pēsil*” (if I have to list out all the benefits that I have obtained from you, you are the ācārya (teacher) too)

For whom is emperumān like these?

**sārndavarkku** – he is like these for those who attain him.

**than āṟṟān** – being dissatisfied with himself for not having done enough for his followers despite doing all these. īsn’t he the entity who says as in mahābhāratam “*gōvindēti yadāgrandat krūṣṇā mām dūravāsinam* I *ruṇam pravruddamiva mē hrudayānnāpasappati*  īī ” (the crying out of draupadi who cried out *gōvindā* when I was at a far away distance is not leaving my heart just like a loan which has kept accruing interest)!

**nēmiyān** – one who manifests himself with his divine disc which removes enemies and which enables his followers to enjoy him.

**māl vaṇṇan tān kodukkum** – being the epitome of affection, one who keeps giving benefits like the above despite saying that he has not given anything.

**pinnāl tān seyyum pidir** – even after giving everything on earth as well in heaven etc, he gives *paramapadam* to his followers. *pidir* **–** remaining. In other words, he will give after he has enjoyed. After emperumān has enjoyed the cētana (sentient entity) and is deeply immersed in the ocean of nectar, the cētana will be very joyous seeing the happiness of emperumān and in turn will immerse himself in emperumān’s ocean of nectar. Just as it is mentioned in taittirīya upaniśats “*aham annam aham annam*” īśvara enjoys cētana  as his food and is joyous. Looking at that joy, cētana will enjoy that joy as the *śēśabhūtam* (remaining food after partaking by emperumān). vēdānta dēsika said of this as “*bhungktē svabhōgamakhilam patibhōga śēśam*” (mukta (soul liberated from samsāram) enjoys as the remainder of emperumān’s enjoyment)

It is proper for emperumān to grant mōkśam for his follower. But is it proper on his part to grant happiness in this world and *svarga*? Since he is *sakalapalapradan*  (one who grants all benefits), it is not wrong on his part to grant other benefits too, based on the desire of his follower. For the sake of pirātti (sītāppirātti) when jatāyu gave up his life, not knowing what he is doing, did śrī rāma not grant everything without any distinction between the pleasures on earth and mōkśa as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam ślōkam 68-30 “*yā gatir yajña śeelānām āhitāgnēśca yā gati:* I *aparāvartinām yā ca yā ca bhūmippradāyinām* I *mayā tvam samanugjñātō gacca lōkānanuttamān*  īī” (For those who observe rituals, for those who carry out rites with fire, for those who donate land and for those *muktas* who do not return here ever, whatever great benefits are granted, you too, who are directed by me, will enjoy all those benefits in all those worlds )!

We will take up the 84<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 84. Pidirum manam illen

*(vhatever may be) avan enakku nērān – tat rudra vill not compare vit me (vho is a permanent servitor)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**pidirum manam illēn – I do not have a wavering heart. I have a heart which is focussed on matters relating to emperumān. āḻvār re-establishes what he had said in pāsuram 81 “kadavu manam enṟum kāṇalām enṟum kudaiyum vinai āvi tīrndēn”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that just as emperumān is the most distinguished when it comes to bestowing benefits, he \[āḻvār\] is also incomparable when it comes to being affectionate towards emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: pidirum manam illēn pinjagan tannodu edirvan avan enakku nērān adirum kaḻaṟkāla mannanaiyē kaṇṇanaiyē nāḷum toḻum kādal pūṇḍēn toḻil adirum kaḻal kāla – one who is donning the resounding anklet on his divine foot mannanai – the king of kings kaṇṇanaiyē – kaṇṇapirān (kriṣṇa) nāḷum – every day toḻum toḻil – making the activity of vorśipping as profession kādal pūṇḍēn – I, who am having affection \[for emperumān\] pidirum manam illēn – do not have a wavering heart pinjagan tannodum edirvan – I will be equalling rudra in knowledge*

When asked whether there is anyone who is comparable to him in having a focussed mind, he says…

**pinjagan tannodu edirvan** – I will be equivalent to Siva who is very knowledgeable and who bestows knowledge on others as mentioned in mātsya purāṇam 67-41 “*īśvarāt jñānam anviccēt*” (let the cētana desire knowledge from Siva).

When asked whether there is total equality between rudra and him in all aspects, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says . . .

**avan enakku nērān** – rudra will not equal me in other matters. When *satva guṇas* (purely good qualities) gain prominence rudra will say as in īśvara samhitai “*dāsabhūtās svata: sarvē hyātmāna: paramātmana:*  I *atō’hamapi tē dāsa iti matvā namāmyaham* īī” (all the jīvātmās (sentient entities) are by nature servile to you; hence, knowing that I am also your servitor, I am bowing down to you) and as in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-33-41 “*kriṣṇa kriṣṇa mahābāhō jānētvām puruśōttamam* ” (Oh kriṣṇa, with mighty arms! I know that you are the greatest among all souls). Since rudra says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the protector, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that rudra is his equal to an extent. When qualities of *rajas* (passion) and *tamas* (ignorance) are prominent, rudra will say as in [śrī bhagavat gītā 16-14](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/16-14/) “*īśvarōham*” (I am the controller) and will even oppose emperumān in warfare. Such rudra will not be equal to me, says āḻvār.

āḻvār mercifully explains the basis for the distinction that he has to say that rudra is not equal to him, in the coming lines.

**adirum kaḻaṟkāla mannanaiyē** – the only one who annihilates his enemies by the sheer roaring sound of his anklets, thus protecting his followers. This indicates his greatness. Now, in the next word, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) indicates his simplicity too.

**kaṇṇanaiyē** – kriṣṇa who was born in the clan of covherds.

**nāḷum tozum toḻil** – vorśipping [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as full-time profession.

**kādal pūṇḍēn** – as the basis for that, I developed affection towards emperumān. Thus, for me who is a permanent servitor for kaṇṇan, rudra, who fought against kaṇṇan in the bāṇāsura *yuddam* (war) cannot be an equal, implies [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/).

We will take up the 85<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 85. Tolil enakku

*Hence enakku – for me toḻil – profession tollai māl tan nāmam ētta – is only to praise te divine names of tat long-standing sarvēśvara (supreme being) enakku – for me maṟṟaduvē – only vit recitation of śrī rāma’s divine names, as mentioned earlier poḻudu pōdum – time vill pass on*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**toḻil enakkut tollai māl than nāmam ētta – the profession for me is to praise the sarvēśvara (supreme being) who is there for a very long time.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*A few samsāris asked āḻvār “You are saying that you have made vorśipping emperumān as your profession. Is it not enough if you are involved with emperumān for sometime and be involved with other activities at other times?” āḻvār tells them “I have just enough time for meditating on the victorious deeds of cakravarty tirumagan (śrī rāma, son of emperor daśarata). Where is the time for engaging with other activities?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: toḻil enakkut tollaimāl than nāmam ētta poḻudu enakku maṟṟaduvē pōdum kaḻi sinatta vallāḷan vānararkōn vāli madanaḻitta villāḷan nenjattuḷan kaḻi sinatta – one who has lot of anger vallāḷan – and who has lot of strength vānarar kōn – being the king of monkeys vāli – vāli’s madan – arrogance aḻitta – one who destroyed vil āḷan – śrī rāma, who was controlling kōdaṇḍam, his bow nenjattu – in my heart uḷan – has taken residence*

āren’t we \[samsāris\] carrying out a lot of other work and in between, reciting [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine names? Why cannot you too \[[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/)\] do like that?

**poḻudu enakku maṟṟaduvē pōdum** – for me, time is spent in engaging with *bhagavat viśayam* (matters relating to emperumān). He says next that even among the matters relating to emperumān, there is time for engaging with only one of his incarnations, śrī rāma and there is no time for other matters.

**kaḻi sinatta vallāḷan** – one can see vāli’s cruel anger in his act of rejecting his younger brother who fell at his feet and begged him \[for forgivance\].

**vallāḷan** – one could see his great strength in his act of tying rāvaṇa as if he were an insect

**vānarar kōn** – one who controlled and ruled over the monkeys through his anger and strength and was their king

**kaḻi sinatta vallāḷan vānarar kōn** – It is clear that unlike śrī rāma who engaged his citizens through his divine form and qualities such as magnanimity etc to make them say “*iccāmōhi mahābāhum*” (we desire to see śrī rāma who has strong shoulders as the king), vāli had subjugated the monkeys through his anger and strength and ruled over them.

**vāli madanaḻitta** – one who destroyed the arrogance of vāli

**suḻi sinatta vāli madanaḻitta** – the meaning implied here is that śrī rāma destroyed the vanity of vāli since vāli had condemned his younger brother \[sugrīva\] who had fallen at his feet seeking refuge.

**vallāḷan madanaḻitta** – the opinion here is that vāli, instead of utilising his strength for controlling people like rāvaṇa, used it for befriending them and hence is apt to be controlled.

**vānararkōn madanaḻitta** – the meaning here is that since vāli is a dangerous animal, it is fit to destroy his pride by hitting him by hiding, insead of fighting with him in direct combat.

**madanaḻitta villāḷan nenjattuḷan** – the one who had destroyed the arrogance of vāli is constantly residing in my heart. The opinion here is that when such is the case, where is the time for me to engage with other activities.

We will move on to the 86<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 86. Ulan kanday

*nal nenjē – ōh heart vho is apt for me! uttaman – (one vho protects vitout expecting anyting in return) emperumān vho is te best among all puruśas (souls) uḷan kaṇḍāy – see tat he exists (to protect us) enṟum uḷan kaṇḍāy – see tat (at all places) at all times (in protecting us) he exists uḷḷuvār uḷḷattu – in te minds of tose vho accept to tink of him uḷan kaṇḍāy – see tat he exists permanently tan oppān īsan – (since tere is none vho equals him) tat emperumān vho is his ovn equal tamiyēṟku – for me, vho is vitout any means en oppāṟku – and for tose vho, like me, are vitout any means tān āy uḷan kāṇ – see tat he himself exists as te protector imai – knov tis*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**uḷan kaṇḍāy – āḻvār is not instructing us regarding the existence of emperumān. Then, what is he implying? He tells his heart to see emperumān, who is there as the protector. He tells his heart to see that even if unavoidable problems crop up due to the heart or due to anyone else, he is there, capable of avoiding those problems.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the earlier pāsurams, āḻvār spoke extensively, for the all the world to know, about the supreme being’s basic nature, divine form, auspicious qualities and wealth. In this pāsuram he tells his divine mind “I had explained the presence of emperumān even to those who denied his existence. You also meditate on this meaning”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meaning: uḷan kaṇḍāy nannenjē uttaman enṟum uḷan kaṇḍāy uḷḷuvār uḷḷattu uḷan kaṇḍāy tannoppān tānāy uḷan kān tamiyēṟku ennoppārkku īsan imai*

**nannenjē** – Oh heart which has the capability to instruct others regarding this! Since it has the capability to instruct others, is it required to say that is has the capability to listen about this?

**nannenjē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) celebrates his heart just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) did in his [tiruvāimoḻi 1-10-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/10/thiruvaimozhi-1-10-4-nenjame-nallai/) ”*nenjamē! nallai nallai unnaip peṟṟāl en seyyōm ini enna kuṟaivinam* ” (Oh heart! What can I not do if I get such a good entity like you! What shortcoming will I have!)

When [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) becomes a protector, will he expect anything?

**uḷan kaṇḍāy nannenjē uttaman** – āḻvār says that emperumān is the best among all, who does not expect anything when he protects. One who carries out a deed for his own benefit is called as *adaman* (lowliest among all); one who carries out a deed for his as well as others’ benefit is called as *madyaman* (in-between); one who carries out a deed for the benefit of only others is called as *uttaman* (best among all). īsn’t emperumān *ōngi ulagaḷanda uttaman* (the best entity who grew up and measured all the worlds, for the benefit of his followers)!

Being a totally independent entity, though he has been protecting so far without expecting anything, what will he do now?

**uttaman enṟum uḷan kaṇḍāy** – just as he had been protecting in samsāram all along without any reason, hereafter he will give himself to be enjoy in śrīvaikuṇṭam and protect.

For getting this, should the jīvātmā have any qualification?

**uḷḷuvār uḷḷattu uḷan kaṇḍāy** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will reside in the heart of one who agrees to think of him instead of vowing ‘I will not think about you’. It is implied that the only quality required for the cētana (sentient entity) to make emperumān as a protector is to agree to think about emperumān.

**uḷḷuvār uḷḷattu uḷan** – [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) and his divine daughter, [āṇḍāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/andal/), mercifully mentioned respectively, in [tiruppallāṇḍu 4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu-4-edunilam/) “*kūdumanam udaiyīrgaḷ varampoḻi vandollaik kūduminō*” (if you have the desire to be with us, break out of the boundary of one enjoying himself/herself and join with us immediately) and in [tiruppāvai 1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2014/12/thiruppavai-01-margazhi-thingal/) “*pōduvīr pōduminō*” (those who are desirous (of being together with us while taking bath), join us).

**tannoppān īsan** – Just as śvetāśvatara upaniśat said “ *na tatsamaścābhyadigaśca druśyatē* ” (there is none equalling you; we cannot see anyone superior to you) and [tiruvāimoḻi 2-3-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/02/thiruvaimozhi-2-3-2-oththar-mikkarai/) “*ottār mikkāraiyilaiyāya māmāyā*” (Oh amazing entitiy who does not have anyone equal or superior!), sarvēśvara is one who is his own equal. The opinion is that there is none to equal him in protecting others causelessly.

**īsan** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) hints here that since emperumān is the owner, he is bound to protect his possession.

**tamiyēṟku ennoppārkkut tānāy uḷan kāṇ** – (āḻvār tells his heart to see that) the supreme being is the protector for him as well as for those like him who are without any means.

**tamiyēṟku** – one who is secluded. Seclusion here refers to the quality of being without any means in protecting oneself just as it is mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-7-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/thiruvaimozhi-5-7-1-norra-nonbilen/) “*nōṟṟa nōnbilēn nuṇṇaṟivilēn*” (I am without karmayōga (path of attaining emperumān through carrying out ordained deeds) or jñāna yōga (path of knowledge for attaining emperumān))

**tānāyuḷan** – emperumān is there as the protector. The quality of protection is emperumān’s basic nature; it is the essential quality to identify him.

**imai** – \[[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) tells his heart\] know this meaning .

We will take up the 87<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.
