## 87. Imayap perumalai pol

*(īs it not emperumān vho did all tese?)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**imayap perumalai pōl indiranārkkitta samaya virundu uṇḍku Ar kāppār – every year, the herdsmen at gōkulam used to offer food to indra as a way of expressing their gratitude for giving them good rain, as mentioned by periyāḻvār in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi “attukkuvi sōṟṟup paruppadamum tayir vāviyum neyyaḷaṟum ”(food cooked and heaped like a mountain, with a stream of curd and rice made with ghee (clarified butter)). One year, the food, which was as huge as the himālaya mountain, was denied to indra and eaten by someone else \[kriṣṇa himself\]. Out of anger of being denied the annual food, indra started raining hailstorm on gōkulam. Who ate the food and lifted the hill gōvardana for seven days, protecting the herdsmen from the hailstorm?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Just as āḻvār mentioned in the previous pāsuram “ennoppārkkut tānāyuḷan”, he demonstrates through varied examples in this pāsuram as to how emperumān helped those who are without any means, just like him \[āḻvār\]. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: imayap perumalai pōl indiranārkkitta samaya virundu uṇḍu Ar kāppār samayangaḷ kaṇḍān avai kāppan kārkkaṇḍan nānmuganōdu uṇḍāṇ ulagōdu uyir imayap perumalai pōl – being like a huge himālaya mountain indiranārkkitta – cooked for the sake of indra (lord of celestial entities) samayam virundu – usual worship uṇḍu – when he was eating (from the danger of hailstorm which happened) Ar kāppār – who saved? samayangaḷ – philosophies based on sacred texts kaṇḍān – one who saw (in the beginning) Ar – who? avai kāppān – one who protects (without their being ruined) Ar – who? kārkkaṇḍan nānmuganōdu kāppān Ar – who protects brahmā and Siva (from huge dangers)? ulagōdu uyir uṇḍān Ar – (during deluge) who swallowed and protected the worlds and the creatures (in the world)?*

**Ar kāppār** – when a favourable person such as indra himself turned into an unfavourable person and rained hailstorm, did the herdsmen protect themselves or did [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) save them? Can this not be analysed?

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) shows through the subsequent incidents that it was not only here that emperumān became a protector to those who are without means, like himself (āḻvār) but in other places too.

**samayangaḷ kaṇḍān Ar** – just as it has been mercifully mentioned in śrī rangarāja stavam 2-1 “*mānam pradīpamiva kāruṇikō dadāti* ” ([sarvēśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) who is like an ocean of mercy offers vēdas (sacred texts) like a lamp), who has seen the philosophy of vēdas and spread it in the world?

**samayangaḷ** – instead of saying it in the singular (*samayam*) why is [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) saying it in plural (*samayangaḷ*)? Since the sacred texts show that there are different end goals such as *aiśvaryam* (going after wealth), *kaivalyam* (ātmā enjoying itself) and *bhagavat prāpti* (attaining [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) and there are different means to attain them such as *bhakti* (devotion towards emperumān), *prapatti* (surrendering to emperumān), *tirunāma sangīrttanam* (reciting the divine names of emperumān) etc, these can be considered as a collection of different philosophies and usage of plural noun is acceptable. Since this is quoted as proof of helping those without any means, we cannot construe this to mean other religious beliefs.

**avai kāppān Ar** – who protects these philosophies so that they do not get ruined? This act is also carried out to protect those who are helpless.

**kārkkaṇḍan nānmuganōdu kāppān Ar** – Who protects brahmā, rudra et al when they are troubled by rāvaṇa et al and become helpless and who removes their sorrows?  Alternatively

**kārkkaṇḍan nāmuganōdu uṇḍān ulagōdu uyir** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that during deluge, when very knowledgeable people such as brahmā, rudra et al, along with other jīvātmās (souls) were caught and were wandering here and there \[not knowing how to protect themselves\] without any distinction between them, it was [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who kept them safely in his stomach and protected them. [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) mentioned mercifully in his periya tirumoḻi 11-6-2 “*ellārum aṟīyārō emperumān uṇḍumiḻnda eccil dēvarallādār tāmuḷarē*” (doesn’t everyone know that there is no deity who has not been swallowed during deluge and has later been spat out?)

**ulagōdu uyir uṇḍān Ar** – Who kept all the worlds and the cētanas \[and acētanas\] living in those worlds in his stomach and protected them? It was based on this that [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) (one of our preceptors) wrote  mercifully in his [stōtra ratnam 11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/12/sthothra-rathnam-11/) “*kasyōdarē haravirincimukha: prapanca:*” (in whose divine stomach brahmā, rudra, the universe took shelter).

We will take up the 88<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 88. Uyir kondu

*udal oḻiya – leaving te body behind uyir koṇḍu – taking te prāṇa (vital air) ōdum pōdu – vhen (tis ātmā) runs ōdi – (emperumān himself) runs ayarvu enṟa – everyting vhic is considered as a difficulty tīrppān – emperumān vho removes pēr – divine names pādi – reciting heartily seyal tīra – tere is noting tat ve need to do (pravrutti) as means sindittu – understanding te basic nature vāḻvārē – only tose vho comtemplate to live vāḻvār – vill prosper siṟu samayam pandanaiyār – tose vho hold on to means vhic are lovly, vhic are conditional and vhic are te reason for being bound to samsāram (materialistic realm) vāḻvu ēl – any prosperity tat it may beget paḻudu – is useless*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**uyir koṇḍu udal aḻiya ōdumpōdu – when this ātmā, propelled by the messengers of yama (deity of righteousness), runs with its vital airs, after shedding the body**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār continues with the benefits that emperumān carries out for his followers who are without any means, in  this pāasuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: uyir koṇḍu udaloḻiya ōdumpōdōdi ayarvenṟa tīrppān pēr pādi seyal tīrac cindittu vāḻvārē vāḻvār siṟu samayap pandanaiyār vāḻvēl paḻudu*

**ōdi** – there is an entity \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] who will be witnessing the troubles of his followers. Instead of sending his servitors to remove the troubles (just like a person appoints a representative to carry out deeds ordained by śāstram such as sandyāvandanam etc), this entity will run to remove the troubles of his followers, himself.

**ōdi** – his (that entity’s) deed will be one with which his followers will be involved, as mentioned by [śrī paraśāra bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) in śrī rangarājastavam 2-57 “*bhagavatas tvarāyai nama:*” (I salute the speed of emperumān) \[reference here is to the act of emperumān who came running to save the life of his follower gajēndra (an elephant) who was troubled by a crocodile\].

**ayarvenṟa tīrppān** – he will remove all the troubles of his follower who is under the control of messengers of yama. He removes the trouble undergone by his follower when he is shivering at the mere sight of yama’s messengers, when he is dragged out of his body by yama’s messengers etc.

**ayarvenṟa** – The opinion here is that , in reality, only that which is experienced by the follower at the hands of messengers of yama is apt to be termed as trouble. All the others are joyous experience only, when compared to this.

**tīrppān pēr pādi** – heartily reciting the divine names of the entity who removes the trouble.

**seyal tīrac cidittu vāḻvārē vāḻvār** – only those who live considering [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself as the means \[for attaining him\], rejecting all other means which are to be carried out by them, will prosper.

**seyal tīrac cindittu vāḻvār** – these are the people who think that there is nothing that they can do in order to get rid of their troubles and to experience utmost happiness. These are the people who think that only emperumān, who comes running to protect them when they are at the hands of yama’s messengers, is the means. These are the people who have been referred to by [toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) in his [tirumālai 38](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/12/thirumalai-38-part-3/) as “*kadaittalaiyirundu vāḻum sōmbar*” (those who have given the responsibility of looking after them to emperumān).

Till now, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) has been talking about the greatness of those who are without any means. Now he talks about the lowliness of those who attempt to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) on their own.

**siṟu samayap pandaniyār vāḻvēl paḻudu** – there is no benefit to those who attempt to follow the lowly means of *karma yōgam* (path of carrying out ordained deeds), *jñāna yōgam* (path of knowledge leading to carrying out those deeds), *bhakti yōgam* (path of devotion towards emperumān) which are to be observed over very long periods of time, referred to as “*janmāntara sahasrēśu*” (thousands of successive births) and which lead to cultivation of ego in the mind of samsāri. This is opposite of his *svabhāvam* (basic quality) of being a dependent servitor.

**siṟusamayam** – lowly means unlike śrīman nārāyaṇa as mentioned in nārāyaṇa sūktam  “*nārāyaṇam pārāyaṇam*” (nārāyaṇa who is the superior means).

**samayap pandanai** – the opinion here is that these are means which bind the jīvātmā (soul) to samsāram (materialistic realm) but which go in the name of being the means for attaining mōkśam; in reality, these means take the jīvātmā far away from matters relating to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) for a very long time, just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mentioned in periya tiruvandādi 6 “*neṟi kātti nīkkudiyō*” (will you reject me by showing me other means?)

**vāḻvēl paḻudu** – if one analyses, it would be clear that there is no benefit for these jīvātmās by following such means.

**samayam** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) refers to these means as *samayam* since they have been classified based on the interest of each jīvātmā. It should be understood here that these jīvātmās, who are *siṟusamayap pandanaiyār,* are firmly engaged with *pravrutti mārgam* (the path of carrying out deeds on one’s own efforts to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) as opposed to *nivrutti mārgam* (in which the jīvātmā entrusts the responsibility of making him attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with emperumān himself) which is indicated by the words *seyal tīrac cindittu vāḻvār*. It should not be taken here that āḻvār is talking about jivātmās who are following other philosophies since āḻvār has already corrected these jīvātmās who are following other philosophies through his instructions and there is no need to talk ill of them now. One of the vyākyātas for this prabandam, śrī appiḷḷai, says here that through the verse *pandanaiyār vāḻvēl paḻudu* āḻvār is rejecting the other means since even for those who follow *karmayōgam* etc, unless emperumān is convinced, he will not grant mōkśam and also due to the fact that many persons such as *kśatrabandu*, *gantākarṇan* et al reached mōkśam without carrying out any karmayōgam.

We will move on to the 89<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 89. Paludagada onru

*(Hear me, as to vhat it is) pāl kadalān pādam – te divine feet of kśīrābdināta (lord of milky ocean) vaḻuvā vagai ninaindu – meditating vitout any error toḻuvārai – tose vho constantly vorśip kaṇḍu – (reacing and) having darśan of (seeing) iṟainji – vorśipping vāḻvār – tose vho live (devotees of emperumān’s follovers) kalanda vinai keduttu – getting rid of te bad deeds connected vit ātmā (soul) viṇ tiṟandu – opening te entrance to paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) mikku – vit greatness vīṟṟiruppār – vill be residing*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**paḻudāgādonṟu aṟindēn – I knew of the superior means which will not go useless. The means which will not go useless are: 1. Attaining emperumān; 2. Attaining followers of emperumān; 3. Taking shelter under the wisdom of followers of emperumān. All these three means are very distinguished. Of these three, āḻvār is mercifully describing the second means (of attaining the followers of emperumān) in this pāsuram and the first (attaining emperumān) and third means (totally devoted to the wisdom of emperumān’s followers ) in the next pāsuram.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Unlike means such as karmayōgam which are of no use, is there any means which is without any fault? āḻvār responds saying that attaining emperumān and attaining emperumān’s followers are faultless means. He says that of the two, attaining emperumān’s followers is superior. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: paḻudāgādonṟaṟindēn pāṟkadalān pādam vaḻuvā vagai ninaindu vaigal toḻuvārai kaṇḍiṟainji vāḻvār kalanda vinai keduttu viṇḍiṟandu vīṟṟiruppār mikku paḻudu āgādu onṟu – faultless (superior) means aṟindēn – I knew*

What is the means which is faultless?

**pāṟkadalān pādam** – the divine feet of the entity who, leaving aside paramapadam which is the place for enjoyment, takes residence in the milky ocean in order to get his followers.

**vaḻuvā vagai ninaindu** – meditating without slipping on it. In other words, keeping them as both the means and the benefit for attainment. īsn’t asking for lowly materials from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), after attaining him, a faulty way of asking, much similar to asking for loin cloth from *kaṟpaga vrukśam* (wish fulfilling tree)? īsn’t begging for other benefits a wrong methodology? Instead, thinking of those divine feet as means and goal.

**vaigal toḻuvārai** – will not the sweetness of the matter (the divine feet of emperumān) be such that if they are vorśipped once, they will make the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) to vorśsip every day?

**toḻuvāraikkaṇḍu** – seeking the followers of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), on their own. With this, the difference between these people and the distinguished entities who will be mentioned in the next pāsuram is indicated. Just as the saying goes “*prajāpatim tvō vēda*” these people will go on their own and attain the followers of emperumān. The ones who will be mentioned in the next pāsuram are those who will be worthy of being under the wisdom of emperumān’s followers.

**kaṇḍu iṟainji vāḻvār** – those who prosper by heartily seeing the followers of emperumān and vorśsipping them. appiḷḷai, one of the vyākyātas for this prabandam will say that *iṟainji* would mean those who bow down their heads fully.

**kalanda vinai keduttu viṇ tiṟandu vīṟṟipruppār mikku** – getting rid of the sins which are inseparably intertwined with ātmā, these people will be servitors to nityasūris who are the heads of those who carry out kainkaryam to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), and who have unlimited knowledge and devotion (towards emperumān), in paramapadam.

**mikku vīṟṟiruppar** – they will reside such that their knowledge and devotion will be explicitly manifested.

**vīṟṟruppar** – they will reside with their excellence fully displayed. They will be fully servile to the great followers of emperumān who will be superior to nityasūris in carrying out kainkaryam to emperumān.

We will move on to the 90<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 90. Virrirundu

*vīṟṟirundu – being vit greatness (in tis vorld) viṇ – paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) āḷa vēṇḍuvār – one vho viśes to rule vēngadattān pāl – tovards tiruvēngadamudaiyān (lord of tiruvēngadam) pal malargaḷ – different types of flovers tirunda – in a good manner \[folloving te procedure mentioned in śāstras\] vaittārē – offered varum madi pārttu – knoving te toughts (in emperumān’s divine mind) anbinar āy – being vit devotion maṟṟavarkkē – to tat emperumān only tāḻvu āy iruppār – being servitors tamar – tose vho are tought of vit respect mēl tirunda vāḻvār – vill live vit more distinction tan tose vho have been mentioned in te earlier part.*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vīṟṟirundu viṇṇāḷa vēṇḍuvār – they will be fully immersed in being a servitor to emperumān’s followers, with their eminence fully on display, with a desire to get the benefit of carrying out service to those in paramapadam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār mentioned about attaining emperumān’s followers as one of the faultless means \[for attaining emperumān\] in the previous pāsuram. When asked “could you not mercifully tell us the other faultless means too?” āḻvār says in this pāsuram that “those who attain emperumān and those who are in the caramōpāyam (ultimate means) of being fully engaged with the followers of emperumān are distinguished people”. In matters relating to followers of emperumān, svagata svīkāram (attaining through one’s own efforts) was mentioned in the previous pāsuram. paragata svīkāram (attaining through the causeless mercy of emperumān) in the matters relating to followers of emperumān is mentioned in the 18th pāsuram of this prabandam (māṟāya dānavanai) and in this pāsuram. Has it not been mentioned in bhāradvāja samhitai that just as in the matter related to emperumān, there are also the same two types, namely svagata svīkāram and paragata svīkāram in matters related to emperumān’s followers and that these two are faultless means through the verse  “guruṇā yō’bhimanyēta gurum vā yōbhimanyatē I tāvubhau paramām siddim niyamādubhagaccata: īī ” (both the one who is respected by the teacher and the one who respects  the teacher attain the ultimate benefit of mōkśam without fail)! Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: vīṟṟirundu viṇṇāḷa vēṇḍuvār vēngadattān pāl tirunda vaittārē panmalargaḷ mēl tirunda vāḻvār varumadi pārttu anbinarāy maṟṟavarkkē tāḻvāy iruppār tamar*

**vēngadattān pāl tirunda vaittārē panmalargaḷ** – they are the people who have kept different types of flowers, without slipping on the procedure mentioned in śāstras (sacred texts) and vorśipped tiruvēngadattān who has taken residence in tiruvēngadam in order to uplift simple people. They have carried out service to emperumān \[here\] and will get to serve him in paramapadam too.

Next āḻvār mercifully says that engaging with followers of emperumān fully is superior to this attainment through emperumān (as mentioned in the earlier verse).

**varu madi pārttu** – Just as it is mentioned in the *vēdavākhyam* (line from vēdas) “*yēna yēna dātā gaccati tēna tēna saha gaccati*” (in whichever way emperumān thinks, he \[one who has reached *paramapadam* after getting liberated from materialistic realm\] also thinks in the same way, knowing forehand what emperumān thinks in his divine mind).

**anbinarāy maṟṟavarkkē tāḻvāyiryuppār** – they will be devoted to appropriate kainkaryam to emperumān and be servile to him.

**tāḻvāy iruppār tamar mēl tirunda vāḻvār** – they are the people who  are respected as “he is our person” by emperumān’s followers. āḻvār says that more than attaining emperumān, they will lead a very happy life serving the followers of emperumān, which is much superior to the former. The opinion here is that those who are fully engaged with emperumān’s followers will get to serve the followers of emperumān, in line with the saying “*yatōpāsanam tatā palam*”( benefit is related to one’s devotion).

We will move on to the 91<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 91. Tamar avar

*mālvaṇṇan – emperumān vit dark complexion tāḷ tāmarai – at his divine, lotus-like feet malargaḷ ittu – offering flovers iṟainji – vorśipping tāḷ tāmarai adaivōm enṟu – vill attain tose divine, lotus-like feet tamar āvār – tose vho are follovers tāmarai mēlārkkum – to brahmā vho vas born in te lotus śooting out of \[emperumān’s\] navel ādu aravu ārttāṟkum – to śiva vho has tied (to his body) snakes vhic are dancing amararkkum – to nityasūris (permanent dvellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam) yāvarkkum – and to all oters amarargaḷ – vill be deities*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**tāḷ tāmarai malargaḷ ittu – without looking at what type of flower it is, offering any type of flower at the divine feet of emperumān. Just as it is mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 1-6-1 “purivadum pugai pūvē” there is no flower which is disliked by emperumān. Alternatively, this can be construed as “superior lotus flower for the superior, divine feet of emperumān”. This activity (of offering flowers to emperumān) is one involving the body, also called as kāyika seyal (serving emperumān through the body) \[malargaḷ ittu\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that for those who follow these three means (please refer to the introduction given under pāsurams 89 and 90), devotion to emperumān is a common denominator and that these people are superior to those who dwell in both nitya vibhūti (spiritual realm) and leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: tamarāvār yāvarkkum tāmarai mēlārkkum amararkkum ādaravārttāṟkum amarargaḷ tāḷ tāmarai malargaḷittiṟainji mālvaṇṇan tāḷ tāmarai adaivōm enṟu*

**iṟainji** – praising him whole heartedly. This is an activity of speech (praising).

**māl vaṇṇan tāḷ tāmarai adaivōm enṟu tamarāvār** – those who are totally devoted to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) thinking that they would attain the divine lotus-like feet of emperumān who has a dark complexion. This is the activity of mind (thinking).

**adaivōm enṟu tamarāvār** – meditating through the mind, keeping only emperumān who said “surrender to me” as the means.

**tamarāvār** – these people will be servile to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) through all three faculties of mind, speech and body \[as mentioned above\].

**tāmarai mēlāṟkum** – brahmā who stayed on the lotus and created the worlds; the lotus, which is the origin for creation of all the worlds.

**ādaravārttāṟkum** – Siva, the deity for annihilation, who has tied down many snakes, which keep dancing, as ornaments.

**yāvarkkum** – to all the other dēvas (celestial entities) such as indra, candra et al and to all the people. This indicates all the entities who are in *leelā vibhūti* (materialistic realm)

**amararkkum** – to the indefatigable nityasūris. This indicates all the entities in the *nitya vibhūti* (spiritualistic realm).

**amarargaḷ** – these people are superior to all the entities in both the realms.

**amarargaḷ** – deities; very pious people. ḍidn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too praise such people in his tiruviruttam 79 “*sīdanaiyē toḻuvār vinnuḷārilum sīriarē*” (those who worship only [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) are superior to even nityasūris who dwell in paramapadam) and in tiruvāimoḻi 7-10-11 “*dēvar vaigal tīrttangalē enṟu pūsittu nalgi uraippar tam dēviyarkkē* ” (nityasūris will tell their consorts constantly, and with joy that such people are very pious)!

We will take up the 92<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 92. Enrum marandariyen

*karu irunda nāḷ mudalā – from te time ī vas in te vomb kāppu – since ī received protection (from emperumān) tiru enṟum irunda mārvan sirīdaranukku – to tat tirumāḷ vho has pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) residing permanently on his cest āḷ āy – being a servitor nedu mālai – tat supreme being en nenjattu – in my heart vaittu – firml establiśing ninṟum irundum enṟum – in all states suc as standing, sitting etc maṟandaṟiyēn – ī kept tinking (of him) vitout forgetting*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**enṟum maṟandaṟiyēn en nenjattē vaittu ninṟum irundum – keeping sarvēśvaran in my heart without any distinction between whether I am standing or sitting, i.e. at all times, and not forgetting him ever. This is similar to nammāḻvār mercifully saying in tiruvāimoḻi 9-6-10 “ennil munnam pārittut tān ennai muṟṟum paruginān ” (before I could enjoy him, emperumān devoured me fully) and in tiruvāimoḻi 10-10-10 “adanil periya en avāvaṟac cūḻndāyē ” (emperumān made his desire to be with āḻvār much bigger than the desire of āḻvār, and joined āḻvār with his consorts, primordial matter, nityasūris et al ).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār affirms that he is one of emperumān’s followers. He says that he has never forgotten emperumān at any time, since emperumān has been protecting him from the time that he (āḻvār) has been in the womb. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: enṟum maṟandaṟiyēn en nenjattē vaittu ninṟum irundum nedumālai enṟum tiruvirunda mārban sirīdaranakku āḷāy karu irunda nāḷ mudalāk kāppu*

**ninṟum irundum** – just as it is mentioned in nāradīyam “*āsīnā vā śayānā vā tiśtantō yatra kutra vā* I *namō nārāyaṇēti mantraika śaraṇā vayam* īī ” (we have taken tirumantram as our refuge whether we are sitting anywhere, or standing or lying down, without any disctinction), this implies that even when carrying out normal activities such as standing or sitting, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) is fully meditating on nārāyaṇa.

Is it only that āḻvār did not forget [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) ever? He says further that he was engaging himself fully with service to emperumān ….

**enṟum tiruvirunda mārvan sirīdaranukku āḷāy** – śrīdēvi (śrī mahālakśmi) is always residing in the divine chest of emperumān as mentioned in [śrī viṣṇu sahasranāmam](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/srivishnu-sahasranamam/) “*nitya śrī:*” (always with śrī mahālakśmi) and in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/06/thiruvaimozhi-6-10-10-agalagillen-part-1/) “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum enṟu alarmēl mangai uṟai mārbā*” (Oh emperumān who has on your chest śrī mahālakśmi, who says that she will not separate from you even for a moment!) in order to ensure that followers attain the divine feet of emperumān the moment they cry out for it. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that he was able to carry out service to [śrīdaran](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the  one who has [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) on his divine chest.

**enṟum tiruvidunda mārvan sirīdaran** – while other entities may be called as śrīmān as a term of respect  even though they have no connection with śrī mahālakśmi (just as a worm, which has no connection with the fruit mango is called as mango-worm), in the case of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) is always residing in his chest and hence he is aptly called as sirīdaran.

**sirīdaranukku āḷāy** – just as [tiruppallāṇḍu 11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/11/thiruppallandu-11-al-vazhakku/) says “*tirumālē nānum unakkup paḻavadiyēn*” (Oh śriman nārāyaṇa! I have been your servitor for a very long time) [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) implies that the meaning of receptacle of kainkaryam is only the couple \[in other words, kainkaryam (service) is carried out only to emperumān and pirātti together) and that the reason for his attaining such distinction is only because of pirātti’s mercy.

What is the reason for not forgetting [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and for carrying out service to him?

**karu irunda nāḷ mudalāk kāppu** – āḻvār says that right from the time when he was inside (his mother’s) womb, emperumān had blessed him, made him as his follower and protected him.

**karu irunda nāḷ mudalāk kāppu** – I received this huge distinction because [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) showered his mercy on me from the time I was in the womb, and protected me. Has it not been mentioned in mahābhāratam mōkśa parvam “*jāyamānam hi puruśam yam paśyēt madusūdana:* I *sātvikas sa tu vijñēya: sa vai mōkśārta cintaka:* īī ” (the one who is blessed by emperumān even as he is born is known to be full of sātvika (composed of purely good qualities); only he will think about mōkśam)! Did not [poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) and [bhūdattāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) too mercifully proclaim that they were blessed even when they were inside the womb, as mentioned in [mudal tiruvandādi 6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-6/) “*karuvarangatkidandu kai toḻudēn kaṇḍēn*” (while lying inside the womb, I saluted emperumān and saw him) and in [iraṇḍām tiruvandādi 87](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/02/irandam-thiruvandhadhi-87/) “*karukkōttiyutkidandu kai toḻudēn kaṇḍēn*” (same as mentioned above)!

We will move on to the 93<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 93. Kappu marandariyen

*(once again) ākkai koduttu – giving physical forms aḷitta – one vho gave mercifully kōnē – ōh benefactor! kāppu – te protection (tat you are giving mericully) maṟandaṟiyēn – ī vill never forget kaṇṇanē enṟiruppan – ī vill be very firm tat it is only kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) \[vho is bot means and goal\] guṇam – by te auspicious qualities paranē – ōh te supreme lord! mey teḷindār tām – tose vho knov (te basic nature) truly and clearly unnai vida tuṇiyār – vill not let go of you*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**kāppu maṟandaṟiyēn – Oh emperumān! Did you think that I have forgotten the protection that you have been giving me right from the time that I was inside the womb? Is there any way by which I can forget you when you have been my protector from the day that I was formed?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān asks of āḻvār “āḻvīr! You are talking about past when you say maṟandaṟiyēn. Is it not possible to forget in the future?” āḻvār responds “Oh benefactor! For those who know your protection and your nature, is it possible to ever forget you?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: kāppu maṟandaṟiyēn kaṇṇanē enṟiruppan āppangaḻiyavum palluyirkkum ākkai koduttaḷitta kōnē guṇaparanē unnai vidat tuṇiyār mey teḷindār tām angu – during the time of that deluge pal uyirkkum – for all ātmās (souls) āppu oḻiyavum – even if the body and the senses had got destroyed*

When asked whether he would not forget if he goes after other means and worldly matters…

**kaṇṇanē enṟiruppan** – Is there any possibility for me to go after other means and other goals when you, who incarnated as kriṣṇa, are the means and the goal? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) asks further whether it is possible to give up emperumān if one meditates on the benefits that he has given and his auspicious qualities.

**āppangu oḻiyavum palluyirkkum ākkai koduttu aḷitta kōnē** – Oh svāmy (lord)! You gave forms to all the souls and protected them when they were struggling after losing their bodies and senses \[during the time of deluge\]. *āppu* – the body and senses are referred to by the term *āppu* since they are the implements with which the soul is bound to samsāram (materialistic realm).

**kōnē** – This implies that since he is the ‘owner’ he has protected the owned.

**āppangaoḻiyavum . . . .kōnē** – Is it possible to go away from you after seeing the way you had been protecting from the initial days? Is it possible to go away from you after seeing your being the lord?

**guṇapparanē** – Just as it is mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-1-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/03/thiruvaimozhi-1-1-1-uyarvara-uyarnalam/) “*uyarvaṟa uyar nalam udaiyavan*” one who is much superior to all due to your auspicious qualities!  Is it possible to leave you after seeing your auspicious qualities?

**unnai vidat tuṇiyār mey teḷindār tām** – those who know you truly, will never think of leaving you ever.

**unnai . . . tām** – can we leave you saying that you are not the protector? Can we leave you saying that you are not the benefactor? Can we leave you saying that you have faulty qualities? Can we leave you saying that you are not the ordained lord? How do we leave you?

We will move on to the 94<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 94. Mey telindar

*(tus reducing te heavy load on eart) teḷinda pāmbin aṇaiyāy – ōh ḷord, vho reaced te sveet mattress of ādiśēśan vho has vell spread hoods adiyēṟku – for me, your servitor aruḷāy – śov your grace ēnṟu – if one desires to have it vēmbum kaṟiyāgum – even te leaves of neem tree are fit to be cooked and eaten mey teḷindār – tose vho knov te trut ‘as it is’ en seyyār – vhat vill tey not do? (tey vill accompliś vhatever tey tink of)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**mey teḷindār en seyyār – What will those, who know the truth as it is and who know as to what will complement their qualities, not do? Oh emperumān! Would protecting those without any fault bring you laurels or would protecting someone like me, with faults, bring you laurels? Once you analyse this, what protective action will you not do for me? Alternative meaning: If you analyse your nature of protecting those who attain you, what will you not do for me? āren’t you one who desires to protect those with faults, just as you did for rāvaṇa, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇa yudda kāṇḍam 18-3 “yadivā rāvaṇa: svayam” (if it is rāvaṇa himself) so that you would get the fame for protecting those with faults?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān asks āḻvār “Even if you do not give me up, what will you do if I give you up, after seeing your faults?” āḻvār responds saying “Oh emperumān! You are speaking forgetting your basic nature. If you were to analyse yourself, the epitome of protecting those who have attained you, not only have you been protecting me all this time, but you will continue to protect me until the entity called time exists. If one knows that it is the reason for good health, even the \[bitter\] leaves of neem would be eaten as a desirable vegetable. In the same way, you must also consider me in your divine mind” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: mey teḷindār enseyyār vēṟānār nīṟāga kai teḷindu kāttik kaḷappaduttu paiteḷinda pāmbin aṇaiyāy aruḷāy adiyēṟku vēmbum kaṟiyāgum ēnṟu vēṟu ānār – duryōdana et al, who are different from the rest (being inimical) nīṟu āga – to be annihilated to become ashes teḷindu – deciding (that it is correct to kill them in the matter relating to followers) kai kātti – providing all assistance (to the pāṇḍavas) kaḷam paduttu – killing those enemies (in the war)*

emperumān further asks [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) “Where did you see that I protect those who surrender to me?” āḻvār says further that he had seen it in the case of protecting pāṇḍavas . .

**vēṟānār** – duryōdana et al who hate pāṇḍavas, and whom [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is the father for all the words, out of disgust, called as “*enemies*”

**vēṟānār nīṟāga** – making duryōdana et al to become a fistful of ashes

**teḷindu kaikātti** – instead of feeling agitated thinking “Oh, I have to kill them”, deciding firmly that enemies of those who attain him are fit to be killed and helping the followers by conducting the army (in the varfront) etc.

**kaikātti** – it can also be construed as “revealing their secrets”

**kaḷappaduttu** – making them lose their lives in the battlefield.

**pai teḷinda pāmbin aṇaiyāy** – Oh one who reclined on his divine mattress in order to rest awhile after removing the hurdles of his followers!  Oh one who reclined on the divine mattress of ādiśēśa whose hoods became cool and comfortable on contacting with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)!

**aruḷāy adiyēṟku** – just as you protected those “apt five” (the five pāṇḍavas), who are with good qualities and who had something in them, you should also protect me who has no good qualities and who has nothing in him. You will win more laurels in protecting me than in protecting them.

When [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) asks “But how can I desire you, who have faults?” . . . .

**vēmbum kaṟiyāgum ēnṟu** – if one were to analyse its qualities such as being good for health etc, even the bitter leaves of neem tree \[which are liked by no one\] would be cooked and eaten.

**mey teḷindār en seyyār** – if one knows your true nature, what is amazing about what you will do in future just as there is nothing amazing in what you have done in the past! This is similar to what has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-36 “*kimatra citram darmajña lōkanāta sukhāvaha* I *yat tvamāryam prabhāśētā: satvavān satpatē stita:*  īī” (Oh one who knows what is righteousness! Oh the lord of the world! Oh one who gives comforts! What is amazing about your saying such a good word when you are standing in the correct path!)

We will take up the 95<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 95. Enren adimai

*adimai – servitorśip ēnṟēn – ī took upon piṟappu idumbai – ahankāram (ego) and mamakāram (possessiveness) vhic result from birt iḻindēn – ī got rid of amararkku – for dēvas suc as brahmā et al amarāmai – not to approac anyvhere near me ānṟēn – ī vas filled up (vit knovledge, devotion etc) kadan nādum – places suc as svarga (heaven) etc maṇ nādum – and tis eart kai vittu – ridding of tem mēlai – superior to everyting else idam – place (appropriate for follovers) nādu – te divine paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) kāṇa – to see and enjoy ini – nov ānṟēn – ī am filled up (vit paramabhakti (inability to live if separated from emperumān))*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**ēnṟēn adimai – I established myself firmly in carrying out servitude to emperumān  without any break. Even though there was an inclination towards servitude even earlier, it was limited due to connection with prakruti (samsāram), similar to the inclination of a patient towards medicine. Now the inclination towards servitude has become unbounded.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Just as emperumān showers his grace on āḻvār as requested (in the previous pāsuram), āḻvār speaks about how he got iśta prāpti (granting of desirables) and aniśta nivrutti (removal of undesirables), in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: ēnṟēn adimai iḻindēn piṟappidumbai ānṟēn amararkku amarāmai ānṟēn kada nādum maṇṇādum kaivittu mēlai ida nādu kāṇa ini*

What is the reason for being inclined towards servitude now?

**iḻindēn piṟappidumbai** – I was able to remove the hurdles of *ahankāram* and *mamakāram* (ego and possessiveness respectively)

**iḻindēn** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says that *ahankāram* and *mamakāram* were like a huge mountain and he climbed down from that mountain.

**ānṟēn amararkku amarāmai** – I filled myself with knowledge and devotion to such an extent that instead of my going to other dēvatās (deities), now the other dēvatās would feel shy to approach me.

**kadanādum maṇṇādum kaivittu mēlai ida nādu kāṇa ini** – giving up *svarga* (which is the land for enjoying the results of virtuous deeds carried out in earlier birth) and earth, which is the place for carrying out sacred deeds, I filled myself with *paramabhakti* (this is the ultimate state among three states of devotion in which the person will not be able to live even for a moment if separated from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) so that I could see the ancient and spacious paramapada*m*. *kadan nādu* – svargam (heaven) wherein one has to necessarily enjoy the result of *puṇya* (virtuous deeds) just as one has to necessarily repay the loan taken from someone. *idanādu* – paramapadam as mentioned in śrī rangarāja stavam 1-38 “*sarvātma sādāraṇa nātagōśī pūrē’pi duśpūra mahāyakāśam* ” (even if all the groups of emperumān’s followers, which are common to all ātmās, get together, there will still be space in paramapadam).

We will next move on to the last pāsuram in this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 96. Iniyarinden

*(all tese) nān nangu aṟindēn – ī knev very vell*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**iniyaṟindēn īsaṟkum nānmugaṟkum deyvam – I have now seen that you are the Lord for Siva, who is famously known as īsan (one who controls) and brahmā, who has four heads. I have seen now something which I had known only through śāstras (sacred texts). In any literary composition, since there is a rule that  the beginning and end should be congruent, āḻvār speaks about the same context that he had started with in the first pāsuram “nānmuganai nārāyaṇan padaittān nānmuganum tān mugamāy sankaranaittān padaittān ” (nārāyaṇa created brahmā and brahmā, with nārāyaṇa as his indvelling soul, created siva).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*The root cause for knowing everything, for jīvātmā, at the time of attaining liberation, is blossoming of knowledge. āḻvār mentions how he got that and brings this prabandam to its end. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: iniyaṟindēn īsaṟkum nānmugaṟkum deyvam iniyaṟindēn emperumān unnai iniyaṟindēn kāraṇan nī kaṟṟavai nī kaṟpavai nī naṟkirisai nāraṇan nī nangaṟindēn nān emperumān – Oh emperumān! (my Lord) īsaṟkum – to rudra nānmagaṟkum – to brahmā deyvam – as Lord iniyaṟindēn – now, I know (you) firmly ini – now unnai aṟindēn – I know your basic nature (truly) kāraṇan nī – you are the causative entity (for the universe) kaṟṟavai nī – you are all the entities learnt before kaṟpavai nī – you are all the entities to be learnt in future nal kirisai – one who carries out protecting all without any reason nāraṇan nī – you are nārāyaṇa*

**iniyaṟindēn emperumān unnai** – now I have seen you, as you are, directly.

What did you see?

**kāraṇan nī** – I knew that you are the causative entity for all the universe.

**kaṟṟavai nī kaṟpavai nī** – whatever entities I have known before and now, after having my knowledge blossomed (due to your grace), whatever entities I will know in future, are all following  your commands.

**naṟkirisai nāraṇan nī** – I knew that you are nārāyaṇa, carrying out the great deed of protecting all, without any reason, and that you are the lord of all. *nāraṇan* – one who has the quality of being the lord for all entities which cannot be obliterated.

Haven’t you known this before?

**nangaṟindēn nān** – while I had the knowledge about this through śāstram (sacred texts) now I have known this well, by seeing it directly.

This brings us to the end of this prabandam.
